# Ibn Ezra on Numbers

אבן עזרא על במדבר

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers)

פירוש פשט המשלב ביאורים המבוססים על דקדוק ובלשנות; חובר במאה ה-12.

12th-century commentary focusing on the simple meaning of the text and incorporating grammar and linguistics.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Ibn Ezra/Torah

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): On Your Way (Segments: 1111, License: Public Domain)

Version (En): Ibn Ezra's commentary on the Pentateuch, tran. and annot. by H. Norman Strickman and Arthur M. Silver. Menorah Pub., 1988-2004 (Segments: 1111)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:1:1)


**בשם** אשר פקודי רום ותחת חבר. החל לפרש על הפשט ספר וידבר **במדבר סיני.** להודיע כי לא עלה משה אל הר סיני אחר היות הכבוד באהל מועד:


In the name of God who counted those numbered above¹. and below;²I begin to explain according to the plain meaningthe Torah portion which begins with *va-yedabber*.³ (spoke). IN THE WILDERNESS. This verse⁴. informs us that Moses did not go up to Mount Sinai⁵ since God’s glory was now present in the tent of meeting.⁶

footnotes:
¹ The stars. See Ps. 147:4, He counteth the number of the stars
² Israel.
³ The Book of Numbers begins with the word va-yedabber
⁴ Which states that the Lord spoke unto Moses in the wilderness of Sinai, in the tent of meeting
⁵ For revelation.
⁶ Otherwise, what follows would have been told to Moses on Mount Sinai.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:1:2)


**באחד לחדש השני.** לתקן את הדגלים ואיך יסעו ואיך יחנו בעבור המקדש כי בעשרים לחדש השני נסעו:


ON THE FIRST DAY OF THE SECOND MONTH. To tell him to arrange the standards and to instruct him how the Israelites were to journey and how they were to camp with reference to the tabernacle,⁷ (with reference to the tabernacle) to *be’evre ha-mishkan* (on the sides of the tabernacle). for they journeyed on the twentieth day of the second month.⁸

footnotes:
⁷ Filwarg suggests emending ba’avur ha-mishkan
⁸ Hence it was necessary to inform Moses of these things prior to their journey.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:1)


**שאו את ראש.** כמו כי תשא ושם מפורש:


TAKE YE THE SUM. *Se’u et rosh bene yisra’el* (take ye the sum of all the congregation of the children of Israel) is similar to *ki tissa et rosh bene yisra’el* (when thou takest the sum of the children of Israel) (Ex. 30:12). I have explained its meaning there.⁹ literally means: when you lift up the head. One who takes a census writes the particulars after the sum, which he writes at the top of the page. Thus when Scripture speaks of raising up the head, it means take up the sum.

footnotes:
⁹ See I.E. on Exodus, (Vol. 2, p. 631). I.E. explains that ki tissa et rosh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:2)


**לבית אבותם.** כי משפחת האם אינה קרואה משפחה:


BY THEIR FAMILIES BY THEIR FATHER’S HOUSES. For pedigree is not determined by the mother.¹⁰ with *by their fathers’ houses*.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Hence Scripture qualified by their families


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:2:3)


**לגלגלותם.** פירשתיו:


BY THEIR POLLS. I have already explained the meaning of *gulgelotam* (their polls).¹¹ (their polls) literally means their skulls. In his short commentary (S.C.) on Exodus, I.E. explains that the word *gulgolet* means a head. The head is so called because it is round. Compare, *galgal* (wheel, i.e., a round object). See I.E. on Ex. 16:16 (S.C.).

footnotes:
¹¹ The word gulgelotam



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:3:1)


**בישראל.** להוציא ערב רב:


IN ISRAEL. This excludes the mixed multitude.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Foreigners who joined Israel during the Exodus. See Ex. 12:38.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:4:1)


**איש ראש.** דרך קצרה והראוי להיות איש איש:


EVERY ONE HEAD OF HIS FATHERS’ HOUSE. *Ish rosh le-vet avotav* (every one head of his fathers’ house) is an abridged phrase. The text should have read, *ish ish rosh le-vet avotav* (every one head of his fathers’ house).¹³ (a man of every tribe). Hence, in keeping with the rules of parallelism, the second part of the verse should read, *ish ish rosh le-vet avotav hu*) (every one head of his father’s house).

footnotes:
¹³ The first part of the verse reads, ish ish la-matteh



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:5:1)


**לראובן.** החל מהבכור כטעם ולא להתיחס לבכורה:


OF REUBEN. Scripture begins with Jacob’s first-born,¹⁴ as it is stated, *yet not so that he was to be reckoned in the genealogy as first-born* (I Chron. 5:1).¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁴ Even though Jacob took away Reuben’s birthright. See Gen. 49:3,4.
¹⁵ I Chronicles explains that even though Reuben lost his birthright to Joseph, he was always to be reckoned as the first-born as far as genealogy was concerned.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:10:1)


**לבני יוסף.** אחר בני לאה בעבור כבוד רחל על השפחות והחל מאפרים על דרך יעקב אבינו והקדים אפרים ומנשה על בנימין כי הם מקום יוסף ואחר כן החל מדן שהוא בכור השפחות ואחריו אשר כי השם ידע שהוא יהיה ראש החונים על דגל דן ואחר כן גד כי הוא בכור שפחת לאה:


OF THE CHILDREN OF JOSEPH. The children of Joseph are mentioned after the children of Leah because Rachel’s status was greater than that of the handmaids.¹⁶ Scripture begins with Ephraim, in keeping with the actions of our father Jacob.¹⁷ Scripture places Ephraim and Manasseh¹⁸ before Benjamin because they are in place of Joseph.¹⁹ Scripture then begins with Dan,²⁰ who was the first-born of the handmaids.²¹ Asher follows Dan²² because God knew that Asher would be the head of those who camp under the standard of Dan.²³ Scripture then mentions Gad because he was the first-born of Leah’s handmaid.²⁴

footnotes:
¹⁶ Hence Rachel’s descendants are mentioned before those of the handmaids even though the children of the handmaids precede them in birth.
¹⁷ Manassah was older than Ephraim. However, Jacob placed Ephraim before Manasseh. See Gen. 48:21.
¹⁸ Joseph’s sons.
¹⁹ Joseph was born before Benjamin.
²⁰ The son of Bilhah, Rachel’s handmaid.
²¹ Of the children born to the handmaids’. See Gen. 30:6.
²² Gad and Asher were the sons of Zilpah, Leah’s handmaid. According to Gen. 30:10-13, Gad was Zilpah’s first-born. Thus Scripture should have mentioned Gad before Asher. Hence I.E.’s comment.
²³ Asher pitched next to Dan and was therefore next in importance under that standard. Gad was third under the standard of Reuben. See Num. 2:14,27.
²⁴ See Gen. 30:10,11.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:1)


**קרואי העדה.** הטעם שהעדה לא יעשו דבר עד שיקראום:


THESE WERE THE ELECT OF THE CONGREGATION. The meaning of *keru’e ha-edah* (the elect of the congregation)²⁵ is that the congregation does nothing unless they are called.

footnotes:
²⁵ Literally, the called of the congregation. Hence I.E.’s comment.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:2)


**מטות אבותם.** הטעם על השבטים והנה כל אחד נשיא שבט:


THE TRIBES OF THEIR FATHERS. The meaning of *mattot avotam* is the tribes of their fathers. Thus each one²⁶ was prince of a tribe.

footnotes:
²⁶ Each one of those listed in verses 5-16.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:16:3)


**ראשי אלפי ישראל.** כל אחד ראש על כל אלפי השבט כי יש שר לאלף:


THEY WERE THE HEADS OF THE THOUSANDS OF ISRAEL. Each one was a leader over all the thousands that comprised a tribe, for there was also a chief over one thousand.²⁷ (chief). See Ex. 18:21, *sarei alafim* (rulers over thousands). Also see I Sam. 17:8, *sar ha-elef* (the captain of their thousand). Each one listed in verses 5-15 was the head of all the captains of the thousands of their respective tribes.

footnotes:
²⁷ The tribes were divided into units of thousands. Each of these units was headed by a sar



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:17:1)


**ויקח משה ואהרן.** עמם:


AND MOSES AND AARON TOOK. With them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:17:2)


**האנשים אשר נקבו.** שהם מפורשים כמו אשר פי ה' יקבנו:


THAT ARE POINTED OUT BY NAME. *Nikkevu* means that are pointed out. Compare, *yikkvennu* (mark out) in *Which the mouth of the Lord shall mark out* (Is. 62:2).



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:18:1)


**ויתילדו.** בקשו מתי נולדו בעבור חשבון כ' שנה אז נכתבו תולדתם:


AND THEY DECLARED THEIR PEDIGREES. They were asked for their birth dates,²⁸ (and they declared their pedigrees). I.E. translates this as: they gave their birth dates. for only those from twenty and above were to be counted. Their progeny were then recorded.²⁹. 38 reads, “Or, their progeny were recorded.” According to this interpretation, *va-yityalledu* means: and they declared their children.

footnotes:
²⁸ The Biblical text reads, va-yityalledu
²⁹ See verse 2. Vat. Ebr



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:19:1)


**ויפקדם.** משה שהוא העיקר והחל מראובן כי הוא הבכור ואחריו שמעון הנולד אחריו ואחריו גד בכור שפחת לאה והנה דגל ראובן ואחר כך דגל יהודה ואחר כך דגל אפרים ואחר כך דגל דן והחונים על כל דגל ודגל כאשר הם בחנוכת המזבח ודע כי אחר שהיו כל השבטים שנים עשר שיצא לוי והיו ליוסף בנים שנים ורוחות העולם ארבע הנה לכל רוח שלשה והנה יש ללאה חמשה בנים יחסר האחד ויהיו לה שני דגלים השלימו הדגל עם בכור שפחתה ושמוהו עם דגל ראובן שהוא גם בכור והדגל השני ליהודה והחונים עליו אחיו הקטנים ממנו ולרחל דגל בפני עצמה ואפרים מקום יוסף הוא קודם מנשה כדברי יעקב והדגל הרביעי לשפחת רחל שהוא הבכור שלה ואחריו אשר ואם הוא קטן בשנים מנפתלי בעבור היות הדגל לבן שפחת רחל שם זאת המעלה לבן שפחת לאה ואם תסתכל היטב אז תדע כי הראש הוא הדרום והזנב הוא הצפון ולא אוכל לפרש על כן ראובן בדרום כי הוא הראש ודן בצפון ודע כי לא היו גבורים בשבטים כשבט יהודה הנמשל לגור אריה וכשבט דן הנמשל כן בדברי משה על כן היו הם בראשונה ובאחרונה. והזכיר לגלגלותם בראובן גם בשמעון להורות על האחרים כי הכתוב אחז דרך קצרה:


SO DID HE NUMBER THEM. The reference is to Moses,³⁰ (so did he count them) is in the singular. Hence I.E.’s comment. who was the most important.³¹ Scripture begins³² with Reuben,³³ as he was the first-born. Simeon is next³⁴ because he was born after Reuben. Gad the first-born of Leah’s handmaid,³⁵ follows. We thus have Reuben’s standard.³⁶ Next³⁷ comes the standard of Judah,³⁸ followed by the standard of Ephraim³⁹ and the standard of Dan.⁴⁰ Those who camped around each standard did so in the same manner in which they offered the sacrifices dedicating the altar. Note, there were twelve tribes,⁴¹ for Levi was not counted and Joseph’s two sons were. Now the world has four sides.⁴² Behold, three tribes camped on each one of the four sides.⁴³ Now Leah had five sons,⁴⁴ for one of her six sons⁴⁵ was not counted. She had two standards.⁴⁶ They [Moses and Aaron] completed one of her standards⁴⁷ with [Gad] the first-born of her handmaid.⁴⁸ They placed Gad with the standard of Reuben, who was also a first-born. Judah made up the second standard [of Leah]. His younger brothers camped with him.⁴⁹ Rachel had a standard of her own.⁵⁰ Ephraim was in place of Joseph. He preceded Manasseh, in keeping with the words of Jacob.⁵¹ The fourth standard was that of Rachel’s handmaid. It was led by [Dan] her first-born.⁵² Asher followed⁵³ even though he was younger than Naphtali.⁵⁴ The son of Leah’s handmaid⁵⁵ was so honored because the standard belonged to Rachel’s handmaid.⁵⁶ If you look carefully into things, you will know that the south is the head and the north the tail.⁵⁷ I cannot explain the aforementioned.⁵⁸ Reuben was therefore placed in the south⁵⁹ because he was the head.⁶⁰ Dan was placed in the north.⁶¹ Observe, there was no one among the tribes comparable in might to Judah, who was compared to a lion’s whelp (Gen. 49:9),⁶² and to the tribe of Dan, who is likewise compared to a lion by Moses.⁶³ These tribes were thus first and last.⁶⁴ Scripture mentions *by their polls* (le-gulgelotam) with regard to Reuben⁶⁵ and also with regard to Simeon⁶⁶ to show that it was the same case with the other tribes,⁶⁷ for Scripture was being brief.

³⁰ Va-yifkedem
³¹ Actually Aaron and the heads of the tribes also participated in counting the people (see verse 44). However, Scripture here mentions only Moses, because he was the most important.
³² The count.
³³ Verse 20.
³⁴ Verse 22.
³⁵ See note 24.
³⁶ In verses 20-25. The tribes listed in verses 20-25 (Reuben, Simeon, and Gad) made up the standard of Reuben. See Num. 2:10-16.
³⁷ Verses 26-30.
³⁸ See Num. 2:3-9.
³⁹ Verses 32-37. Also see Num. 2:18-24.
⁴⁰ Verses 38-43. Also see Num. 2:25-31.
⁴¹ In verses 20-43; and in Num. 2:10-31.
⁴² North, south, east, and west.
⁴³ For purposes of symmetry.
⁴⁴ That is, five of Leah’s six sons were counted among the twelve tribes of Israel. They were Reuben, Simeon, Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun.
⁴⁵ Levi.
⁴⁶ The standards of Judah and Reuben. See Num. 2:3-16.
⁴⁷ The standard of Reuben. See Num. 2:10-16.
⁴⁸ See note 20.
⁴⁹ Issachar and Zebulun.
⁵⁰ The standard of Ephraim. See Num. 2:18-24.
⁵¹ See Gen. 48-14-20.
⁵² See Num. 2:25-31.
⁵³ That is, Asher pitched next to Dan. See Num. 2:27.
⁵⁴ The standard of Dan consisted of Dan, Asher, and Naphtali. Asher, who was younger than Naphtali, pitched next to Dan.
⁵⁵ Asher.
⁵⁶ In other words, Asher, son of the handmaid Zilpah, was pacified for being under the standard of Dan, the son of the handmaid Bilhah, by being placed before Naphtali, another son of Bilhah.
⁵⁷ The sun in its annual journey moves from north to south. The face of the sun is thus to the south while its back, the tail, is to the north (see Weiser).
⁵⁸ When I.E. uses this expression it means that he is unable or does not want to explain further.
⁵⁹ The standard of Reuben was on the south side of the tabernacle. See Num. 2:10.
⁶⁰ He was the first-born.
⁶¹ That is, at the tail of the camp. See Num. 2:25.
⁶² In Jacob’s blessing.
⁶³ See Deut. 33:22.
⁶⁴ When the tribes marched, the standard of Judah marched first and that of Dan last. See Num. 10:14-25.
⁶⁵ Verse 20.
⁶⁶ Verse 22.
⁶⁷ Even though the term is not mentioned with regard to the other tribes.



### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:45:1)


ופירוש **ויהיו כל פקודי בני ישראל.** שלא נפקדו רק מי שהוא מבן עשרים שנה ואחר כך ויהיו כל הפקודים בכללם:


[AND ALL THOSE THAT WERE NUMBERED OF THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL BY THEIR FATHERS’ HOUSES, FROM TWENTY YEARS OLD AND UPWARD.] It means that only those who were aged twenty and above were counted. Scripture then goes on to say,⁶⁸ *even all those that were numbered*. The latter refers to their⁶⁹ total number.⁷⁰

footnotes:
⁶⁸ In verse 46.
⁶⁹ Those 20 and older.
⁷⁰ In other words, our verse speaks of the age of those numbered, while verse 46 speaks of the total number of those who were counted.



### Verse 47

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:47:1)


**התפקדו.** משני בנינים. כי הוא בנין התפעל ועם בנין שלא נקרא שם פועלו:


WERE NOT NUMBERED AMONG THEM. The word *hotpakdu* (were not numbered) is of two forms. It is both a *hitpa’el* and a *hofal*.⁷¹ and *tav* placed in front of the root is the sign of the *hitpa’el*. The *kamatz katan* placed beneath the *heh* is the sign of the *hofal*.

footnotes:
⁷¹ The heh



### Verse 49

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:49:1)


**אך את מטה לוי.** פירוש לא התפקדו כי השם צוה כן:


HOWBEIT THE TRIBE OF LEVI THOU SHALT NOT NUMBER. This explains *were not numbered* (v. 47),⁷² that the Levites were not numbered because this is what God commanded.

footnotes:
⁷² Otherwise, why inform us once again that the Levites were not counted.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:49:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:49:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:49:2)


**ואת ראשם לא תשא.** כללם כאשר פירשתי:


NEITHER SHALT THOU TAKE THE SUM OF THEM. Their total number, as I have explained.⁷³ (neither shalt thou take the sum of them). This literally means *neither shalt thou lift up their heads*. I.E. earlier noted that *to lift up the head* means to write the total sum at the top of a page. See I.E.’s comment on verse 2.

footnotes:
⁷³ Scriptures reads, ve-et rosham lo tissa



### Verse 50

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:50:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:50:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/1:50:1)


**ואתה הפקד את הלוים.** באר למה לא התפקדו כי פקודת המשכן עליהם על כן לא יצאו בצבא:


BUT APPOINT THOU THE LEVITES OVER THE TABERNACLE. Scripture explains here why the Levites were not counted. They were not counted because the charge of the tabernacle was upon them. Therefore they did not serve in the army.⁷⁴

footnotes:
⁷⁴ I.E. believes that those who were counted were counted for military purposes. This is also the view of Rashbam. See Rashbam on verse 2.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:50:2

**משכן העדת.** הוא אהל מועד והמשכן לארון ששם העדות:


THE TABERNACLE OF THE TESTIMONY. The reference is to the tent of meeting and the tabernacle of the ark, which contains the testimony.⁷⁵) (Weiser). *Vat. Ebr*. 38 reads: The tabernacle of testimony refers to the ark wherein the testimonies are (that is, the tablets on which the ten commandments were written).

AND OVER ALL THE FURNITURE⁷⁶, which means articles, utensils, or vessels. This is the way I.E. interprets the term. THEREOF. Such as the menorah, the table, and the altars.

AND OVER ALL THAT BELONGETH TO IT. The reference is the utensils which serve the furniture.⁷⁷

AND THEY SHALL BEAR THE TABERNACLE. They are the very ones who carry the tabernacle when the camp journeys, and they are the ones who shall minister unto the tabernacle when they camp. They shall camp around the tabernacle.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 1:53:1

BUT THE LEVITES SHALL PITCH ROUND ABOUT THE TABERNACLE OF TESTIMONY. Our verse explains why the earlier verse⁸¹ stated that the Levites were to encamp round about the tabernacle:⁸² (v. 50). Our verse explains why the earlier verse stated that the Levites were to encamp round about the tabernacle.” so that no one from the congregation of Israel should draw close to the tabernacle and die. This is the meaning of *that there be no wrath upon…the children of Israel*, as there was in the case of Uzzah, who touched the ark.

Scripture there states, *And the anger of the Lord was kindled against Uzzah* (II Sam. 6:7). ## Chapter 2


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:1:1

וטעם **אל משה ואל אהרן.** בעבור כי אהרן ובניו ישימו משא הקהתים בכתף:


[AND THE LORD SPOKE UNTO MOSES AND UNTO AARON.] Scripture reads, *unto Moses and unto Aaron*¹ because Aaron and his sons were to command the Kohathites as to which burdens they were to carry upon their shoulders.²

footnotes:
¹ In most cases, only Moses is mentioned when God addresses commands. Hence I.E.’s comment.
² For the burden of the Kohathites, see Num. 4:4-19.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:2:1)


**באתת.** סימנים היו בכל דגל ודגל וקדמונינו אמרו שהיה בדגל ראובן צורת אדם מכח דרש דודאים ובדגל יהודה צורת אריה כי בו המשילו יעקב ובדגל אפרים צורת שור מטעם בכור שור ובדגל דן צורת נשר עד שידמו לכרובים שראה יחזקאל הנביא:


ACCORDING TO THE ENSIGNS. There were signs upon each and every standard. The ancients³ said that the banner of Reuben had the form of a person on it.⁴ 2:6. Their statement is based on a midrashic interpretation concerning the mandrakes found by Reuben.⁵ The ancients also tell us that there was an image of a lion on the standard of Judah, for Jacob had compared Judah to a lion.⁶ They also tell us that the banner of Ephraim⁷ had the image of an ox upon it, in keeping with the verse, *His firstling bullock* (Deut. 33:17). The flag of Dan had the image of an eagle. Thus the banners were similar to the cherubim which the prophet Ezekiel saw.⁸

footnotes:
³ The Talmudic sages.
⁴ See Ba-Midbar Rabbah
⁵ The mandrakes have the appearance of a person. See I.E. on Gen. 30:14.
⁶ See Gen. 49:9.
⁷ The son of Joseph.
⁸ Each one of the cherubim seen by Ezekiel had four faces, the face of a man, an ox, an eagle, and a lion. See Ezek. 1:10. The camp of Israel similarly carried each one of these four images. In Ezekiel’s vision these cherubim supported the divine throne. Similarly, according to the sages, God’s spirit rested upon the camp of Israel.

A GOOD WAY OFF. The word *mi-neged* means a good way off.⁹ literally means opposite. Hence I.E.’s comment.

⁹ The word neged

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:3:1)


**קדמה מזרחה.** תחלת מזרח:


ON THE EAST SIDE TOWARD THE SUNRISING. The beginning of the east.¹⁰. This literally means toward the east. According to I.E., *kedemah mizrachah* is a technical term for the beginning of the east, i.e., northeast. See I.E. on Ex. 27:9 and the notes thereto (Vol. 2, pp. 581-2).

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:4:1)


**ופקודיהם.** או ופקודיו. אין הפרש ביניהם:


AND HIS HOST (*U-FEKUDEHEM*). Scripture could have read *u-fekudav* (and his host).¹¹ There is no difference between the two terms.¹² literally means and their host. *U-fekudav* means and his host. I.E. indicates that Scripture employs the singular for the plural, as the Bible uses collective nouns.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:5:1)


**והחונים עליו.** כמו עמו והם אחריו וקודם השלישי:


AND THOSE THAT PITCH NEXT UNTO HIM. *Ve-hachonim alav* means those that pitch with him.¹³ literally means on him. Thus the literal meaning of *ve-hachonim alav* (and those that pitch next unto him) is, and those that pitch upon him. Hence I.E.’s comment that *alav* means with him. Issachar was behind Judah and in front of Zebulun who was third.

¹³ Alav

THEY SHALL SET FORTH FIRST. The meaning of *rishonah yissa’u* is, they shall set forth first.¹⁴ should be read as if written *ba-rishonah yissa’u*. (Krinsky).

ON THE SOUTH SIDE. The word *temanah* (on the south side) comes from the same root as the word *yemin* (right).¹⁵

¹⁵ When a person faces east, the south is at his right.

SECOND. *Sheniyyim* (second) is an adjective.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:1)


**ונסע אהל מועד בתוך המחנות.** הנזכרות כי הגרשונים והמררים נוסעים בין דגל יהודה ובין דגל ראובן והקהתים נוסעים ועמהם אהרן ובניו בין דגל יהודה ובין דגל אפרים:


THEN THE TENT OF MEETING, WITH THE CAMP OF THE LEVITES, SHALL SET FORWARD IN THE MIDST OF THE CAMPS. In the midst of the above-mentioned camps,¹⁶ for the Gershonites and Merarites journeyed between the banner of Judah and the banner of Reuben.¹⁷ And the Kohathites, and with them Aaron and his sons, journeyed between the banner of Reuben and the banner of Ephraim.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁶ The camps of Judah and Reuben.
¹⁷ Num. 10:14-20.
¹⁸ Num. 10: 21-24.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:2)


**כאשר יחנו כן יסעו.** שב אל המחנות שהם ישראל לא אל מחנה הלוים כי יחנה דגל יהודה למזרח כי הגרשוני שהוא למערב המקדש ומררי בצפון יחדו יסעו:


AS THEY ENCAMP, SO SHALL THEY SET FORWARD. The reference is to *the camps*, which refers to the camp of the Israelites. It¹⁹. does not refer to the camp of the Levites. The Gershonites, who pitched on the west²⁰ of the sanctuary, and the Merarites on the north²¹ journeyed together,²² after the camp of Judah who pitched on the east.²³

footnotes:
¹⁹ As they set encamp, so shall they set forward
²⁰ Num. 3:23.
²¹ Num. 3:35.
²² Num. 10:17. Unlike the way they camped.
²³ Judah who pitched on the east marched first, followed by Reuben who pitched on the south, followed by Ephraim who pitched on the west, followed by Dan who pitched on the north. However, the Levites who camped on all four sides of the tabernacle did not follow this order.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/2:17:3)


**על ידו.** פאה ומקום וכן יד הירדן ויד תהיה לך:


EVERY MAN IN HIS PLACE. The meaning of *al yado* (in his place) is, in his corner and place.²⁴ literally means his hand. Hence I.E.’s comment. The same applies to *yad ha-yarden* (by the side of the Jordan) (Num. 13:29) and *ve-yad tiheyah lekha* (and thou shalt have a paddle) (Deut. 23:13).²⁵, literally hand, is to be rendered as place in both of these verses.

²⁵ The word yad

THESE ARE THEY THAT WERE NUMBERED OF THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. When they journeyed,²⁶ they were the same number as on the day that they were counted.²⁷ Thus not one of them died in twenty days. This is a great wonder.

**והלוים לא התפקדו.** לבאר שלא נחשוב אחד מהם עם הפקודים בנסעם:


BUT THE LEVITES WERE NOT NUMBERED AMONG THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL. Scripture makes it clear that we should not count even one Levite among those who were counted when they journeyed.²⁸

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 2:34:1

## Chapter 3


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:1:1)


**ואלה תולדות אהרן ומשה.** הטעם שלא הולידו בניהם ולא הוסיפו בקרוב מעשרה חדשים כאשר דבר ה' עם משה בהר סיני בעלותו להתכפר על מעשה העגל אז נבחר שבט לוי ואמר השם למשה הקרב את מטה לוי גם פרשת ואני הנה לקחתי וכאשר הוקם המשכן אמר השם פקוד את בני לוי והשכינה במשכן על כן כתב במדבר סיני והנה היו תולדות אהרן ומשה במדבר סיני כאשר היו בהר סיני רק חסרו שני בני אהרן על כן אמרתי כי הלוים לא נתנו כפר נפשם כי כן כתוב לכל העובר על הפקודים ויש אומרים כי לא פקדם משה טרם עשות המשכן רק ידע פקודיהם בדרך נבואה והכתוב יכחישם והוא כי תשא:


NOW THESE ARE THE GENERATIONS OF AARON AND MOSES. Scripture states this¹ because it wants to inform us that their offspring did not beget children and did not increase² in close to ten months.³ God chose the tribe of Levi at the time God spoke to Moses on Mount Sinai, when Moses ascended to atone for the making of the golden calf. God then said to Moses, *Bring the tribe of Levi near* (v. 6). The Lord then also related all that is contained in the section⁴ that opens with *And I, behold, I have taken the Levites from among the children of Israel* (v. 12). When the tabernacle was erected, God told Moses, *Number the children of Levi* (v. 15). God’s presence was then manifest in the tabernacle. Scripture therefore reads, *in the wilderness of Sinai* (v. 14).⁵ Now the descendants of Aaron and Moses in the wilderness of Sinai numbered the same as they did at Mount Sinai. However, two sons of Aaron were missing.⁶I therefore⁷ said that the Levites did not give a ransom for their souls, for Scripture clearly states, *for every one that passed over to them that are numbered* (Ex. 38:26). Some say that Moses did not count the Israelites before the tabernacle was constructed. He knew their number only by way of prophecy. However, Scripture contradicts them. It clearly states, *When thou takest the sum of the children of Israel* (Ex. 30:11).

footnotes:
¹ Our Torah portion deals with the census taken in the second year following the Exodus (see Num. 1:1; 3:14). The question thus arises, why does Scripture then tell us how many offspring Moses and Aaron had on the day that the Lord spoke to Moses on Mount Sinai?
² In other words, the generations of Moses and Aaron were the same at the time of the census as they had been on the day that Moses stood on Mount Sinai ten months earlier. Hence it was not necessary to count them again when the Levites were counted. See verse 15.
³ Ten months passed from the day that Moses stood on Mount Sinai in the month of Sivan and the census was taken on the first day of the second month (Iyyar) following the Exodus.
⁴ Verses 12 and 13.
⁵ And not on Mount Sinai. The point is that the Levites were numbered when the tabernacle stood at the foot of Mount Sinai. Scripture tells us that God spoke to Moses in the wilderness of Sinai and told him to number the Levites, because once the tabernacle was erected God’s presence was manifest there, and God spoke to Moses there. Thus what is reported in 3:1-13 took place on Mount Sinai, and what is reported in 3:14-39 took place in the wilderness of Sinai.
⁶ This is the point of verse 4.
⁷ According to I.E. only those who went forth to war were counted by offering half a shekel as ransom for their souls. However, the Levites were in the service of God and did not have to offer a ransom for their souls. See I.E.’s short commentary on Ex. 38:25.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:3:1)


**המשחים.** בשמן המשחה. שהיו כהנים גדולים:


THE PRIESTS THAT WERE ANOINTED. With the anointing oil, for they were high priests.⁸) gave the sons of Aaron the status of high priests.

footnotes:
⁸ Being anointed with the anointing oil (shemen ha-mishkhah


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:3:2)


ולא הניחו בנים גדולים או קטנים על כן חסר ממספר הלוים כי הגדולים יתכן שיולידו:


[AND THEY HAD NO CHILDREN.]⁹ appears in verse 4, but I.E. comments upon it here. They did not leave behind any grown or small children. Therefore the Levites were lacking in number,¹⁰ for it is possible for the grown children to beget children.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ And they had no children
¹⁰ For the other tribes increased in number from the first census. The tribe of Levi did not do so due to the loss of Nadab and Abihu.
¹¹ Hence Scripture tells us that Nadab and Abihu did not have any.

**על פני.** פירשתיו:


IN THE PRESENCE OF. I have previously explained the meaning of *al pene* (in the presence of).¹² means in the presence of. The literal meaning of *al pene* is, upon the face of.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:6:1

BEFORE AARON THE PRIEST. Who was the prince of the tribe. The meaning of *ve-ha’amadt* (and set them)¹³ is, *that they may minister. As the Lord liveth, before whom I stand* (amadti) (II Kings 5:16) is similar.¹⁴.

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:9:1)


**נתונים.** לעולם או הם ובניהם העובדים תחתם:


THEY ARE WHOLLY GIVEN UNTO HIM. Forever. Or to them and their descendants who take their place.¹⁵ (wholly given). This literally means, given given. I.E.’s first interpretation is that the repetition of the tern *netunim* indicates forever. His second interpretation is that the first *netunim* refers to them, and the second to their children.

¹⁵ The Hebrew reads, netunim netunim

**לאהרן ולבניו.** הנמצאים ועומדים תחתיהם:


UNTO AARON AND TO HIS SONS. Both those who are presently alive and those who will arise after them.

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:10:1)


**ואת אהרן ואת בניו.** ובכלל בניו בני בניו תפקדם לתת פקודה לכל אחד והם ישמרו את כהנתם שלא יבואו לידי פסולות:


AND THOU SHALT APPOINT AARON AND HIS SONS. *His sons* includes the sons of his sons. The meaning of *and thou shalt appoint* (tifkod) is, you shall appoint each one of them.¹⁶ can meant to count. Hence I.E.’s comment. The meaning of *that they may keep their priesthood* is that they not do anything that will disqualify them from being priests.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Tifkod


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:10:2)


**והזר הקרב.** לכהן להיות עם כהנתם ישראלי או לוי **יומת** ופרשת ואני הנה לקחתי גם היא דבקה כי יש בתורה פרשיות דבקות בטעם ואם הם מפורשות כמו ויהי כאשר תמו כל אנשי המלחמה כן ויהי ביום דבר ה' אל משה:


AND THE COMMON MAN THAT DRAWETH NIGH. To the priest, to serve as a priest. [SHALL BE PUT TO DEATH.] An Israelite or a Levite who serves as a priest shall be put to death. The chapter that opens with the words *And I, behold, I have taken the Levites*¹⁷ is also¹⁸ connected to the chapter which preceded it,¹⁹ for the Torah contains chapters that are separated but are connected by content. Similarly, *Moreover the hand of the Lord was against them, to discomfit them from the midst of the camp, until they were consumed* (Deut. 2:15),²⁰ and, *And it came to pass on the day when the Lord spoke unto Moses in the land of Egypt* (Ex. 6:28).²¹

footnotes:
¹⁷ Verses 12 and 13.
¹⁸ Like many other such chapters.
¹⁹ In other words, verse 12 is connected to verse 10. In the Masoretic Bible, verse 11 opens a new chapter.
²⁰ Deut. 2:16 is connected to Deut. 2:17 even though they are in different chapters in the Masoretic Bible. See I.E. on Deut. 2:16.
²¹ According to I.E., Ex. 6:28 is connected to that which follows even though they are in different chapters in the Masoretic Bible. See I.E. on Ex. 6:28 (Vol. 2, pp. 141-2).

ON THE DAY THAT I SMOTE. This means at the time that I smote.²³ There are many such cases.²⁴

MINE THEY SHALL BE. This means they are Mine and they shall be Mine,²⁵ for all human first-born and similarly the first-born of unclean cattle²⁶ shall be redeemed.

FROM A MONTH OLD AND UPWARD. Until the sun and moon are once again in conjunction,²⁷) (Rabbi Joseph Tov Elem). or until the moon returns to its approximate place of departure²⁸ and its original position and its present place are near each other.²⁹ The intelligent person will understand this.³⁰

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:23:1)

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:29:1)


**תימנה.** ובעבור שבני קהת נכבדים מכל בני לוי הושם קהת בימין כי הוא נכבד מהשמאל ומהאחור ואין אחר מזרח נכבד ממנו ובעבור שהיה גרשון בכור לוי יש לו מעלה גדולה על מררי כי הוא נושא את הקדש כנגד משא מררי על כן חנה גרשון במערב כמו דגל אפרים ומררי בצפון כדגל דן שהוא בכור השפחה והנה הקהתים מצד דגל ראובן על כן התחבר קרח עם דתן ואבירם ועוד אפרשנו:


SOUTHWARD. Kohath was placed at the right⁴⁴ because he was the most important of all the sons of Levi, and the right is more important than the left⁴⁵ or the back.⁴⁶ There is no side, with the exception of the east,⁴⁷ more important. Gershon had an advantage over Merari, for Gershon was the first-born of Levi. Hence he carried that which was holy,⁴⁸ that is above it. See Num. 4:25. in contrast to what Merari carried.⁴⁹ Gershon⁵⁰ therefore camped on the west,⁵¹ like the standard of Ephraim.⁵² Merari camped on the north⁵³ like Dan,⁵⁴ who was the son of the handmaid. Now the Kohathites camped next to the banner of Reuben. Korah⁵⁵ therefore joined with Dathan and Abiram.⁵⁶ I will explain this later.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ The south when one faces east.
⁴⁵ The north when one faces east.
⁴⁶ The west.
⁴⁷ Where Moses and Aaron encamped.
⁴⁸ Gershon carried the covering of the tabernacle, the tent of meeting, its covering, and the covering of tachash
⁴⁹ For the burden of Merari, see verse 36.
⁵⁰ The first-born of Levi.
⁵¹ Verse 23.
⁵² The first-born of Joseph. Thus the two first-born camped together.
⁵³ Verse 35.
⁵⁴ Num. 2:25.
⁵⁵ A Kohathite.
⁵⁶ See Num. 16:1.

AND THE ALTARS. Both of them.⁵⁷

**וכלי הקדש אשר ישרתו בהם.** הם כלי המנורה והשלחן והמזבחות ורבים השתבשו בפירוש והמסך וכל עבודתו והאמת ברורה שהוא מסך פתח אהל מועד:


AND THE VESSELS OF THE SANCTUARY WHEREWITH THE PRIESTS MINISTER. The reference is to the vessels of the menorah, the table, and the altars. Many erred in explaining the meaning of *and the screen*,⁵⁸ *and all that pertaineth to the service thereof*. It is clearly true that the reference is to the screen of the door of the tent of meeting.

AND ALL THAT PERTAINETH TO THE SERVICE THEREOF. Its cords.



### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:32:1)


**ונשיא נשיאי הלוי.** שהם שלשה גם מלת נשיאי הלוי מושכת עצמה ואחרת עמה כמו לא רבים יחכמו וכן הוא נשיאי פקודת שומרי משמרת הקודש והטעם כי אלו שלשה פקידים על השומרים שהם הלוים:


PRINCE OF THE PRINCES OF THE LEVITES. There were three princes of Levi.⁵⁹ The term *nesi’e* (princes) in the phrase *nesi’e ha-levi* (princes of Levi) applies to the word *levi* and also to another word.⁶⁰ (having the oversight of) in the second part of our verse. It is like the word *lo* (not) in *It is not the great that are wise* (Job 32:9).⁶¹. According to I.E. the word *lo* is carried over to the second half of the sentence, which is to interpreted, and the aged do not (*lo*) discern judgment. The second part of our verse is to be interpreted as if written *the princes having the oversight of them that keep the charge of the sanctuary*.⁶² Its meaning is that these three princes had oversight over the keepers of the charge of the sanctuary, who were Levites.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ See verses 24, 30, and 35.
⁶⁰ It also applies to the word pekudat
⁶¹ The second part of Job 32:9 literally reads, and the aged discern judgment
⁶² I.E. interprets our verse as if written, Eleazar the son of Aaron the priest being prince of the princes of the Levites, the princes having the oversight of them that keep the charge of the sanctuary.



### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:35:1)


ויתכן היות **צפונה.** מגזרת צפנת ליראיך וצפון לצדיק כי אין בנגב שום דבר:


NORTHWARD. It is possible that the word *tzafonah* (northward) is derived from the word *tzafanta* (laid up) in *Thou hast laid up for them that fear Thee* (Ps. 31:20) and from the word *tzafon* (laid up) in *laid up for the righteous* (Prov. 13:22). The north is so called because there is nothing in the south.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶³ I.E. apparently believed that no one lives south of the equator, for the temperature there is too hot. It is only in the north where the necessary elements to sustain life are found. See I.E. on Gen. 12:9 (Vol. 1, p. 153).



### Verse 38

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:38:1

**קדמה מזרחה.** הוא מזרחה שמש במקום התחברות העגלות והנה במזרח אהל משה ואהל אהרן ואהל בניו ובני בניו וכל ביתם:


EASTWARD…TOWARD THE SUNRISING. *Mizrachah* means toward the sunrising. It refers to the place where the circles⁶⁴ (wagons). *Vat. Ebr*. 38 reads, *agulot* (circles). It isn’t clear what I.E. is referring to. Meijler suggests that the reference is to where the heaven and earth appear to touch, i.e., the horizon. For other interpretations, see Weiser and Krinsky. come together. Observe, the tent of Moses, the tent of Aaron, and the tent of his children and his children’s children and all their entire households were in the east.⁶⁵

AND THE COMMON MAN THAT DREW NIGH. To the holy place.⁶⁶ Note, all the Levites camped around the tabernacle in order to guard it.

### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:39:1)


**שנים ועשרים אלף.** מכלל וכשאתה מחבר הפרטים תמצא שיותירו שלש מאות ויש אומרים כי הכתוב אחז דרך קצרה להזכיר האלפים ועזב המאות וזה לא יתכן בעבור מספר העודפים שהם פחותים משלש מאות והאמת דברי הקבלה אין בכור מפקיע בכור ויהיה פירוש הפסוק כל פקודי הלוים חוץ מהבכורים. ויהודה הפרסי דקדק להביא ראיה מהמספר כי בכורי הלוים הם חלק משלשה ושבעים מלוים וכשאתה לוקח במספר הזה מפקודי ישראל ואחר כן תקח כמספר פקודי הלוים הבאים לצבא כנגד פקודי הלוים מבן חדש יהיו קרובים ולא אמר כלום כי ישראל נפקדו מבן עשרים שנה ואין קצב אחרת והלוים נפקדו מבן שלשים ועד בן חמישים ויש ביניהם הפרש גדול רק נסמוך על דברי הקבלה:


WERE TWENTY AND TWO THOUSAND. In all. When you add up the figures you will find that they come to three hundred above the figure given in Scripture.⁶⁷ Some say that Scripture is being brief and mentions only the thousands and neglects the hundreds.⁶⁸ However, this cannot be the case. Scripture mentions less than three hundred over and above a round number.⁶⁹ The truth is in accordance with tradition which states that a first-born does not free a first-born.⁷⁰ 4a. In other words, there were 300 first-born who were Levites. These 300 did not free Israelites from the necessity of redemption, because they had to free themselves. The meaning of our verse thus is, all that were numbered of the Levites with the exception of the first-born [were twenty and two thousand]. Judah the Persian⁷¹ calculated to prove, from the number that the first-born of the Levites were one seventy-third of the Levites.⁷² He went on to say that if you divide the number of Israelites⁷³ by seventy-three,⁷⁴ and take the number of Levites who came to serve in the tabernacle⁷⁵ rather than the number of Levites age one month and above,⁷⁶ then the number will be close.⁷⁷ However, all this is nothing. The Israelites were counted from twenty and above. No other fixed unit was used.⁷⁸ However, the Levites were counted from twenty to fifty. There is a large difference between them.⁷⁹ We will rely only on tradition.⁸⁰

footnotes:
⁶⁷ 7,500 Gershonites (v. 22), 8,600 Kohathites (v. 28), 6,200 Merarites (v. 34). This comes to 22,300.
⁶⁸ In other words, 22,000 is a round number.
⁶⁹ In listing the first-born, Scripture gives their number as 22,273 (v. 43). We thus see that Scripture does not employ round numbers in our chapter.
⁷⁰ See Bekhorot
⁷¹ An early Karaite scholar. See I.E. on Ex. 12:2 (Vol. 2, p. 203).
⁷² 22,000 divided by 300 comes to 73 and a fraction.
⁷³ There were 603,550 Israelites (2:32).
⁷⁴ The result of which is 8,267.
⁷⁵ 8,580 (Num. 4:8).
⁷⁶ Of whom there were 22,273.
⁷⁷ If we deduct the 300 uncounted Levites from the number of Levites who served in the tabernacle (8,580), we get 8,280. This is a number close to the 8,267. We thus see that 300 Levites were uncounted in order to provide a relationship between the Levites who served in the tabernacle and one seventy-third of the Israelites (Weiser).
⁷⁸ There was no other age limitation.
⁷⁹ We cannot draw any conclusion from the number of Israelites and the number of Levites given by Scripture, for they apply to different age groups.
⁸⁰ That the 300 were first-born. We cannot prove that this was the case from Scripture itself.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:41:1

**ואת בהמת הלוים.** שלא יפסידו הלוים ובכור כל בהמה גם הוא לשם:


AND THE CATTLE OF THE LEVITES.⁸² So that the Levites do not lose. The firstlings among the cattle are also for the Lord.⁸³

⁸² The cattle of the Levites which redeemed the first-born cattle of the Israelites remained with the Levites. Thus the Levites did not lose.

### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 3:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/3:45:1)


**קח את הלוים.** פעם אחרת כאילו אמר כאשר תקח את הלוים יהיו העודפים פדיום ונסמכה מלת פדויי אל המספר וכמוהו רבים יש מכחישים אמרו מזה נלמוד כי פדיון כל בכור חמשה שקלים ואינו ראיה כי זאת מצוה בפני עצמה והאמת דברי קבלה:


TAKE THE LEVITES. A second time.⁸⁴ It is as if it stated, when you take the Levites, then those who are over and above will be redeemed.⁸⁵ The word *peduye* (redemption of) is connected to the number.⁸⁶. Now a word is usually not in the construct with a number. We thus say, *shene chakahamim* (two wise men), not *chakhme shenaim* (wise men of two) (Filwarg). Hence I.E.’s comment. There are many other such instances. The disputers say⁸⁷ (there are those who dispute). This is a term usually applied to Karaites. However, in view of the fact that what the alleged disputers say is not in contradiction to tradition, it is hard to understand why I.E. refers to them as disputers. Indeed, Motot suggests changing the reading to *yesh mefareshim* (some interpret), and *Vat. Ebr*. 38 reads, *Some say*. Meijler suggest that the disputers also brought proof from this that (contrary to *halakha*) the first-born of Levites require redemption, for the first-born of the Levites did not exempt the first-born of Israel for all time. that here we learn that the redemption price of every first-born is five shekels. However, there is no proof here,⁸⁸ for this commandment stands by itself.⁸⁹ The truth is in keeping with tradition.⁹⁰

footnotes:
⁸⁴ In other words, Scripture repeats itself, for it has already told us in verse 41 that God told Moses to take the Levites.
⁸⁵ In other words, take the Levites should be interpreted, when you take the Levites. Furthermore, when you take the Levites is connected to verse 46.
⁸⁶ Our text reads, peduye ha-sheloshah
⁸⁷ Hebrew, yesh makhhishim
⁸⁸ That the redemption money for a first-born is five shekalim for all time.
⁸⁹ Our text deals with a specific case; one cannot derive a law for all times from it.
⁹⁰ That the redemption money is five shekalim. However, we do not derive it from our chapter.

### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:5:1)


**פרכת המסך.** שתסוך על הארון:


THE VEIL OF THE SCREEN. Which covers the ark.³. This literally means the screen which covers. I.E. explains that the screen is so called because it covers the ark. See Ex. 40:3.

³ The Hebrew reads, parokhet ha-masakh



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:1)


**ונתנו עליו.** על המסך ויש אומרים כי ישוב עליו אל מלת את שהוא את פרכת וכמוהו ותצפנו:


AND SHALL PUT THEREON. On the cover.⁴ (and put thereon). However, *parokhet* is feminine. Thus our text should read *aleha* rather then *alav*. Hence I.E. notes than *alav* refers to the word *masakh* (covering/screen). Others say that the word *alav* (thereon) refers to the word *et* (v. 5), which precedes *parokhet* (the veil).⁵ (the sign of the accusative) serves both the masculine and the feminine. Hence I.E. believes that either a masculine or a feminine proportional pronoun may follow a word which has *et* in front of it (Filwarg). Thus the word *alav* can refer to *et parokhet*. *Va-titzpeno* (and hid him)⁶ (Josh. 2:6) is similar.⁷, for the reference is to the two spies sent by Joshua. Now *hid him* is preceded by *et shene ha-anashim* (the two men). However, since the word *et* is used both with the singular and the plural, a singular or a plural may follow a phrase preceded by *et*.

footnotes:
⁴ Our verse reads, ve-natenu alav
⁵ The word et
⁶ Translated literally. See next note.
⁷ The text should have read, and hid them


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:2)


**ופרשו בגד כליל תכלת.** כלו תכלת:


AND SHALL SPREAD OVER IT A CLOTH ALL OF BLUE. *Kelil tekhelet* means all of blue.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:3)


**מלמעלה.** י"א על כסוי עור תחש והנכון בעיני שפירושו וכבר פרשו בגד כליל תכלת מלמעלה לפרכת המסך ואם לדרך כבוד יתכן גם הראשון ועור תחש מפני הגשם והאבק:


OVER IT. Some say, over the covering of *tachash* (sealskin). However, it appears to me that its meaning is, and they had already spread a cloth all of blue over the screen covering.⁸ (and shall spread) is a pluperfect and is to be rendered, and they had already spread. According to this interpretation, the blue cover was placed on the ark, and the *tachash* (sealskin) covering was placed over the blue covering. If the blue cover was used in order to glorify the ark, then the first interpretation appears correct.⁹ The *tachash* covering served to protect [the ark] from the rain and the dust.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁸ In other words, u-fareshu
⁹ Blue is a royal color. Hence it stands to reason that if it was placed on the ark, it should be visible.
¹⁰ Unlike the blue covering, which served as a decoration.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:6:4)


**ושמו בדיו.** הנה יסירום עד שיכסו הארון ויש אומרים שמו בדיו על כתפות הנושאים:


AND SHALL SET THE STAVES THEREOF. Note, they removed the staves until the ark was covered.¹¹ Some say that they shall set the staves upon the shoulders of the bearers.¹² is, they shall set the staves upon the bearers of the ark. Thus our verse does not imply that the staves were ever removed from the ark. See *Tosafot Yoma* 72a.

¹² In other words, the meaning of and they shall set the staves thereof

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:15:1)


**משא בני קהת באהל מועד.** כי האהל והמשכן הם לא ישאוהו:


THESE THINGS ARE THE BURDEN OF THE SONS OF KOHATH IN THE TENT OF MEETING. For they did not carry the tent and the tabernacle.²⁶, because they did not do any carrying.

footnotes:
²⁶ Scripture states, These things are the burden



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:1)


**ופקדת.** מגזרת פקיד:


AND THE CHARGE OF. *U-fekudat* (and the charge of) is related to the word *pakid* (overseer).²⁷

footnotes:
²⁷ One who assigns tasks.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:2)


**שמן המאור.** יש אומרים כי הוא היה נושא שמן המאור לילה אחד וכן את מנחת התמיד והנכון בעיני שהוא פקיד עליה לתת שמן המאור וטעם הכלי וכן כלי שמן המשחה וכלי הקטורת לבני הקהתים:


THE OIL FOR THE LIGHT. Some say that Eleazar carried oil for one night’s light and similarly the continual meal offering.²⁸ 10:13. Eleazar carried oil for the lighting in his right hand and the sweet incense in his left hand, while the continual meal offering hung from his arm. However, I believe that Eleazar was in charge of giving *the oil for the light*, that is, of the utensil containing the oil for the light, and similarly the vessel containing the anointing oil and the vessel containing the sweet incense, to the children of Kohath.

footnotes:
²⁸ The daily continual meal offering. See Yerushalmi, Sabbath


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:16:3)


וטעם **פקדת כל המשכן.** כי יש לו גם פקידות במשכן ובכל כליו עם אחיו איתמר רק לגודל מעלת אלעזר היה הוא פקיד על השמן הקטרת והמנחה לבדו:


[HE SHALL HAVE THE CHARGE OF ALL THE TABERNACLE.] Scripture states this because Eleazar had charge over the tabernacle along with his brother Ithamar.²⁹ However, Eleazar alone had charge over the oil, the sweet incense, and the meal offering because of his great status.

footnotes:
²⁹ If Eleazar was in charge of the entire tabernacle, why does Scripture tell us that he was in charge of the oil, the sweet incense, and the meal offering? Hence I.E.’s comments.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:17:1)


וטעם **אל משה ואל אהרן.** כי מצוה על אהרן ובניו להשמר שלא יבאו הקהתים לידי כרת:


[UNTO MOSES AND UNTO AARON.] Scripture states this because Aaron and his sons were commanded to take heed that the sons of Kohath do not come to the penalty of excision (*karet*).



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:18:1)


ומלת **אל תכריתו.** למשה אהרן ובניו:


The command *Cut ye not off* was given to Moses, Aaron and his sons.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:1)


**וחיו.** יוסיפו חיים:


THAT THEY MAY LIVE. That their lives may be extended.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:2)


**ולא ימותו.** בכרת והטעם כי אם ישמרו יקבלו שכר ואם לא יענשו כי יתכן שלא יענשו על כן ולא ימותו:


AND NOT DIE. By excision. The meaning of our verse is that the family of the Kohathites will be rewarded if they observe and will be punished if they do not. Without the phrase *and not die*, our verse could be taken to imply that they will not be punished.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:3)


**ושמו אותם.** הארון והשולחן והמנורה והמזבחות:


AND APPOINT THEM.³⁰, which literally means, and put them. This can be interpreted as referring to the *kohanim*. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. The ark, the table, the menorah, and the altars.

footnotes:
³⁰ Hebrew, ve-samu otam


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:4)


**על עבודתו.** בכלי הקדש:


EVERY ONE TO HIS SERVICE. This refers to the holy vessels.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:19:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:19:5)


**ואל משאו.** לשאת בכתף:


AND TO HIS BURDEN. To be carried on the shoulder.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:20:1)


**ולא יבואו.** הקהתים אל אהל מועד לראות כבלע את הקדש והטעם כאשר יוסר בנינו יוסר מסך הפרכת ונגלה הארון ויש אומרים כי כבלע כמו ככסות והטעם כאשר יכסו הארון לשאת אותו וזה טעם קרוב מהראשון והנה שתים אזהרות שלא יגעו בקדש כי אם בבדים ישאוהו ולא יראו הקדש:


BUT THEY SHALL NOT GO IN. The Kohathites shall not go in to the tent of meeting to see the holy things as they are being covered. The meaning of *ke-vala et ha-kodesh* (the holy things as they are being covered) is, when the structure is taken down, the veil of the curtain is removed and the ark is revealed.³¹ is, when the holy (i.e., the tabernacle) is taken down. Others say that the meaning of *ke-vala* is, when they are being covered. Thus the meaning of *ke-vala et ha-kodesh* is, when they cover the ark in order to carry it.³² means when it is covered. The word *bala* means swallowed. Thus *ke-vala* literally means when he swallows, i.e., when he covers. This interpretation is more plausible than the first one. Now there are two prohibitions. One, not to touch the holy vessels-they are to be carried only by staves. Two, the Kohathites shall not see the holy things.

footnotes:
³¹ According to I.E. the meaning of the holy things as they are being covered
³² In other words, ke-vala



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:22:1)


**נשא.** שם הפעל כמו זכור את יום השבת וטעם **נשא.** שא:


TAKE THE SUM. *Naso* (take the sum) is an infinitive. It is like *zakhor* (remember) in *Remember the sabbath day, to keep it holy* (Ex. 20:8).³³ The meaning of *naso* is, take the sum.³⁴ is an infinitive, it has the meaning of an imperative. The same applies to *zakhor*.

footnotes:
³³ Which is an infinitive too.
³⁴ In other words, even though naso


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:22:2)


**גם הם.** בעבור שנשא את ראש הקהתים:


ALSO. Because he had already counted the Kohathites.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:24:1)


**לעבוד.** להקים המשכן ולעשות הלחם ולשחוט ולשמור:


IN SERVING. To put up the tabernacle, to make the showbread, to slaughter, and to guard.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:24:2)


**ולמשא.** שם הפועל וכמוהו ולמסע את המחנות כי משקלי שמות הפעלים משתנים:


AND IN BEARING BURDENS. *Le-massa*³⁵. (bearing burdens) is an infinitive. Similarly *le-massa*³⁶. (causing to set forward) in *and for causing the camps to set forward* (Num. 10:2). The forms of verbs change.³⁷ in our verse is vocalized *kamatz, pattach*. *Le-massa* in Num. 10:2 is vocalized *pattach, pattach*. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
³⁵ From the root nun, sin, alef
³⁶ From the root nun, sin, ayin
³⁷ Le-massa



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:25:1)


**ומכסה התחש אשר עליו.** אחז הכתוב דרך קצרה שלא הזכיר מכסה עורות אילים ויש אומרים ששני המכסים היו דבקים:


AND THE COVERING OF SEALSKIN THAT IS ABOVE IT. Scripture is being brief, for it does not mention the ramskin covering.³⁸ Some say that the two coverings were attached.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁸ Which was above the sealskin covering. See Ex. 26:14.
³⁹ Thus the covering of sealskin includes the ramskin covering.



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:26:1)


**ואת כל כלי עבודתם.** כשולחנות ויתדות ומיתרות:


AND ALL THE INSTRUMENTS OF THEIR SERVICE. Such as tables, pegs, and cords.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:26:2)


**ואת כל אשר יעשה.** **להם.** לכלי העבודה או למשכן ולמזבח:


AND WHATSOEVER THERE MAY BE TO DO WITH THEM. The instruments of service, or the tabernacle and the altar.



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:29:1)


**בני מררי.** לא הזכיר לשאת שמותם כי הפרשה דבקה:


AS FOR THE SONS OF MERARI. Scripture does not mention that they were counted, for this section⁴⁰ is connected to the one above.⁴¹ in verse 22 also applies here.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Verses 29-37.
⁴¹ Verses 21-28. Thus the command Take the sum of



### Verse 47

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:47:1)


**עבדת עבדה.** כאשר פרשתי והנה טעם כל הפקודים שהיו מבן ל' שנה:


THE WORK OF SERVICE. This is to be understood as I have already explained.⁴² applies to the carrying of the tent and the tabernacle, which was the work assigned to the sons of Gershon and Merari, while the term *the work of bearing burdens* refers to the work done by the sons of Kohath (Weiser). Now the meaning of *All those that were numbered* (v. 46) is, all those from thirty years old that were numbered.⁴³

footnotes:
⁴² In his comments on verse 15, I.E. hints that the term work of service
⁴³ In other words, our verse is a continuation of verse 46.



### Verse 49

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:49:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:49:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:49:1)


**איש איש.** על גרשון קהת ומררי:


EVERY ONE. The reference is to Gershon, Kohath, and Merari.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 4:49:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:49:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/4:49:2)


**על עבדתו ועל משאו.** על כן ופקדיו. ונסמכו פרשת וישלחו מן המחנה בעבור היות השכינה בתוך המחנות בנסעם ובחנותם:


TO HIS SERVICE, AND TO HIS BURDEN. Hence *and his numbered*⁴⁴. The singular *u-fekudav* is employed because the terms which precede it (*avodato*, his service, and *masa’o*, his burden) are i n the singular. It should be noted that J.P.S. renders *u-fekudav* as *they were also numbered*. follows. The section *put out of the camp every leper, and every one that hath an issue, and whosoever is unclean by the dead* (Num. 5:2) follows, because God’s presence was in the camps of Israel when they journeyed and when they rested.⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁴ Hebrew, u-fekudav
⁴⁵ Hence all those who were unclean had to leave the camp.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:2:1)


**כל צרוע וכל זב וכל טמא לנפש.** אלה הם מטומאת שבעה והם מטמאים אחרים על כן לא יצא בעל קרי מחוץ למחנה ואין יד במחנה לצאת חוץ רק כל אחד חופר באהלו כמנהג כל המחנות כלן כי תינוקים יש ביניהם. ופרשת כי תצא מחנה על מחנה קטן ידבר שאין שם אשה והארון ביניהם ואין (ס"א והם) סובבים כי במחנה ישראל השכינה באמצע ועוד אפרשנו במקומו:


EVERY LEPER, AND EVERY ONE THAT HATH AN ISSUE,¹. A *zav* or a *zavah* is a person who has an abnormal discharge from the genital organs. AND WHOSOEVER IS UNCLEAN BY THE DEAD. These are states of uncleanliness that last for seven days. They also render others unclean.² and the *zavah*, see Lev. 15:1-28. For the uncleanliness of those who come into contact with the dead, see Num. 19:1- 22. Therefore a person who had a seminal omission does not leave the camp.³ Similarly, no place was setaside outside the camp⁴ for the performing of bodily functions.⁵ On the contrary, each person dug in his own tent⁶ as is the custom with all the camps, for there were children among them.⁷ Now the section which opens with *When thou goest forth in camp against thine enemies* (Deut. 23:10) speaks of a small camp wherein there are no women, and the ark is among them and they do not surround the ark.⁸ [They have to leave the camp],⁹ for God’s presence is in the center of any Israelite camp. I will explain this in its proper place.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹ Hebrew, zav
² For the uncleanliness of the zav
³ The camp discussed in our chapter, i.e., the camp of the entire people of Israel. I.E. comments thus because one who has a seminal mission is obligated to leave a camp of soldiers who go to war. See Deut. 23:10-14.
⁴ See note 3.
⁵ As there was in the case of a camp of soldiers. See Deut. 23:10-14.
⁶ Dug a pit for his bodily excretions.
⁷ It would be very difficult to take children outside of the camp every time they had to relieve themselves.
⁸ With a fence as they do in a large camp (see I.E. on Deut. 23:10). Thus in a small camp, or a war camp, the ark is exposed. Some manuscripts read, and they surround the ark; i.e., they are in close proximity to the ark.
⁹ A war camp.
¹⁰ See I.E. on Deut. 23:10. For another interpretation, see Weiser.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:3:1)


**נקבה.** זבה או נדה או מצורעת או טמאת מת:


FEMALE. A woman who has an irregular menstrual flow,¹¹ a normal menstrual flow,¹² a woman who is a leper¹³ or who was made unclean by a dead body.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹¹ See Lev. 15:25-28.
¹² Lev. 15:19-24.
¹³ See Lev. 13:1-46.
¹⁴ Num. 19:11-22.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:4:1)


**ויעשו כן.** מיד קודם שנסעו והטמאים בנסעם יסעו בין דגל אפרים לדגל דן על דרך סברא כי לא פורש. ונסמכה פרשת איש או אשה כי הצרעת והזוב יבואו בעבור מעל. וטעם להזכיר זאת הפרשה פעם אחרת להזכיר ואם אין לאיש גואל:


AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL DID SO. Immediately, before they journeyed. When the unclean journeyed, they journeyed between the standard of Ephraim¹⁵ and the standard of Dan.¹⁶ The aforementioned is based on logic,¹⁷ for this is not explained in Scripture. The section that begins with *When a man or woman* [*shall commit any sin that men commit, to commit a trespass against the Lord*]¹⁸ follows the previous chapter¹⁹ because leprosy and unclean issue²⁰. are brought about by trespassing against the Lord.²¹ 16b. The reason this section is repeated²² is that the Torah wants to mention *but if the man have no kinsman* (v. 8).²³

footnotes:
¹⁵ The standard of Ephraim journeyed in front of the standard of Dan.
¹⁶ The standard of Dan journeyed at the rear. See Num. 10:25.
¹⁷ For it is hard to believe that the unclean traveled outside of the standards and were thus exposed to danger.
¹⁸ Verses 6-10.
¹⁹ Verses 1-4.
²⁰ Hebrew, zav
²¹ They are punishments for sin. See Arakin
²² The material in verses 5-10 repeats what has already been stated in Lev. 5:20-26.
²³ This law is not mentioned in Lev 5:20-26.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:1)


**והתודו.** איזה מהם שיהיה איש או אשה:


THEN THEY SHALL CONFESS. Whichever one it be, a man or a woman.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:2)


**בראשו.** כאשר הוא. לא פחות ממנו:


IN FULL. *Be-rosho* (in full) means as it is, not less than it is.²⁴ literally means at its head, or at first. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
²⁴ Be-rosho


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:7:3)


**וחמישתו יוסף עליו.** אם הוא מתודה מעצמו ואם יש עדים עליו יוסף שני חמישיות ומעתיקי הדת אמרו חומש החומש ודעתם רחבה מדעתנו:


AND ADD UNTO IT THE FIFTH PART. If he confesses on his own. However, if there are witnesses then he shall add two fifths. Those who transmitted the law²⁵ say a fifth of a fifth.²⁶ 108a. See Rashi on Lev. 5:24, “If he denies the fifth (i.e., he asserts that he has repaid both capital and fifth, but has not really paid the latter, for which a claim is now made against him) and takes an oath that he has paid it but afterwards admits the claim, then he must now bring (pay) a fifth in addition to this fifth (a fifth of the original fifth which now become the *keren* (capital) in addition to it).” (Rosenbaum and Silbermann translation). Their minds are greater than ours.

footnotes:
²⁵ The Talmudic sages.
²⁶ See Baba Kamma



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:8:1)


**המושב לה'.** בעבור יראת השם השיבו:


THE RESTITUTION…SHALL BE THE LORDS. For he made restitution because of his fear of the Lord.²⁷

²⁷ He confessed because of his fear of the Lord.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:10:1)


**ואיש את קדשיו לו יהיו.** טעמו ברשות המקדיש הם שיתנם לאיזה כהן שירצה והזכיר זה בעבור מתנות כהונה:


AND EVERY MAN’S HALLOWED THINGS SHALL BE HIS. Its meaning is that they belong to the one who hallows them. He may give them to any *kohen* that he wants to. This is noted because Scripture mentions the gifts given to the *kohanim*.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ In verse 9.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:12:1)


וטעם **תשטה.** בעבור ומעלה בו מעל:


The portion dealing with a straying wife follows²⁹ because the straying wife commits a trespass against her husband.³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ The portion that deals with one who trespasses against the Lord.
³⁰ They are thus thematically connected.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:12:2)


**תשטה.** תסור מהדרך הישרה וכן שטה מעליו ועבור:


IF ANY MAN’S WIFE GO ASIDE. *Tisteh* means will go aside from the right way.³¹ means to go aside. Here, to go aside from the right path. Similarly *seteh* (go aside) in *Go aside from it* (Prov. 4:15).

footnotes:
³¹ In other words, the word tisteh



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:1)


**שכבת זרע.** השכיבה הידועה כי עקרה לזרע:


CARNALLY. *Shikhvat zera* (carnally) refers to carnal laying. It is called *shikhvat zera*³² because its purpose is for seed.³³

footnotes:
³² Literally, laying of seed.
³³ Its purpose is for ejaculation (Weiser). Or its purpose is to produce children.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:2)


**ונסתרה** שלא גלתה היא הדבר ואפילו עד אחד אין בה שתהיה חשודה בעבורו:


AND IT BE HID. She did not reveal the matter. There is not even one witness through whom she is suspect.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:13:3)


**והיא לא נתפשה.** בעדים אנשים או על ידי נשים עד שתהיה חשודה:


NEITHER SHE BE TAKEN IN THE ACT. By witnesses, men or women, so that she be suspected.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:14:1)


**ועבר עליו.** לשון זכר וכן רוח גדולה וחזק מאד:


AND THE SPIRIT OF JEALOUSY COME UPON HIM. *Ru’ach* (spirit) is masculine here.³⁴ (come upon), the verb governing *ru’ach*, is masculine. Similarly *ru’ach* (wind) in *and a great and strong wind* (I Kings 19:11).³⁵ (strong), one of the verbs governing *ru’ach*, is masculine.

³⁴ Avar
³⁵ Here too chazak

וטעם **עבר.** שעל' במחשבתו:


COME. Come upon his mind.



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:1)


**והביא.** בעל כרחה:


BRING. Against her will.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:2)


**עליה.** עמה או בעבורה:


FOR HER. *Aleha* (for her) means with her or because of her.³⁶ is, upon her. Hence I.E.’s comment.

³⁶ The usual meaning of aleha


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:15:3)


**קמח שעורים.** אמרו רבותינו היא עשתה מעשה בהמה תביא מאכל בהמה ומנחת הנדבה היא סולת חטים לזכרון לטוב. וזאת המנחה מזכרת עון אם היה שם על כן היא גרוע להיותה שעורים בלא שמן ובלא לבונה:


BARLEY MEAL. Our rabbis said, she acted like a beast, let her bring the food of a beast.³⁷ 2:1. A free-will meal offering consists of pure wheat flower so that the celebrant will be remembered for good. However, this meal offering recalls a sin, if a sin was committed. Hence it is an inferior offering and consists of barley without oil and without frankincense.

footnotes:
³⁷ Mishnah, Sotah

AND SET HER⁴⁰ is to make the woman stand before the Lord. BEFORE THE LORD. Before the altar.

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:17:1)


**מים קדשים.** מהכיור כי כבר נקדשו:


HOLY WATER. From the basin, for it has already become holy.⁴¹

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:17:2)


**בקרקע.** ידוע תחת הקרקע:


ON THE FLOOR OF THE TABERNACLE. The reference is to the well-known floor.⁴² (dust) has a definite article before it, a *heh* with a *segol* beneath it. Hence I.E.’s comment. The word *afar* (dust) has a definite article before it. It refers to the earth which is beneath the floor.⁴³ (in the dust) refers to the earth which is beneath the floor of a certain spot in the tabernacle. See Mishnah *Sotah* 2:2.
