### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:1)


**ופרע את ראש האשה.** יגלה ולפי דעתי יוציא פרע ראשה שהיה מכוסה:


AND LET THE HAIR OF THE WOMAN’S HEAD GO LOOSE. *Para* (go loose) means he shall uncover.⁴⁴. However, I believe that it means he shall take out the hair of the woman, which was covered, and let it go loose.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ According to this opinion the meaning of our verse is, and he shall uncover (u-fara) the hair of the woman


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:2)


**מי המרים.** לפי דעתי שמלת מי סמוך ומלת המרים תאר השם אם כן סודו ידוע גם יתכן שנקראו על שם סופם כמו ובגדי ערומים תפשיט והעד ובאו בה המים המאררים למרים להורות כי האלות הנקראות המאררים ישימו המים מרים אחר היותם מתוקים:


THE WATER OF BITTERNESS. It is my opinion that the word *me* (water) is in the construct⁴⁵ and the word *ha-marim* is an adjective.⁴⁶ If so, then its secret is known.⁴⁷ is short for *me ha-sammim ha-marim* (the water containing the bitter ingredient). See *Sotah* 20a. It is also possible that the waters were so called because of their ultimate taste.⁴⁸ Compare, *And stripped the naked of their clothing* (Job 22:6).⁴⁹ *And the water that causeth the curse shall enter into her and become bitter* (v. 24) is proof of this, for this verse indicates that the *oaths*,⁵⁰ which *they that cause the curses*⁵¹ refers to, will make the sweet waters bitter.

footnotes:
⁴⁵ With a missing word.
⁴⁶ Describing the missing word.
⁴⁷ We know what word is missing. According to I.E. the phrase me ha- marim
⁴⁸ The waters were not yet bitter. They only turned bitter at the end. Hence I.E. explains that they are called the bitter waters because they were ultimately bitter.
⁴⁹ One cannot strip a naked person. Thus what Scripture means is that the one who was ultimately naked was stripped.
⁵⁰ Mentioned in verse 19. The waters turned bitter after the oaths were dissolved in it.
⁵¹ The curses come about through her false swearing.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:18:3)


ומלת **המאררים.** מהבנין הכבד כמו אשר אררה ה':


The word *ha-me’arerim* (that causeth the curse) is a *pi’el*. It is like the word *erarah* (cursed) in *which the Lord hath cursed* (Gen. 5:29).⁵². The root *alef, resh, resh* usually comes in the *kal*. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁵² It too is a pi’el



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:1)


וטעם **והשביע.** שתענה האשה אמן אמן:


[SHALL CAUSE HER TO SWEAR.] Its meaning is that the woman shall answer, *Amen, Amen*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:2)


**אם לא שכב איש אתך.** הטעם איש אחר בחזקה:


IF NO MAN HAVE LAIN WITH THEE. Its meaning is, if another man has not forced himself upon you.⁵³ cannot be taken literally because we are dealing with a married women. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁵³ If no man have lain with thee


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:3)


**ואם לא שטית.** ברצונך ודקדוק שטית טומאה כמו לא תבנה אתהן גזית:


AND IF THOU HAST NOT GONE ASIDE. Willingly. *Satit* means you have gone aside and committed an unclean act.⁵⁴ is short for *satit tume’ah*. It is similar to *thou shalt not build it of hewn stones* (Ex. 20:25).⁵⁵. I.E. believes that this is short for: you shall not build a hewn structure out of them. See I.E. on Ex. 20:25 and the notes thereto (Vol. 2, p. 443-4).

footnotes:
⁵⁴ In other words, satit
⁵⁵ Ex. 20:25 literally reads, you shall not build them uncut stones


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:19:4)


**הנקי.** תהיה נקייה כמו ומות בהר ויתכן להיות והשביע הראשון בשם והשני באלה כאשר הוא מפורש:


BE THOU FREE. The meaning of *hinnaki* is, be thou free.⁵⁶ is an imperative. It literally means free yourself. However, it cannot mean that here, for it is not in the power of the woman to free herself. Hence *hinnaki* must be taken as an imperfect rather than as an imperative, even though it has the form of the latter. It is similar to *and die in the mount* (Deut. 32:50).⁵⁷ (and die), an imperative, must be interpreted as if written *ve-tamut* (and you will die), for the time of one’s natural death is not in one’s hands. See I.E. on Gen. 1:22 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 42). It is possible that the first time Scripture says, *and the priest shall cause her to swear*⁵⁸ means, and the priest shall cause her to swear by invoking God’s name. The second time this phrase is used⁵⁹ it means, as Scripture notes,⁶⁰ via the curses.

footnotes:
⁵⁶ Hinnaki
⁵⁷ U-mot
⁵⁸ That is, in our verse.
⁵⁹ That is, in verse 21.
⁶⁰ In verse 21.

THY THIGH…THY BELLY. They are the neighbors.⁶¹

TO SWELL. The word *tzavah* means to swell. The word is not found anywhere else in Scripture.⁶²

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:22:1)

TO FALL AWAY. The *lamed* of *lanpil* (to fall away) is vocalized with a *pattach* because the *heh* of the form⁶³. is missing.⁶⁴ is short for *le-hanpil*. The same is true of the word *lashmid* (to destroy) (Is. 23:11).⁶⁵ is short for *le-hashmid*.

AMEN, AMEN. The word amen is repeated for emphasis.⁶⁶ says.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:23:1

THE MEMORIAL-PART THEREOF.⁶⁸. This literally means her memorial. Hence I.E.’s comment. That which served as her memorial, for good or bad in accordance with her deeds.

### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:27:1)

AND HAVE ACTED UNFAITHFULLY. *Va-timal ma’al* (and have acted unfaithfully) is to be understood here as I have explained.⁶⁹ It refers to something covered.⁷⁰ means to remove a cover (*me’il*), to uncover that which should remain covered. *Va-time’ol ma’al* thus means, and have committed an act of treachery.

### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:28:1)


**ונזרעה זרע.** שיתן לה השם זרע בשכר הקלון שאירע לה:


AND SHALL CONCEIVE SEED. God will give her seed in recompense for the shame which befell her.



### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:31:1)


**תשא את עונה.** אם נטמאה:


SHALL BEAR HER INIQUITY. If she was defiled.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 5:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/5:31:2)


וטעם **ונקה האיש מעון.** כי אם עבר עליו רוח קנאה הנה היא אסורה לו שישכב עמה והשוכב יש לו עון. יש אומרים שטעם הסמך פרשת נזיר לפרשת סוטה שיהיה לה בן נזיר אם לא נטמאה ולפי דעתי כי נסמכה בעבור נזירת האשה שהיא הפך המועלת כי רובי העבירות סבתם היין ואשה שלא תתקן שער ראשה איננה מבקשת להבעל:


Our verse states, *And the man shall be clear from iniquity*.⁷¹ For if a spirit of jealousy came upon him she is prohibited to him. He is not allowed to be with her.⁷² If he sleeps with her, he is guilty of sin. Some say that the section dealing with the Nazirite follows the section dealing with the wife who is accused of adultery because if the latter was not defiled, then she will have a son who is a Nazirite. However, I believe that the chapter dealing with the Nazirite follows [the chapter dealing with the wife who is accused of adultery] because it lays down the law regarding a female Nazirite who is the reverse of the unfaithful wife. Now wine⁷³ causes most sins. Furthermore, a woman who does not fix her hair⁷⁴ does not seek sexual relations.⁷⁵

footnotes:
⁷¹ If the woman is exonerated and he has relations with her.
⁷² If a man suspects his wife of adultery, he is prohibited from having sexual relations with her until she is exonerated.
⁷³ Which the Nazirite forswears.
⁷⁴ The Nazirite is prohibited from cutting hair.
⁷⁵ Thus the female Nazirite who abstains from wine and does not cut her hair is the reverse of the unfaithful wife who drinks wine and does cut her hair.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:1)


**יפלא.** יפריש או יעשה דבר פלא כי רוב העולם הולכים אחר תאותם:


SHALL CLEARLY UTTER A VOW. The word *yafli* means to set aside. On the other hand, it might mean doing something out of the ordinary,¹ is related to the word *pele* (wonder). for most people follow their lusts.²

footnotes:
¹ The word yafli
² But this person took an oath to separate himself from the pleasure of drinking. Hence what he did was wondrous.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:2)


**נדר נזיר.** פירוש נדר להיות נזיר כי נזיר הוא תאר השם:


THE VOW OF A NAZIRITE. *Neder nazir* means an oath to be a Nazirite, for the word *nazir* (Nazirite) is an adjective.³ modifies the person who takes the oath, for according to I.E. the meaning of *neder nazir* is to be a Nazirite person. See Weiser. The word *ve-yenazeru* (that they separate) (Lev. 22:13) is related to it.

footnotes:
³ Nazir


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:2:3)


**נזיר** מגזירת וינזרו שירחיק מהתאות ועשה זה לעבודת השם כי היין משחית הדעת ועבודת השם ובפרשת כי תצא מחנה ארמוז לך סוד:


[TO CONSECRATE HIMSELF UNTO THE LORD.]⁴. This literally means to separate himself unto the Lord. Hence I.E.’s comment. To distance himself from lust. He does this in order to serve God, for wine destroys reason and the proper service of God.⁵ means to separate (himself from wine) in order to serve God. I will hint at a secret in the chapter that opens with *When thou goest forth in camp against thine enemies* (Deut. 23:10).

footnotes:
⁴ The Hebrew reads, le-hazir la-Adonai
⁵ Thus le-nazir la-Adonai



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:1)


וטעם **מיין ושכר.** אם נדר להיותו נזיר וקדוש מיין ושכר יזיר על דעת המעתיקים ששכר גדול מהתירוש ואחרים אמרו שכר ממש כל דבר שישתכר האדם ממנו:


[HE SHALL ABSTAIN FROM WINE AND STRONG DRINK.] Its meaning is, if he took a vow to be a Nazirite and holy, then he shall separate himself from wine and strong drink. According to the transmitters of tradition, *shekhar* (strong drink) is aged wine.⁶ refers to old wine while *yayin* refers to young wine. See Targum and Rashi. Others say that *shekhar* is to be taken literally; it refers to any drink that intoxicates a person.

footnotes:
⁶ In other words, shekhar


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:2)


וטעם **חומץ יין** והמשרה והענבים. לשום גדר שירחק מן היין כלל ובאשת מנוח פי' כל טומאה כל אשר יצא מגפן היין:


[HE SHALL DRINK NO VINEGAR OF WINE…NEITHER SHALL HE DRINK ANY LIQUOR OF GRAPES, NOR EAT FRESH GRAPES.] This explains the oath.⁷ He must distance himself from anything connected to wine.⁸ In the account of the wife of Manoah,⁹ Scripture states, *and eat not any unclean thing* (Jud. 13:7). *She*¹⁰ *may not eat of any thing that cometh of the grape-vine* (Jud. 13:14).

footnotes:
⁷ Taken by the Nazirite.
⁸ Hence he must not eat any grapes or grape products even if they are not intoxicating.
⁹ The mother of Samson.
¹⁰ Samson’s mother.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:3:3)


**משרת.** אין לו חבר והתי"ו לסמיכת לשון נקבה וטעמו כל דבר שימס עם ענבים ויש אומרים משקה מבושל:


LIQUOR OF. The word *mishrat* (liquor of) is not found elsewhere in Scripture. The *tav* indicates that the word is feminine and is in the construct. *Mishrat anavim* refers to anything that has been mashed with grapes. Others say that *mishrat anavim* refers to a cooked drink.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Made from grapes.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:4:1)


**מחרצנים.** אין לו ריע וכן זג והמפרשים אמרו חרצנים חצונים וזג פנימי' ואחרים הפכו הפירוש ויהיה פירוש מחרצנים דבק עם מכל אשר יעשה כל מה שיעשה מאלה שניהם:


PRESSED GRAPES. The word *chartzannim* (pressed grapes) is not found anywhere else in Scripture. The same applies to the word *zag* (grape-stone). The commentaries tells us that *chartzannim* refers to the outer skin of the grape and *zag* to the pits. Others say the reverse.¹² 34b. According to these interpretations, *me-chartzannim* (from the pressed grapes)¹³ (from the pressed grapes even to the grape-stone). is connected to *nothing that is made of the grape-vine*. The meaning of our verse is that the Nazirite shall eat nothing made from the grapevine, that is, from these two things.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹² See Nazir
¹³ That is, me-chartzannim ad zag
¹⁴ The outer skin and the pit of the grape.

**לאביו ולאמו.** ואף כי לאשתו ולבתו ולאחרים ויש אומרים כי מלת נזיר מגזרת נזר והעד כי נזר אלהיו על ראשו ואיננו רחוק ודע כי כל בני אדם עבדי תאות העולם והמלך באמת שיש לו נזר ועטרת מלכות בראשו כל מי שהוא חפשי מן התאות:


FOR HIS FATHER, OR FOR HIS MOTHER. And certainly not for his wife, his daughter, and others. Some say that the word *nazir* (Nazirite) is related to the word *nezer* (crown). *Because his consecration* (nezer) *unto God is upon his head*¹⁹) his God is upon his head.” is proof of this. This interpretation is not farfetched.²⁰. Note that most people are slaves to worldly lusts. The true king, the one who has a crown, the one who wears the royal diadem, is the person who is free from this lust.²¹, the one who wears the crown.

ONE FOR A SIN-OFFERING AND THE OTHER FOR A BURNT-OFFERING. Its secret was explained by the sages. They said that the reward of sin is sin.²⁷ 4:2. I.E.’s point is that the Nazirite must have been guilty of some sin for uncleanliness to have befallen him during his period of Naziriteship (Filwarg).

FOR THAT HE SINNED BY REASON OF THE BODY.²⁸. J.P.S. renders it as dead. The reference is to a dead body. ### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:13:1)


**מלאת.** שם הפועל מבעלי האל"ף על מנהג בעלי הה"א באחרונה וכמוהו לבלתי קראת לנו:


FULFILLED. *Melot* (fulfilled) is an infinitive. Its final root letter is an *alef*.³³. It is conjugated like a word that has a *heh* as a final root letter.³⁴. Words that end in an *alef* end in an *o* in the infinitive. Thus the infinitive of *mem, lamed, alef* should be *melo*. It is like the word *kero’ot* (call)³⁵, but whose infinitive form follows the paradigm of a word ending in a *heh*. in *that thou didst call us* (Jud. 8:1).

footnotes:
³³ Its root is mem, lamed, alef
³⁴ Hence the ending ot
³⁵ Which comes from the root kof, resh, alef


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:13:2)


**יביא אותו.** יביא נפשו כמו וירעו הרועים אותם או יביא הכהן אותו בצווי בעל כרחו להקריב את קרבנו:


HE SHALL BRING IT.³⁶. *Oto* can mean him or it. I.E. renders our phrase, he shall bring him. *Yavi oto* (he shall bring it) means he³⁷ shall bring himself.³⁸ (him) here has the meaning of himself. Compare, *but the shepherds fed themselves* (Ezek. 34:8).³⁹). However, in order to makes sense of the verse we must render *otam* as themselves. Similarly, *oto* (him) in our verse is to be rendered as himself. Or *yavi oto* means that the *kohen* shall bring him⁴⁰ against his will by commanding that he be forced to bring his sacrifice.

³⁷ The Nazirite.
³⁸ In other words, oto

⁴⁰ The Nazirite if he refuses to bring his offering.

### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:21:1)


וטעם **מלבד אשר תשיג ידו.** חיוב שיתן כפי ממונו גם כפי ימי הנדר אם רבים אם מעטים ונסמכה פרשת ברכת כהנים לפרשת נזיר כאשר השלים תורת הנזיר שהוא קדוש הזכיר תורת כהנים שהם קדושים והמכחישים אמרו כי זאת הברכה היה הכהן מברך לכל מביא מנחה ונסמכה בעבור על פי אשר תשיג יד הנודר והאמת כמו שהעתיקו חז"ל והעד וישא אהרן את ידיו אל העם ויברכם ביום השמיני ונכתבה זאת הפרשה שהשם צוה בהקמת המשכן שיברך אהרן את העם ושם כתיב נשיאות כפים כי הכפים כידים רק ידים כללים:


[BESIDE THAT FOR WHICH HIS MEANS SUFFICE.] It means that he is obligated to give in accordance with his wealth. He is also obligated to give in accordance with the days of his vow, whether they are few or whether they are many.⁵¹ The chapter dealing with the blessings of the *kohanim*⁵² follows the chapter dealing with the Nazirite because, after concluding the laws of the Nazirite who is holy, Scripture mentions the laws of the *kohanim* who are holy. Those who contradict Rabbinic tradition⁵³ say that the *kohen* used this blessing to bless all who brought a gift. They claim that the blessing of the *kohanim* follows the law of the Nazirite because the latter concludes with “beside that which the means of the celebrant suffice.”⁵⁴. However, the truth is in keeping with the tradition of our sages of blessed memory.⁵⁵ *And Aaron lifted up his hands toward the people, and blessed them* (Lev. 9:22)⁵⁶ on the eighth day⁵⁷ is proof of this. This portion⁵⁸ was written because God commanded Aaron to bless the people when the tabernacle was erected. Scripture there⁵⁹ mentions, *nesi’at kappayim* (the lifting of the hands),⁶⁰. for the word *kappayim* means the same as *yadayim* (hands).⁶¹ (lifted up his hands). According to I.E. this means the same as *va-yissa et kapav*. Thus Lev. 9:22 refers to what the rabbis call *nisi’at kappayim*, that is, the blessing given by the *kohanim*. However, the term *yadayim* is a more general term.⁶² technically refers to the fingers and their sockets (Weiser).

⁵³ The Karaites.
⁵⁴ I.E.’s paraphrase of beside that for which his means suffice
⁵⁵ The rabbis teach that this is a blessing to be given to all of Israel. This is so even though it is written in the singular.
⁵⁶ We thus see that Aaron blessed all of Israel. I.E. assumes that the reference is to the blessing mentioned in verses 24-26.
⁵⁷ Of consecration when the tabernacle was dedicated.
⁵⁸ Verses 24-26.
⁵⁹ In Lev. 9:22.
⁶⁰ The Rabbinic term for the blessing given by the kohanim
⁶¹ Lev. 9:22 reads: Va-yissa et yadav
⁶² It takes in the entire hand, while kappayim

AND KEEP THEE. God will protect what he has increased so that no one steals it.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:25:1)


**יאר ה' פניו אליך.** כטעם באור פני מלך חיים והטעם וכל אשר תבקש ממנו ובשעת שתדרשו יאר פניו והטעם יקבל אותך וירצה למלא' שאלתך מיד:


THE LORD MAKE HIS FACE TO SHINE UPON THEE. Its meaning is the same as *In the light of the king’s countenance is life* (Prov. 16:15). It means may God make his face to shine upon you when you ask something of Him and at the time that you make the request. May God accept you and may His will be to immediately fulfill your request.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:25:2)


ואם בקשת אותו בעת צרה הוא **יחנך** גם יתכן היות ויחנך ויתן לך בקשתך כטעם חנוני אתם אשר חנן אלהים מגזרת חנן:


[AND BE GRACIOUS TO THEE.] If you make a request of God in a time of trouble, may He be gracious to you. *Ve-yechunekka* (and be gracious to thee) is similar to *channuni* (have pity) in *have pity upon me, O ye* (Job. 19:21) and *chanan* (graciously given) in *The children whom God hath graciously given* (Gen. 33:5).⁶⁴. *Ve-yechunekka* comes from the same root as *chanan*.⁶⁵

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:26:1

THE LORD LIFT UP HIS COUNTENANCE UPON THEE. The reverse of *I will hide Mine eyes from you* (Is. 1:15). As I have already explained,⁶⁶ its meaning is, wherever you go My face shall be lifted up to you.

AND GIVE THEE PEACE. Its meaning is similar to, *no evil* — from stones, from evil animals, from any enemy — *touch thee* (Job. 5:19).



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:27:1)


**ושמו את שמי.** כטעם לשום שמו שם שיהיו מקודשים בשם או טעמו כאשר יזכירו שמי עליהם כי בכל אחד מהשלשה פסוקים השם הנכבד והנורא:


SO SHALL THEY PUT MY NAME. Its meaning is similar to *to set His name there* (Deut. 14:24),⁶⁷ that is, the children of Israel be sanctified by God’s name. Or it means that when they shall mention My name over the children of Israel [I will bless them], for God’s revered and awe-inspiring name is found in each one of the three verses.⁶⁸ refers to.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 6:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/6:27:2)


**ואני אברכם.** ויתכן היות מ"ם אברכם סימן הכהנים המברכים או יהיה סימן לישראל והטעם אם הם יברכום אני אברכם והטעם שאקיים ברכתם לפי דעתי שמ"ם אברכם סימן לכלם כהנים וישראלים:


AND I WILL BLESS THEM. It is possible that the *mem*⁶⁹ of *va-avarekhem* is a pronominal suffix meaning them. of *avarekhem* (I will bless them) refers to the *kohanim* who utter the blessing. It means they will bless Israel, and I will bless those who bless.⁷⁰ is: the *kohanim* shall bless Israel and I will bless the *kohanim*. It is also possible that the *mem* of *avarekhem* refers to Israel.⁷¹ means and I will bless Israel. Its meaning is, if the *kohanim* bless Israel, then I will bless Israel; that is, I will fulfill the blessing of the *kohanim*. In my opinion, the *mem* of *avarekhem* refers to all of them, the *kohanim* and the Israelites.⁷² is, I will bless both Israel and the *kohanim*.

## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:1)


וטעם **ויהי ביום כלות משה.** אחר ברכת כהנים כי כן היה כי ביום שנשא אהרן את ידיו אל העם ויברכם החלה חנוכת המזבח ופסוק ויהי ביום כלות משה להקים את המשכן לעד על דברי המעתיקים כי היה סותרו ומקימו וחוזר לסותרו ולהקימו כל ז' ימי המלואים להרגיל' כי טעם ויקדש אותם בהזאת הדם בשבעת ימי המלואים:


[AND IT CAME TO PASS ON THE DAY THAT MOSES HAD MADE AN END OF SETTING UP THE TABERNACLE.] Moses made an end to the setting up of the tabernacle after the blessing of the *kohanim*,¹ blessed Israel. See Num. 6:24-26; Lev. 9:22. as this was how events unfolded.² at the end of chapter 6. On the day that Aaron lifted up his hands toward the people and blessed them,³ the dedication of the tabernacle began.⁴ The verse *And it came to pass on the day that Moses had made an end of setting up the tabernacle* is proof of the words of our sages, that Moses took the tabernacle down, raised it up and took it down again, and erected it again throughout the seven days of dedication to train the *kohanim*.⁵ indicates that the tabernacle had been set up and taken down a number of times. See I.E. on Ex. 40:2 and the notes thereto (Vol. 2, p. 754). It should be noted that I.E. there rejects the opinion that Moses erected and took down the tabernacle during the seven days of dedication. For the meaning of *and sanctified them* is that he sanctified them [the furniture, the altar, and the vessels] by the sprinkling of the blood⁶ during the seven days of dedication.⁷

¹ After the kohanim
² Chapter 7 follows the blessing of the kohanim
³ Lev. 9:22.
⁴ Our verse.
⁵ According to the rabbis, had made an end of setting up the tabernacle
⁶ See Ex. 29:21; Ex. 29:35-7; Lev. 8:30.
⁷ When the tabernacle was set up and taken down.

AND THE ALTAR AND ALL THE VESSELS THEREOF. This is connected to *on the day that Moses had made an end of setting up the tabernacle*.⁸ also applies to *and the altar*. It means that he set the altar up in its place.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:3)


**ואת כל כליו וימשחם.** בשמן המשחה:


AND ALL THE VESSELS THEREOF, AND HAD ANOINTED THEM. With the anointing oil


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:1:4)


**ויקדש אותם.** בדם והזכיר המזבח בעבור החנוכה:


AND SANCTIFIED THEM. With blood. Scripture mentions the altar because of its dedication.⁹, the altar is included in *the furniture thereof*. Hence I.E. points out the altar is singled out for special mention because it had a special dedication. 
According to I.E. sex is one of the things that creates a barrier between man and God (*Yesod Mora*, chapter 7, pp. 107 and 108). The Nazirite is an individual who takes a vow to abstain from wine and haircutting. I.E. connects wine and hair to sexuality. He believes that most sexual taboos are violated because the transgressor has imbibed wine. As to the prohibition of cutting the hair, I.E. writes, “A woman who does not fix her hair does not seek to engage in sexual relationships” (Num. 5:31). He similarly notes that the Torah states that “a razor shall not come upon the head of a Nazirite so that he does not make himself attractive to women.” (*Yesod Mora*, p. 62).

⁹ Prima facie

**ויקריבו נשיאי ישראל.** שם כלל והטעם שעשו קרבן לה' אל פתח אהל מועד:


THAT THE PRINCES OF ISRAEL…OFFERED. Scripture employs a general term.¹⁰ Its meaning is that they brought their offering to the Lord before the opening of the tabernacle.

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:1)


**צב.** כמו ובצבים ובפרדים מין ממיני השוורים המושכים את העגלות ויש אומרים שהוא כטעם מלאות יותר מדאי מגזרת ואת בטנך צבה בדרך רחוקה:


COVERED. The word *tzav* (covered) here is similar to the word *tzabbim* (Utters) in *and in litters, and upon mules* (Is. 66:20). The reference is to a type of ox that pulls wagons.¹¹ (covered wagons) means wagons pulled by a *tzav*. This commentary renders Is. 66:20, *and upon oxen and mules*. Others say that *eglot tzav* (covered wagons) refers to a wagon that is overloaded and that *tzav* is connected to the word *tzavah* (swell) (Num. 5:21). However, this is farfetched.

footnotes:
¹¹ According to this interpretation, here eglot tzav


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:2)


כבר בארתי בספר מאזנים שמ"ם שנים עשר דבק ומוכרת והטעם שנים ועשר ונסמך אל עשר לקצר כמו שלש עשרה וכן שלש אלה ובאה מלת שני עשר בחסרון מ"ם דרך קצרה:


[AND TWELVE OXEN.] I have already explained in my work *Moznayim*¹² that the *mem* in *shenem asar* (twelve) remains both in the absolute¹³). and in the construct.¹⁴ (twelve). When a word ending in a *mem* is connected to another word, the *mem* is dropped. Hence I.E.’s comment. The meaning of *shenem asar* (twelve) is two (*shenayim*) and ten (*eser*).¹⁵ is short for *shenayim ve-asar*, Hence the change in the vowel, *shenem* rather than *shenayim*. The word *shenem* is connected to *aser* for purposes of brevity. The same applies to *shelosh elleh* (these three) (Ex. 21:11).¹⁶ is in the construct with *elleh*. Otherwise the phrase would be written *shalosh elleh*. See I.E. on Ex. 21:11 (Vol. 2, p. 464). The phrase *shene asar* (twelve)¹⁷ without the *mem* is an abridged form.¹⁸ is short for *shenem asar*.

¹³ That is, in the word two (shenayim
¹⁴ In the phrase shenem asar
¹⁵ In other words, shenem asar
¹⁶ Shelosh
¹⁷ This is the form used in our verse.
¹⁸ Shene asar

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:3)


**על שני הנשאים.** כמו לשני.


FOR EVERY TWO OF THE PRINCES. *Al shene ha-nesi’im* means for every two of the princes.¹⁹ usually means on. I.E. points out that here it means for.

¹⁹ The word al


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:3:4)


אמר הכתוב וקרבנו כי כבר הזכיר המקריב והוי"ו יש לו טעם כאומר וזה קרבנו ואמר בשני הקריב את קרבנו כי כן דרך המקרא כמו ויאמר יצחק אל אברהם אביו ויאמר אבי בעבור שארכו לו הדברים וכן ויאמר המלך אחשורוש ויאמר לאסתר המלכה ולא הזכיר ביתר הנשיאים הקריב כי אחז דרך קצרה וכן לגלגלותם במספר ראובן ושמעון לבדם וכן רבים:


[13. AND HIS OFFERING.] Scripture reads, *and his offering* because Scripture had already stated, *And he that presented* [*his offering*] (v. 12). The *vav* of *ve-korbano* (and his offering) means, and this was his offering. Scripture writes with regard to the second day, *hikriv et korbano* (he offered his offering)²⁰. There is no reason to say he offered his offering. Scripture should have read, he offered. (v. 18), for this is the style of Scripture. Compare, *And Isaac spoke unto Abraham his father, and said: ‘My father*’ (Gen. 22:7). The word father is repeated because Scripture is being lengthy. *Then spoke the king Ahasuerus and said unto Esther the queen* (Esth. 7:5) is also similar.²¹ and *spoke*). Scripture does not mention the term *hikriv* (he presented) with regard to the other princes,²² for Scripture wanted to be brief. Similarly the term *le-gulgelotam* (by their poles) is used only in the numbering of Reuben²³ and Simeon.²⁴ There are many such examples.²⁵

²¹ Here too Scripture employs a lengthy style (said
²² It is used only in regard to the offering of Nethanel the son of Zuar.
²³ Num. 1:20.
²⁴ Num. 1:22. Also see I.E. on 1:19.
²⁵ Where Scripture employs an abbreviated style.



### Verse 48

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:48:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:48:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:48:1)


**ביום השביעי.** יש אומרים כי בשבת הקריבו והיא הוראת שעה ויש אומרים כי היום הוא שביעי לחנוכת המזבח והמכחישים אמרו כי לא היה תחלת החנוכה יום ראשון לשבוע וזה לא יועיל כי לא יתכן שלא יהיה שבת בין שנים עשר יום. והנכון בעיני כי הוא כפירוש השני זבח השלמים וכן ביום השביעי בהקפת יריחו וכן ז' הימים בחנוכת הבית בעבור יום ענוי נפש:


ON THE SEVENTH DAY. Some say that Elishama the son of Ammihud presented his offering on the Sabbath. It was a ruling for that time.²⁶ Others say that the reference is to the seventh day of the dedication of the tabernacle.²⁷ Those who disagree with our sages say that the first day of the altar’s dedication did not fall on the first day of the week.²⁸ However, this is of no avail, for it is impossible that there be no Sabbath in twelve days.I believe that the second interpretation²⁹ is correct, because of the peace offering.³⁰ The seventh day of the circling of Jericho is similar.³¹ Likewise the seven days of the dedication of the temple (I Kings 8:65) because of the Day of Atonement.³²

²⁸ Hence the seventh day of dedication did not fall on the Sabbath.
²⁹ That the seventh day refers to the seventh day of dedication, not to the seventh day of the week.
³⁰ Brought by Elishama. Peace offerings were not brought on the Sabbath.
³¹ See Josh. 6:6-15. According to I.E. the seventh day on which the Israelites circled Jericho was not a Sabbath.
³² The second series of seven days of the dedication of the first temple apparently took in the Day of Atonement. Now if this is the case, then the people ate on Yom Kippur. This is the opinion of the sages of the Talmud. According to I.E. these seven days do not include Yom Kippur, for there were no dedication festivities on that day.

**פר אחד בן בקר.** קטן כמו בן יונה:

### Verse 53

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:53:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:53:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:53:1)


**בקר שנים.** גם הם פרים זכרים וטעמו מין הבקר והעד וכל בקר זבח השלמים עשרים וארבעה פרים:


TWO OXEN. These were also male bullocks.³⁴ The meaning of *bakar* (oxen) is, from the kind of cattle.³⁵ (two oxen) literally means two of the kind of cattle, the reference being to two bulls. *And all the oxen for the sacrifice of peace-offerings twenty and four bullocks* (v. 88) is proof of this.³⁶ refers to bullocks.

### Verse 72

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:72:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:72:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:72:1)


**ביום עשתי עשר יום.** כבר בארתי בספר מאזנים למה השתנה זה המספר וטעם **עשתי.** כמו עשתנותיו מה שיולידו מחשבותיו כאילו העשר הוליד והוא סוד גדול. ויאמר רבי יונה הספרדי כי פירושו על שתי עשר והטעם החשבון שהוא קודם ממנו והנה טעה שתי טעיות גדולות. האחד כי חשבון שנים עשר הוא על אחד עשר הפך דבורו והעד הנאמן מבן עשרים שנה ומעלה. והטעות השנית כי אילו היה על שתי היה ראוי שיאמר עשני עשר ולא עשתי עשר כי עשתי לשון נקבה רק עשתי מלה אחת:


ON THE ELEVENTH DAY. I have already explained in my work *moznayim* why this number³⁷ (eleven). differs [from the other numbers].³⁸ (2) *asar*; 13, *sheloshah* (3) *asar* (10); etc. However, the word for eleven in our verse is *ashte asar* and not *ahad* (1) *asar* (10). The word *ashte* in (*ashte asar*, eleven) is similar to the word *eshtonotav* (his thoughts) (Ps. 146:4). It (*ashte*) refers to that which a person’s thoughts give birth to. The number ten, as it were begets [eleven, etc.]. This is a great secret.³⁹, p. 163). Rabbi Jonah the Sephardi⁴⁰ says that *ashte asar* is short for *al shete asar*. Its meaning is, the number that precedes twelve.⁴¹ means eleven. Now Rabbi Jonah made two great mistakes. One, contrary to his words, the number twelve is beyond (*al*) the number eleven.⁴² means above, not before. Hence if *ashte asar* is short for *al shetem asar*, then its meaning is, not before 12 but beyond 12. *From twenty years old and upward* (va-ma’alah) (Num. 1:3) is proof of the aforementioned.⁴³ and *al* come from the same root. We thus see that *al* means upward or beyond, not before. His second mistake is that if the word *ashte* is short for *al shete*, then the text should have read *ashne asar*,⁴⁴. not *ashte asar*. For *ashte* is in the feminine.⁴⁵ is short for *shetem asar*. *Shetem asar* is feminine. Now *yom* is masculine. Why would Scripture employ a feminine? *Ashte* is therefore one word.

³⁹ According to I.E., “Ten is the foundation of all sums, for all sums that follow ten are made up of a part or parts of ten, or come into being by doubling ten, multiplying ten, or a combination of the two” (The Secret of the Torah
⁴⁰ Jonah ibn Janach, the eleventh-century grammarian.
⁴¹ In other words, ashte asar
⁴² In other words, the word al
⁴³ Va-ma’alah
⁴⁴ Because the word for 12 is shenem asar
⁴⁵ According to Rabbi Jonah, ashte



### Verse 89

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:89:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:1)


וטעם **ובבא משה.** יתכן שתחלת הדבור שהוא ויקרא אל משה היה כאשר נשלמה החנוכה וכל המדקדקים אמרו כי מדבר אליו מבנין התפעל והתי"ו מובלע ולפי דעתי שהוא שם הפועל כמו הנה לא ידעתי דבר והמ"ם תחת מן למשפט הלשון:


[AND WHEN MOSES WENT IN.] It is likely that the beginning of God’s word to Moses [in the tabernacle], which is what *And the Lord called unto Moses* (Lev. 1:1) refers to, was uttered when the dedication of the tabernacle was completed. All the grammarians say that the word *middabber* (speaking) in *speaking unto him* is a *hitpa’el*⁴⁶ with the *tav* swallowed.⁴⁷. In other words, *middabber* is short for *mitdabber*. However, in my opinion *middadbber* is an infinitive. It is similar to the word *dabber* (speak)⁴⁸ in *behold, I cannot speak* (Jer. 1:5). The *mem* prefixed to *middabber* is in place of the word *min*, as is the rule in the Hebrew language.⁴⁹ placed in front of a word has the meaning of from. I.E. renders *va-yishma et ha-kol middabber elav* (then he heard the voice speaking unto him) as, then he heard the voice of the One who spoke to him.

footnotes:
⁴⁶ So too Rashi.
⁴⁷ By the dallet
⁴⁸ Which is an infinitive.
⁴⁹ A mem


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:89:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:2)


וטעם **וישמע.** שהוא לבדו היה שומע הקול ולא ישמענו מי שהוא באהל מועד מחוץ לפרוכת גם זה נכון כי השם הוסיף בהרגשת אזניו כאשר הוסיף באור עיני נער אלישע וכן ויגל ה' את עיני בלעם:


The meaning of *va-yishma* (then he heard) is that only Moses heard the voice. Anyone in the tent of meeting outside of the curtain did not hear the voice. The following is also correct: God made Moses’ ear supersensitive⁵⁰ as he added to the sight of Elisha’s young man (II Kings 6:17). Similarly *Then the Lord opened the eyes of Balaam* (Num. 22:31).⁵¹

footnotes:
⁵⁰ So that Moses heard what others did not
⁵¹ Which, according to I.E., means God made his eyes more sensitive than the eyes of other people.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 7:89:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/7:89:3)


**וידבר אליו.** כן הי' משפט הדבור תמיד:


AND HE SPOKE UNTO HIM. This was the way God’s word always came to Moses.⁵²

footnotes:
⁵² Once the tabernacle was erected.



## Chapter 8


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:2:1)


**דבר אל אהרן.** כי הוא חייב במצות הדלקת הנר:


SPEAK UNTO AARON. For he is obligated to fulfill the commandment of lighting the lamps.¹

footnotes:
¹ See Ex. 27:21.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:2:2)


**אל מול פני המנורה.** פירוש והאיר אל עבר פניה והנה מלת האיר יוצאה כמו תאיר נרי ואין כן יאירו והארץ האירה ונסמכה זאת הפרשה להורות כי הדבור יהיה גם בלילה כי שם יהיה הנר דלוק ולא יכבה:


IN FRONT OF THE CANDLESTICK. This explains *to give light* (he’ir) *over against it* (Ex. 25:37).² is, shall give light in front of the candlestick. Now the word *he’ir* (give light)³ is transitive. It is similar to the word *ta’ir* (dost light) in *For Thou dost light my light* (Ps. 18:29). However, this is not the case with the word *ya’iru* (shall give light)⁴ (shall give light) is intransitive and means shall shine, not shall shine upon. I.E. renders our clause, *shall shine in front of the candlestick*. and the word *he’irah* (did shine) in *and the earth did shine* (Ezek. 43:2). This section⁵ follows⁶ in order to teach that God’s word also came at night, for the light was to burn in the tabernacle and was not to be extinguished.

footnotes:
² In other words, the meaning of to give light over against it
³ In Ex. 25:37.
⁴ In our verse. According to I.E., ya’iru
⁵ Verses 1-4.
⁶ The statement telling us that God spoke to Moses in the tent of meeting.

**פרחה.** ידוע ולא היה כח בחרש חכם לעשותה כן לולי שהשם הראה אותה למשה במראה הנבואה:


THE FLOWERS THEREOF. The meaning of the word *pirchah* (flowers thereof) is well known.¹⁰ If God had not shown Moses the menorah in a prophetic vision, then no wise craftsman would have had the ability to make the menorah described in Scripture.

SO HE MADE. This refers to Moses. The meaning of *made* is, made by command.¹¹ The same applies to *And thou shalt make a candlestick of pure gold* (Ex. 25:31).

וטעם הסמך פרשת הלוים להורות כי אהרן יתעסק בהדלקת הנר ובשאר העבודות יעזרוהו הלוים ויעמדו לפניו לעשות מה שיצוה:


The section dealing with the Levites follows the one dealing with the lighting of the menorah to teach us that Aaron shall occupy himself with the kindling of the lamps.¹² However, the Levites shall assist him in the other services and shall stand before him to do all that he commands.¹³

### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:7:1)


**מי חטאת.** כמו תחטאני או טעמו כחטאת הקרבן:


THE WATER OF PURIFICATION. *Chattat* (purification)¹⁶ usually means sin. Hence I.E.’s comment. here is similar to the word *techatteni* (purify me) (Ps. 51:9).¹⁷ Or it is possible that the *me chattat* (the water of purification) was similar to a sin offering (*korban chattat*).¹⁸ (the water of purification) means sin water, water which serves as a sin offering. In other words, the water atones for sin.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The word chattat
¹⁷ Translated according to I.E.
¹⁸ In this case, me chattat


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:7:2)


**הזה עליהם.** אמר רבי משה שפ"א הפעל נו"ן כמו הכה תכה ונכון הוא:


SPRINKLE…UPON THEM. Rabbi Moses¹⁹ says that the first root letter of *hazzeh* (sprinkle) is a *nun*.²⁰ is *nun, zayin, heh*. Compare, *hakkeh*²¹ (and of *takkeh*) is *nun, kof, heh*. Thus *hazzeh* is like *hakkeh*. They are both in the *hifil* and they both come from a root that opens with a *nun*. *takkeh* (thou shalt surely smite) (Deut. 13:16). Rabbi Moses is correct.

footnotes:
¹⁹ Moses ibn Gikatilla, an 11th-century Spanish philologist and Bible commentator.
²⁰ In other words, the root of hazzeh
²¹ The root of hakkeh

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:8:2)

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:8:3)


**תקח לחטאת.** בציווי כי הם יקחו והטעם יתנו כמו ויקחו לי תרומה או יקחו עמם כמו ויקחו להם איש שה לבית אבות:


SHALT THOU TAKE. By issuing a command, for the Levites shall take.³¹ (the Levites) *take a young bullock*. Thus it is the Levites who do the taking. The meaning of *Then let them take* (ve-lakechu) is, then let them give.³² means shalt thou offer. Compare, *ve-yikechu li terumah* (that they take for Me an offering).³³) to Me an offering. We thus see that the Hebrew word *yikkach* (to take) sometimes has the meaning of to give. On the other hand, the meaning of *Then let them take* might be that they shall take with them.³⁴ (then let them take) has its usual meaning. Compare, *they shall take* (ve-yikechu) *to them every man a lamb, according to their fathers’ houses* (Ex. 12:3).

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:16:1)


**כי נתונים נתונים.** הם ובניהם ישראל נתנום לי ואני לקחתי אותם לי והטעם קבלתים ולא נתנום בעבור פדיון פטרת כל רחם. ופטרת ופטר שתי לשונות כמו צדקה וצדק או על משקל אחר:


FOR THEY ARE WHOLLY GIVEN UNTO ME. They, and their children, are given unto Me.⁴⁰ (for they are wholly given unto Me). I.E. believes that the first *netunim* refers to the Levites and the second to the children of the Levites. The Israelites gave them to Me⁴¹ *and I took them unto Me*;⁴². that is, I accepted them. Why are they given [unto Me]?⁴³. 38. The printed texts apparently read, they are not given (unto me). This is an obvious error. In order to redeem all that openeth the womb. The word *pitrat* (openeth) and *peter* (openeth)⁴⁴ (that openeth the womb). The Book of Exodus refers to the first-born as *peter…rechem* (openeth the womb) (Ex. 13:2). are two different words.⁴⁵ Compare, *tzedek* (righteousness) and *tzedakah* (righteousness).⁴⁶ refers to any act of righteousness. *Tzedakah* in rabbinic literature refers to a gift of charity. Or they might be the same word in two paradigms.⁴⁷ and *pitrat*.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ The Hebrew reads, ki netunim, netunim li
⁴¹ See verses 9 and 10.
⁴² “And I took them unto Me” is I.E.’s paraphrase of have I taken them unto Me
⁴³ So Vat. Ebr
⁴⁴ Our verse refers to the first born as pitrat…ṙechem
⁴⁵ There is a difference in nuance between these two words. However, I.E. does not explain how they differ. For another interpretation, see Filwarg.
⁴⁶ Tzedek
⁴⁷ In other words, there is no difference between peter

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:18:1)


וטעם **ואקח את הלוים.** וכבר אמר לקחתי אותם לי כי כן דרך המקרא והוא דבק עם הפסוק הבא אחריו וכאשר לקחתי לי הלוים נתתים אני לאהרן ולבניו:


[AND I HAVE TAKEN THE LEVITES.] Scripture has already stated, *have I taken them unto Me* (v. 16). This is the style of Scripture.⁵¹ *And I have taken the Levites* is connected to the verse which follows. It means, when I took the Levites, I gave them to Aaron and his sons (v. 19).

footnotes:
⁵¹ To repeat itself after digressing.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:19:1)


**ולכפר.** להיותם כפר נפשם כאשר פירשתי:


AND TO MAKE ATONEMENT. To be an atonement for the souls of the children of Israel, as I have already explained.⁵²

footnotes:
⁵² See I.E. on Ex. 32:29.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:20:1)


**ויעש משה ואהרן.** משה בצווי ואהרן שהניפם וישראל שסמכו עליהם:


THUS DID MOSES AND AARON. Moses by command, Aaron by waving them, and Israel by the laying of their hands on them.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:21:1)


וטעם **ויתחטאו.** כי גם הם עשו:


[AND THE LEVITES PURIFIED THEMSELVES.] Scripture tells us that they also performed [purification rituals].


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:21:2)


**ויכפר עליהם.** וכבר כפר עליהם ויש אומרים כי בתחלה התחטאו ואחר כן סמכו ידיהם על הפרים ואיננו רחוק ואם כן יהיה פירוש והעמדת את הלוים וכבר העמדתם בתחלה:


AND AARON MADE ATONEMENT FOR THEM. And Aaron had already made atonement for them.⁵³ is a pluperfect that means had made atonement. Others say that the Levites first purified themselves and afterwards laid their hands on the bullocks.⁵⁴ This interpretation is not far off the mark.⁵⁵ (and made atonement). If this is so, then the meaning of *And thou shalt set the Levites* (v. 13) is: and you have already set the Levites⁵⁶ at the beginning.⁵⁷ (had atoned) is not a pluperfect.

footnotes:
⁵³ Before Aaron offered them as a sacred gift before the Lord. Thus va-yekhapper
⁵⁴ After which Aaron offered them for a wave offering and made atonement for them (Krinsky).
⁵⁵ For it is based on a literal interpretation of va-yekhapper
⁵⁶ The atonement ritual was the final act in the cleansing ritual of the Levites.
⁵⁷ That is, before Aaron made atoonement fot them. Thus our verse does not contradict verse 13. In this case, va-yekhapper



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:22:1)


**כן עשו להם.** תמיד בני אהרן לבניהם:


SO DID THEY UNTO THEM. So did the sons of Aaron always unto the children of the Levites.⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁸ The reference is to the commandments in our chapter regarding the Levites (verses 6-14). This apparently applies to future generations as well.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:24:1)


**זאת אשר ללוים.** והטעם זאת הקצבה ואין מבן שלשים שנה מכחיש זה הפסוק כי מבן שלשים שנה הוא לעבודת משא ומבן חמש ועשרים לעבודת האהל:


THIS IS THAT WHICH PERTAINETH UNTO THE LEVITES. Its meaning is, this is the set time of service which pertains to the Levites.⁵⁹ (this is) is short for *zot ha-kitzvah* (the set time of service). The verse reading *from thirty years and upward* (Num. 4:23)⁶⁰. does not contradict our verse,⁶¹ 24a, they begin their studies at the age of 25 and their service at the age of 30. See Rashi. for *from thirty years and upward* refers to the service of bearing burdens (Num. 4:24), while our verse, which reads *from twenty and five years all and upward*, refers to the service in the work of the tent of meeting.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ In other words, zot
⁶⁰ Num. 4:23 gives as the age of all that enter…to do service in the tent of meeting
⁶¹ According to Hullin


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:24:2)


וטעם **לצבא.** להמנות עם צבא העובדים:


The meaning of *li-tzeva* (to perform the service) is, to be counted among the host of workers.⁶² means a host. Hence I.E.’s interpretation.

footnotes:
⁶² The word tzava



### Verse 26

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 8:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/8:26:1)


**במשמרותם.** על הזקנים ונדבקה פרשת הפסח כי החנוכה היתה בחדש הראשון ואז נבדלו הלוים ובאה זאת הפרשה בעבור שהוא כתוב בפסח והיה כי תבאו אל הארץ והיה כי יביאך ה' צוה שהם חייבים גם במדבר:


TO KEEP THE CHARGE. This command is directed to the elders.⁶³ The section dealing with the Passover follows the section dealing with the consecration of the Levites because the dedication of the altar took place in the first month.⁶⁴ The Levites were separated⁶⁵ at that time.⁶⁶ The section dealing with Passover⁶⁷ is written here because, concerning the Passover, it was written, *And it shall come to pass, when ye be come to the land* (Ex. 12:25); *And it shall be when the Lord shall bring thee into the land* (Ex. 13:5).⁶⁸ This chapter⁶⁹ records that God commanded that they were obligated to offer the Passover sacrifice in the desert also.⁷⁰

⁶³ The Levites who are above the age of 50.
⁶⁴ See Num. 7:1, Ex. 40:2, and Lev. 9:1.
⁶⁵ From Israel and consecrated.
⁶⁶ See Num. 3:1 and Ex. 32:29.
⁶⁷ Num. 9:1-8.
⁶⁸ These verses imply that Israel was obligated to offer the Passover sacrifice only in the Land of Israel.
⁶⁹ Num. 9:1.
⁷⁰ Sinai.

**ויהי אנשים.** לא יתכן שיהיה מחנה ישראל גדול ושלא ימותו שם מתים בכל יום:


BUT THERE WERE CERTAIN MEN. It is impossible for a camp to be big as that of the Israelites without people dying every day.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:7:1

**למה נגרע.** מבנין נפעל:


WHEREFORE ARE WE TO BE KEPT BACK. *Niggara* (kept back) is a *nifal*.

STAY YE. At the opening of the tent of meeting.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:10:1)


**או בדרך רחוקה.** אין לחפש כי כבר העתיקי חז"ל כמה היא הרחוקה:


IN A JOURNEY AFAR OFF. We do not have to search for the meaning of this phrase, for our sages have already transmitted to us the meaning of *afar off*.¹. 9:2, *afar off* means outside of the threshold of the temple courtyard. For other opinions see *Pes*. 9:2.

¹ According to the Mishnah, Pes


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:10:2

**לכם.** לטמא נפש:


OF YOU. This refers to being unclean by reason of a dead body.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:10:3)


**או לדורותיכם.** בטמא נפש ודרך רחוקה:


OR OF YOUR GENERATIONS. This refers to both a person who is unclean by reason of a dead body and to one in a journey far off.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:13:1)


**ובדרך לא היה.** או בדרך וכן ומכה אביו ואמו:


AND IS NOT ON A JOURNEY. *U-ve-derekh* (and on a journey) is to be rendered, or is on a journey.² of *u-ve-derekh* is to be rendered “or,” not “and.” I.E. comments thus because he believes that the law of being far off did not apply in the wilderness (I.E. on verse 10). Hence he reads our verse as follows: But the man that is unclean in the wilderness, and is on a journey in future generations. So Filwarg. For additional interpretations see Krinsky and Avi Ezri. *U-makkeh aviv ve-immo* (and he that smiteth his father, or his mother)³ (Ex. 21:15) is similar.⁴

² In other words, the vav
³ Literally, and he that smiteth his father and his mother.

**חטאו ישא.** הוא בעצמו:


SHALL BEAR HIS SIN. He alone.⁵

⁵ Not his descendants.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:14:1)


**וכי יגור אתכם גר.** גם הוא יעשה פסח שני כמשפט ויש אומרים כי הטעם על פסח ראשון:


AND IF A STRANGER SHALL SOJOURN AMONG YOU. He too shall observe the second Passover⁶ in accordance with the ordinance. Others say that the reference is to the first Passover.⁷

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:15:1)


**וביום הקים את המשכן.** החל לספור מסעי ישראל הוצרך להזכיר הענן שחנה על המשכן כי בחנותו יחנו ובנסעם יסעו:


AND ON THE DAY THAT THE TABERNACLE WAS REARED UP. Scripture begins to count the journeys of Israel. It was thus compelled to make mention of the cloud that rested on the tabernacle, for when it camped they camped and when it journeyed⁸. 38. Our texts read, *when they journeyed, they journeyed*. they journeyed.

footnotes:
⁸ So Vat. Ebr


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:15:2)


**לאהל העדות.** על אהל ורבים כן:


EVEN THE TENT OF THE TESTIMONY. *Le-ohel* (even the tent) is to be rendered, on the tent.⁹ usually has the meaning of to. Hence I.E.’s comment. I.E. renders our verse, the cloud covered the tabernacle, on the tent of the testimony. There are many such instances in Scripture.

footnotes:
⁹ The lamed



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:18:1)


**על פי ה' יסעו.** הטעם ידוע כי הענן השם יניחנו ויסענו:


AT THE COMMANDMENT OF THE LORD THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL JOURNEYED. Its meaning is clear, for God caused the cloud to rest and God caused it to journey.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:19:1)


**ובהאריך.** ואם היה מאריך והנה דרך כלל ואחר כן אמר ימים מספר יסעו או יחנו על פי ה':


AND WHEN THE CLOUD TARRIED. The meaning of *u- ve-ha’arikh* (and when it tarried) is to be rendered, and if it tarried.¹⁰ Scripture first speaks in general terms.¹¹ It then goes on to say that sometimes the Israelites would journey for a few days or encamp according to the commandment of the Lord.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Nahmanides interprets similarly.
¹¹ That Israel travelled and camped in the wilderness.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:21:1)


**או יומם ולילה.** הנה פעמים יסעו בלילה ויש אומרים כי פירושו יומם ולילה כמשמעו והוא הנכון בעיני והעד או יומם:


OR IF IT CONTINUED BY DAY AND BY NIGHT. They thus occasionally journeyed at night.¹² (by day and by night) is to be rendered, by day *or* by night. Our verse reads as follows: or if it (the cloud) continued by day or by night, when the cloud was taken up they journeyed. In other words, sometimes the cloud stayed over the tabernacle during the day and was taken up at the beginning of the night. Thus Israel at times took up their journey at night. Others say that *yomam va-laylah* (by day and by night) is to be interpreted literally.¹³ of *yomam va-laylah* is to be translated literally. Thus *yomam va-laylah* means by day *and* by night. Therefore what the verse is telling us is that the cloud continued on the tabernacle a day and a night and was taken up in the morning, and then Israel journeyed. The verse thus teaches that Israel journeyed in the morning, not at night. This interpretation appears correct to me. The term *yomayim* (two days) (v. 22)¹⁴. 38. See also Filwarg. The printed texts have *yomam*. is proof that this interpretation is correct.¹⁵ follows *yomam va-laylah*. *Yomayim* refers to two days and two nights. Hence *yomam va-laylah* must refer to a day and a night.

footnotes:
¹² This means that yomam va-laylah
¹³ In other words, the vav
¹⁴ So Vat. Ebr
¹⁵ The term yomayim

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:22:1)


**או ימים.** שנה תמימה:


OR A YEAR. The word *yomam* means a full year.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ See Lev. 25:29.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 9:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/9:23:1)


**על פי ה' יחנו.** באחרונה כל ימי שבתם במדבר:


AT THE COMMANDMENT OF THE LORD THEY ENCAMPED. This is stated at the end of the chapter to indicate that this was the procedure as long as Israel lived in the wilderness.



## Chapter 10


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:2:1)


**חצוצרת.** שם הפועל בחסרון הצד"י בקריאה ואם הוא כתוב:


MAKE THEE TWO TRUMPETS OF SILVER. *Chatzotzerot* (trumpets) is a noun. In its verbal form, one of the *tzadis* is not pronounced even though it is written.¹ (did blow) in I Chron. 15:24. *Machtzerim* is spelled with two *tzadis*. However, only one of them is pronounced.

footnotes:
¹ See machtzerim


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:2:2)


**למקרא.** שם הפועל וכן ולמסע או הוא שם והטעם לעת מקרא ולעת מסע והראשון נכון בעיני:


FOR THE CALLING. *Le-mikra* (for the calling) is an infinitive. The same applies to *u-le-massa* (and for causing to set forward). On the other hand, they might be nouns. In this case, the word *le-mikra* (for the calling) means at the time of calling, and *le-massa* (and for causing to set forward), at the time of journeying.² The first interpretation appears correct to me.

footnotes:
² According to this interpretation the last clause of our verse reads, and they shall be unto thee (for use) at the time of the calling of the congregation and at the time when the camps set forward.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:3:1)


**ותקעו בהן.** התוקעים כמו ויאמר ליוסף אשר ילדה אותה ללוי והנה הטעם אם יתקעו בשתיהן יתחברו הנשיאים והעדה:


AND WHEN THEY SHALL BLOW WITH THEM. And when the trumpeters shall blow with them.³ It is similar to *and he said to Joseph*⁴ (Gen. 48:1) *and whom she bore to Levi* (Num. 26:59).⁵ Now its meaning is,⁶ if they blow both trumpets,⁷. then the princes and the congregation shall gather.⁸.

³ The subject of “and when they shall blow” is not in the text. Hence I.E.’s comment.
⁴ Translated literally.
⁵ See I.E. on Gen. 48:1 (Vol. 1, p. 412) and the notes thereto. See also I.E. on Num. 26:58.
⁶ The meaning of our verse.
⁷ I.E.’s paraphrase of with them
⁸ I.E.’s interpretation of all the congregation



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:4:1)


**ואם באחת יתקעו.** פירוש בחצוצרה אחת יבואו הנשיאים לבדם ואם היתה תרועה יסע דגל מחנה יהודה אף על פי שהענן על המשכן ולפי דעת רבים שענן אחר הוא הנוסע לפניהם:


AND IF THEY BLOW BUT WITH ONE. This means if they blow with one trumpet, then the princes alone shall come. If a *tru’ah* (an alarm)⁹ is a wavering sound. sounds, then the standard of Judah shall journey, even though the cloud is upon the tabernacle.¹⁰ In the opinion of many, another cloud¹¹ journeyed before the Israelites.¹².

⁹ According to tradition a tru’ah

¹² For Num. 14:14 clearly states, And Thou goest before them, in a pillar of cloud



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:5:1)


**ונסעו המחנות.** כי הם שלש:


THE CAMPS THAT LIE ON THE EAST SIDE SHALL TAKE THEIR JOURNEY. For they consisted of three camps.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ The reference is to the standard of Judah (Num. 2:3). Now the standard of Judah (and so every other standard) consisted of three camps. Hence Scripture refers to the standard of Judah by the term “camps.”



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:6:1)


**תרועה שנית.** שיסע דגל ראובן ולא יתקעו עוד כי הקהתים ועמם הכהנים התוקעים נוסעים קודם דגל אפרים:


AN ALARM THE SECOND TIME. So that the standard of Reuben will take up its journey. They did not blow the trumpets again,¹⁴ for the family of Kohath and with them the *kohanim* who blew the trumpets journeyed before the standard of Ephraim.¹⁵ journeyed between the standard of Reuben and the standard of Ephraim. Thus when it came time for Ephraim to journey, the *kohanim* had already journeyed and were not in a position to sound the trumpets.

footnotes:
¹⁴ After the tribe of Reuben journeyed.
¹⁵ The kohanim

**תרועה יתקעו.** הכהנים למסעיהם כי אחר דגל ראובן נוסעים:


THEY SHALL BLOW AN ALARM FOR THEIR JOURNEYS. The *kohanim* shall blow an alarm for their journeys, for they journeyed after the standard of Reuben.¹⁶

### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:8:1)


**והיו לכם לחקת עולם.** כי המחנות נוסעים להלחם הלא תראה דברי משה בנסוע הארון והנה פירוש זאת החוקה שתעשו כן שיריעו הכהנים בחצוצרות בלכתם למלחמה להלחם אל ארץ אויביהם וכן כי יבא אויב בארצכם ותבואו למלחמה עמו:


AND THEY SHALL BE TO YOU FOR A STATUTE FOR EVER. The camps now journeyed to battle.¹⁷ Is this not Moses’ words¹⁸ when the ark journeyed?¹⁹ Note that Scripture explains this statute.²⁰ refers to. It says that Israel shall do the following: The *kohanim* shall sound the alarm upon trumpets when Israel goes forth to war, to battle in the land of their enemies, and similarly if an enemy enters their land and Israel goes forth to fight them.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Israel now journeyed to Canaan where they were going to engage the Canaanites.
¹⁸ Which allude to war.
¹⁹ See verses 35 and 36.
²⁰ Scripture explains what a statute for ever

**ונזכרתם.** כי עשיתם מה שצוה לכם השם הנכבד גם התרועה זכר לנפשו' לצעוק לשם:


AND YE SHALL BE REMEMBERED. For you have done what God the glorious commanded you do. The alarm (*tru’ah*) also serves as a reminder to the people to cry out to God.²¹.

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:10:1)


**וביום שמחתכם ובמועדיכם.** ששבתם מארץ אויב או נצחתם האויב הבא עליכם. וקבעתם יום שמחה כימי פורים ושבעת ימי חזקי"ה (ס"א חנוכה) שמחה. רק המעתיקים פירשו וביום שמחתכם שבת והתקיעה שישמעו ישראל וידעו כי העולות יקריבו ויכונו לבם לשם:


ALSO IN THE DAY OF YOUR GLADNESS, AND IN YOUR APPOINTED SEASONS. When you return from the land of your enemies, or you defeat the enemy who attacks you²² and you establish a day of rejoicing, such as the days of Purim or the seven days of rejoicing in the days of Hezekiah.²³ However, our transmitters of tradition explained that the day of your gladness refers to the Sabbath.²⁴ on this verse. The trumpets were sounded²⁵ so that Israel would hear that the burnt offerings were being offered and that they should direct their hearts to God.

### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:29:1)


מצאנו שצפורה היא בת רעואל כי כן כתוב ויתן את צפרה בתו ועוד להשקות צאן אביהן אל רעואל אביהן והנה חובב הוא בן רעואל והנה הוא אחי צפורה ולפי שקול הדעת הוא יתרו בעבור חנותנו במדבר ואמר על דבר יתרו כאשר בא אל המדבר אשר הוא חנה שם ואם יטעון טוען כי הכתוב אמר על יתרו חותן משה יש להשיב כי דרך המקרא לקרוא אבי הנערה ואחיה חותן והעד לחובב בן רעואל המדיני חותן משה וכבר נתברר שחובב אחי צפורה וקראו חותן משה ואם אמר אין חותן משה דבק עם חובב רק עם רעואל התשובה מבני חובב חותן משה ורבים אמרו שחובב הוא יתרו והוא אבי צפורה ורעואל היה זקנה ואמר הכתוב אל רעואל אביהן כאשר אמר יעקב אלהי אבי אברהם ואמר הכתוב בתו כאשר אמר על בלשצר שהוא בן נבוכדנצר והוא בן בנו וחכמינו אמרו שבעה שמות היו לו ליתרו ושבעים פנים לתורה:


[29. AND MOSES SAID UNTO HOBAB, THE SON OF REUEL.] We find Scripture telling us that Zipporah was the daughter of Reuel, for it is written, *and he* [Reuel] *gave Moses Zipporah his daughter* (Ex. 2:21). Furthermore, Scripture states, *to water their father’s flock*³² (Ex. 2:16), *and to Reuel their*³³ *father* (Ex. 2:18). Now Hobab was the son of Reuel.³⁴ Thus Hobab³⁵ was the brother of Zipporah.³⁶ According to a rational analysis of Scripture, Hobab is to be identified with Jethro, because the Torah states, *as thou* [Hobab] *knowest how we are to encamp in the wilderness* (v. 31) and it states with regard to Jethro when he came to the wilderness, *into the wilderness where he was encamped* (Ex. 18:5). Should one disagree³⁷ and argue that Scripture refers to Jethro as *choten mosheh* (Moses’ father-in-law) (Ex. 18:1), then one can respond that it is the way of Scripture to refer to the father and the brother of a young woman by the term *choten* (father-in-law).³⁸ means a father or brother-in-law. *Unto Hobab, the son of Reuel…choten mosheh* (Moses’ father-in-law)³⁹ is proof of the aforementioned. For it has already been made clear to you that Hobab was the brother of Zipporah, and Scripture calls him *choten mosheh* (Moses’ father-in-law). Should one argue that *choten mosheh* (Moses’ father-in-law) is not connected to Hobab but to Reuel, then the response is, Scripture explicitly states *Hobab choten mosheh* (the father-in-law of Moses) (Jud. 4:11). Many say that Hobab and Jethro are one and the same. They maintain that Jethro/Hobab was the father of Zipporah and that Reuel was the grandfather of Zipporah. They argue that Scripture says, *to Reuel their father* (Ex. 2:18)⁴⁰ in the same manner that Jacob said, *O God of my father Abraham* (Gen. 32:10).⁴¹ Scripture similarly refers to Zipporah as Reuel’s daughter in the same manner that it refers to Belshazzar as Nebuchadnezzar’s son,⁴² when in fact he was Nebuchadnezzar’s grandson. Our sages say that Jethro had seven names.⁴³ 1. There are seventy faces to the Torah.⁴⁴, a late aggadic Midrash on the alphabet composed sometime between 700-900 C.E. Some manuscripts read, there are seven faces to the Torah. According to the rabbis, Jethro, Hobab, and Reuel are one and the same person (see Rashi on Ex. 18:1 where he quotes all seven names). Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
³² The reference is to Reuel’s daughters, that is, Zipporah and her sisters.
³³ Zipporah and her sisters.
³⁴ This is stated in our verse.
³⁵ The son of Reuel.
³⁶ The daughter of Reuel.
³⁷ With the identification of Hobab with Jethro.
³⁸ Thus according to I.E. the word choten
³⁹ Our verse.
⁴⁰ The father of Zipporah and her sister.
⁴¹ Isaac, not Abraham, was Jacob’s father. Thus father is used for grandfather. Similarly, in Ex. 2:18 father refers to grandfather.
⁴² See Dan. 5:2; 18:22.
⁴³ Mekhilta,Va-yishma Yitro
⁴⁴ A quote from Otiyyot de-Rabbi Akiva

**אל ארצי.** שאדור שם היום ושם נולדתי:


TO MINE OWN LAND, AND TO MY KINDRED.⁴⁵. This literally means my birthplace. Where I was born and where I live unto this very day.⁴⁶.

### Verse 31

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:31:1)


**אל נא.** כמו עתה וכן כל נא שבמקרא והוא הפוך בלשון ישמעאל:


LEAVE US NOT, I PRAY THEE. The word *na* (I pray thee) means now. The same applies to the word *na* in all of Scripture. The opposite is the case in Arabic.⁴⁷ or *malan*. See I.E. on Gen. 12:11 (Vol. 1, p. 154).

footnotes:
⁴⁷ In Arabic the word for now is an


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:31:2)


**כי על כן ידעת.** כמו כי על כן לא נתתיה לשלה והטעם הואיל וידעת חנותינו שידעת המקום שחנינו שם:


FORASMUCH AS THOU KNOWEST. *Ki al ken* (forasmuch) here means the same as *ki al ken* in *forasmuch as I gave her not to Shelah my son* (Gen. 38:26). Since (*ki al ken*)⁴⁸ is since. you know the place where we encamped, come with us.

⁴⁸ Thus the meaning of ki al ken

**לכה אתנו והיית לנו לעינים.** להראות הדרך ורבים אומרים כי והיית לשון עבר רמז על העצה שנתן למשה שהראה לו מה שלא ידע וזה טעם לעינים:


AND THOU SHALT BE TO US INSTEAD OF EYES. You will show us the way.⁴⁹ is a metaphor for a guide (Weiser). Many say that *ve-hayita* (and thou shalt be) is to be taken as a perfect⁵⁰ and alludes to the advice which Jethro gave Moses.⁵¹ He showed Moses what Moses did not know. They say this is what eyes refers to.

### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:33:1)


לפי דעתי שהמסע הראשון לבדו לא היה כשאר המסעות והפסוק שאמר ונסעו הקהתים אחר נסוע שני הדגלים בכל המסעים חוץ מזה כי הארון היה נוסע לפניהם וזה היה שלשת ימים בלבד וזה טעם דרך שלשת ימים וכמוהו וילכו שלשת ימים במדבר איתם אין פירושו שהלכו ביום אחד מהלך שלשה ימים ויש אומרים שהלכו מהלך שלשה ימים ביום אחד ויש אומרים שהלכו מהלך שלשה ימים כנגד המסעים שנסעו בצאתם ממצרים והארון היה רחוק מהם דרך שלשה ימים וזה טעם וישכן הענן במדבר פארן ויפרשו ויסעו בראשונה כי על פי משה נסעו כי הענן נסע מיד וזה לא יתכן כי משה לא יסור מהארון והעד ויהי בנסוע הארון ויאמר משה ובנחה יאמר והנכון בעיני אשר אמרתי. וטעם וישכן הענן במדבר פארן שם כלל לתבערה שהוא קברות התאוה ולחצרות ורתמה ומסעים רבים והעד שלא הזכיר הכתוב בפרשת אלה מסעי במדבר פארן ועוד אפרשנו:


[33. AND THEY SET FORWARD FROM THE MOUNT OF THE LORD THREE DAYS’ JOURNEY.] In my opinion the first journey was unlike the rest of the journeys. The verse which states, *and the Kohathites…set forward* (v. 21) after the two standards journeyed refers to all the other journeys except this one, for⁵⁴ the ark journeyed before the camp of the Israelites.⁵⁵ However, this was the case for only three days. This is the meaning of *three days* (v. 33). Similarly, *And they went three days’ journey in the wilderness of Etham* (Num. 33:8) does not mean that they traveled a three days journey in one day.⁵⁶ Others say⁵⁷ that they traveled a three-day journey in one day.⁵⁸ Others say that they traveled a journey of three days corresponding to the journeys when they left Egypt,⁵⁹ and the ark was a three-day distance from them.⁶⁰. This is the meaning of *and the cloud abode in the wilderness of Paran* (v. 12).⁶¹ They explain that *And they took their first journey* (v. 13), means that they journeyed by the command of Moses, for the cloud traveled immediately.⁶² However, this is not so, for Moses did not distance himself from the ark, as proved by *And it came to pass, when the ark set forward* (v. 35) *And when it rested* (v. 36). What I said above appears correct to me.⁶³ *Paran*, in *and the cloud abode in the wilderness of Paran* (v. 12), is a general name for Taberah,⁶⁴ which is the same as Kibroth-hattaavah (v. 34), Hazeroth (Num. 33:17), Rithmah (Num. 33:18), and many other journeys.⁶⁵ The fact that Scripture does not mention the wilderness of Paran in the Torah portion *Elleh Mase* (these are the stages)⁶⁶ is proof of this. I will explain this later.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁵⁴ During the first journey.
⁵⁵ And in all of the other journeys, it journeyed amidst the camp of the Israelites. See Num. 2:17.
⁵⁶ It means they traveled for three days.
⁵⁷ That the meaning of our verse is.
⁵⁸ This is the opinion of Rashi.
⁵⁹ When Israel crossed the Sea of Reeds, they journeyed for three days (Ex. 15:22). They similarly journeyed for three days when they left the wilderness of Sinai.
⁶⁰ This is how others interpret, And the ark of the covenant…went before them three days’ journey to seek out a resting-place for them
⁶¹ According to this interpretation Paran is three days’ journey from Sinai. The ark traveled three days’ distance from Israel and rested in the wilderness of Paran. When the ark rested, Israel began its journey.
⁶² With the ark. In other words, the cloud which led Israel was with the ark, not with the camp of Israel.
⁶³ That the first journey was different from all other journeys (Weiser). Or, that the ark did not travel a three days’ journey from the camp but that it journeyed in front of the camp (Krinsky).
⁶⁴ Num. 11:3.
⁶⁵ In other words, is the name of the district where Israel encamped in the wilderness.
⁶⁶ Which lists the journeys of the children of Israel. See Num. 33:1-49.
⁶⁷ The meaning of verse 12. See I.E. on Num. 12:16.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:35:1
