###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:2)


וטעם **ויפוצו.** כאשר יראו שקמת לעשות מלחמה מיד יפוצו אויביך:


[BE SCATTERED.] When Your enemies see that You arose to do battle, they will immediately be scattered.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:35:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:3)


מלת **משנאיך.** מהבנין הכבד הנוסף והנו"ן קל להקל על הלשון:


[THEM THAT HATE THEE.] The word *mesanekha* (them that hate Thee) is a *pi’el*.⁶⁹. *Pi’el* is referred to as the heavy conjugation. There is apparently an error in our texts and manuscripts. The *nun*⁷⁰. does not receive a *dagesh*⁷¹ receives a *dagesh*. Hence I.E.’s comment. in order to simplify the enunciation of the word.

footnotes:
⁶⁹ The term used by I.E. is the heavy added conjugation. I.E. usually uses this phrase to describe a hifil
⁷⁰ Of mesanekha
⁷¹ The middle root letter of words in the pi’el


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:35:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:35:4)


**ויפוצו וינוסו.** כדרך כל הנבואות לכפול במלות משונות כאשר אפרש בפרשת בלעם:


SCATTERED…FLEE. Scripture repeats itself as is its style in all prophecies,⁷² as I will explain in the portion dealing with Balaam.⁷³

footnotes:
⁷² See I.E. on Gen. 49:3 (Vol. 1, p. 149) and the notes thereto.
⁷³ See I.E. on Num. 23:7.



### Verse 36

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:36:1)


**ובנחה.** בנוח הארון ונכתב בה"א כמו ה"א אהלה כי אותיות אהו"י מתחלפים:


AND WHEN IT RESTED. When the ark rested. *U-venucho* (when it rested) is spelled with a *heh*⁷⁴. The pronominal suffix *o* is usually spelled with a *vav*. Hence I.E.’s comment. in the same way that the word *oholo* (his tent) (Gen. 13:3) is,⁷⁵ ends in a *heh* and not, as expected, in a *vav*. for the letters *alef, heh, vav, yod* interchange.⁷⁶ was substituted for a *vav* in *oholo* and *u-venucho*.

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Rather than a vav
⁷⁵ Oholo
⁷⁶ In other words, a heh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 10:36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/10:36:2)


**שובה ה'.** אמר רבי יהודה המדקדק הראשון כי כל שב שטעמו מגזרת תשובה הוא פועל עומד ולא יוצא בבנין הקל רק אם היה טעמו בשלוה והשקט כמו בשובה ונחת תושעון כן שובה ה' רבבות אלפי ישראל שיניח' ולא ירגזו' מאויב. ודע כי רבבה מגזרת רב והטעם כמו רבבה כצמח השדה נתתיך ויתכן שנקרא עשרת אלפים כן בעבור היותו מספר רב על כן נסמכה מלת רבבות לאלפי ומשה התפלל שיניח וישקיט השם כל ישראל ואם הם רבים:


RETURN, O LORD. Rabbi Judah, the first grammarian, says that every time the word *shav* in the *kal* conjugation is related in meaning to *teshuvah* (return), it is intransitive, not transitive.⁷⁷ has the meaning of return, it is intransitive. However, when it means peace and quiet, as in *in sitting still* (be-shuvah) *and rest shall ye be saved* (Is. 30:15), and similarly in *Return* (shuvah), *O Lord, unto the ten thousands of the families of Israel*,⁷⁸ it means that God will give them rest and they will not be disturbed by any enemy.⁷⁹), O Lord, unto the ten thousands of the families of Israel. Note, the word *revavah* (ten thousand) comes from the same root as the word *rav* (many). *Revavah* in our verse is similar to the word *revavah* (increase) in *I cause thee to increase* (Ezek. 16:7). It is possible that ten thousand is called a *revavah* because it is a great number. The word *revavah*⁸⁰. is therefore connected to the word *alfe* (thousands).⁸¹). Moses prayed that God would grant rest and quiet to all of Israel, though they be many.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ In other words, whenever the word shav
⁷⁸ Then it is transitive.
⁷⁹ According to I.E. our clause should be rendered, give rest (shuvah
⁸⁰ In its plural form revevot
⁸¹ Thus I.E. renders our clause, Give rest, O Lord, unto the many thousands of Israel (revevot alfe yisra’el



## Chapter 11


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:1)


**ויהי העם.** נסמכה זו הפרשה כי אחר שנסע הארון מהר סיני חנו בקברות התאוה והנה ספר מה שאירע שם:


AND THE PEOPLE. This chapter is connected to the one before,¹ because the Israelites camped in *kibroth-hatta’avah*², the name of the place as quoted in verse 3. See I.E. on Num. 10:31. after the ark journeyed from Sinai. Scripture now tells what happened there.³.

footnotes:
¹ Which deals with the journey of the ark.
² Which, according to I.E., is the same as taverah
³ At kibroth-hatta’avah


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:2)


**כמתאוננים.** מגזרת און וכן מחשבות אונך:


AS MURMURERS. *Mitonenim* (murmurers) is related to the word *aven* (evil).⁴. *Onekh* (evil) in *thy evil* (onekh) *thoughts* (Jer. 4:14) is similar.⁵ (and the people were as murmurers) as: and the people uttered evil words.

footnotes:
⁴ Both words come from the root alef, vav, nun
⁵ I.E. renders va-yehi ha-am ke-mitoninim


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:3)


וטעם **וישמע ה'.** שדברו דברי און:


[AND WHEN THE LORD HEARD.] The meaning of *when the Lord heard* is, and when the Lord heard that they spoke evil.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:1:4)


**ותאכל.** מלעיל כמו ויאמר ובמוכרח מלרע הפך הלשון רק כן מצאנו:


AND DEVOURED. *Va-tokhal* (and devoured) is penultimately accented. Compare, *va-yomer* (and he said) (Gen. 14:19).⁶ However, when it ends a clause,⁷) or at the end of a verse (*sof pasuk*). then it is ultimately accented. The latter is contrary to the laws of Hebrew grammar,⁸ but we find it to be so.

footnotes:
⁶ When this word comes at the beginning of a clause, it is penultimately accented.
⁷ When it comes in the middle of a verse (at an etnachta
⁸ For the change of accent indicates a change in tense (Weiser).



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:2:1)


**ויצעק העם אל משה.** בפיוס:


AND THE PEOPLE CRIED UNTO MOSES. In a conciliatory manner.⁹ (cried) literally means screamed. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁹ The word va-yitzaku


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:2:2)


**ותשקע האש.** כטעם ותכבה ואין לו אחר:


AND THE FIRE ABATED. *Va-tishka* (abated) means extinguished. The word is not used again in Scripture¹⁰ means to sink. This root is used in the sense of extinguished only here. with this meaning.

footnotes:
¹⁰ The root shaka



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:3:1)


**תבערה.** עוד אפרשנו:


TABERAH. I will explain this term later.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ See I.E. on Deut. 9:22.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:1)


**והאספסף.** שנאספו אל ישראל ואינם מהם והם ערב רב והמלה כפולה כמו סחרחר חמרמרו:


AND THE MIXED MULTITUDE. Who were gathered unto the children of Israel but were not of Israelite stock. The *asafsuf* (the mixed multitude) refers to the *erev rav* (the mixed multitude).¹² The word is constructed by doubling two root letters.¹³. Compare, *secharchar*¹⁴, doubling the *chet* and *resh*. (fluttereth) (Ps. 38:11) and *chamarmaru*¹⁵, doubling the *mem* and *resh*. (burn) (Lam. 1:20).

footnotes:
¹² Mentioned in Ex. 12:38.
¹³ Its two final root letters, samekh, feh
¹⁴ From the root samekh, chet, resh
¹⁵ From the root chet, mem, resh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:2)


**וישובו ויבכו.** אחר התאוה:


AND…WEPT. After they lusted.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:4:3)


**גם בני ישראל.** עמם:


THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL…ON THEIR PART. With them.¹⁶ (and the children of Israel also wept on their part). I.E. interprets *va-yashuvu va- yivku* as referring to the mixed multitude. He reads our verse as follows: And the mixed multitude that was among them fell a-lusting, and after lusting turned and wept, also the children of Israel with them. I.E.’s point is that the mixed multitude caused Israel to weep and ask for meat (Krinsky).

footnotes:
¹⁶ The Hebrew reads, va-yashuvu va-yivku gam bene yisra’el



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:1)


**הדגה.** שם המין:


THE FISH. The word *dagah* (fish) is a general term.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ For all the different species of fish.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:2)


**חנם.** בזול כאילו היא חנם:


FOR NOUGHT. Cheaply, as if we got it for nothing.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:3)


**הקשואים.** באל"ף תחת ה"א מקשה כמו ועמי תלואים ורבים כן:


THE CUCUMBERS. An *alef* has been substituted for a *heh* in the word *kishu’im* (cucumbers), for *kishu’im* is related to the word *mikshah* (cucumber) (Is. 1:8).¹⁸ is spelled with a *heh*. Compare, *telu’im* (suspense) in *And My people are in suspense about returning to Me* (Hos. 11:7).¹⁹ is spelled with an *alef*. However, it comes from the root *tav, lamed, heh*, and should read *teluyim*. Thus here too an *alef* has been substituted for a *heh*. There are many such instances.

footnotes:
¹⁸ Mikshah
¹⁹ Telu’im


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:4)


**אבטיחים.** ידוע מלשון קדר ואין לו ריע במקרא:


MELONS. We can ascertain the meaning of *avatichim* (melons) from Arabic.²⁰. It has no cognate in Scripture.²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ The Arabic word for melon is batikha
²¹ Literally, no neighbor in Scripture.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:5)


**חציר.** הירקות שהם עשב השדה וכן המצמיח הרים חציר והנה הוא שם כלל והמתרגם ארמי אמר שהוא שם פרט גם הוא נכון:


AND THE LEEKS. The word *chatzir* (leeks) refers to vegetables,²² (literally, greens). which are the grass of the field.²³ means grass. However, people do not eat grass. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *Who maketh the mountains to spring with grass* (chatzir) (Ps. 147:8). *Chatzir* is thus an all-inclusive noun.²⁴ However, the Aramaic translation²⁵ says that it refers to a specific kind.²⁶ by the term *kerate* (leeks). It too is correct.

footnotes:
²² Hebrew, yerakot
²³ Chatzir
²⁴ It refers to all types of greens.
²⁵ Onkelos.
²⁶ Onkelos renders chatzir


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:5:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:5:6)


**הבצלים והשומים.** ידועים והם קרובים בלשון קדר:


AND THE ONIONS, AND THE GARLIC. The meaning *be-tzalim* (onions) and *shumim* (garlic) is known.²⁷ They have similar names in Arabic.²⁸ and *tumeh*.

footnotes:
²⁷ I.E. could find no synonyms for these words. Hence his comment that everybody knows what they mean.
²⁸ Basal



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:1)


**נפשנו יבשה.** היא הנפש המתאוה הנטועה בכבד:


BUT NOW OUR SOUL IS DRIED AWAY. The reference is to the lusting soul, which is implanted in the liver.²⁹, page 96. According to I.E. there are three souls in the body, *neshamah, ru’ach*, and *nefesh*. The *neshamah* (rational soul) is manifested in the brain; the *ru’ach* (spirit) is located in the heart; and the *nefesh* (the lusting soul), the part of the psyche that desires food and sex, is in the liver.

footnotes:
²⁹ See The Secret of the Torah


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:2)


**אין כל.** חסרנו כל דבר:


THERE IS NOTHING AT ALL. We lack everything.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:6:3)


**עינינו.** כי מן השמים ירד:


TO LOOK TO. For the manna came down from heaven.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ We watch the manna come down from the heavens (Weiser).



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:1)


**והמן.** ספר הכתוב חסרון דעת המתאוין כי המן היה כזרע גד והוא קל ללקוט והיה נראה כי הוא לבן ועוד כי יוכלו לאכלו כאשר היה גם לטחנו ברחים ולעשות עוגות או לדכותו ולבשלו בפרור ויש לו טעם נכבד כמו לשד השמן ועוד היה יורד על מקום נקי אחר שירחץ הטל את המקום:


NOW THE MANNA. Scripture³¹ describes the stupidity of those who lusted, for the manna was like coriander seed, which is easy to pick, and it was easy to see, for it was white. Furthermore, they could eat it as it was. They could also grind it in mills and make cakes out of it. They could also beat it in mortars and cook it in pots. It had an especially good taste, for it tasted like [food] with the finest oil. Additionally, it came down after the dew had washed the place [upon which it fell]. It thus descended upon a clean place.

footnotes:
³¹ In this verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:2)


**כזרע גד.** סמוך כמו נטע שעשועים:


LIKE CORIANDER SEED. *Zera* (seed) is in the construct.³² rather than with a *segol* beneath the *zayin*. It is like the word *neta* (plant of)³³ rather than with a *segol* beneath the *nun*. in *the plant of His delight* (Is. 5:7).

footnotes:
³² Hence it is vocalized with a sheva
³³ Which is vocalized with a sheva


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:7:3)


**גד** י"א כסברתא וי"א חרדל מגזרת גדין ואנכי לא ידעתי:


CORIANDER. Some identify this with *kusbarta*. Others say that it refers to mustard. They connect *gad* (coriander) with the word *gidin* (sinews).³⁴ However, I do not know its meaning.

footnotes:
³⁴ Something hard (bitter) to the taste, i.e., mustard seed. See I.E.’s short commentary on Ex. 16:31.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:1)


**שטו העם.** מגזרת משוט בארץ וזה יורה כי לא היה נופל זה עם זה רק מפוזר ומפורד:


THE PEOPLE WENT ABOUT. The word *shatu* (went about) is related to the word *shut*³⁵. (going to and fro) in *From going to and fro* (mi-shut) *in the earth* (Job 1:7). This shows that the individual pieces of manna did not fall in one place³⁶ but were spread and scattered.

footnotes:
³⁵ From the root shin, vav, tet
³⁶ Literally, together. It did not fall in one large pile but evenly over some distance.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:2)


**ברחים.** של היד ולא יתכן להפריד זה השם כי משנים יהיו הרחים:


IN MILLS. Operated by hand. The word *rechayim* (mills) does not come in the singular,³⁷ is in the plural. However, it also refers to the singular, that is, to a mill as in our verse, for *rechayim* (mills) is parallel to *medochah* (mortar), which is singular. for a mill consists of two parts.³⁸

footnotes:
³⁷ The word rechayim
³⁸ An upper and a lower stone.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:3)


**במדוכה.** עץ או אבן שידוכו בה גם יתכן שיעשו עוגות מאשר ידוכו:


IN MORTARS. A vessel made out of wood or stone. It is called a *medocha* (mortars)³⁹ because they pound in it.⁴⁰ comes from the root *dalet, caf, heh*, which means to crush. It was also possible to make cakes from what they pounded.⁴¹. I.E. notes that this should be interpreted, and seethed it in pots, *or* made cakes of it.

footnotes:
³⁹ Literally, a mortar. See previous note.
⁴⁰ The word medocha
⁴¹ In addition to cooking it. Scripture reads, and seethed it in pots, and made cakes of it


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:4)


**עוגות.** כמו ועשי עוגות:


AND MADE CAKES. Compare, *and make cakes* (Gen. 18:6).⁴²

footnotes:
⁴² We thus see that the word make can mean bake.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:8:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:8:5)


**לשד.** י"א כי הלמ"ד נוסף כלמ"ד שלאנן ושלו והיה ראוי להיות השמן מלרע בעבור שהוא תאר השם והנכון בעיני שהלמ"ד לשד שורש כמו נהפך לשדי והוא הפך בחרבוני קיץ והיא הלחה הנכבדת העולה למעלה מכל שמן ויהיה השמן מלעיל כמשמעו:


A CAKE. Some say that the *lamed* of *leshad* (cake) is superfluous.⁴³ (breast). According to this interpretation, *leshad ha-shamen* means breast of the oil. See *Sifre* on Num. 11:8. “Just as the suckling imbibes, so to speak, every possible flavor with the mother-milk, so the Israelites found all flavors in the manna” (translated by Rosenbaum Silbermann, Num. p. 54). So too Rashi, “Our Rabbis explained it as meaning breasts.” It is similar to the *lamed* of *shalanan* (at ease)⁴⁴. in *at ease and quiet* (Job 21:23). The word *ha-shamen* (with oil) should have been ultimately accented⁴⁵ is ultimately accented. When the word *shemen* or *shamen* is a noun, it is penultimately accented; when an adjective, it is ultimately accented. because⁴⁶ the word *shamen* (oil) is an adjective.⁴⁷. However, I believe that the *lamed* of *leshad* is a root letter. The word *leshad* is similar to the word *leshadi* (my sap) in *My sap was turned* (Ps. 32:4). *My* (leshadi) *sap* stands in contrast to *the droughts* (charvone) in *the droughts of summer* (*Ibid*.)*. Leshad* refers to the finest part of the oil that floats on top of all oil. In this case, the word *ha-shamen* is penultimately vocalized in accordance with the meaning of the word.⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴³ The basic word is shad
⁴⁴ The basic word being sha’anan
⁴⁵ However, ha-shamen
⁴⁶ According to this interpretation.
⁴⁷ Describing leshad
⁴⁸ For the word is a noun.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:1)


**בוכה למשפחותיו.** שהתחברו המשפחות לבכות כאשר יעשו בבכותם על מת:


WEEPING, FAMILY BY FAMILY. The families gathered together to cry, as they do when they mourn for the dead.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:2)


**איש לפתח אהלו.** בפרהסיא:


EVERY MAN AT THE DOOR OF HIS TENT. In public.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:10:3)


**ובעיני משה רע.** פעל עבר או תחסר מלת היה:


AND MOSES WAS DISPLEASED. The word *ra* (displeased) is a perfect.⁴⁹ On the other hand, the word *hayah* (was) might be missing in our text.⁵⁰. The latter literally means: and in the eyes of Moses displeased. I.E. suggests that our verse be interpreted as if it read, *u-ve’ene moshe hayah ra* (and it was displeasing in the eyes of Moses). In this case, our verse is a continuous present.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ And also a present form. Here it is a past.
⁵⁰ Our verse reads, u-ve’ene moshe ra



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:1)


**למה הרעות לעבדך.** הכתוב דבר על הבא במהרה:


WHEREFORE HAST THOU DEALT ILL WITH THY SERVANT. Scripture describes an impetuous action.⁵¹

footnotes:
⁵¹ Moses spoke impulsively, as one is wont to do under pressure.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:2)


**ולמה לא מצאתי חן בעיניך.** כאשר אמרתי שלח נא ביד תשלח:


AND WHEREFORE HAVE I NOT FOUND FAVOUR IN THY SIGHT. When I said, *send, I pray Thee, by the hand of him whom Thou wilt send* (Ex. 4:13).⁵²

footnotes:
⁵² You chose me then. Why, then, do I not find favor in your eyes now?


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:11:3)


ומלת **מצתי** חסרת אל"ף ורבים כמוה כי אין תימה לחסרון המלך יהו"א:


The word *matzati*⁵³. (found) is missing an *alef*.⁵⁴. There are many such instances. There is nothing to wonder about when king *Yehu*⁵⁵, or *alef* is dropped. These letters spell the name Yehu, who was a king of Judah. I.E. employs the king’s name as a mnemonic device. is missing.

### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:1)


**הריתי.** האני אמם:


HAVE I CONCEIVED. Am I their mother?


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:2)


. **ילדתיהו.** או אני אביהם כמו ילד את שלח:


HAVE I BROUGHT THEM FORTH. Or am I their father? *Yelidtihu* (brought them forth) is like the word *yalad* (begot) in *and Shelah begot Eber* (Gen. 10:24).⁵⁶

footnotes:
⁵⁶ I.E. renders our clause: Did I beget them?


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:12:3)


**שאהו.** עד בואו אל האדמה:


CARRY THEM. Until they come to the land.⁵⁷. This literally means, carry them…upon the land. Hence I.E. points out that the word *al* here means unto, and *al ha-adamah* is to be rendered as *unto the land*.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ Scripture reads, sa’eh…al ha-adamah



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:13:1)


**מאין.** במ"ם שם מקום ובלא מ"ם כמו לא ונמצא מלת הן אתם מאין:


WHENCE.⁵⁸. When the word *ayin* has a *mem* prefixed to it,⁵⁹ it means from which place. When *ayin* appears without a *mem* prefixed to it, it means no.⁶⁰ We also find the following clause, *Behold, ye are nothing* (me-ayin) (Is. 41:24).⁶¹ has the meaning of nothing even though it has a *mem* prefixed to it.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ Hebrew, me-ayin
⁵⁹ As in our verse.
⁶⁰ Or nothing.
⁶¹ In this case, me-ayin



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:14:1)


**כי כבד.** פעל עבר כמו כי זקן יצחק או יחסר הוא:


BECAUSE IT IS TOO HEAVY FOR ME. The word *kaved* (heavy) is a verb in the perfect.⁶² Compare, *zaken* (was old) in *And it came to pass, that when Isaac was old* (Gen. 27:1).⁶³ can be a perfect or a present form. In Gen. 27:1, it is a perfect. On the other hand, the word *hu* (it) might be missing from our text.⁶⁴. In this case, *khaved* is a present.

footnotes:
⁶² And also a present. In our case, it is a past.
⁶³ The word zaken
⁶⁴ In other words, our text should be read as if written ki khaved hu mi-menni



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:1)


**את.** לשון זכר ונקבה כמו לך גם בך ועמך ואתך ואתה לשון זכר לבדו:


THOU. The word *at* (Thou) is used for both males and females.⁶⁵ (thou) is usually used when addressing a female. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is like *lakh* (to you), *bakh* (in you), *immakh* (with you), and *ittakh* (with you).⁶⁶) and end of a Biblical verse. However, *attah* (you) is only used for males.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ The word at
⁶⁶ These words are used for both masculine and feminine in the middle (at an etnachta


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:2)


**הרגני.** כמו המיתני זה עשה לי בצדקתך ובעבור פיוסי וזה טעם ואם מצאתי חן בעיניך:


KILL ME. *Horgeni* (kill me) means put me to death. Do this to me because of Your righteousness and my request. This is the meaning of *if I have found favour in Thy sight*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:15:3)


**ואל אראה.** עוד ברעה שאני בה ואין צורך לתקון הסופרים:


AND LET ME NOT LOOK. Anymore upon the evil circumstances in which I find myself. There is no need for any scribal emendation.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁶⁷ See Rashi: “It ought to have written and let me not see their evil, only that Scripture modified the expression [i.e., it used a euphemistic expression referring the evil to himself instead of to the whole people]. This is one of the variations occurring in the Torah such as writers make for the purpose of modifying and improving the phrase” (Rosenbaum and Silbermann translation). See Vol. 1, p. 18, and p. 195, note 33.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:16:1)


**אספה לי.** מלה זרה ויתכן שהיתה כן בעבור אות הגרון וכן ערכה לפני התיצבה:


GATHER UNTO ME. The word *esfah* (gather) is irregular.⁶⁸ of *esfah* is vocalized with a *segol*. However, it should have been vocalized with a short *kamatz*. Compare, *zokhrah* (Neh. 5:19). It is possible that this is so because of the guttural.⁶⁹ of *esfah*. *Erkhah* (set in order) in *Set thy words in order before me, stand forth* ((Job 33:5) is similar.⁷⁰ in *erkhah* is vocalized with a *segol* rather than with a *kamatz katan*.

**ושוטריו.** כי יש זקנים שבאו בימים ואינם ראוים להיות שוטרים גם יש שוטרים ואינם זקנים:


AND OFFICERS. For there are elders who are advanced in age but are unfit to be officers. There are also officers who are not elders.⁷¹ is the elders of the people who also serve as officers.

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:1)


**וירדתי.** הכבוד ירד באש בתוך הענן:


AND I WILL COME DOWN. God’s glory descended in fire in the midst of the cloud.⁷²

footnotes:
⁷² In other words, it was God’s glory that descended, not God himself.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:2)


**ודברתי אתך שם.** וכאשר תשמע את הקול ישמעו גם הם:


AND SPEAK WITH THEE THERE. When you hear the voice they will also hear.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:17:3)


**ואצלתי.** כטעם אקח מאשר אצלך ודע כי הרוח כמו החכמה ואם תנתן מחכמת ראובן לשמעון לא תחסר חכמת ראובן רק נשארה כאשר היא והמשל בנר:


AND I WILL TAKE OF THE SPIRIT. The meaning of *ve-atzalti* (and I will take of the spirit) is, I will take from that which you possess.⁷³ means near, or in one’s possession. Note that spirit⁷⁴ means wisdom. If some of the wisdom of Reuben is given to Simeon, Reuben’s wisdom is not diminished. It remains as is.⁷⁵ Let the light of a candle serve as an example.⁷⁶

footnotes:
⁷³ Etzel
⁷⁴ Here.
⁷⁵ Thus Moses’ wisdom was not diminished by having some of it imparted to Joshua.
⁷⁶ A candle’s light is not diminished if it is used to light another candle.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:18:1)


**התקדשו.** נמצא לשבח ולגנאי לשון התקדש וכן היקדש שהוא לשון טומאה:


SANCTIFY YOURSELVES.⁷⁷. Words from the root of *kodesh* (to sanctify) are found in the positive and the negative.⁷⁸ (sanctify yourself) in our verse has a negative connotation. See Rashi, who explains the word to mean “prepare yourselves to receive punishment” (Rosenbaum and Silbermann translation). *Ha-yikdash* (Haggai 2:12), which means *shall it be unclean*, is similar.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ Hebrew, hitkaddeshu
⁷⁸ According to I.E. the word hitkaddeshu



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:1)


**ולא יומים.** כפל:


NOR TWO DAYS. Twice as much.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁹ Scripture speaks of one day, two days, five days, ten days, and twenty days. I.E. now goes on to explain the reason for these numbers.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:2)


**חמשה ימים.** כנגד אצבעות היד האוכלים:


NOR FIVE DAYS. Corresponding to the fingers of the hand of those who eat.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:19:3)


**עשרה.** כפל בשתי ידיו גם עשרים כמשל האומרים יאכל כל מה שיאכל באצבעות ידיו ורגליו:


NEITHER TEN DAYS. Twice the amount, corresponding to the fingers of the two hands of those who eat.⁸⁰ *Nor twenty days* has a similar meaning.⁸¹ This may be compared to the saying: Let him eat with the fingers of his hands and with the toes of his feet.⁸²

footnotes:
⁸⁰ Of those who demanded meat to eat.
⁸¹ The number 20 corresponds to the hands and toes of those who demanded meat.
⁸² Let him gorge himself.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:1)


**חדש ימים.** פירשתיו שישוב ערך הלבנה באורה כאשר היתה וכן שנתים ימים:


BUT A WHOLE MONTH. I have already explained the meaning of the word *chodesh yamim* (a whole month).⁸³ The phrase *chodesh yamim* alludes to the time that it takes for the moon’s light to return to its original amount.⁸⁴ *Shenatayim yamim* (two full years) (Gen. 41:1) is similar to *chodesh yamim*.⁸⁵

footnotes:
⁸³ See I.E. on Gen. 4:4 and Ex. 12:2.
⁸⁴ It refers to the time that it takes for the moon to appear the same way that it did when it started its cycle in the sky. In other words, it refers to a complete month.
⁸⁵ It means two complete years.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:2)


וטעם **מאפכם.** כי הריח באף ויצא הריח ממנ' ולא ימצא:


[AT YOUR NOSTRILS.]⁸⁶ (at your nostrils) literally means from your nostrils. Hence I.E.’s comments. The sense of smell is located in the nose. The sense of smell will depart from your nostrils, and will not be found there.⁸⁷

footnotes:
⁸⁶ Me-appekhem
⁸⁷ In other words, you will lose your sense of smell and will no longer enjoy the taste of meat.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:20:3)


**לזרא.** באל"ף תחת ה"א כמו קראן לי מרא כי אותיות אהו"י מתחלפין וטעם **לזרא.** הוא דבר זר נתעב לתולדת שלא הרגיל אדם בו:


AND IT BE LOATHSOME. The word *le-zara* is spelled with an *alef*⁸⁸ in place of the *heh*. Compare, *mara* in *call me Mara*⁸⁹ in place of a *heh* at the end of the word. (Ruth 1:20), for the letters *alef, heh, vav, yod* interchange. The word *zara* (loathsome) refers to something which is strange⁹⁰. and abhorrent to the nature of a person who is not used to it.

footnotes:
⁸⁸ At the end of the word.
⁸⁹ Which has an alef
⁹⁰ The Hebrew word for strange is zar



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:21:1)


**שש מאות אלף רגלי.** ואין צורך להזכיר הפחותים. ורבים פירשו זאת הפרשה לטעמים רבים יש אומרים כי משה אמר מה שיאמרו לו ישראל בעת שישאלו מה הוא הבשר והתשובה שיאמר להם אין לכם צורך לשאול **היד ה' תקצר.** ולא אדע למה הצרה הזאת רק הדבר כמשמעו כי משה לא ידע כי השם יחדש אות או מופת כי אם להצדיק נביאו כאשר אפרש:


SIX HUNDRED THOUSAND MEN ON FOOT. There is no need to mention the lesser ones.⁹¹ Many interpret this section in various ways.⁹² Some say that Moses paraphrased what the children of Israel would say to him. When they would ask, what sort of meat,⁹³ Moses would answer them, you have no need to ask,⁹⁴ for *is the Lord’s hand waxed short*?⁹⁵ has been detached from the verse and opens a new comment on verse 23. However, this is an error. See Meijler.I do not know why we must suffer in this manner.⁹⁶ The text must be interpreted according to its plain meaning. Moses did not know that God would create a sign or a miracle.⁹⁷ He thought, as I will explain, that he would do so only to justify his prophet.⁹⁸

footnotes:
⁹¹ Those under the age of twenty. If these were included then the number would be much higher. Or the reference is to the 3,550 Israelites above the number of 600,000, for the Israelites numbered 600,350 (Num. 2:32).
⁹² The commentaries were bothered by Moses apparently doubting the ability of God to provide meat for all of the Israelites.
⁹³ In other words, verses 21 and 22 are Moses’ paraphrase of what the people would tell him when he told them that God would provide meat for them.
⁹⁴ See verse 23.
⁹⁵ In some of the printed editions as well as in the Vatican manuscript, is the Lord’s hand waxed short
⁹⁶ Of seeing the meaning of verses 21 and 22 perverted.
⁹⁷ To satisfy the people’s craving for meat.
⁹⁸ When the prophet is challenged, as in the case of the rebellion of Korach. See Num. 16.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:22:1)


**ומצא להם.** כדברי המתרגם ארמית ומה נכבד דרש קדמונינו במלת ישחט על הבהמה ויאסף על הדג:


WILL THEY SUFFICE THEM. *U-matza la-hem* (will they suffice them) is to be interpreted as in the Aramaic translation.⁹⁹ as, will they suffice them. The literal meaning of *u-matza la-hem* is: and he will find them. Hence I.E.’s comment. How precious is the interpretation of our ancients to the effect that the word *yishachet* (be slain) applies to cattle, and *ya’asef* (be gathered) refers to fish.¹⁰⁰) but fish do not. See *Hullin* 27b.

footnotes:
⁹⁹ Onkelos renders u-matza la-hem
¹⁰⁰ Scripture speaks of slaughtering sheep and gathering fish. According to the rabbis this verse teaches us that cattle require ritual slaughter (shechitah

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:1)

AND TOOK OF THE SPIRIT. In my opinion, the word *va-yatzel* (and took of the spirit) is a *hifil*.¹⁰³).

¹⁰³ Thus the meaning of our clause is: He caused some of the spirit that was upon him to move (va-yatzel


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:2)


וי"ו **ויתנבאו** כפ"א רפה בלשון ישמעאל:


[AND THEY PROPHESIED.] The *vav* of *va-yitnabbe’u* (and they prophesied) is like an unaspirated *fa* in Arabic.¹⁰⁴ usually introduces a new clause. Here it does not. Hence I.E. points out that sometimes the conversive *vav* merely serves as a conjunction, like the *fa* in Arabic.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁴ A verb that begins with a conversive vav


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:25:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:25:3)


**ולא יספו.** פעם שנית וכן קול גדול ולא יסף ולא יסף עוד לדעתה ואין מדקדוק הלשון להיותו מגזרת פן תספו בכל חטאתם אמרו חכמים ז"ל שלקח משה מכל שבט ששה והנה שנים ושבעים ובעבור שאמר לו השם שבעים איש הניח השנים וזה נכון:


BUT THY DID SO NO MORE.¹⁰⁵. A second time.¹⁰⁶ is, they did not add. Hence I.E. points out that this term is an idiom meaning they did not repeat it. The same applies to the phrase *ve-lo yasaf* (and it went on no more) in *with a great voice, and it went on no more*¹⁰⁷ (Deut. 5:19) and *ve-lo yasaf* (now more) in *and he knew her again no more*¹⁰⁸ (Gen. 38:26). According to the rules of Hebrew grammar, the word *yasaf* is not related to the word *tissafu* (swept away)¹⁰⁹ comes from the root *yod, samekh, peh*, while the root of *tissafu* comes from the root *samekh, peh, heh*. If our word came from the latter root, it would have read *safu* not *yasefu*. in *lest ye be swept away in all their sins* (Num. 16:26).¹¹⁰ Our sages of blessed memory say that Moses took six men from each tribe. Their number thus came to seventy-two. However, Moses dropped the two¹¹¹ because God told him to gather seventy men.¹¹². 17a. This is correct.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ Hebrew, ve-lo yasaf
¹⁰⁶ The literal meaning of lo yasefu
¹⁰⁷ It did not repeat itself.
¹⁰⁸ Its meaning is, he knew her not a second time.
¹⁰⁹ For the word yasefu
¹¹⁰ If it were, then the meaning of our phrase would be: but they were not destroyed.
¹¹¹ Eldad and Medad.
¹¹² San

### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:27:1)

AND THERE RAN A YOUNG MAN. The word *na’ar* (young man) has the definite article prefixed to it because it refers to the young man¹¹⁵ who, aside from Joshua, was known to serve Moses. For Joshua’s service was not the usual service rendered by young men.¹¹⁶

### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:28:1)


**מבחוריו.** יש אומרים שפירושו מבחרותו והטעם שלא אמר כדברי זקן ויש מדביקים מבחוריו עם משרת משה והטעם ששרת אותו מיום שהיה בחור וזה איננו נכון כי זה המעשה היה בשנה השנית והנכון בעיני שהוא מבחוריו הוא מהמובחרים לשרת משה כי אחרים יש עמו ודע כי מלת בחור בלשון רבים פעם תשתנה ופעם לא תשתנה וכן מלת סריס אולי הם שני משקלים:


FROM HIS YOUTH UP. Some say that the meaning of *mi-bechurav* (from his youth up) is, from his youth. It means that he did not speak as an elder speaks.¹¹⁷ Others connect *mi-bechurav* (from his youth up) to *mesharet mosheh* (the minister of Moses);¹¹⁸, meaning the minister of Moses from his (Joshua’s) youth on. this means the one who served Moses from the time of his youth.¹¹⁹ However, this is incorrect, for this event occurred in the second year.¹²⁰ In my opinion, the word *mi-bechurav* means from the chosen. It means that he was one of those chosen to serve Moses, for there were others with him.¹²¹ Note that the word *bachur* (youth, or chosen) sometimes changes in the plural and sometimes does not.¹²² (Ps. 78:31) and at other times with a *sheva* as in our verse (*bechurav*, rather than *bachurav*). The same applies to the word *saris* (officer).¹²³ is both *sarisim* (Esth. 2:21) and *serisim* (Gen. 40:7). It is also possible that they belong to two different paradigms.¹²⁴ comes from the singular *bachur* (vocalized *pattach, shurik*), *bechurav* from the singular *bachur* (vocalized *kamatz, shurik*). Similarly *sarism*, and *serisim*. According to rules of Hebrew grammar, in the former form the *kamatz* changes to a *sheva* in the plural. Compare, *davar, devarim*. However, in the latter case the *pattach* is maintained in the plural form.

footnotes:
¹¹⁷ He spoke as a youth speaks.
¹¹⁸ They read our text as follows: mesharet mosheh mi-bechurav
¹¹⁹ So Onkelos and Rashbam.
¹²⁰ Following the Exodus. However, Scripture nowhere tells us that Joshua served Moses before this date.
¹²¹ According to I.E. our clause should be interpreted: And Joshua the son of Nun, the minister of Moses, one of his chosen, answered and said.
¹²² In the plural the word is at times vocalized with a pattach
¹²³ The plural of saris
¹²⁴ Bachurav

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:28:2)


**כלאם.** נפתח הלמ"ד בעבור אות הגרון הבא אחריו:


SHUT THEM IN. The *lamed* of *kela’em* (shut them in) is vocalized with a *pattach*¹²⁵. There is either a scribal error or I.E. refers to a *pattach* here by the term *kamatz*. He occasionally does so in his long commentary on Exodus. because a guttural¹²⁶. follows it.¹²⁷.

¹²⁵ That is, a kamatz
¹²⁶ An alef
¹²⁷ Compare, kara’em, shelachem



### Verse 29

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:29:1)


ונפתחה ה"א **המקנא.** והיה ראוי להיותו בשו"א ובפת"ח ובעבור היותו תחת המ"ם שוא נע ושני שואים נעים לא יתחברו לעולם:


[ART THOU JEALOUS.] The *heh* of *ha-mekanne* (art thou jealous) is vocalized with a *pattach*. However, it should have been vocalized with a *sheva* and *a pattach*.¹²⁸, for the *heh* of *ha-mekanneh* is an interrogative *heh*, and interrogative *hehs* are vocalized with a *chataf pattach*. It was so vocalized because the *mem* has a *sheva na*¹²⁹. beneath it and two *sheva nas*¹³⁰ beneath an interrogative *heh* is a *sheva na*. are never joined together.

footnotes:
¹²⁸ A chataf pattach
¹²⁹ A vocative sheva
¹³⁰ The sheva

[WOULD THAT.] *Mi yitten*¹³¹ literally means who will give. Hence I.E.’s comment. (would that) is an expression of desire here. It is like saying, who will grant me this request and desire?

Thus God cut off some of the quail from them.¹³⁶ *Va-yagaz* comes from a double root.¹³⁷. It is like the word *va-yacham*¹³⁸. (waxed warm) in *and the flesh of the child waxed warm* (II Kings 4:34).

¹³⁸ From the root chet, mem, mem


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 11:31:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:31:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/11:31:4)


**ויטש.** כמו ויפרוש והטעם כדבר נטוש אין כח בו לסור וכן והנם נטושים על פני כל הארץ:


AND LET THEM FALL. The meaning of *va-yittosh* (and let them fall) is, *and they spread*. The quail were spread [all around the camp] and did not have the strength to fly away.¹³⁹ Compare, *they were spread* (netushim) *abroad all over the ground* (I Sam. 30:16).

## Chapter 12


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:1)


**ותדבר מרים.** היא דברה גם אהרן הסכים או החריש על כן נענש:


AND MIRIAM…SPOKE. Miriam spoke. Aaron agreed or was silent.¹ is to be interpreted: And Miriam spoke against Moses, and Aaron remained silent (or agreed). I.E. interprets thus because our verse reads, *va-tedabber miriam ve-aharon*.

footnotes:
¹ In other words, And Miriam and Aaron spoke against Moses


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:2)


וטעם **ותדבר.** עם בי"ת דרך גנאי כמו וידבר העם באלהים גם ימצא לשבח גם בדרך נבואה. יש אומרים כי משה מלך על כוש ולקח כושית והמתרגם אמר שפירתא וטעמו לשון כבוד כאשר יקראו הישמעאלים לזפת הלבן גם אנחנו נקרא העור סגי נהור. והנה לא יתכן שנקרא שם שהוא לשבח להפכו לגנאי ויש אומרים כי כוש בן ימיני הוא שאול וכן הלא כבני כושיים וכבר פירשתיו והישר בעיני שזו הכושית היא צפורה כי היא מדינית ומדינים הם ישמעאלים והם דרים באהלים וכן כתוב ירגזון יריעות ארץ מדין ובעבור חום השמש אין להם לבן כלל וצפורה היתה שחורה ודומה לכושית:


Now *va-tedabber* is in the feminine. Thus our verse is to be rendered, And Miriam spoke, and Aaron. If both spoke, then our text should have read, *va-yedabberu miriam ve-aharon*. Hence he was also punished.*Va-tedabber* (spoke) is followed by a *bet*² (And Miriam and Aaron spoke against Moses). and has a negative connotation. It is similar to *va-yedabber ha-am be-Elohim* (And the people spoke against God) (Num. 21:5). The verb *dabber* (spoke) followed by a *bet* is also found with a positive connotation.³ (adabberah): *Peace be within thee* (bakh) (Ps. 122:8). It also appears in prophetic statements.⁴ (II Sam. 23:2). Some say that Moses was king of Cush⁵ and took a Cushite woman.⁶ on *Shemot* 168; 247. Onkelos renders Cushite, beautiful.⁷ (the Cushite woman) as the beautiful women (*ittetah shappirtah*). According to Onkelos, Cushite is an honorific term. The Arabs similarly call pitch white. We too refer to a blind person by the term *sege nahor* (rich of light).⁸ 58a. But it is illogical⁹ to turn a positive name into a negative one.¹⁰ Some say that *Cush the Benjaminite* (Ps. 7:1) refers to Saul.¹¹. See Rashi and I.E. on Ps. 7:1. They say the same with regard to *Are ye not as the children of the Ethiopians* (Khushiyyim) *unto Me* (Amos 9:7).¹² I have already explained this.¹³I believe that the Cushite woman is to be identified with Zipporah, for Zipporah was a Midianite.¹⁴ The Midianites are Ishmaelites¹⁵ and they live in tents.¹⁶ Scripture similarly writes: *The curtains*¹⁷ *of the land of Midian do tremble* (Hab. 3:7). Because of the sun¹⁸ they do not have any whiteness at all. Zipporah was black and was like a Cushite.

footnotes:
² As it is in our verse, which reads, va-tedabber miriam ve-aharon be-mosheh
³ Compare, I will now say
⁴ Compare, The spirit of the Lord spoke by me
⁵ Ethiopia.
⁶ See Yalkut Shimoni
⁷ Onkelos renders ha-ishah ha-cushit
⁸ See Berachot
⁹ However, see Rashi, who explains Zipporah “was called a Cushite because of her beauty. This is similar to the case of a man who calls his beautiful son Cushi so that the evil eye will not harm him.” I.E. apparently did not believe in the evil eye. Hence he did not accept Rashi’s interpretation.
¹⁰ When something has a negative connotation we employ a euphemism. However, we do not refer to something positive by a negative term. Hence there is no reason to refer to a beautiful woman as a Cushite.
¹¹ They believe that Saul was very handsome and hence was called cush
¹² They say that its meaning is: Are ye not as beautiful children unto me.
¹³ See I.E. on Amos 9:7, where he explains this verse to mean: You are acting like Cushites in that you do not serve me.
¹⁴ See Ex. 2:15-21.
¹⁵ See Jud. 8:24. Also see I.E. on Gen. 37:28 (Vol. 1, p. 351).
¹⁶ Which are pitched in the wilderness under the hot sun.
¹⁷ The reference is to the curtains of the tents. We thus see that the Midianites live in tents.
¹⁸ They are very sunburned. See Cant. 1:5.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:1:3)


וטעם **כי אשה כשית לקח.** זה הדבור שדברה מרים ומה נכבד דברי קדמונינו שאמרו על הזקנים אשריהם ואוי לנשיהם והנה חשדו משה כי לא נמנע לשכב עם צפורה רק בעבור שאיננה יפה:


[FOR HE HAD MARRIED A CUSHITE WOMAN.] These are the words that Miriam spoke. How precious are the words of the ancients who said with regard to the elders, “Happy are they, but woe to their wives.”¹⁹ 17b; *Sifre, Devarim* 99; and *Yalkut Shimoni, Devarim* 738. They suspected that Moses refrained from sleeping with Zipporah only because she was not beautiful.²⁰ is to be understood as: because he refrained from sleeping with her because she was a Cushite.

footnotes:
¹⁹ According to the rabbis, Zipporah complained that Moses ceased having sexual relations with her ever since he was chosen to receive Divine revelation. The rabbis say that Miriam spoke on behalf of Zipporah and argued that she too was a prophetess and yet did not abstain from relations with her husband. See Mo’ed Katan
²⁰ And not because he was a prophet. According to I.E., because of the Cushite woman whom he had married



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:1)


**ויאמרו הרק אך במשה.** הביאה ראיה כי לא עשה זה לקדושת השם כי הם היו נביאים ואין המשכב אסור להם:


AND THEY SAID: HATH THE LORD INDEED SPOKEN ONLY WITH MOSES? Miriam offered proof that Moses did not act in this manner because of the sanctity of God, for they too were prophets and they were not prohibited from engaging in sexual intercourse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:2)


**הרק אך.** והאחד יספיק רק הוא דרך צחות כמו המבלי אין קברים:


ONLY WITH. Scripture reads, *ha-rak akh*.²¹ means only; so does *akh*. Thus our clause, which reads *ha-rak akh be-mosheh dibber Adonai*, literally reads: Hath the Lord spoken only only with Moses. Hence I.E.’s comment. One term²² or *akh*. Our text should have read, *ha-rak be-mosheh*, or *ha-akh be-mosheh*. would have sufficed. However, Scripture is employing elegant language.²³ Compare, *ha-mi-mi-beli en kevarim* (because there were no graves) (Ex. 14:11).²⁴ and *en* mean the same thing. See I.E. on Ex. 14:11 (Vol. 2, p. 277).

footnotes:
²¹ Rak
²² Rak
²³ It is considered poetic to repeat certain words in certain contexts.
²⁴ Beli


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:2:3)


**וישמע ה'.** ואם דברו בסתר:


AND THE LORD HEARD IT. Even though they spoke in secret.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ And did not publicly criticize Moses. Miriam and Aaron spoke only to each other about this. They would not publicly discuss such an intimate matter.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:3:1)


**ענו מאד.** במשקל שלו והטעם שלא בקש גדולה על אחיו:


WAS VERY MEEK. The word *anav*²⁶. (meek) is similar to the word *shalev*²⁷. (at peace) (Job 16:12).²⁸. In both words a *vav* has been substituted for a *heh*. Its meaning is that Moses did not seek honor over his brother.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁶ From the root ayin, nun, heh
²⁷ From the root shin, lamed, heh
²⁸ Both words come from roots that end in a heh
²⁹ Contrary to what Miriam had implied in verse 2, Moses did not consider himself superior to Aaron.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:4:1)


**פתאום.** המ"ם נוסף כמ"ם שלשום והוא מגזרת פתי דבר שלא עלה על לב:


SUDDENLY. The *mem* of *pitom* (suddenly) is superfluous. It is like the mem of *shilshom* (three days ago).³⁰ of which is superfluous. *Pitom* is related to *peti* (a thoughtless one) (Prov. 9:4). It refers to something that one did not think of.³¹ is a person who does not anticipate the consequences of his acts. Similarly, the word *pitom* (suddenly) means unanticipated.

footnotes:
³⁰ The mem
³¹ According to I.E. a peti


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:4:2)


**צאו שלשתכם ויצאו שלשתם.** כל אחד מאהלו והנה דרך כלל ואחר כן פרט וירד ה' בעמוד ענן ויקרא אהרן ומרים ומשה שומע:


COME OUT YE THREE UNTO THE TENT OF MEETING. Each one from his tent. Scripture first speaks generally.³² is a general statement. It then goes into detail, namely, *And the Lord came down in a pillar of cloud…and called Aaron and Miriam* (v. 5), and Moses heard.³³

footnotes:
³² In other words, Come out ye three unto the tent of meeting
³³ What God told Aaron and Miriam.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:1)


**שמעו נא.** כמו עתה:


HEAR NOW MY WORDS. The word *na* means now.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ See I.E. on Num. 10:31.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:2)


**נביאכם.** מי שיהיה מכם נביא ה':


A PROPHET AMONG YOU. If there be a prophet of God among you.³⁵, which literally means your prophet. In this case, our verse reads, if your prophet will be. However, this reading makes no sense within the context of our verse. Hence I.E. maintains that *im yiheyeh nevi’akhem* be rendered, if there be a prophet among you.

footnotes:
³⁵ The Hebrew reads, nevi’akhem


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:3)


**במראה.** פירושו אם היה נביאכם שהוא נביא ה' כמו והנבואה עודד הנביא האהלה שרה אמו כסאך אלהים עולם:


IN A VISION The meaning of *im yiheyeh nevi’akhem Adonai*³⁶ (if there be a prophet among you, I the Lord) is, if there be a prophet, one who is a prophet of God among you.³⁷ should be read as if written *nevi’akhem nevi Adonai*. Compare, *ve-hanevu’ah oded ha-navi*³⁸ (even the prophecy, prophecy of Oded the prophet), for the word *nevu’ah* has a definite article prefixed to it and thus cannot be in the construct with *oded*. See I.E. on Gen. 2:9 (Vol. 1, p. 54). (even the prophecy of Oded the prophet (II Chron. 15:8); and *ha-ohelah sarah immo* (his mother Sarah’s tent) (Gen. 24:67);³⁹ (the tent, tent of Sarah his mother). See I.E. on Gen. 24:67 (Vol. 1, p. 243). and *kisakha Elohim olam*⁴⁰ (Thy Throne, Throne of God for ever). See I.E. on Gen. 24:67 (Vol. 1, p. 243). (Thy throne given of God is for ever) (Ps. 45:7).

footnotes:
³⁶ This literally reads, if they will be your prophet Lord.
³⁷ In other words, nevi’akhem Adonai
³⁸ According to I.E. this should be read as if written, ve-ha-nevu’ah nevu’ot oded ha-navi
³⁹ According to I.E. this is to be read as if written, ha-ohelah ohel sarah immo
⁴⁰ According to I.E. this should be read as if written, kisakha kisse Elohim olam


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:6:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:4)


**במראה.** במראות הלילה:


IN A VISION. In the visions of the night.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:6:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:6:5)


**בחלום אדבר בו.** כפול כדרך הנבואות:


I DO SPEAK WITH HIM IN A DREAM. Scripture repeats itself as is its style in prophecies.⁴¹ repeats *I…do make myself known unto him in a vision*.

footnotes:
⁴¹ I do speak with him in a dream



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:7:1)


**בכל ביתי נאמן הוא.** טעמו כבן בית שיכנס בלא רשות ואם יצטרך ידבר צרכיו ואתם כאשר אתודע לכם בחלום תדעו ואם לא אין לכם רשות לשאול:


HE IS TRUSTED IN ALL MY HOUSE. It means that Moses is, at it were, a member of My house. He enters without permission. If necessary he states his needs. However, in your case when I make myself known to you in a dream, then you are informed. If not, then you have no permission to ask.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:1)


**פה אל פה אדבר בו.** הטעם בלא אמצעי:


WITH HIM DO I SPEAK MOUTH TO MOUTH. The meaning is, I speak to him without an intermediary.⁴²

footnotes:
⁴² According to I.E., God at first spoke to Moses via an angel. However, at the giving of the second set of tablets and onward, he spoke to Moses without an intermediary. See I.E. on Ex. 33:21 (Vol. 2, p. 693).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:2)


**במראה ולא בחידות.** הטעם שאראה לו הדבור כאשר הוא כצורת המשכן:


EVEN MANIFESTLY, AND NOT IN DARK SPEECHES. This means I illustrate my word to him as I did when I commanded the erection of the tabernacle.⁴³.

footnotes:
⁴³ See Ex. 25:40, And see that thou make them after their pattern, which is being shown thee in the mount


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:3)


**ולא בחידת.** כמו הנשר הגדול:


AND NOT IN DARK SPEECHES. Compare, *A great eagle* (*…Came unto Lebanon, And took the top of the cedar*) (Ezek. 17:3).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:8:4)


**ותמונת ה' יביט.** כטעם הראני נא את כבודך או שהוא בהקיץ:


AND THE SIMILITUDE OF THE LORD DOTH HE BEHOLD. As in the case of *Show me, I pray Thee, Thy glory* (Ex. 33:18).⁴⁴ Or its meaning is⁴⁵. that God communicates with Moses when Moses is awake.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ And God responded, as it were, by showing Moses His back.
⁴⁵ The meaning of and the similitude of the Lord doth he behold



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:9:1)


**וילך.** הכבוד והעד והענן סר:


AND HE DEPARTED. The reference is to God’s glory. *And when the cloud was removed*⁴⁶ (v. 10) proves this.

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Which immediately follows.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:10:1)


**מעל האהל.** מעל פתח האהל:


FROM OVER THE TENT. From over the opening of the tent.⁴⁷

footnotes:
⁴⁷ Where it was up to this time. See verse 5.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:10:2)


**והנה מצורעת.** תחסר מלת היא כמו כאשר קאה:


AND…BEHOLD, MIRIAM WAS LEPROUS. *Ve-hinneh metzora’at*⁴⁸ is to be rendered, and behold, “she” [Miriam] was leprous.⁴⁹ is to be read as if written, *ve-hinneh hi metzora’at*. Compare, *ka’asher ka’ah*⁵⁰, for *ka’asher ka’ah* means as spit out. (as it vomited out) (Lev. 18:28).

footnotes:
⁴⁸ Which literally means, and behold, leprous.
⁴⁹ In other words, ve-hinneh metzora’at
⁵⁰ Which is to be read as if written, ka’asher hi ka’ah



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:1)


**בי אדני.** פירשתיו:


OH MY LORD. I have already explained the meaning of *bi adoni* (Oh my lord).⁵¹ is an expression introducing a request, or the phase of *bi adoni* is short for: upon me (*bi*), my lord, is the sin. See I.E. on Gen. 43:20 (Vol. 1, p. 392); and Ex. 4:10 (Vol. 2, p. 99).

footnotes:
⁵¹ Bi


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:2)


**אל נא.** אל עתה:


LAY NOT, I PRAY THEE. The meaning of *al na*⁵² means now. Hence his comment. (…not, I pray thee) is, now, do not.⁵³

footnotes:
⁵² According to I.E. the word na
⁵³ According to I.E. our verse should be rendered: oh my lord, do not now lay sin upon us.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:11:3)


**נואלנו.** מבנין נפעל ואין לו ריע כי אם נואלו שרי צאן ויש אומרים שהוא הפוך מאולת קשורה וטעמו ידוע:


WE HAVE DONE FOOLISHLY. The word *no’alnu* (we have done foolishly) is a *nifal*. It has no cognate⁵⁴ does not appear in Scripture with the meaning of foolishness except when it is in the *nifal* form. It has a different meaning in the *hifil*. See Filwarg. except for the word *no’alu* (are become fools)⁵⁵ forms. See Jer. 5:4 (Filwarg). in *The princes of Zoan are become fools* (Is. 19:13). Some say that the root of *no’alnu*⁵⁶ comes from the root *yod, alef, lamed*. was formed by inverting the root *ivvelet*⁵⁷ (*yod*), *lamed*. (foolishness). Compare, *Foolishness is bound up* (Prov. 22:15) (in the heart of a child). The meaning of *no’alnu* is known.⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁴ That is, the root yod, alef, lamed
⁵⁵ And similar nifal
⁵⁶ No’alnu
⁵⁷ From the root alef, vav
⁵⁸ Its meaning is, we have done foolishly.



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:12:1)


**אל נא תהי כמת.** אל עתה תהיה אחותינו כנפל המת כאשר יצא מבטן אמו נאכל חצי בשרו וכן בשר המצורע ואין צורך לתיקון סופרים:


LET HER NOT, I PRAY, BE AS ONE DEAD. Let our sister not now be as a stillborn child, half of whose flesh is consumed when it comes out of its mother’s womb. The same is true of the flesh of a leper.⁵⁹ There is no need for a scribal emendation.⁶⁰ should be read as if written our mother’s womb, and “half of his flesh” as if it read half our flesh, for Scripture employed the third rather than the first person plural because it wanted to avoid using non-flattering language regarding Moses and Aaron. See *Sifre debe Rav, Be-Ha’alotekha* 105. Also see Rashi on our verse. I.E. apparently believes that the Midrash was of the opinion that the scribes changed the original text of the Torah from the first person to the third person. He disagrees and maintains that there is no need to change the reading when interpreting our verse.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Hence Moses compared Miriam to a stillborn child.
⁶⁰ According to the Midrash, His mothers womb



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:13:1)


**ויצעק משה.** זה יורה שהיה בצער על אחותו:


AND MOSES CRIED. This shows that Moses was pained because of what happened to his sister.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:13:2)


**אל.** אתה שיש הגבורה בידך עתה רפא עתה לה על כן השיב השם ואביה ואילו אביה כעס עליה וירק בפניה הלא תכלם לראות פניו שבעת ימים:


O GOD. You who hold the power in Your hands,⁶¹. According to I.E. this name alludes to God’s power. heal her now. God therefore answered him as follows: If her father was angry at her and spat in her face, would she not hide in shame from seeing his face for seven days?

footnotes:
⁶¹ The term used for God in our verse is El



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:14:1)


**תסגר.** כמו המצורעים שלא יזיקו אחרים:


SHUT UP. As lepers are, so that they do not harm others.⁶²

footnotes:
⁶² By their proximity to them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:14:2)


**ואחר תאסף.** תחשב מהיישוב וכן ואספתו מצרעתו ואחר שבאה אל המחנה נסעו העם מחצרות:


AND AFTER THAT SHE SHALL BE BROUGHT IN AGAIN. *Ve-acher te’asef* (and after that she shall be brought in again) means she shall once again be considered as a member of the community. *Va-asafto* (and you shall bring him in) in *and you shall bring him in from his leprosy* (II Kings 5:3) is similar. After Miriam returned to the camp, the people journeyed from Hazeroth.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 12:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/12:16:1)


**ויחנו במדבר פארן.** על דעת רבים שהענן היה שם בעבור שמצאו וישכן הענן במדבר פארן ויפרשו הענן סר מעל האהל כמשמעו ולפי דעתי אין זה הפירוש נכון:


AND PITCHED IN THE WILDERNESS OF PARAN. Many believe that the cloud was there.⁶³ They interpret thus because they found Scripture stating *and the cloud abode in the wilderness of Paran* (Num. 10:12).⁶⁴ They interpret *And when the cloud was removed from over the Tent* (v. 10) literally.⁶⁵ However, I believe that this is not the correct meaning.⁶⁶

footnotes:
⁶³ In Paran.
⁶⁴ See I.E. on Num. 10:31.
⁶⁵ That the cloud left the tent of meeting and took up its journey to Paran. See Num. 10:11.
⁶⁶ Its meaning is that it left the front of the tent and moved to the top of the tent. See I.E. on verse 10.



## Chapter 13


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:1)


**שלח לך.** כתוב שהשם אמר לישראל עלה רש והם אמרו נשלחה אנשים אז אמר השם שלח לך אנשים:


SEND THOU MEN. Scripture records that God told Israel: *go up, take possession* (Deut. 1:21)¹ and Israel answered: *Let us send men before us* (Deut. 1:22).² God then said, *Send thou men*.³

footnotes:
¹ Of the land of Canaan.
² Thus it appears that the spies were sent to Canaan in response to Israel’s request. However, our Torah portion seems to imply that Moses sent spies to Canaan in response to God’s command.
³ In other words, what is reported in our verse was in response to Israel’s request reported in Deut. 1:21,22.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:2)


וטעם **אנשים.** שהם ידועים גבורים וכן כלם אנשים וחזקת והיית לאיש כי איש היה:


The meaning of *men* is, men known for their might. The same applies to *all of them men*⁴ (v. 3) and to *be thou* [Solomon] *strong and show thyself a man*⁵ is: and show thyself to be a man of valor. (I Kings 2:2), for he [Solomon] was a man.⁶ cannot be taken literally.

footnotes:
⁴ Its meaning is, all of them were men known for their might.
⁵ The meaning of and show thyself a man
⁶ Thus and show thyself to be man


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:3)


**ויתורו.** ויחפשו וכן ולא תתורו:


THAT THEY MAY SPY OUT. The meaning of *ve-yaturu* (that they may spy out) is that they may search out. Compare, *ve-lo taturu*⁷ as: and you shall not search out after what your eyes see. (and that ye go not about) (Num. 15:39).

footnotes:
⁷ I.E. renders ve-lo taturu


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:2:4)


**כל נשיא בהם.** כל אחד הוא נשיא בהם או תהיה מלת אחד מושכת עצמה ואחרת עמה וכן ושנים אנשים שרי גדודים היו בן שאול ורבים כן:


EVERY ONE A PRINCE AMONG THEM. *Kol nasi ba-hem*⁸ is to be interpreted: each one was a prince among them.⁹ is short for *kol ehad hu nasi be-hem*. On the other hand, the word *ehad* mentioned earlier in our verse also applies here.¹⁰ is not missing in our text, and *kol echad nasi ba-hem* is not short for *kol echad hu nasi ba-hem*. There doesn’t seem to be much of a difference between the two interpretations offered by I.E. See Filwarg and Krinsky. Compare, *And two men, captains of bands, were* [captains of] *the son of Saul*¹¹ (II Sam. 4:2).¹² (sare) *bands, were captains of the son of Saul*, for *captains of* (sare) is to be read as if written twice. There are many such examples.¹³

footnotes:
⁸ Literally, every prince among them. Hence I.E.’s comment.
⁹ In other words, kol nasi ba-hem
¹⁰ Thus the word ehad
¹¹ Translated literally.
¹² This verse is to be interpreted: And two men, captains of
¹³ Where a word in a verse is to be read as if written twice in a verse. See I.E. on Gen. 6: 13 (Vol. 1., p. 100, note 36).



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:16:1)


וטעם **אלה שמות האנשים.** אחר שאמר ואלה שמותם להודיע כי כן היה שמותם לעולם ולא השתנו כמו הושע:


[THESE ARE THE NAMES OF THE MEN.] The reason that Scripture states, *these are the names of the men*, after earlier stating, *And these were their names* (v. 4),¹⁴ after And *these were their names* appears redundant. Hence I.E. comment. is to teach us that, unlike Hoshea,¹⁵ their names were never changed.

footnotes:
¹⁴ These are the names of the men
¹⁵ Whose name was changed to Joshua (v. 16).



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:17:1)


**עלו זה בנגב.** עלו זה הדרך בנגב וידוע כי מצרים הוא נגב ארץ ישראל כאשר פירשתי בספר דניאל והראיה כי רוחב מצרים פחות משלשים מעלות ורוחב ירושלים שלשים ושלש ומדבר פארן בנגב ארץ מצרים והנה פירוש בנגב אינו נגב המחנה רק פירושו בנגב של ארץ כנען והעד בחברון שהוא ליהודה והכתוב אמר יהודה יעמוד על גבולו מנגב:


GET YOU UP HERE INTO THE SOUTH. Get you up this way in the south.¹⁶ is to be rendered: get you up this way i n the south (of the land of Canaan). The literal meaning of *alu zeh ba-negev* is: get you up this into the south. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is known that Egypt is south of the land of Israel, as I have explained in the Book of Daniel.¹⁷ The following is proof of this. Egypt is less than thirty degrees north of the equator.¹⁸, a term referring to the distance of a plane to the equator. Jerusalem is thirty-three degrees north of the equator.¹⁹ The desert of Paran²⁰ is south of the land of Canaan.²¹ Observe that the meaning of *in the South*²². is not south of the camp²³ but in the south of the land of Canaan. Proof of the aforementioned is that Scripture tells us concerning Hebron, which belonged to the tribe of Judah, *Judah shall abide in his border on the south* (Josh. 18:5).²⁴

footnotes:
¹⁶ In other words, alu zeh ba-negev
¹⁷ See I.E. on Dan. 11:5.
¹⁸ Hebrew, rochav
¹⁹ See above.
²⁰ Where Israel was encamped when Moses sent out the spies.
²¹ The text reads, south of the land of Egypt. Paran was the place where Israel was when it sent out the spies. Geographically speaking, Paran is not south of Egypt. It is north of Egypt and lies between Egypt and Canaan. Hence a number of commentaries suggest emending the text to read, the wilderness of Paran is south of the land of Israel, or the wilderness of Paran is north of the land of Egypt. See Filwarg, Weiser, Krinsky, and Meijler.
²² Hebrew, ba-negev
²³ For taking a road south of the camp would lead them back into Egypt, as Paran is north of Egypt.
²⁴ We thus see that the south (the Negev) is a district in the Land of Israel.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:18:1)


**וראיתם את הארץ מה היא.** ומה העם:


AND SEE THE LAND, WHAT IT IS. And the people, what is it?²⁵ (what is it) is carried over from *ha-aretz* (and the land) to *ha-am* (the people). Thus our text is to be read as if written, *et ha-aretz mah hi vet ha-am ma hu* (the land, what it is and the people what is it,) i.e., what is their nature.

WHETHER THEY ARE STRONG. In battle. Scripture then²⁶ goes on to explain, *and see the land*. It says, *and what the land is…whether it is good*, that is, whether its air and water are good.

[AND CAME UNTO HEBRON.] Our sages of blessed memory said that *and came* refers to Caleb the son of Jephunneh,²⁸ 34b. Also see Rashi. *And came* (va-yavo) is in the singular. Hence the rabbinic interpretation. for each one of the men went to a different part of the country.²⁹

²⁸ Sotah

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/13:22:2)


**צען מצרים.** שם מדינה סמוכה אל מצרים וכן מבית לחם יהודה ויש אומרים שפירושו מגזרת אהל בל יצען בדרך רחוקה:


ZOAN IN EGYPT. Zoan is the name of a city in Egypt.³⁰ literally, Zoan Egypt. Now a proper name does not come in the construct. Hence I.E. points out that here it does, contrary to the rule. According to I.E. *tzo’an mitzrayim* is short for *tzo’an shel mitzrayim*, Zoan of Egypt. *Of Beth-lehem Judah*³¹ (Ruth 1:1)³² is similar.³³ Some say that the word *tzo’an* (Zoan) is connected to the word *yitzan* (be removed) in *A tent that shall not be removed* (Is. 33:20).³⁴ as meaning the establishment of Egypt However, this is farfetched.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 13:32:1

**ויוציאו דבת הארץ.** דבר שלא היה ואין כן ויבא יוסף כי ויבא הפך ויוציאו:


AND THEY SPREAD AN EVIL REPORT OF THE LAND. *Va-yotzi’u dibbat ha-aretz* (and they spread an evil report) means: and they spread a lie. This is not the meaning of *va-yave yosef et dibbatam ra* (and Joseph brought evil report of them) (Gen. 37:2), for *va-yave* (brought) is the reverse of *va-yotzi* (and they spread).⁴² follows *va- yave*, it refers to the truth. When it follows *va-yotzi*, it refers to a lie.

EVIL REPORT. The word *dibbah* (evil report) comes from a double root.⁴³, even though it is spelled *dalet, bet, heh*, which, *prima facie*, appears to be its root. It comes from the same root as the word *dovev* (moving gently) in *Moving gently the lips of those that are asleep* (Cant. 7:10). I have explained it in its place.⁴⁴ means to speak. It is possible the word *dibbah* is similar.” In other words, both *dovev* and *dibbah* refer to speech.

### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:3:1)


**שוב מצרימה.** שם הפועל כמו לא אוכל קום ומלך אלקום עמו:


TO RETURN INTO EGYPT. *Shuv* (to return) is an infinitive.⁴ can also be an imperative. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is like the word *kum* (to stand)⁵ can also be an imperative. in *I am not able to stand* (Lam. 1:14) and *kum* (rising up) in *And the king, against whom there is no rising up* (Prov. 30:31).

footnotes:
⁴ Shuv
⁵ Which is an infinitive, even though the word kum

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:9:1

FOR THEY ARE BREAD. We will eat them like bread. *Who eat up My people as they eat bread* (Ps. 14:4) is similar.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:9:2)


**סר צלם.** כי הגבור אם אין לו מגן שיגן עליו ויהיה צל לו לבו ירא:


THEIR DEFENSE IS REMOVED FROM OVER THEM.⁸ For the heart of a warrior frets when he does not have a shield to protect him and to serve as a shade for him.⁹

⁸ Literally, their shadow is removed from over them. Hence I.E.’s comment.

### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:13:1)


**ושמעו מצרים כי העלית.** טעמו ואשר העלית ואחר שאמר ושמעו ואמרו אל יושב הארץ הזאת היא ארץ כנען פירש מה שמעו על כן פעם אחרת שמעו כי אתה ה' כבודך בקרב העם הזה:


WHEN THE EGYPTIANS SHALL HEAR–FOR THOU BROUGHTEST UP THIS PEOPLE. The meaning of *ki he’elita* (for thou broughtest up) is: that you brought up.¹² is to be interpreted as if written *asher he’elita*. In other words, the *ki* (for) in our verse is to be rendered *asher* (that). After Scripture states, *When the Egyptians shall hear* (ve-shame’u)…they *will say to the inhabitants of this land*, the reference being to the land of Canaan, it goes on to explain¹³ what the Egyptians heard. Scripture therefore repeats the word *shame’u* (have heard) and states they *have heard* (shame’u) *that Thou Lord* [Thy glory] *art in the midst of this people* (v. 14).¹⁴ in verse 14 picks up the thought which was interrupted by *they will say to the inhabitants of the land*.

footnotes:
¹² Ki he’elita
¹³ In verse 14.
¹⁴ In other words, the word shame’u



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:14:1)


**אשר עין בעין.** על הכבוד שראו הזקנים או על מראה כבוד ה' לעיני בני ישראל והוא הנכון:


ART SEEN FACE TO FACE. The reference is to the glory which the elders saw (Ex. 24:9,10), or to *the appearance of the glory of the Lord…in the eyes of the children of Israel* (Ex. 24:17). The latter is correct.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:14:2)


**נראה.** פעל עבר וטעמו נראת אתה ה' וכן ונאשאר אני וכן כי אומלל אני בעבור היות הלמ"ד בפת"ח גדול:


ART SEEN. The word *nirah* (seen) is a verb in the perfect.¹⁵ The meaning of *attah Adonai nirah* is, Thou Lord were seen.¹⁶) is combined with the third person perfect (*nirah*) we follow the pronoun. *Ve-nishar*¹⁷ is a third person perfect. *ani* (and I was left) (Ezek. 9: 8) is similar.¹⁸ So is *ki umlal*¹⁹ is a third person perfect. *ani*²⁰ is a first person pronoun. (for I languish away) (Ps. 6:3), because the *lamed* of *umlal* is vocalized with a *pattach*.²¹ indicates that *umlal* is a third person perfect. However, see I.E. on Psalms 6:3.
