¹⁵ It is a third person perfect. Its meaning is, he was seen.
¹⁶ When a second person pronoun (attah
¹⁷ Nishar
¹⁸ In that we have an anomalous combination of a pronoun and a verb.
¹⁹ Umlal
²⁰ Ani
²¹ The pattach

**עמד עליהם.** בשבתם:


STANDETH OVER THEM. When they camp.

### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:16:1)


**יכלת.** שם כמו עד יבושת המים ואיננו לשון מדה כדרך הדרש:


ABLE. The word *yekholet* (able) is a noun. It is similar to the word *yevoshet* (dried up) in *until the waters were dried up* (Gen. 8:7). It does not mean a measure as in the midrashic interpretation.²³ to the word *yakhil* (held a measure). See Kings I 7:26. According to Krinsky the reference is to *Midrash Tanhumah* on our verse.

²³ A Midrash connects the word yekholet

**וישחטם במדבר.** הטעם ויהרגם ואמר וישחטם בעבור המדבר כי שם ירעו הצאן:


THEREFORE HE HATH SLAIN THEM IN THE WILDERNESS. The meaning of *va-yishchatem* is, He hath slain them. Scripture employs the term *va-yishchatem*²⁴ because sheep graze in the wilderness.

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:17:1)


**יגדל נא.** קרוב מאריכות אפים כי כל מי שהוא ארך אפים יש לו כח גדול לשבור כעסו והעד כאשר דברת שאתה ארך אפים ואלה המדות פירשתים בפרשת כי תשא:


LET THE POWER OF THE LORD BE GREAT. *Let the* power…be *great* connotes being slow to anger, for all who are long-suffering have the great power to break their anger. *As Thou hast spoken*, that you are slow to anger²⁵ (v. 17): *The Lord is slow to anger* (v. 18). is proof of this. I have explained these attributes²⁶ in the Torah portion *Ki Tissa*.

²⁶ The attributes mentioned in verse 18.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:19:1)


**סלח נא.** אחר שמצאנו שאמר אם יראו את הארץ אחר סלחתי כדברך ידענו כי מלת סלח נא אריכות אף וכן ונסלח לו עד שיעשה תשובה שלימה:


PARDON, I PRAY THEE. Since we find Scripture saying *surely they shall not see the land* (v. 23) after *salachti ke-devarekha* (I have pardoned according to thy word) (v. 20), we learn that the term *salach* (pardon) refers to being slow to anger.²⁷ meant full pardon, then (he people would have been permitted to enter the land. Similarly, the meaning of *ve-nislach lo* (and he shall be forgiven) (Num 15:28) is [the punishment shall be held in abeyance] until he fully repents.²⁸ in all cases means that punishment is not meted out immediately but is delayed. In the case of Num. 15:28, a sacrifice delays punishment and the person is pardoned if he truly repents.

²⁷ If salach
²⁸ Salach

ACCORDING UNTO THE GREATNESS OF THY LOVINGKINDNESS. The meaning of *ke-godel chasdekha* (according to the greatness of Thy lovingkindness) is: because of Thy great lovingkindness.²⁹ usually has the meaning of like or according. Hence I.E.’s comment that here it means because of.

AND ACCORDING AS THOU HAST FORGIVEN. The word *nasata* (Thou hast forgiven) is similar to the word *nose* (forgiving) in *forgiving sin* (Ex. 34:7).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:19:4)


**ועד הנה.** עד המקום הזה או מיום שיצאו ממצרים ועד זה היום כי יש הנה שם המקום כמו באו הנה הלילה גם סימן זמן כמו ולא ראיתיו עד הנה וכבר פירשתיו:


EVEN UNTIL NOW. Until this place or from the day that they left Egypt until this very day, for the word *hennah* (now) can refer to a place or to a specific time. Compare, *there came men in hither* (hennah) (Josh. 2:2) and, *and I have not seen him until now* (hennah) (Gen. 44:28).³⁰ I have already explained this term.³¹ in Gen 44:28 refers to time.

AS I LIVE. It means as I live, so shall My word come to pass.³² is short for, As I live, so shall my word come to pass.

SHALL BE FILLED WITH THE GLORY. As My glory fills the earth.³³ (shall be filled) is to be taken as a present. [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:22:1)

### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:24:1)


**ועבדי כלב.** הזכירו לבדו בעבור ויהס כלב והלוים והכהנים אינם בכלל השבועה כי לא הלך מהם נשיא מרגל ועוד לכל פקודיכם לכל מספרכם אף על פי שלא היו הכהנים גם הלוים הם רבים:


BUT MY SERVANT CALEB. Caleb alone³⁸, and Levites are not mentioned in this verse even though they entered the land. is mentioned because he *stilled the people* (Num. 13:30). The Levites and the *kohanim* are not included in the oath,³⁹ for no prince from among them went to spy out the land. Furthermore, Scripture states, *all that were numbered of you, according to your whole number* (v. 29).⁴⁰ and the Levites were not among those numbered (Num. 1:49), God’s decree did not apply to them. Now even though there were no *kohanim* there,⁴¹ I.E. means few *kohanim*, for Aaron and his sons were there. there were in reality many Levites there.⁴²

ANOTHER SPIRIT. In contrast to the spies.

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:25:1)


**והעמלקי והכנעני.** הוא האמורי כי כל אמורי הוא כנעני ולא חשש הכתוב להפרישו בעבור העמלקי שהוא מבני שם וכנען שהוא אבי האמורי מבני חם וכן וירד העמלקי והכנעני והעד ויצא האמורי ולא הזכיר העמלקי כי האמורי הוא העיקר:


NOW THE AMALEKITE AND THE CANAANITE. The Canaanite refers to the Amorite, for every Amorite is a Canaanite.⁴³ Scripture does not bother to mention the Amorite by name because the other nation mentioned is the Amalekite, who is of the children of Shem.⁴⁴. However, when it speaks of one of the Canaanite nations along with a non-Canaanite nation, it does not mention its specific name but uses its general name. On the other hand, Canaan, who is the father of the Amorite,⁴⁵ is of the children of Ham.⁴⁶ *Then the Amalekite and the Canaanite…came down* (Num. 14:45) is similar.⁴⁷ *And the Amorites…came out* (Deut. 1:44)⁴⁸ is proof of this. Scripture does not mention the Amalekite in the aforementioned verse, as the Amorite was the more important.⁴⁹

footnotes:
⁴³ The Amorites are descended from Canaan. See Gen. 10:16.
⁴⁴ Scripture distinguishes between the Canaanite nations when it speaks of the Canaanites per se
⁴⁵ Hence the term Canaanite includes the Amorites.
⁴⁶ The Canaanite is mentioned by name and not called the Hammite because Ham had other sons.
⁴⁷ There too the reference is to the Amorite.
⁴⁸ The reference is to the same incident as that in our verse.
⁴⁹ The Amorite carried the brunt of the battle (Weiser).

DWELT IN THE VALE. To ambush you. Others say that the meaning of our verse is, even though the Amalekite and the Canaanite dwell in the valley, turn toward the way of the Red Sea and do not fear.⁵⁰ Now the Israelites did not repent, hence God’s word.⁵¹

CONCERNING WHICH I LIFTED UP MY HAND. Scripture speaks as humans do, for the sky is above.⁵²

YOUR CARCASSES.⁵⁴. Literally, and your carcasses, you. Hence I.E.’s comment that the aforementioned is to be interpreted, and your carcasses, namely, you. In other words, Scripture is being explicit. Namely, you.

SHALL BE WANDERERS.⁵⁵. Literally, will shepherd. Hence I.E.’s comment. For it is the way of shepherds not to remain and rest in one place.⁵⁶ *My habitation is plucked up and carried away from me As a shepherd’s tent* (Is. 38:12) is proof of this.

### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:34:1

WHICH YE SPIED OUT. By command.⁵⁷

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:34:2)


**את תנואתי.** אניא דברי או מי יניאני והטעם שישבור את שבועתי כמו ואם הניא:


MY DISPLEASURE.⁵⁸. I.E. renders this as my breaking. Hence his comments. I will break my word.⁵⁹. 38 reads: If I will break My word. In this case, the reference is to God’s oath, which He took not to bring the generation that left Egypt into Canaan. In view of the belief of I.E. that God is unchangeable (see his comments on Gen. 6:6, Vol. 1, p. 96), the reading of *Vat. Ebr*. 38 appears correct. Or, it means⁶⁰, My breaking. who shall break Me; that is, who shall break My oath.⁶¹ Compare, *But if her father break* (heni) *her* (Num 30:6).

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:43:1

FORASMUCH AS YE ARE TURNED BACK. *Ki al ken shavtem* (forasmuch as ye are turned back) means, since you turned back.⁷⁰ means since. The literal meaning of *ki al ken* is “for on yes.” Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *since* (ki al ken) *I gave her not* (Gen. 38:26).

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:44:1

BUT THEY PRESUMED TO GO UP. *Va-yapilu* (but they presumed) is related to the word *ofel* (mound) in *the mound and the tower* (Is. 32:14). The meaning of *va-yapilu* is, they went up the hill.



### Verse 45

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 14:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/14:45:1)


**ויכתום.** אמר רבי משה הכהן הספרדי ז"ל כי היה ראוי להיותו ויכיתום וכן וידביקו גם המה והנכון בעיני שהוא מפעלי הכפל והיא מלה זרה:


AND BEAT THEM DOWN. Rabbi Moses the Spaniard, of blessed memory, says that our text⁷¹ (and beat them down). should have read *va-yakkitum* (and beat them down).⁷² from the root *nun, kaf, tav*. Rabbi Moses is only commenting on the irregularity of *va-yakketum*. The meaning remains the same. *Va-yadbeku* (followed hard) in *even they also followed hard after them* (I Sam. 14:22) is similar.⁷³ rather than *va-yadbeku*, for the word is in the *hifil*. However, I believe that *va-yakketum* (and beat them down) comes from a double root⁷⁴. and is an irregular word.⁷⁵.

⁷² For the word is a hifil

⁷⁴ The root kaf, tav, tav

**עד החרמה.** שם מקום ויש אומרים עד שהחרימום:


EVEN UNTO HORMAH. Hormah is the name of a place. Others say that the meaning of *even unto Hormah* is, until they destroyed them.⁷⁶ means destruction, and *even unto Hormah* means even unto destruction.

## Chapter 15


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:2:1)


**כי תבואו אל ארץ מושבתיכם.** נסמכה זאת הפרשה בעבור שנחלשו ויתאבלו לנחם הבנים להודיעם כי יבאו אל הארץ והנכון בעבור שנשאו קול כל העדה (וחטאו) ונסלח להם בעבור תפלת משה אמר וכי תשגו והעד ונסלח להם והוצרך להזכיר משפט מנחות כל עולה וזבח ובסוף והנפש אשר תעשה ביד רמה רמז למעשיהם והזכיר דבר המקושש כי עשה ביד רמה ומרוב חמלת השם על ישראל שם הציצית לזכר שלא יעשה האדם ביד רמה או שלא ישכח:


WHEN YE ARE COME INTO THE LAND OF YOUR HABITATIONS. This chapter follows [the one dealing with the spies] in order to console the children by informing them that they would enter the land, for the Israelites grew weak and mourned.¹ However, the real reason that this chapter follows the one dealing with the spies, is because all the congregation shouted and sinned.² God forgave their sin because of the prayer of Moses.³ Scripture says, *And when ye shall err* (15:22).⁴ *And they shall be forgiven* (15:25) is proof of this.⁵ Scripture found it necessary to mention the law of the meal offering and of all burnt offerings and sacrifices.⁶ At the end,⁷ it states, *But the soul that doeth aught with a high hand* (v. 30). The latter alludes to Israel’s deeds.⁸ Scripture mentions the gatherer of wood (verses 32-34)⁹ because he acted with a high hand. God designated the fringes as a sign¹⁰ because of His great pity on Israel, so that a person will not act with a high hand or¹¹ forget.¹² issues. Thus in regard to the law concerning fringes, I.E. notes it is possible that Biblical rules require that the corners of garments be left unwoven so that their threads hang down to form fringes. He further notes that the law of fringes applies to pants. However, he then goes on to say that this interpretation is of no consequence, for the rabbis say that the fringes are specially prepared threads which are placed on the corner of any garment having four corners. Therefore pants are not included in the law of fringes.

footnotes:
¹ See Num. 14:39. This interpretation is found in Rashi.
² Num. 14:1-4.
³ See Num. 14:13-21.
⁴ Which is precisely what Israel had just done.
⁵ The theme connecting the chapters is atonement for sin.
⁶ Since Scripture mentioned the sin offering and its accompanying meal and drink offering, it felt compelled to mention the same regarding the burnt and other offerings.
⁷ After mentioning the various offerings.
⁸ The incident of the spies.
⁹ After the sin offering.
¹⁰ Of the commandments.
¹¹ Deliberately violate God’s commandments.
¹² Any of God’s commands. While I.E. maintains an independent position regarding Biblical interpretations, he always defers to the sages of the Talmud on halakhic



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:3:1)


**לפלא.** לפרש וכן והפלה ה' ואם הוא בה"א כי אותיות אהו"י מתחלפות:


CLEARLY UTTERED. *Le-fale* (clearly uttered) means to separate.¹³ (in fulfillment of a vow clearly uttered) is to be rendered, in fulfillment of a vow set aside. Similarly the word *ve-hiflah* (shall make a division) in *And the Lord shall make a division* (Ex. 9:4). This is so even though *ve-hiflah* is spelled with a *heh*,¹⁴ is spelled with an *alef*. for the letters *alef, heh, vav, yod* interchange.9.¹⁵

¹⁴ And le-fale

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:11:1)


**שה כבשים.** או עזים וכן פירש בסוף לכבש האחד או אם הקריב איל תהא מנחתו כפלים כי הכבש הוא קטן. ולא הזכיר עם בן הבקר נדבה והזכיר שלמים בעבור שלא הזכירו בתחלה ומשפט הכל אחד:


THE HE-LAMBS. Or the kids.¹⁷ Furthermore, I.E. goes on to say, “Scripture similarly states at the end, *for each lamb*.” We do not find the phrase *for each lamb* in any of the following verses. Filwarg suggests that the heading verse 11 is an error and should be eliminated and that our note is a continuation of the previous one. It should read as follows:
[Verse 4.] A MEAL-OFFERING OF…FINE FLOUR. A tenth of an ephah for each one of the flock, (that is) he-lambs or kids. Scripture similarly states at the end (of the verse), *for each lamb* (v. 5). See note 15. Scripture similarly states later, *for each lamb*.¹⁸ If the celebrant offers a ram, then his meal offering is doubled,¹⁹ for a lamb is a small animal. Scripture does not mention the free-will offering²⁰. along with the bullock.²¹ It mentions the peace offering²². [with the bullock] because it did not mention it above.²³ They all have one law.²⁴ (vow) mentioned in verses 3 and 8 and the *nedavah* (free-will offering) mentioned in verse 3 all have one law. They are considered as *shelamim* (peace offerings). See Lev. 7:16-20 (Weiser).

footnotes:
¹⁷ This comment as it stands appears very difficult. First of all, what is I.E. adding to the Scriptural text, which reads: for each of the he-lambs, or of the kids?
¹⁸ We thus see that the term flock refers to lambs (and by extension also to kids).
¹⁹ Verse 6.
²⁰ Hebrew, nedavah
²¹ In verse 8. It does so in verse 3. Hence I.E.’s comment.
²² Hebrew, shelamim
²³ In verse 3.
²⁴ The neder

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:14:1

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:15:1)


**הקהל.** יש אומרים כי זה הה"א הוא לקריאה ולפי דעתי שאין סימן קריאה בלשון הקדש והעד הארבעה והאחד מהם משה משה רק זה הה"א הוא ה"א הדעת וסימן הקריאה איננו:


AS FOR THE CONGREGATION. Some say that the *heh* prefixed to the word *kahal* (congregation) is the sign of the vocative.²⁶), there shall be one statute both for you, and for the stranger. However, I believe that there is no vocative letter in the holy tongue. The four instances²⁷ are proof of this.²⁸, otherwise why repeat the common noun. One of them is *Moses, Moses*.²⁹ as an example because he is expounding the Book of Numbers, and Moses is the central human character. On the contrary, this *heh*³⁰ prefixed to *kahal*. is the sign of the definite object.³¹ is, the congregation. It is not a sign of the vocative.

footnotes:
²⁶ According to this interpretation our verse reads: O congregation (ha-kahal
²⁷ Where a common noun is repeated. They are: Moses, Moses (Ex. 3:4); Abraham, Abraham (Gen. 22:11); Jacob, Jacob (Gen. 46:2); Samuel, Samuel (I Sam. 3:1).
²⁸ That there is no vocative heh
²⁹ Ex. 3:4. I.E. probably quotes Moses, Moses
³⁰ The heh
³¹ Thus the meaning of ha-kahal


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:15:2)


**ככם כגר.** כבר ביארתי משפט הכפי"ן בהתחברם:


AS YE ARE, SO SHALL THE STRANGER BE. I have already explained the rule governing two *kafs* joined together.³² are affixed to two words following each other it indicates an abridged clause. Thus our verse, which states *ka-khem ka-ger* (as you are, so shall the stranger be) is to be interpreted: as you are, so shall the stranger be; as the stranger is, so shall you be. See I.E. on Gen. 44:18 (Vol. 1, p. 396).

footnotes:
³² According to I.E. when two kafs



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:16:1)


וטעם **תורה אחת.** לבד מהעולות כי העולות הם בחיוב חקה ועוד אמר השם כאשר תביאו מנחה על העולה מהסלת כן תתנו גם מעריסותיכם ואין צורך להזכיר מה שקבלו קדמונינו ז"ל כי הם שתים חלה ותרומה ושיעורן לפי שכל דבריהם אמת וזולתם הבל הנה שעור נוסף בין שעשו בשגגה מה שצוו שלא לעשות ובין שלא עשו בשגגה מה שצוו לעשות:


[ONE LAW.] Its meaning is, one law aside from the burnt offerings, for the burnt offerings are obligated by statute.³³ (statute) refers to. Verse 15 states, *there shall be one statute* (chukkah) *both for you, and for the stranger that sojourneth with you*. Verse 16 states, *One law* (Torah) *and one ordinance shall be both for you, and for the stranger. Prima facie*, Scripture is redundant. Hence I.E. comments that verse 15 deals with that which is obligatory, namely, the burnt offering (for it concludes the law mentioned in verse 14, which deals with sacrifices), and verse 16 deals with all the other commandments, for it speaks of Torah (Filwarg). God also says,³⁴ as you bring a meal offering of fine flour along with the burnt offering, thus shall you do with your dough.³⁵ follows the law of the meal offering. There is no need to mention what our ancients, of blessed memory, received by tradition, namely, that one is obligated to set aside two offerings, a portion of the dough³⁶. and a portion of the grain,³⁷. In other words, one has to set aside a portion from the dough, even though he has already set aside a portion from the grain out of which this very dough was made (Filwarg). and their amount.³⁸ Their words are true. Everything else is nonsense.

footnotes:
³³ For that is what the word chukkah
³⁴ In verses 19-21.
³⁵ One must set aside a portion of one’s dough as a gift unto God. Hence the law of hallah
³⁶ Hallah
³⁷ Terumah
³⁸ How much one has to set aside.

[ONE HE-GOAT.] Scripture adds a he-goat.³⁹ The congregation must bring a sin offering whether they erred by doing something they were prohibited from doing or erred in not doing something they were obligated to do.

### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:25:1)


**וחטאתם.** היא השעיר ובעבור שהוא סמוך אל עזים הוא קטן והעתיד הפך:


AND THEIR SIN-OFFERING. The reference is to the he-goat.⁴⁰ refers to the bullock, *and their sin-offering before the Lord* refers to the he-goat. A *sei’r izzim* (he-goat) refers to a young goat because the word *se’ir* (he-goat) is connected to *izzim* (goats). The reverse is the case with the term *atud* (he-goat).⁴¹. 38. An *atud* is a mature goat. Some texts read *atid* (the future) in place of *atud* (he-goat). However, the latter is practically impossible to explain. See Filwarg.

### Verse 39

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:39:1)


**והיה לכם לציצת.** והנה ישוב הפתיל להיותו בקצה כמו הציצית. והפירוש השני כאשר העתיקו חז"ל ובעבור שיש עדים נאמנים על הפירוש השני בטל הראשון והם העתיקו כי זאת המצוה עם בגד שיש לו ארבע כנפים והציצית הם הגדילים ועוד אפרשנו. והנה מצוה על כל מי שיש לו בגד בארבע כנפים שיתכסה בו ביום תמיד ולא יסירנו מעליו למען יזכרו והמתפללים בטלית בשעת התפלה יעשו זה בעבור שיקראו בקריאת שמע והיה לכם לציצית ועשו להם ציצית רק לפי דעתי יותר הוא חייב להתעטף בציצית בשאר השעות משעת התפלה למען יזכור ולא ישגה ולא יעשה עבירה בכל שעה כי בשעת התפלה לא יעשה עבירה:


AND IT SHALL BE UNTO YOU FOR A FRINGE. The thread at the end was like the fringe.⁶⁸ The other interpretation is the one which the rabbis of blessed memory transmitted.⁶⁹ Now the first interpretation is nullified in view of the fact that there are true witnesses⁷⁰ to the second explanation. The rabbis transmitted to us the law that this commandment applies to a garment that has four corners and that the tzitzit are fringes.⁷¹ I will explain this law later.⁷² Now this commandment states that everyone who has a four-cornered garment shall always cover himself with it during the day. He shall not remove it so that he remembers⁷³. 38. *Mikro’ot Gedolot* reads, so that they remember. the commandments. Those who pray with a *tallit* during the time of prayers do so because they read in the *shema*,⁷⁴ is part of the morning and evening prayers. It consists of Deut. 6:4-9; Deut. 11:13-21; Num. 15:37-41. *And it shall be unto you for a fringe* (v. 39) and *that they make them throughout their generations fringes* (v. 38). However, I believe that one is more obligated to enwrap oneself in fringes when he is not at prayer than during the time of prayer, so that he remembers the commandments and does not err and trespass during the other hours of the day, for in the hour of prayer he will not sin.⁷⁵, p. 79.

⁶⁸ The blue thread that was placed on the edge of the garment was to resemble one of the loose hanging threads.
⁶⁹ That the fringes are not part of the garment but are attached to the corners of the garment.
⁷⁰ Jewish practice throughout the ages.
⁷¹ Hanging from but not part of the garment.
⁷² The reference apparently is to I.E.’s commentary on Deut. 22:11.
⁷³ So Vat. Ebr
⁷⁴ The shema
⁷⁵ “Now, the person who envelops himself in a tallit [during prayer] does not fully observe the commandment. In reality, one is obligated to wear fringes the entire day, for when a person is in the market and his eyes wander, his heart lusts after what his eyes see much more than it does during prayer.” See The Secret of the Torah

AFTER YOUR OWN HEART. Which lusts. The eye sees and the heart desires. The fringes thus serve as a sign and a mark that a person should not pursue the thoughts of his heart and all that his eyes desire.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 15:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/15:40:1)


וטעם **למען תזכרו.** וכבר אמר וזכרתם את כל מצות ה'. שאם תזכרו תהיו קדושים ולא תתגאלו בתאות הלב המטנפת הרוח המשכלת:


THAT YE MAY REMEMBER. Scripture has previously stated, *and remember all the commandments of the Lord* (v. 39).⁷⁶. The meaning of our verse is: if you remember the commandments of God, you will be holy and you will not defile yourselves with the lusts of the heart which defile the rational soul.⁷⁷

## Chapter 16


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:1:1)


**ויקח קרח.** זה הדבר היה במדבר סיני כאשר נתחלפו הבכורים ונבדלו הלוים כי חשבו ישראל שמשה אדונינו עשה מדעתו לתת גדולה לאחיו גם לבני קהת שהם קרובים אליו ולכל בני לוי שהם ממשפחתו והלוים קשרו עליו בעבור היותם נתונים לאהרן ולבניו וקשר דתן ואבירם בעבור שהסיר הבכורה מראובן אביהם ונתנה ליוסף אולי חשדוהו בעבור יהושע משרתו גם קרח בכור היה כי כן כתוב. ודגל ראובן חונה בנגב וקרח בנגב המשכן כי הוא מבני קהת ואלה נשיאי העדה היו בכורים והם היו מקריבים את העולות על כן לקחו מחתות והראיה על זה הפירוש מופת המטה שראו כל ישראל כי השם בחר שבט לוי תחת הבכורים על כן כתוב ותכל תלונתם כי התלונה על זה היתה. גם אמר משה כי לא מלבי בעבור שחשדוהו כי מלבו עשה ועוד לא ה' שלחני בשליחות הזה כי כבר האמינו בו כל ישראל ועוד ראיה גמורה כי כל העדה כלם קדושים וזה רמז לבכורים שהם קדושים כי כן כתוב קדש לי כל בכור והם היו הכהנים הנגשים אל ה' והם עיקר כל העדה:


NOW KORAH, THE SON OF IZHAR…TOOK. This event occurred in the wilderness of Sinai when the first-born were set aside¹ and the Levites were placed in their stead.² Israel thought that our master Moses acted out of his own will in giving his brother an elevated position. They suspected that he acted similarly towards the sons of Kohath, who were his relatives, and to all the sons of Levi who were of his family. The Levites rebelled against Moses because they were subservient to Aaron and his sons. Dathan and Abiram rebelled because Moses³ removed the birthright from Reuben their forefather, and gave it to Joseph. It is also possible that they suspected that Moses favored the tribe of Joseph because Joshua was his assistant.⁴ Korah too was a first-born, as Scripture clearly states.⁵ The banner of Reuben camped in the south.⁶ So did Korah, for he was a Kohathite.⁷ The princes of the congregation were first-born. They offered the burnt offering. They therefore took the fire pans. The miracle of the staff,⁸ which demonstrated to all of Israel that God chose the Levites in place of the first-born, is proof that this explanation is correct.⁹ Hence Scripture reads, *that there may be made an end of their murmurings against Me* (Num. 17:25), for the murmuring was an account of this.¹⁰ Moses also said, *that I have not done them of mine own mind* (v. 28). He said the aforementioned because Israel had come to believe that he had acted on his own. Furthermore, Moses said, *then the Lord hath not sent me* (v. 29) in this thing,¹¹ take the place of the first-born and the Levites be subservient to the *kohanim*. because Israel had come to believe in him.¹². It does not appear in the verse. Here is another rigorous proof: *seeing all the congregation are holy* (v. 3). The aforementioned alludes to the first-born who are holy, for Scripture states, *Sanctify unto Me all the first-born* (Ex. 13:2). The first-born were the priests *that come near to the Lord* (Ex. 19:22). They were the most important ones of the congregation.

footnotes:
¹ From serving as priests.
² See I.E. on Ex. 32:29 (Vol. 1, p. 680).
³ Actually Jacob did so on his death bed. However, Moses enforced Jacob’s decree. Hence Dathan and Abiram blamed Moses.
⁴ Joshua was descended from Ephraim the son of Joseph.
⁵ Ex. 6:21.
⁶ Num. 2:10.
⁷ The family of Kohath camped south of the tabernacle. See Num. 3:29. Thus these two disgruntled factions camped next to each other. Their proximity facilitated their rebellion.
⁸ The blossoming of the staff of Levi. See Num. 17:23.

¹⁰ The substitution of the Levites for the first-born.
¹¹ To have the kohanim
¹² That Moses acted in accordance with God’s command in all other things. “In this thing” is part of I.E.’s interpretation of the Lord hath not sent me


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:1:2)


**ויקח קרח.** אנשים דרך קצרה כמו חמור לחם ורבים ככה ויאמר ר' יונה כי פירוש ויקח לקח לקום על משהו:


NOW KORAH…TOOK. This is short for, now Korah took men.¹³ Compare, *an ass bread*¹⁴ (I Sam. 16:20).¹⁵ is short for an ass carrying bread. There are many such instances in Scripture.¹⁶ Rabbi Jonah says that the meaning of *Now Korah took* is now Korah took himself to rise against Moses.¹⁷ (v. 1) to *and they rose up* (v. 2).

### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:2:1)


**לפני משה.** שלא קמו בסתר ויש הפרש בין לפני ובין מפני:


IN THE FACE OF MOSES. They did not rise up in secret. There is a difference between *li-fene* (in the face of) and *mi-pene* (before the face of) (Gen. 3:8).¹⁸ is the respectful form. Compare, *mi-pene sevah takkum* (thou that rise up before the hoary head) (Lev. 19:32).

¹⁸ Mi-pene

THE ELECT MEN OF THE ASSEMBLY. They were called to the tent of meeting.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:2:3)


**אנשי שם.** קודם צאתם ממצרים ומדקדק אמר כי נשיאי עדה הם הנשיאים העומדים על הפקודים והם פעולים למלת לקח כאילו כתיב ויקח קרח ודתן ואבירם ואון נשיאי העדה לדבר אליהם וזה מעט רחוק:


MEN OF RENOWN. Before they left Egypt.A grammarian says that *the princes of the congregation* are the leaders who stood over those that were numbered (Num 1:4).¹⁹ He says that they²⁰ are the object of took. Our text should be read as if written, And Korah and Dathan and Abiram and On took the princes of Israel to speak to them.²¹ This interpretation is a bit far-fetched.²²

footnotes:
¹⁹ This grammarian, unlike I.E., does not identify the princes of the congregation with the first-born.
²⁰ The princes.
²¹ To convince them to join the revolt. This commentator reads our text as follows: Now Korah…and Dathan and Abiram…and On…and two hundred and fifty took the princes of the congregation, the elect men of the assembly, men of renown.
²² The interpretation that the princes were the heads who stood over Israel when they were numbered.

AND THE LORD IS AMONG THEM. For the Levites were chosen after God’s glory dwelt amidst the children of Israel.²⁴ Now if Moses knew when he was on Mount Sinai that the tribe of Levi was chosen, Israel did not know.²⁵

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:7:1)


**רב לכם בני לוי.** וזה פירושו שאמר להם המעט ואמר הכתוב ויאמר משה אל קרח כי הדבור הראשון עם קרח ועם כל עדתו וזה עם הלוים:


YE TAKE TOO MUCH UPON YOU, YE SONS OF LEVI. The meaning of our clause is found in the following words of Moses: *Is it but a small thing unto you* (v. 9).²⁸. Scripture says, *And Moses said unto Korah* (v. 8),²⁹ [Moses] *spoke unto Korah*. Thus *And Moses said unto Korah* appears redundant. Hence I.E.’s comment. for the first statement³⁰ was addressed to Korah and to all of his company. However, this one was addressed to the Levites.

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:11:1)


**לכן.** על כן קשרתם אתה וכל עדתך שאתם נועדים על השם והטעם כמו ורוזנים נוסדו יחד על ה' ועל משיחו שעשו וועד להריב עם השם וכן בהצותם על ה' וי"א כי ה"א הנועדים נוסף:


THEREFORE. Therefore you and your whole congregation that art gathered together, rebel against the Lord.³³ means against the Lord, and is to be interpreted as rebel against the Lord. The literal meaning of *al Adonai* is on the Lord. Thus the literal meaning of our verse is: Therefore you and your whole congregation that are gathered together on the Lord (*al ha-Adonai*). Hence, I.E.’s comment. Its meaning is similar to *And the rulers take counsel together, Against the Lord*,³⁴ (literally on the Lord). However, its meaning is against the Lord. *and against His anointed*³⁵ (literally, and on His anointed). However, its meaning is, *and against His anointed*. (Ps. 2:2). They gathered in order to fight with God. *When they strove against the Lord*³⁶. (Num. 26:9) is similar.³⁷ has the meaning of against. Some say that the *heh* prefixed to *no’adim* (gathered together) is superfluous.³⁸ (that art gathered together) is to be read *no’adim* (gathered together). If the *heh* is not superfluous, then our verse is to be interpreted, *Therefore thou and all thy company that are gathered together* (ha-no’adim) *against the Lord*. If the *heh* is superfluous, then our verse reads: Therefore thou and all thy company are gathered (*no’adim*) together against the Lord.

footnotes:
³³ In other words, al Adonai
³⁴ Hebrew, al Adonai
³⁵ Hebrew, ve-al meshicho
³⁶ Hebrew, al Adonai
³⁷ Here too al
³⁸ In other words, the word ha-no’adim

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:12:1)


**לא נעלה.** יתכן שהיה אהל מועד בתוך המחנה במקום גבוה על כן מלת העלו מסביב או מי שילך לעבודת השם או אל המקום הנבחר יקרא עולה:


WE WILL NOT COME UP. It is possible that the tent of meeting was on a high place in the middle of the camp.³⁹ is to be taken literally. Scripture therefore says, *Get you up from about* (v. 24). On the other hand, one who goes to serve God or to the chosen place is said to go up.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:13:1)


**המעט.** הדבר קל כי העליתנו מארץ זבת חלב ודבש כי מצרים בנגב ארץ ישראל והבא ממצרים לארץ כנען באמת הוא עולה:


IS IT A SMALL THING. Is it a small thing that thou hast brought us up out of a land flowing with milk and honey?Scripture reads, *brought us up*, because Egypt is south of the Land of Israel, and one who comes from Egypt to the land of Canaan is in truth going up.⁴⁰ is to be taken literally.

BUT THOU MUST NEEDS MAKE THYSELF ALSO A PRINCE OVER US. You brought us out of Egypt in order to make yourself a prince over us.

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:14:1)


**אף לא אל ארץ זבת חלב ודבש.** והטעם שהוצאתנו ממקום טוב ואילו הביאותנו למקום טוב כמוהו ונתת לנו שדה וכרם כאשר היה לנו במצרים ותבקש שררה היינו סובלים רק הסירותנו ממקום טוב משדותינו וכרמנו ולא הביאותנו אל מקום כמוהו ולמה תבקש שררה:


MOREOVER THOU HAST NOT BROUGHT US UNTO A LAND FLOWING WITH MILK AND HONEY. Its meaning is: You brought us out of a good place.⁴² Now if you had brought us to a place which is as good as the place you took us out of, and if you had given us fields and vineyards such as we had in Egypt,⁴³ and you sought power over us, then we would tolerate it. However, you removed us from a good place, from our fields and vineyards, and you did not bring us to a place as good as the one that you brought us out of. Why then do you seek power?⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴² Verse 13.
⁴³ According to I.E. our verse implies that Israel had fields and vineyards in Egypt.
⁴⁴ Verse 13.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:14:2)


**העיני האנשים ההם.** טעמו תרצה לנקר עיני האנשים ההם רמז ליוצאים ממצרים כאילו אמר תרצה לנקר העינים שלא תראינה כי זה שעשית לנו נראה הוא לעינים ודרך משל לומר סגורות הן עיני פלוני על כן לא יראה על כן לא נעלה ויש אומרים התרצה להחשיך העינים שלא תראינה כלומר אחיזת עינים אתה עושה לנו לא נעלה ויש אומרים אילו היית מנקר עינינו לא נעלה ואמר האנשים ההם על דרך בני אדם חסרי דעת שלא ירצו לדבר על נפשם בגנאי והנכון בעיני שמלת האנשים ההם רמז לזקנים שהיו עם משה כי כן כתוב וילכו אחריו זקני ישראל:


WILT THOU PUT OUT THE EYES OF THESE MEN? Its meaning is: you want to put out the eyes of these men. This alludes to those who left Egypt. It is as if Korah said, you want to put out their eyes so that they do not see. But what you did is visible to the eyes. Therefore we will not come up. It is metaphoric to say so and so’s eyes are closed and he therefore cannot see.⁴⁵ is not to be taken literally. Others say its meaning⁴⁶? is: Do you want to darken the eyes so that they do not see? In other words, you are fooling us. We will not come up.⁴⁷, this interpretation seems to be identical to the one above. Hence Filwarg suggests that this interpretation is that *We will not go up* means we will not go up to the Land of Israel. In other words, Korah said you are fooling us, for you have no intention of bringing us to the Land of Israel. Others say its meaning⁴⁸? is: even if you put out our eyes we will not go up. Korah said *of these men* because he spoke in the manner of the unintelligent, who do not want to say anything negative about themselves.⁴⁹ refers to Korah himself. According to this interpretation the meaning of *wilt thou put out the eyes of these men* is, will you put out my eyes? However, I believe that *of these men* alludes to the elders who were with Moses, for Scripture clearly states, *and the elders of Israel followed him* (v. 25).

footnotes:
⁴⁵ Even when the subject’s eyes are open. Similarly, wilt Thou put out the eyes of these men
⁴⁶ The meaning of Wilt thou put out the eyes of these men
⁴⁷ Prima facie
⁴⁸ The meaning of Wilt thou put out the eyes of these men
⁴⁹ In other words, of these men

**ויחר.** זה הדבור כבר פירשתיו כי העולה והמנחה מארכת האף על הרשעים ודתן ואבירם היו אנשים גדולים והקריבו מנחה קודם זה המעשה וזה טעם אל תפן אל מנחתם ופנה אלי כי לא נשאתי חמור אחד מהם:


AND MOSES WAS VERY WROTH. With what Korah said.⁵⁰ (and Moses was very wroth). The latter literally reads, and to Moses it was wroth very much. I.E. suggest that this be interpreted, *va-yichar le-mosheh zeh ha-dibbur me’od* (and Moses was very wroth at this statement).I have already explained that the burnt offering and the meal offering put off the punishment of the wicked. Dathan and Abiram were important men and offered meal offerings before this event. This is the meaning of *Respect not their offering* and turn to me, for I have not placed a burden upon one ass of their asses.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:16:2)


**מחר.** והוא היום שקרא משה לדתן ואבירם ויתכן שקראם קודם והוא הנכון בעבור מלת בקר:


TOMORROW. This occurred on the day that Moses called to Dathan and Abiram.⁵³ It is possible that he called them earlier. The latter appears correct because Scripture reads, *in the morning* (v. 5).⁵⁴

footnotes:
⁵³ See verse 12.
⁵⁴ Verse 5 tells us that Korah and his men were to bring the fire-pans as soon as morning came. This being the case, there would be very little time for Dathan and Abiram to be called.

### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:1)


**ויפלו על פניהם.** להתפלל:


AND THEY FELL UPON THEIR FACES. To pray.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:2)


וטעם **אל.** מורה שיש לו כח לכלותם כרגע והוא אלהי הרוחות והטעם פי' אל כי יוכל לכלותם כי הרוחות בידו הם:


[O GOD.] The term *El* (God) indicates that God has the power to destroy them in a moment.⁵⁷ is mighty. See I.E. on Ex. 3:15. God is *the God of the spirits*. This⁵⁸. explains the term *El*, for God can destroy them because their spirits are in His hand.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ The meaning of El
⁵⁸ The phrase the God of the spirits


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:22:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:22:3)


**האיש אחד יחטא.** הוא קרח שהקהיל על משה ועל אהרן כל העדה וי"א כי טעם אלהי הרוחות כי בידו כח לחפש הרוחות כי הוא אלהיהם והוא ידע כי האחד שהוא קרח חטא לבדו וגם אם החטיא אחרים. והנכון בעיני כי טעם הבדלו מתוך העדה הזאת הם קרח וכל עדתו ואש יצאה ואכלתם וכלתם כרגע:


SHALL ONE MAN SIN. The reference⁵⁹. is to *Korah*, who assembled all of the congregation against Moses and Aaron. There are those who say that the meaning of *O God, the God of the spirits* is: God has the power to investigate human souls, for He is their God. God knows that one man, namely, Korah alone, sinned. He also knows if he [Korah] caused the others to sin.I believe that *Separate yourselves from among this congregation* refers to Korah and his congregation.⁶⁰ A fire went out and devoured and destroyed them in one moment.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ One man
⁶⁰ In other words, the congregation spoken of is Korah’s congregation, not the congregation of Israel.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:24:1)


**למשכן קרח.** הנכון בעיני שהיה לקרח אהל לאדם שלו ולרכושו רחוק ממחנה הלוים והיה עם אהלו אהלי דתן ואבירם כי לא יחנו סביב המשכן כי אם הלוים לבדם ומזה הכתוב נלמוד כי בעל הדגל היה חונה קרוב ממחנה הלוים:


THE DWELLING OF KORAH. I believe that Korah had a tent for his household and his wealth. This tent was far from the camp of the Levites. The tents of Dathan and Abiram were close to Korah’s tent, for only Levites camped around the tabernacle.⁶¹ We learn from this verse that the bearer of the banner⁶² camped close to the camp of the Levites.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶¹ Hence the need for the interpretation that Korah had a tent outside the camp of the Levites.
⁶² The tribe that carried the banner. For the banners, see Num. 2:1-34.
⁶³ Korah’s tent was close to the tent of Dathan and Abiram. The Levites camped around the southern, western, and northern sides of the tabernacle (Num. 3:14-38). The Israelites camped around the four sides of the tabernacle (Num. 2:2). The family of Kohath camped on the south side (Num. 3:29). So did the tribe of Reuben (Num. 2:10). Now Korah’s tent was close to the tent of Dathan and Abiram, who came from the tribe of Reuben, because the tribe of Reuben bore the banner of the tribes that camped on the south and were thus close to the Levites.

### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:28:1)


**לעשות את כל המעשים.** להחליף הבכורים בלוים ודע כי דבר גדול היה ומכעיס לאשר איננו מאמין להסיר הבכורים מכהונתם ולהשיב המעלה למשפחת משה לבדו וכל זה הי' בעבור מעשה העגל כי ויעלו עולות ויגשו שלמים הבכורים העלום כי הם היו לבדם הכהנים ובני לוי הרגו עובדי העגל ויש בכאן שאלה ותשובתם בפרשת וילך משה:


TO DO ALL THESE WORKS. To have the Levites take the place of the first-born. Note, it is a major and grievous act to the one who does not believe that God ordered the first-born to be removed from their position as *kohanim* and to have this status conferred only upon the family of Moses. All of this was done⁶⁶ because of the incident of the golden calf, because *and offered burnt-offerings, and brought peace-offerings* (Ex. 32:6) speaks of the first-born who offered these sacrifices, for they alone were the *kohanim*. Also, the sons of Levi⁶⁷ killed those who served the calf.⁶⁸. There is a question here.⁶⁹ Its answer is found in the Torah portion which opens with the words *va-yelekh mosheh* (Deut. 31:1).⁷⁰.

footnotes:
⁶⁶ The first-born were removed and the Levites substituted in their place.
⁶⁷ Alone.
⁶⁸ The first-born did not join Moses in extirpating the worshippers of the calf. Thus the first-born showed themselves unfit to be kohanim
⁶⁹ It isn’t clear what the question is. I believe it to be the issue of God changing His mind. According to medieval Jewish philosophy, God and His mind are one. Thus God’s mind is immutable. This being the case, how could He change His mind regarding the fitness of the first-born to serve as priests?
⁷⁰ I.E. there explains that God does not change. It is people who do. See I.E. on Deut. 31:16. Here I.E. is probably hinting that it wasn’t God who changed His mind but rather the first-born who changed by becoming unfit to serve as kohanim



### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:30:1)


**בריאה.** י"א שהיא תורה על המצא מה שלא היה וכבר פירשתי שאין המלה רק מגזרת וברא אתהן וכבר נבקעו מדינות רבות וירדו הדרים בהן שאולה והנה פירושה כטעם גזרה:


BUT IF THE LORD MAKE A NEW THING. Some say that the word *beri’ah* (a new thing) refers to the bringing forth of something that was never in existence.I have already explained that the word *beri’ah* is related to the word *u-vara* (hack) in *and hack them* (Ezek. 23:47).⁷¹ Many cities⁷². have been split open, and those who dwelt in them have gone down to the pit.⁷³ The meaning of *bara* is to cut.⁷⁴ (But if the Lord make a new thing) should be interpreted, but if God shall certainly split. An alternative interpretation of I.E. is: The meaning of *bara* is to decree. According to this interpretation I.E. renders *ve-im beri’ah yivra Adonai* (but if the Lord make a new thing) as, but if God shall decree.

footnotes:
⁷¹ See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, pp. 22,23).
⁷² Or countries. The text reads medinot
⁷³ The reference is apparently to earthquakes.
⁷⁴ Thus according to I.E., ve-im beri’ah yivra Adonai


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:30:2)


**ופצתה האדמה את פיה.** כמו ופתחה וכן יפצה פיהו וכבר רמזתי בעבור היות נשמת האדם העליונה תיכונה תדבר על הגבוהים ממנה כדרך ארמונה שהוא הגוף וכן על השפלים ממנה והטעם להבין השומעים:


AND THE GROUND OPEN HER MOUTH. The word *u-fatzetah* means and opened. Compare, *yiftzeh* (doth open) in *doth open his mouth* (Job 35:16).I have already hinted⁷⁵ that man’s highest soul,⁷⁶, which is a feminine noun. which comes from the intermediary world,⁷⁷ employs human language when speaking of things above her,⁷⁸ for the body is its palace.⁷⁹ It similarly employs human language when speaking of things below her.⁸⁰. It does so in order that people understand her.

footnotes:
⁷⁵ See I.E. on Gen. 1:26 (Vol. 1, p. 45).
⁷⁶ The rational soul. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 25). I.E. believes that man has three souls, a vegetable, an animal and a rational soul. I.E. refers to the soul as “her” for the Hebrew word for soul is neshamah
⁷⁷ For the three worlds, see I.E. on Ex. 3:15 (Vol. 2, pp. 86-90).
⁷⁸ Such as when speaking of God.
⁷⁹ Here on earth. Hence it must employ corporeal metaphors. It is philosophically significant that I.E. refers to the body as a palace for the soul.
⁸⁰ Such as the earth. Hence Scripture reads, and the ground open her mouth

**ואת בתיהם.** שם כלל לנשיהם ובניהם וטפם:


AND THEIR HOUSEHOLDS. This is a general term for their wives, [grown] children, and their little ones.

### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:1)


**וירדו הם.** הטעם כאשר ירדו נסתמה הארץ ולא נראו:


SO THEY…WENT DOWN. Its meaning is: when they went down, the earth closed and they were not seen.⁸³, this verse repeats verse 32. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁸³ Prima facie


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:2)


**ותכס עליהם.** הארץ בעצמה:


AND THE EARTH CLOSED UPON THEM. The earth itself.⁸⁴ (and closed upon then) literally means and covered. Our clause thus reads, and the earth covered them. Hence *va-yekhas* may be taken as transitive. In this case, it means that the earth covered them with something. Hence I.E. points out that *va-yekhas* is intransitive.

footnotes:
⁸⁴ The verb va-tekhas


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:33:3)


**ויאבדו.** בעבור מות בניהם העומדים תחתם:


AND THEY PERISHED. For their children who were to take their place died.⁸⁵

footnotes:
⁸⁵ One who dies without descendants perishes from the face of the earth.



### Verse 34

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:34:1)


**לקולם.** בעבור קולם:


AT THE CRY OF THEM. Because of their cry.⁸⁶ prefixed to *kolam* (their voice) means because of. The *lamed* usually means to.

### Verse 35

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 16:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/16:35:1)


ה"א **החמשים** מושכת עצמה ואחרת עמה. י"א כי קרח מהנבלעים היה והראיה ותבלע אותם ואת קרח וי"א כי נשרף היה והעד ואת קרח במות העדה באכול האש וחז"ל אמרו כי נשרף ונבלע ולפי דעתי שלא נבקע רק מקום דתן ואבירם כי לא נזכר וקרח היה עומד עם מחתתו עם אהרן ועם נשיאי העדה מקריבי הקטרה ופירוש הפסוק ואת קרח איננה דבק עם ותבלע אותם רק עם במות העדה ואם יטעון טוען ויאמר למה לא הזכיר קרח עם השרופים התשובה כי אחר שהזכיר הכתוב שנשרפה עדתו שבאה לעזרה אין צורך להזכיר קרח כי מקל וחומר יודע הדבר וכמוהו מרכבות פרעה וחילו ולא הזכיר בשירה פרעה שטבע ובאמת טבע כי כן כתוב ונער פרעה וחילו והעד הנאמן כי קרח היה מהשרופים ולא יהיה כקרח וכעדתו:


[AND FIFTY MEN.] The *heh* of *ha-chamishim* (the fifty) is to be read as if written twice.⁸⁷ (and…fifty men). Our text, which reads *ha-chamishim u-matayim*, should be read as if written, *ha-chamishim ve-ha-matayim* (and…the two hundred and fifty men). The latter is the correct grammatical construction. Hence I.E.’s comment. Some say that Korah was among the ones who were swallowed. They offer as proof, *And the earth opened her mouth, and swallowed them up together with Korah* (Num. 26:10). Others say that Korah was burned. They offer, *with Korah, when that company died; what time the fire devoured* (*Ibid*.) as proof. Our sages say that Korah was burned and swallowed.⁸⁸I believe that only the place of Dathan and Abiram split open,⁸⁹ for no other place is mentioned.⁹⁰ Korah was standing with his fire-pan with Aaron and the leaders of the congregation who were offering incense.⁹¹ *With Korah* (Num. 26:10) is not connected to *and swallowed them up* (*Ibid*.) but rather to *when that company died* (*Ibid*.). This is the way the aforementioned verse is to be interpreted. If someone should argue and say, “Why isn’t Korah mentioned among those who were burned?” the answer is: once Scripture mentions that Korah’s company, which came to his aid, was burned, it is not necessary to mention Korah. For we know this by arguing from a minor to a major premise.⁹² *Pharaoh’s chariots and his host hath He cast into the sea* (Ex. 15:4) is similar.⁹³ Moses does not mention in the song⁹⁴ is stated. that Pharaoh drowned. The truth is that Pharaoh drowned, for Scripture explicitly states, *But overthrew Pharaoh and his host in the Red Sea* (Ps. 136:15). The verse *that he fare not as Korah, and as his company* (Num. 17:5) is proof that Korah was among those who were burned.⁹⁵

footnotes:
⁸⁷ It is also to be placed in front of the word u-matayim
⁸⁸ San. 110:a.
⁸⁹ Thus it was only Dathan and Abiram and their families who were swallowed by the ground.
⁹⁰ Scripture does not mention that any other place split open.
⁹¹ And were consumed by fire.
⁹² If Korah’s followers were burned, then Korah certainly was.
⁹³ In that by employing a similar argument, we learn from this verse that Pharaoh died.
⁹⁴ The song of Moses wherein Pharaoh’s chariots and his host hath He cast into the sea
⁹⁵ For the term company refers to the 250 men who were consumed by fire.

BEATEN PLATES. Compare, *And they did beat* (ve-yerakke’u) *the gold into thin plates* (Ex. 39:3).⁴ (beaten) in our verse is similar in meaning to *va-yerakke’u* (and they did beat).

⁴ In other words, the word reku’e


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:3:4)


ופי' **כי קדשו** כי הקריבום לפני ה':


[FOR THEY ARE BECOME HOLY.] *Because they were offered before the Lord* explains *for they are become holy*.⁵ they are become holy. I.E. believes this should be interpreted as if written, for they are become holy *because* they were offered before the Lord.

footnotes:
⁵ Our verse literally reads, because they were offered before the Lord and


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:3:5)


**ויהיו לאות.** לזכר ולסימן:


THAT THEY MAY BE A SIGN. A memorial and a mark.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ See Rashi, “For a memorial, that people will always say: These plates were from those who disputed the priesthood and were burnt.”



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:4:1)


**וירקעום.** המרקעים:


AND THEY BEAT THEM OUT. The beaters beat them out.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ The subject is omitted. Hence I.E.’s comment. See I.E. on verse 3.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:5:1)


**כאשר דבר ה' ביד משה לו.** לאהרן וי"א כי לו שב אל קרח והוא רחוק בעיני:


AS THE LORD SPOKE UNTO HIM BY THE HAND OF MOSES. *Unto him* (lo) refers to Aaron. Others say that *unto him* refers to Korah.⁸. 110a. However, I believe the latter to be farfetched.

footnotes:
⁸ See San



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:6:1)


**ממחרת.** זה היום כי לעולם הוא סמוך:


ON THE MORROW. Of this day, for *mochorat* (morrow) is always connected to the previous day.⁹ (morrow) can refer to a morrow in the far future.

footnotes:
⁹ In other words, it always refers to the next morrow. I.E. comments thus, for the word machar


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:6:2)


**אתם המתם.** הטעם מה ראיה היתה זאת ששבט לוי הוא הנבחר ונבחר אהרן לכהן גדול ויתכן שבתפלתכם או החכמה שידעתם שרפתם המקריבים:


YE HAVE KILLED. Its meaning is, what proof is this¹⁰ that the tribe of Levi is the chosen one, and that Aaron was chosen to be *kohen gadol*?¹¹ It is possible that those who offered the fire-pans were burned because of your prayer¹² or through your knowledge of the sciences.¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ The fact that the 250 men who offered the incense were burned.
¹¹ High priest.
¹² You prayed that they be burned.
¹³ You may have arranged for some combustible material to be mixed with the incense.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:7:1)


**בהקהל העדה.** כל קהלה ואחריה על היא למריבה:


WHEN THE CONGREGATION WAS ASSEMBLED.¹⁴. Whenever the word *kehillah* (assembly)¹⁵. is followed by the word *al* (on),¹⁶ (when the congregation was assembled against Moses and against Aaron). The word *al* usually means on, not against. Hence I.E.’s comment. it refers to an assembly for the sake of quarreling.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Hebrew, be-hikkahel
¹⁵ That is, a word from the root kof, heh, lamed
¹⁶ As it does in our verse, which reads, be-hikkahel ha-edah al mosheh ve-al aaron



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:8:1)


**ויבא משה ואהרן.** בעבור שראו שנראה הכבוד:


AND MOSES AND AARON CAME. Because they saw that God’s glory appeared.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:10:1)


**הרמו.** שישבו במקום המרומם במחנה הלויה והוא האהל כאשר פירשתי במלת העלו:


GET YOU UP. For they sat in the tabernacle, which was on the high ground in the camp of the Levites, as I explained in my comments on *Get you up* (Num. 16:24).¹⁷ (Get you up) as, remove yourself from the high place. He cites Num. 16:24, but he actually made his comments on 16:12.

footnotes:
¹⁷ I.E. apparently renders herommu


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:10:2)


**ויפלו על פניהם.** להתפלל:


AND THEY FELL UPON THEIR FACES. To pray.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:1)


**את המחתה.** הידועה:


TAKE THY FIRE-PAN. The reference is to a specific fire-pan.¹⁸ (fire-pan) has the definite article *ha* prefixed to it. The literal meaning of *kach et ha-machtah* is, take the fire-pan. According to I.E. *the fire-pan* in our verse means the specific fire-pan used to offer incense (Krinsky).

footnotes:
¹⁸ Hence the word machtah


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:2)


**ושים קטרת.** ולא אמר הקטרת והמשכיל יבין:


AND LAY INCENSE THEREON. Scripture does not say, and lay “the” incense on.¹⁹ (incense), it refers to another incense. The intelligent will understand.²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁹ “The” incense would refer to the incense usually burned in the tabernacle. Since the definite article is not prefaced to ketroret
²⁰ The difference between the two types of incense. Some of the commentators on I.E. believe that he was of the opinion that the incense used by Moses to stop the plague had the power to clean the air of pollutants that caused the plague (Krinsky, Filwarg).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:11:3)


**והולך.** לשון צווי כמו הושב את אביך ושרשו כמו הוליכי וטעמו הובילי כמו ילך עמך:


AND CARRY. The word *holekh* (and carry) is an imperative.²¹ looks like a *kal* participle. In this case the word would mean goes or walks. However, the context negates such a rendering. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is similar to the word *hoshev* (make to dwell)²² imperative. in *make thy father…to dwell* (Gen. 47:6). *Holekh* comes from the same root as *helikhi* (take away) (Ex. 2:9).²³. It means bring,²⁴ means: and bring (and carry) the fire-pan quickly to the congregation. the idea being it²⁵ should go with you.

footnotes:
²¹ In the hifil. Holekh
²² A hifil
²³ Its root is yod, lamed, kaf
²⁴ Ve-holekh meherah el ha-edah
²⁵ The incense-bearing fire-pan.



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:15:1)


וטעם **וישב אהרן.** דבק עם הפרשה הבאה ואם נפסקה וכבר הראיתיך רבות כהנה וכהנה וכאשר שב אהרן ונעצרה המגפה דבר השם:


[AND AARON RETURNED.] *And Aaron returned* is connected to the section that follows,²⁶ even though it is separated.²⁷, verse 16 starts a new section. I have already given you many such examples.²⁸ When Aaron returned and the plague was stayed, God spoke.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁶ Verse 15 is connected to verse 16.
²⁷ According to the masorah
²⁸ Where verses that are in different section are connected. See I.E. on Gen. 1:9.
²⁹ This is the meaning of verses 15 and 16.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:17:1)


**מטה.** י"א כי מטה לוי איננו מטה האלהים כי כבר היה ניכר ומה צורך לכתוב עליו שם אהרן וי"א כי המטה היה כשאר המטות אין הפרש ביניהם וזה הישר בעיני:


RODS. Some say that the rod of Levi is not God’s rod, for God’s rod was already known.³⁰ Why then write Aaron’s name on it.³¹ Others say that the rod³² was like the other rods. There was no difference between them.³³ This interpretation appears correct to me.

footnotes:
³⁰ According to this view the rod of the Lord (the rod employed by Moses to perform miracles in Egypt) was made of different material from that of the other rods.
³¹ If the rod of the tribe of Levi was God’s rod.
³² God’s rod.
³³ According to this view the rod of God was the rod of the tribe of Levi. In this opinion God’s rod was made of the same material as all the other rods. Hence it was necessary to write Aaron’s name on it (Filwarg).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:17:2)


וטעם **לבית אב.** שבטי ישראל כמו ראובן ושמעון:


[ONE FOR EACH FATHER’S HOUSE.] Each father’s house means each tribe. Reuben and Simeon are examples.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ Of tribes and fathers’ houses.



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:18:1)


וטעם **כי מטה אחד.** בעבור שנחלק השבט לכהנים וללוים:


[FOR THERE SHALL BE ONE ROD.] Scripture tells us that there shall be one rod for the tribe of Levi because this tribe was divided into *kohanim* and Levites.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁵ Hence one might think that each of these divisions has a separate rod.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:19:1)


**אשר אועד לכם.** תמיד:


WHERE I MEET WITH YOU. Permanently.³⁶).

footnotes:
³⁶ Scripture uses the future form (ivva’ed



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:20:1)


**והשכותי.** מפעלי הכפל כמו והתמותי (טמאתך) מגזרת וחמת המלך שככה וטעם **והשכותי.** כדרך בני אדם למצוא מנוחה ומטה לוי בתוך השנים עשר והנה יוסף במטה אחד:


I WILL MAKE TO CEASE. The word *ve-hashikkoti* (I will make to cease) comes from a double root.³⁷. It is similar to the word *ve-hatimmoti*³⁸. Both words are *hifils*. (and I will consume) in *and I will consume thy filthiness from out of thee* (Ezek. 22:15). It is related to the word *shakhakhah* (assuaged) in *then was the king’s wrath assuaged* (Esth. 7:10).*Ve-hashikkoti* (I will make to cease) means I [God] will find rest.³⁹ Scripture employs human language.⁴⁰ The rod of Levi was among the twelve. Behold, the tribe of Joseph was represented by one rod.⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁷ Its root is shin, kaf, kaf
³⁸ From the root tav, mem, mem
³⁹ According to I.E. our clause reads: I will find rest from the murmuring of the children of Israel.
⁴⁰ When a person is greatly bothered he says, I seek rest from those who trouble me.
⁴¹ The tribe of Joseph was divided into Manasseh and Ephraim. These are usually counted as two separate tribes. However, here they are not. Otherwise there would have been thirteen rods.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:23:1)


**לבית לוי.** מטה משרת עצמו ואחר עמו מטה אהרן מטה לבית לוי:


FOR THE HOUSE OF LEVI. The word *matteh* (rod of) is to be read as if written twice. Our verse⁴² is to be read as if written: the rod of Aaron, the rod of the house of Levi.

footnotes:
⁴² Which literally reads, the rod of Aaron of the house of Levi.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:23:2)


**ויגמול.** מגזרת ויגדל הילד ויגמל ורבים אמרו בעבור שקדים כי המטה היה ממין האילן ואינה ראיה ודרך דרש כי שקדים כמו כי שוקד אני:


AND BORE RIPE ALMONDS. The word *va-yigmol* (and bore ripe) is related to the word *va-yigammal* (and was weaned) in *And the child grew, and was weaned* (Gen. 21:8). Many say that the rod⁴³ came from an almond tree because the rod bore almonds. However, this is not proof. The word *shekedim* (almonds) has midrashically been related to the word *shoked* (watch) in I *watch over My word to perform it* (Jer. 1:12).⁴⁴

footnotes:
⁴³ Of the tribe of Levi.
⁴⁴ God (watches) is quick to punish those who oppose the priesthood. See Rashi.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:24:1)


**ויראו.** מכתב יד כל אחד:


AND THEY LOOKED. At the handwriting of each one.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 17:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/17:27:1)


**ויאמרו בני ישראל.** די לנו שגוענו ואבדנו והנה עוד כל הקרב אל המשכן ה' ימות כמות השרופים ומי יתן ותמנו לגוע אז אמר השם לאהרן שהוא הנגיד על שבט לוי להזהירו שלא יכשילו הלוים את ישראל. וטעם לאהרן על יד משה או כאשר הוא כי גם אהרן נביא היה גם זאת הפרשה לאות כי במדבר סיני היתה כאשר הוקם המשכן ונבחרו הלוים ונפסלו הבכורים:


AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL SPOKE UNTO MOSES, SAYING. It is enough for us that we perished and were undone.⁴⁵ Now, in addition, everyone that comes near the tabernacle will die like those who were burnt. O that we should cease dying.⁴⁶ God then spoke to Aaron, who was the leader of the tribe of Levi, and cautioned him that the Levites not cause the Israelites to stumble.⁴⁷ The meaning of *unto Aaron* (Num. 18:1) is that God communicated with Aaron through Moses. On the other hand, the clause may be taken at face value,⁴⁸ because Aaron too was a prophet. Also, this section⁴⁹ is proof that the rebellion took place in the desert of Sinai when the tabernacle was erected and the Levites were chosen, and the first-born were disqualified.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁴⁵ I.E.’s paraphrase of verse 27.
⁴⁶ I.E.’s paraphrase of verse 28.
⁴⁷ I.E.’s paraphrase of Num. 18:1.
⁴⁸ That God spoke directly to Aaron.
⁴⁹ In addition to Num. 16. See I.E. on Num. 16:1.
⁵⁰ For the section tells us that the Israelites were convinced that God chose the tribe of Levi only after their rod miraculously produced almonds.



## Chapter 18


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:1)


**את עון המקדש.** שהוא מבית לפרכת והטעם אם לא תשמרוהו העון עליכם:


THE INIQUITY OF THE SANCTUARY. Which is on the inside of the veil.¹ Its meaning is, if you do not guard the sanctuary the sin is upon you.

footnotes:
¹ Which hung in front of the Holy of Holies. See Ex. 26:31-36 and Lev. 16:1-3.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:2)


וטעם **ובית אביך.** כי הם נושאי המקדש:


[AND THY FATHERS’ HOUSE.] For they carried the sanctuary.²

footnotes:
² The children of Kohath carried the tabernacle (Num. 10:21). Kohath was the father of Amram, the father of Aaron.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:1:3)


**את עון כהונתכם.** אם לא תשמרו הכהונה העון עליכם:


THE INIQUITY OF YOUR PRIESTHOOD. If you do not keep the laws of the priesthood, the sin is upon you.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:1)


**שבט אביך.** הוא לוי וחז"ל פירשוהו כמשמעו ולפי דעתי מה שפירשתיו כאילו אמר מטה לוי שהוא שבט אביך:


THE TRIBE OF THY FATHER. The reference is to Levi.³ is to be understood as the tribe of Levi, the tribe of your patriarch Levi. Our sages say that *the tribe of thy father* is to be taken at face value.⁴ However, I believe that it is to be explained in keeping with my earlier interpretation. Our verse is to be read as if written, the tribe of Levi which is the tribe of your father.⁵ is to be read as if written, *matteh levi she-hu shevet avikha*.

footnotes:
³ In other words, the tribe of Levi, the tribe of thy father
⁴ The reference being to Amram.
⁵ Matteh levi shevet avikha


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:2)


**וילוו.** כמו ונספחו וכן ילוה אישי והנה הלוים נלוים בשמם:


THAT THEY MAY BE JOINED. The meaning of *ve-yillavu* is similar to *ve-nispechu* (and they shall join) (Is. 14:1). So too the word *yillaveh* (be joined) in *Now this time will my husband be joined* (Gen. 29:34). Look, the Levites are joined by their names.⁶. Their name indicates that they have joined Aaron. Some editions read, *ke-shemam*. In this case I.E. reads: The Levites are joined [to Aaron] as their names [indicate].

footnotes:
⁶ Hebrew, bi-shemam


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:2:3)


**ואתה ובניך אתך.** תשבו לפני אהל מועד לבדכם ולא הלוים:


THOU AND THY SONS WITH THEE. You and your sons, and not the Levites, shall abide before the tent of meeting.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:3:1)


**גם הם גם אתם.** דרך המקרא כן כמו משל בנו גם אתה גם בנך והטעם כי זה כמו זה:


NEITHER THEY, NOR YE.⁷ (literally, also they, also you). It is the way of Scripture to speak in this manner.⁸). Compare, *Rule thou over us, also you, also thy son*⁹ (Jud. 8:22). That means this one is like this one.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁷ Hebrew, gam hem, gam attem
⁸ To say also…also… (gam… gam…
⁹ Translated literally.
¹⁰ In other words, the same law applies to them and to you.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:4:1)


וטעם **ונלוו עליך.** שלא תצטרכו לזר שהוא ישראל:


[AND THEY SHALL BE JOINED UNTO THEE.] So that you do not need the services of a stranger, namely, an Israelite.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:7:1

AS A SERVICE OF GIFT. And someone who is a common man in regard to the priesthood even if he be a Levite [shall be put to death]. ### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:10:1)


**בקדש הקדשים תאכלנו.** אפי' בקדש הקדשים שהוא אהל מועד ונקרא קדש הקדשים כנגד חצר המשכן:


IN A MOST HOLY PLACE SHALT THOU EAT THEREOF. Even in a most holy place, namely, in the tent of meeting shalt thou eat thereof. The tent of meeting is called a most holy place in contrast to the courtyard of the tabernacle.²⁵

### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:11:1)


**מתנם.** בחסרון תי"ו כמו הולך (נ"א עובר) בשוק אצל פנה שהוא פנתה או הם שני משקלים:


THEIR GIFT. A *tav* is missing in the word *mattanam* (their gift).²⁶. Thus the word for *their gift* should be *mattanatam* rather than *mattanam*. *Mattanam* is similar to the word *pinnah* (corner), which has the meaning of *pinnatah* (her corner) in *Passing*²⁷. Some texts have *holekhat* (going). However, Prov. 7:8 reads *over*. *through the street near her corner* (Prov. 7:8).²⁸ is analogous to *mattanam* in that it is a feminine noun, but it is connected to the pronominal suffix as if it were masculine. On the other hand, we may be dealing with two forms.²⁹ and *mattnah*). *Mattanam* is the word *mattan* plus the pronominal suffix.

footnotes:
²⁶ The usual Hebrew word for gift is mattanah
²⁷ Reading over
²⁸ In other words, pinnah
²⁹ For the word gift (mattan

**חלב יצהר.** הנכבד והמוטב שהוא הקצף כי יצהר מענין צהור ושרשו צהר והוא חלב שמן זית:


ALL THE BEST OF THE OIL. *Chelev* (the best) means the most precious, the best. The reference is to the cream of the oil,³⁰ for the word *yitzhar* (oil) means light.³¹. Others read *nahor*. Both mean light. Its root is *tzadi, heh, resh*.³² The reference is to the fat of the olive oil.

COVENANT OF SALT. A “cut covenant.”⁴² The word *melach* (salt) is related to the word *melechah* (salt)⁴³ in *A fruitful land turned into salt* (Ps. 107:34). A salted place is said to be cut off, for nothing grows there. Compare, *brimstone, and salt*⁴⁴. (Deut. 29:22).

### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:23:1)


**ועבד הלוי הוא.** בעצמו ויש אומרים כי הוא פעול ואיננו נכון כי את עבודת אהל מועד הוא הפעול:


BUT THE LEVITES ALONE SHALL DO THE SERVICE.⁴⁶) shall do the service of the tent of meeting. Hence the interpretations which follow. The word *hu* means himself. Others say that *hu* is the object.⁴⁷ as it. It maintains that *But the Levites he* (hu) *shall do the service of the tent of meeting* should be interpreted: But the Levites shall do the service of it (*hu*), namely, the tent of meeting (Weiser). Filwarg claims that *hu* refers to the *kohen*. In this case, *But the Levites he* (hu) *shall do the service of the tent of meeting* should be interpreted: But the Levites shall do the service of him (the *kohen*) and of the tent of meeting. However, this is incorrect, for the service of the tent of meeting is the object.⁴⁸ (shall do the service).

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:30:1)


וטעם **בהרימכם את חלבו.** וכבר הזכיר הכתוב זה לדבק אחריו ואכלתם אותו והטעם שהוא אסור לכם לאכול ממנו עד שתרימו חלבו ממנו ואחר שיחשב לכם אז תאכלוהו בכל מקום טהור:


[WHEN YE SET APART THE BEST THEREOF.] This has already been mentioned by Scripture.⁵⁴ It is repeated in order to connect it to that which follows. Its meaning is, you⁵⁵ are prohibited to eat of it⁵⁶ until you set apart the best thereof,⁵⁷ and afterwards it⁵⁸ shall be counted [as the grain of the threshing floor] and you⁵⁹ may eat it in every clean place.⁶⁰ as in every clean place. However, according to the *halakha* the Levite may eat the remaining tithe even in a cemetery. See Rashi.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 18:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/18:32:1)


**ולא תשאו עליו חטא.** טעמו כאשר תרימו לא תשאו עליו חטא ובתתכם מקדשו ממנו יהיו קדשי בני ישראל שמורים ואם לא תעשו כן תשאו חטא שעברתם על המצוה וחללתם קדשי בני ישראל בעבור שאכלתם אתם מקדשו:


AND YE SHALL BEAR NO SIN BY REASON OF IT. This means when you set apart the best of it⁶¹ you shall bear no sin through it.⁶² Furthermore, when you give the hallowed part thereof,⁶³. the sacred donations of the children of Israel will be preserved.⁶⁴ However, if you do not offer to tithe, then you will bear a sin, for you will have trespassed the commandment and profaned the holy things of the children of Israel because you ate its holy part.

## Chapter 19


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:2:1)


**זאת חקת.** גם זאת הפרשה במדבר סיני נאמרה כאשר צוה השם וישלחו מן המחנה ובפסח היו טמאי מתים. ונסמכה זאת הפרשה בעבור שהיא לכהן:


THIS IS THE STATUTE. This chapter was also¹ stated at Sinai. It was given when God commanded *that they put out of the camp…whosoever is unclean by the dead* (Num. 5:2). There were also people who were unclean by coming into contact with a dead body when Passover² arrived (Num. 9:6). This chapter is placed here³ because it relates to the *kohen*.⁴ played a major role in the ritual of the red heifer.

footnotes:
¹ Like the incident of Korah. See I.E. on Num 16:1.
² Celebrated by Israel in the second year after the Exodus.
³ Following the laws dealing with the tithes.
⁴ The kohen


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:2:2)


**פרה אדמה תמימה.** שלא תהיה קטנה. אמר הגאון כי מי נדה כמו הדבש שיזיק לבעל המרה האדומה ויועיל לבעל הלחה ואין צורך:


A RED HEIFER, FAULTLESS. The heifer is not to be small.⁵ (cow), not of an *eglah* (heifer). The Gaon⁶ says that *the water of sprinkling* (v. 13)⁷ is similar to honey, which is injurious to the one who has red bile⁸ but stimulates the one who has phlegm.⁹ However, there is no need for this interpretation.

footnotes:
⁵ For the Bible speaks of a parah
⁶ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁷ Which cleansed the unclean but made the clean unclean.
⁸ In medieval medicine red bile was believed to cause a volatile nature. See I.E. on Ex. 23:25 (Vol. 2, p. 516).
⁹ An excess of phlegm. This causes a phlegmatic nature.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:3:1)


**והוציא אותה.** המוציא או בצווי וכן ושחט כי הוא לא ישחטנה והעד לפניו:


AND SHE SHALL BE BROUGHT FORTH. Someone shall bring her forth.¹⁰ is: and he shall bring her forth. However, since Eleazar did not actually slaughter the cow, I.E. believes that he did not bring it out. Hence his comment. Or it means he shall have her brought forth by his command.¹¹ The same applies to *ve-shachat* (and she shall be slain),¹² for Eleazar did not slaughter the heifer. The word *le-fanav* (before his face) proves the latter.¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ The literal meaning of ve-hotzi otah
¹¹ In other words, it means he shall command that it be brought out.
¹² Which literally means: and he shall slaughter it.
¹³ If Eleazar did the slaughtering, then what point would there be for Scripture to say “and he [Eleazar] shall slaughter it before his [Eleazar’s] face.”
