###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:5:1)


**ושרף את הפרה.** השורף והעד לעיניו:


AND THE HEIFER SHALL BE BURNT IN HIS SIGHT. *Ve-saraf et ha-parah* (and the heifer shall be burnt)¹⁴ is: and he shall burn the cow. is to be rendered, and someone shall burn the heifer. The term *le’enav* (in his sight)¹⁵ proves the latter interpretation.

footnotes:
¹⁴ The literal meaning of ve-saraf et ha-parah
¹⁵ Eleazar’s sight.



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:6:1)


**עץ ארז.** כמו למצורע ושם רמזתי סוד:


CEDAR-WOOD. As in the case of a leper.¹⁶ I have hinted there at some secret.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶ Cedar wood was used in cleansing a leper (Lev. 14:4).
¹⁷ I.E. points out that there is a connection between leprosy and death. Hence cedar is employed in cleansing someone who has been contaminated by contract with a leper or a dead body.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:7:1)


**וכבס בגדיו.** הכהן השורף:


THEN THE PRIEST SHALL WASH HIS CLOTHES. The *kohen* who burns it.¹⁸, the reference is to the cow. However, in view of the fact that the latter is stated in the next verse, some say that the reference is to the cedar. See Filwarg and Krinsky.

footnotes:
¹⁸ Prima facie


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:7:2)


**וטמא הכהן.** וכבר ישב טמא ויש אומרי' שיהיה טמא עד הערב שלא יאכל מן הקדשים:


AND THE PRIEST SHALL BE UNCLEAN. The meaning of *ve-tame ha-kohen* (and the priest shall be unclean) is, and the *kohen* has already been unclean,¹⁹ has been made unclean by performing the ritual. According to this interpretation the *vav* prefixed to *ve-tame* means “and.” Hence the meaning of *ve-tame ha-kohen* is: and the *kohen* was unclean. or, as some say, the *kohen* shall be unclean.²⁰ is a future form meaning: and he shall be unclean. The *vav* is a conversive *vav*. *Until the even* refers to the eating of sacred food.

footnotes:
¹⁹ The kohen
²⁰ In this case ve-tame



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:9:1)


**איש טהור.** איננו הכהן השורף:


A MAN THAT IS CLEAN. The reference is not to the *kohen* who burns the cow.²¹ does this (Krinsky). According to the *Sifre* on this verse, any clean person, even if not a *kohen*, may gather the ashes of the red cow. However, I.E. seems to believe that this task was assigned to a *kohen*.

footnotes:
²¹ Another kohen


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:9:2)


**למי נדה.** פירושו רחוק כמו מנדיכם:


A WATER OF SPRINKLING. The meaning of *niddah* (sprinkling) is distance.²² (water of sprinkling) means the water of the distant, i.e., the water to be used to purify the distant. Compare, *me-naddekhem*²³ comes from the same root as *niddah*. When one hates another person, he keeps his distance from him. (that hate you) (Is. 66:5).

footnotes:
²² The person who is unclean is distanced from the sanctuary. Thus me-niddah
²³ Me-naddekhem



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:10:1)


**וטמא עד הערב.** ואחר שאמר שיכבס את בגדיו אין צורך להזכיר שירחץ במים:


AND BE UNCLEAN UNTIL THE EVEN. Once Scripture states that the gatherer of the ashes shall wash his clothes there is no need to mention that he shall wash in water.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ For Scripture earlier linked the two. See verse 7.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:10:2)


**ולגר הגר בתוכם.** בעבור היות ארץ ישראל קדושה כי הכבוד שם:


AND UNTO THE STRANGER THAT SOJOURNETH AMONG THEM. For the Land of Israel is holy because God’s glory is there.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ Hence even a stranger (a non-Jew) must abide by this rule.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:1)


**לכל נפש אדם.** יהודי או עובד כוכבים:


EVEN ANY MAN’S DEAD BODY. Be it the body of a Jew or a non-Jew.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:2)


**אדם.** ולא בהמה:


MAN’S…BODY. Not the body of an animal.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:11:3)


**וטמא.** יהיה טמא והוא פועל עתיד כי הוי"ו השיבו ועל דעתי שהוא פועל עבר:


SHALL BE UNCLEAN. The meaning of *ve-tame* is, shall be unclean. It is a future form, for the *vav* changes the verb²⁶, a third person perfect. from a perfect to an imperfect. However, I believe that *ve-tame* is in the past tense.²⁷ being conjunctive. Thus the meaning of *ve-tame* is: and he was unclean. The import of our verse thus is: and after being unclean for seven days he shall purify himself. See I.E. on verse 7 and the notes thereto.

footnotes:
²⁶ Tame
²⁷ Its vav



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:12:1)


**יתחטא בו.** יסור חטאו בו או כמו תחטאני באזוב ואטהר וידוע כי בסוד החשבון השלישי כמו השביעי:


THE SAME SHALL PURIFY HIMSELF THEREWITH. The meaning of *hu yitchate vo* (the same shall purify himself therewith) is, he shall remove his sin therewith.²⁸ means sin. Hence I.E.’s interpretation. On the other hand, the word *yitchate* (shall purify himself) is similar to the word *techatteni* (purge me) in *Purge me with hyssop, and I shall be clean* (Ps. 51:9).²⁹ means purify. Those who are acquainted with the secret of numbers know that the number three is like the number seven.³⁰

footnotes:
²⁸ That is, by undergoing this ritual he will remove the potentiality of entering the sanctuary in a state of impurity and thus sinning. The word chet
²⁹ In other words, yitchatta
³⁰ The number seven has mystical significance in that seven days make up a lunar quarter. The number three is close to half of the number 7. See I.E. on Gen. 34:25 and the notes thereto (Vol. 1, p. 330).



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:1)


**כי מי נדה.** יש מפרשים כמו אך במי נדה יתחטא:


BECAUSE THE WATER OF SPRINKLING. Some say that that *me niddah* (the water of sprinkling) has the same meaning as *me niddah* (water of sprinkling) in *it shall be purified with the water of sprinkling* (Num. 31:23).³¹

footnotes:
³¹ In other words, both refer to water mixed with the ashes of the red heifer. Num. 31:23 deals with the purification of utensils captured from non-Jews. I.E. prefaces these remarks with “some say” because, according to the rabbis, Num. 31:23 refers to water in which a menstruant immerses herself.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:2)


**לא זורק עליו.** על המים:


WAS NOT DASHED. The word *zorak* (dashed) refers to the water.³² is singular and *me* (water of) is plural.

footnotes:
³² Even though zorak


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:13:3)


**עוד.** תמיד כמו עוד כל ימי הארץ:


IS YET. The word *od* (is yet) means continuously.³³ Compare, *od* (while) in *While the earth remaineth* (Gen. 8:22).

footnotes:
³³ He is continuously unclean until sprinkled.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:14:1)


**באהל.** והבית כאהל רק הזכיר הכתוב האהל בעבור היות ישראל באהלים וכן אשר ישחט שור או כשב ועז במחנה:


IN A TENT. A house has the same law as a tent. Scripture mentions a tent because Israel then lived in tents. *That killeth an ox, or lamb, or goat, in the camp* (Lev. 17:3) is similar.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ The law laid down in Lev. 17:3 applies equally to a town. Scripture speaks of a camp because Israel then lived in tents in a camp.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:14:2)


**וכל אשר באהל.** מבגד:


AND EVERY THING THAT IS IN THE TENT. Garments.³⁵

footnotes:
³⁵ For the next verse deals with vessels.



### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:15:1)


**צמיד פתיל.** שנים פתילים:


COVERING CLOSE-BOUND. Two coverings.³⁶ (close bound). According to I.E. each one of these terms refers to a covering. The *tzamid* goes over the opening, while the petil goes round about the above covering and the rim of the utensil (Krinsky). Others claim that I.E. believes that the cover is made of two parts. See Krinsky, Filwarg, and Weiser.

footnotes:
³⁶ Scripture reads, tzamid, petil



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:16:1)


**או בקבר.** של אדם:


OR A GRAVE. Of a human being.



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:17:1)


ו. **לקחו לטמא.** הנה פירוש איך יתחטא:


AND FOR THE UNCLEAN THEY SHALL TAKE. See, Scripture explains how one who comes into contact with the dead shall purge himself.³⁷. However, it did not describe the procedure. Now it does.

footnotes:
³⁷ Earlier, in verse 12, Scripture had said, the same shall purify himself therewith



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:18:1)


**איש טהור.** והקרוב להיותו כהן:


A CLEAN PERSON. The reference is most probably to a *kohen*.³⁸ are acquainted with the laws of purification.

footnotes:
³⁸ For kohanim



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:20:1)


**ואיש אשר יטמא.** וכבר הזכיר כל הנוגע במת בנפש ונכרתה ועתה הזהיר על העצם ועל הקבר:


BUT THE MAN THAT SHALL BE UNCLEAN. Scripture has already mentioned, *Whosoever toucheth the dead…that soul shall be cut off from Israel* (v. 13). It now warns the one who touches a bone or a grave [not to defile the sanctuary].



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:21:1)


**יטמא עד הערב.** ולא יטהר עד אשר ירחץ:


SHALL BE UNCLEAN UNTIL EVEN. And shall not become clean until he washes.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:22:1)


**וכל אשר יגע בו הטמא יטמא.** שהוא טמא מת או עצם או קבר:


AND WHATSOEVER THE UNCLEAN PERSON TOUCHETH SHALL BE UNCLEAN. The reference is to one who has become unclean by touching a dead body, a bone, or a grave.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 19:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/19:22:2)


**תטמא עד הערב.** ותרחץ:


SHALL BE UNCLEAN UNTIL EVEN. And washes.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁹ Verse 7.



## Chapter 20


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:1)


**בחדש הראשון.** בשנת הארבעים והנה אין בתורה כלל שום מעשה או נבואה רק בשנה הראשונה ובשנת הארבעים:


IN THE FIRST MONTH. In the fortieth year.¹ Look, the Torah does not contain any act or prophecy except in the first and the fortieth year.²

footnotes:
¹ Following the Exodus.
² Scripture describes what happened in the first and the last year of Israel’s sojourn in the wilderness. What happened in the other years is ignored. Actually the Book of Numbers opens with a command to Moses in the second month of the second year. Filwarg explains that I.E. was not being precise. Some editions read, “except for the first year, the fortieth year.” The latter is to be interpreted as if written, and the fortieth year.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:2)


וטעם **ויבאו בני ישראל כל העדה.** בעבור שמתו דור המדבר ועתה באו הבאים אל ארץ כנען:


[AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL, EVEN THE WHOLE CONGREGATION, CAME.] Scripture states this because the generation that left Egypt died in the wilderness and they who were entering Canaan came to the desert of Zin.³ appears to be unnecessary. I.E. says that it refers to those about to enter Canaan. They are now the complete congregation, for none of them need die in the wilderness. Rashi comments similarly.

footnotes:
³ The phrase even the whole congregation


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:1:3)


**וישב העם בקדש.** בעבור שישבו שם ימים רבים כי כן כתיב:


AND THE PEOPLE ABODE IN KADESH. For they dwelt there for many days.⁴, because they dwelt there for many days. The latter is clearly stated in Scripture.⁵.

footnotes:
⁴ Scripture states, and the people abode in Kadesh
⁵ See Deut. 1:46, So ye abode in Kadesh many days



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:2:1)


**ולא היה מים לעדה.** ספר הכתוב שנים מאורעים האחד מות מרים והשני מות אהרן ומשה ולא יכנסו לארץ והחל לספר הסבה:


AND THERE WAS NO WATER FOR THE CONGREGATION. Scripture tells of two incidents.⁶ One, the death of Miriam. Two, the death of Aaron and Moses and their failure to enter the land.⁷ It begins by telling the cause of the latter.

footnotes:
⁶ That happened in Kadesh.
⁷ I.E. considers this one incident, for the latter was dependent upon the former.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:1)


**וירב.** מבנין הפעיל:


AND THE PEOPLE STROVE. *Va-yarev* (strove) is a *hifil*.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:2)


**בגוע אחינו.** שם הפעל כמו כשכב אדני המלך:


WHEN OUR BRETHREN PERISHED. *Bi-geva* (when…perished) is an infinitive. It is similar to *ki-shekav* (when…shall sleep) in *when my lord the king shall sleep with his fathers* (I Kings 1:21).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:3:3)


**אחינו.** הם דור המדבר שמתו והם עבדי השם:


OUR BRETHREN. The generation that died in the desert. They were servants of the Lord.⁸. Why add, *before the Lord*? According to I.E., *before the Lord* indicates that they worshipped the Lord.

footnotes:
⁸ Scripture states, our brethren perished



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:4:1)


**ולמה הבאתם.** ידבר למשה ולאהרן כי כן כתוב ויקהלו על משה ועל אהרן:


AND WHY HAVE YE BROUGHT. This was addressed to Moses and Aaron, for Scripture goes on to say, *and they assembled themselves together against Moses and against Aaron* (v. 2).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:4:2)


וטעם **וירב העם עם משה.** שהמריבה היתה עם משה חזקה:


Scripture reads, *And the people strove with Moses*, (v. 3) because their quarrel with Moses was more intense.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Than their quarrel with Aaron.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:5:1)


**העליתנו.** מלה זרה:


HAVE YE MADE US TO COME UP. *He’elitunu* (have ye made us to come up) is irregular.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ Although this form is grammatically correct, it is not employed in Scripture (Weiser).



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:6:1)


**ויבא משה ואהרן מפני הקהל.** כדמות בורחים:


AND MOSES AND AARON WENT¹¹ FROM THE PRESENCE OF THE ASSEMBLY. Like those who flee.¹² (mi-pene) *of the assembly*. I.E. believes that the latter indicates that they fled.

footnotes:
¹¹ Hebrew, va-yavo’u. This literally means, and they came.
¹² Our verse is to be rendered, And Moses and Aaron fled from the presence of the assembly. I.E. interprets thus because Scripture reads, from the presence


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:6:2)


**ויפלו על פניהם.** להתפלל וי"א לדרוש את השם בנבואה:


AND FELL UPON THEIR FACES. To pray. Other interpret, to prophetically inquire of God.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:1)


**קח את המטה.** יש בכאן פירושים רבים יש בדברי יחיד בעבור שאמר לישראל שמעו נא המורים והם בני אברהם יצחק ויעקב ואילו היה כן למה אמר להם עוד ממרים הייתם ואחרים אמרו כי מלת ודברתם כמו והכיתם וכמוהו ותדבר את כל זרע המלוכה ואין זה נכון כי המלה מגזרת דבר או מן ידבר עמים תחתנו והנה פי' ותדבר כמו ותאבד וכן בספר השני והנה פירוש ודברתם אל הסלע ואבדתם ועוד אם כן הפירוש למה נענש משה גם הם השיבו בעבור שהכה פעמים אם כן למה נענש אהרן ויאמר רבי משה הדרשן ז"ל הספרדי יש אותות נעשות בדבור ויש בפועל ודבור כמו מלת אלישע והשם צוה שיקח המטה להכות בסלע כמשפט הצור והוסיף מלת ודברתם להוציא המים במכה ובדבור ובעבור שהכעיסו ישראל אמר להם המן הסלע הזה נוציא לכם מים והיתה דעתו כי אין יכולת בנו להוציא מים מהסלע כי אם בכח השם והנה לא פירש דבורו היטב וחשבו אנשים בלבם כי דבורו שלא יוכל השם להוציא מים מן הסלע וזה טעם אשר לא קדשתם אותי והביא ראיה מדברי המשורר שאמר כי המרו את רוחו ויבטא בשפתיו והנה החטא היה בבטואו לא במכה גם זה איננו נכון כי משה אמר זה ולמה נענש אהרן ועוד כי אין במעשה זכר דבור והראיה שהביא איננה ראי' כי אין פי' כי המרו את רוחו כי אם אל השם וכן כתוב והמה מרו ועצבו את רוח קדשו וזה פירוש ויקציפו על מי מריבה והטעם שהקציפו השם וכן כתוב המה מי מריבה אשר רבו בני ישראל על ה' וירע למשה בעבורם כי המרו את רוח השם ויגזור על משה שלא יכנס לארץ. ואחרים אמרו כי הדבור אל הסלע שאיננו שומע איננה כי אם במכה ואם כן למה נענש משה. ואחרים אמרו בעבור שלא אמרו שירה כמו עלי באר ענו לה וזה טעם אשר לא קדשתם והאחרים אמרו כי השם צוה שידברו והם לא דברו רק הכה משה על כן נענשו שניהם ונאמר להם מעלתם מריתם לא האמנתם לא קדשתם. ואנשי שקול הדעת אמרו שלא יתכן שיהיה שליח השם ימיר דבור השם ואם הוא המיר איך נאמין בתורתו גם אלה לא דברו נכונה כי אילו היה מחליף במצוה מיד היה נענש כי הנה נענש על דבר שאינו מצוה לעולם ולא תורה לישראל ולא נעשה בזדון רק בשגגה בעבור שהכעיסוהו ישראל וכן אמר גם בי התאנף ה' בגללכם ואחרים אמרו כי ישראל אמרו שיוציא מסלע אחר וראיתם המן הסלע הזה ומשה פחד להחליף דבור השם ובעבור שלא האמין להוציא מים מן הסלע שאמרו על כן נענש וזה טעם לא האמנתם בי גם זה הפירוש איננו נכון כי הכתוב אמר מריתם את פי והנה לא מרו. והפירוש הנכון בעיני אגלנו ברמיזות דע כי כאשר ידע החלק את הכל ידבק בכל ויחדש בכל אותות ומופתים ואמת כי השם אמר למשה ולאהרן ודברתם ולא דברו בעבור מריבת העם עם משה והנה החלק חלק והכה הסלע ולא יצאו מים עד שהכהו פעם שנית והנה לא קדשו השם ומרו ומעלו בשגגה גם יוכל המפרש לפרש ויבטא בשפתיו על משה כי על כן נענש כי לא היה ראוי שידבר דבר עד שימלא שליחות השם גם יהיה דרש קדמונינו ז"ל כי החטא היה בעבור שאמר שמעו נא המורים ורמזו זה הסוד:


TAKE THE ROD. Many interpretations are offered for this incident.¹³ There is an individual opinion that Moses sinned because he said to Israel, who are the children of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob: *Hear now, ye rebels* (v. 10). However, if this were the case why did he again say,¹⁴ *ye have been rebellious against the Lord* (Deut. 9:7). Others say that the word *ve-dibbartem* (and speak ye) has the meaning, and ye shall strike. It is similar to the word *va-tedabber* (and destroyed) in *and destroyed all the seed royal* (II Chron. 22:10). However, this is incorrect, for *va-tedabber* is related to the word *dever* (destruction) or to the word *yadber* (he subdueth) in *He subdueth peoples under us* (Ps. 47:4). *Va-tedabber*¹⁵ thus means, and destroyed. Indeed, it so reads in the other book.¹⁶ (va-te’abbed) *all the seed royal* (II Kings 11:1). The meaning of *ve-dibbartem el ha-sela* (and speak ye unto the rock) thus is,¹⁷ and ye shall destroy the rock.¹⁸ Furthermore, if this is the meaning of the word,¹⁹ means, and you shall strike. why was Moses punished? The above commentaries answer, because he hit the rock twice. However, if this is the case why was Aaron punished?²⁰ Rabbi Moses Ha-Kohen the Spaniard, of blessed memory, says that some signs²¹ are performed by word of mouth, others by action and speech, as in the case of the salt²² (the incident of). which Elisha cast into the water.²³ (Elisha) *went forth unto the spring of the waters, and cast salt therein, and said, Thus saith the Lord: I have healed these waters; there shall be not be from thence any more death or miscarrying*, (II Kings 2:21). God commanded Moses to take the staff and strike the rock as he did in the case of the rock [at Horeb].²⁴ He added the word *ve-dibbartem* (and speak ye)²⁵ so that the water would flow via the speech and the blow. Because Israel angered him, Moses said, are we to bring you forth water from the rock? What Moses meant²⁶? was, we do not have the power to bring water out of the rock. We can only do so by the power of God. Now Moses did not make himself clear. People thought what he said was that God cannot bring water out of the rock. This is the meaning of *because ye sanctified Me not in the midst of the children of Israel* (Deut. 32:51). Rabbi Moses offered proof from the words of the poet who said, *For they embittered his spirit, And he spoke rashly with his lip* (Ps. 106:33). The sin was thus in his expressing, not by striking [the rock]. However, this too is incorrect, for Moses said, *are we to bring you forth water out of this rock* (v. 10). Why was Aaron punished?²⁷ Furthermore, no speech is mentioned in the Torah’s description of the act of striking the rock.²⁸ The proof offered²⁹ by Rabbi Moses is no proof because the meaning of *For they embittered his spirit* is that they embittered God’s spirit. Scripture similarly writes, *But they rebelled, and grieved His holy spirit* (Is. 63:10). This is the meaning of *They angered Him also at the waters of Meribah* (Ps. 106:32). It means they angered God.³⁰ Scripture similarly states, *These are the waters of Meribah, where the children of Israel strove with the Lord* (Num. 20:13).³¹, refers to God. Scripture says, *And it went ill with Moses because of them* (Ps. 106:32) because they embittered God’s spirit, and He decreed that Moses not enter the land.³² Others say that the meaning of speak to the rock can only mean strike the rock, for a rock cannot hear.³³ Now if this is so, why was Moses punished?³⁴ Some say that Moses and Aaron were punished because they did not sing a song of praise³⁵ as in *Spring up, O well-sing ye unto it* (Num. 21:17). They say that this is the meaning of *because ye sanctified Me not* (Deut. 32:51).³⁶ Others say that God commanded Moses and Aaron to speak to the rock, but they did not speak to it. On the contrary, Moses struck the rock.³⁷ Therefore they were both punished and they were told, *ye trespassed* (Deut. 32:51); *ye rebelled* (v. 24); *ye believed not in Me* (v. 12), and *ye sanctified Me not* (Deut. 32:52). Those who are guided by reason³⁸ say that it is impossible that a messenger of God would alter God’s word.³⁹ If Moses transformed God’s word, how can we believe his Torah?⁴⁰ However, the aforementioned⁴¹ also spoke incorrectly, for if Moses had altered a commandment he would have been punished immediately.⁴² Look, Moses was punished with regard to something which was not an eternal commandment and not a law given to Israel. Nor did he knowingly violate God’s command. He only did so inadvertently because Israel angered him. Moses clearly said, *Also the Lord was angry with me for your sakes* (Deut. 1:*37)*.⁴³ Others say that Israel told Moses⁴⁴ to bring water out of a different rock.⁴⁵ They offer *this rock* as proof (v. 10).⁴⁶? implies that we cannot bring water out of this rock, but we can out of another, that is, the rock about which God commanded us. However, Moses was afraid to alter God’s word.⁴⁷ Moses was punished because he did not believe that he would be able to bring forth water from the rock that Israel chose. He was therefore punished.⁴⁸ This is the meaning of *ye believed not in Me* (v. 12). This interpretation is also incorrect, for Scripture states, *ye rebelled against My word* (v. 24). However, they did not rebel.⁴⁹I will reveal in hints the explanation which appears correct to me. Know, when the part⁵⁰ knows the All,⁵¹ he cleaves to the All and creates signs and wonders with the help of the All.⁵² Now it is true that God told Moses and Aaron, *speak ye unto the rock*. They did not speak to the rock because the people were quarreling with Moses. The “part” thus remained a “part.”⁵³ He struck the rock, and water did not come out of it⁵⁴ until he struck it a second time.⁵⁵ Moses and Aaron thus did not sanctify God’s name. They inadvertently rebelled and trespassed. It is also possible to explain that *And he spoke rashly with his lips* (Ps. 106:33) refers to Moses.⁵⁶ It was unseemly for Moses to say anything⁵⁷, etc. (v. 10). before fulfilling God’s command. Moses was therefore punished. The midrashic interpretation of our ancients, of blessed memory, who said that Moses sinned because he said, *Hear now, ye rebels* (v. 10) is also in keeping with this interpretation. The rabbis hint at this secret.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:2)


**אל הסלע.** שהודעתיך או הנמצא סמוך למחנה:


UNTO THE ROCK. Which I pointed out to you or which is found close to the camp.⁵⁸ rock and not of *a* rock.

footnotes:
⁵⁸ Hence Scripture speaks of the


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:8:3)


**ונתן מימיו.** בעבור מימיו טעו רבים ואמרו כי זה הסלע הוא הצור ואלה עורי לב כי הצור הוא בחורב וזה הסלע היה בקצה ארץ אדום כי בקדש היה גם יתכן לפרש מימיו המים שאתן בו:


THAT IT GIVE FORTH ITS WATER. Many erred and said that the rock is to be identified with the stone [at Horeb]⁵⁹ because Scripture reads, *its* water.⁶⁰ water indicates that the stone had already produced water. See Bahya and *Da’at zekenim mi-ba’ale ha-tosafot*. However, they cannot think straight. The stone was in Horeb and this rock was at the border of the land of Edom, for it was in Kadesh.⁶¹ It is also possible to interpret *its water* as meaning, the water which I will place in it.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Ex. 17:6.
⁶⁰ Rather than water. These commentaries assume that its
⁶¹ See verses 1 and 14.



### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:9:1)


**את המטה מלפני ה'.** הוא מטה האלהים והוא המונח לפני העדות:


THE ROD FROM BEFORE THE LORD. The reference is to the rod of God.⁶² It was the rod which was placed before the testimony.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶² See Ex. 4:20.
⁶³ Num. 17:1-24.



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:10:1)


**המן הסלע הזה.** יש לנו כח להוציא לכם מים ממנו:


ARE WE TO BRING YOU FORTH WATER OUT OF THIS ROCK? Do we have the power to bring forth water out of this rock for you?



### Verse 12

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:12:1)


**יען לא האמנתם בי.** היא הסוד שרמזתי שלא נקדש השם על ידם:


BECAUSE YE BELIEVED NOT IN ME. This alludes to the secret at which I hinted, i.e., that God’s name was not sanctified through them.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:13:1)


**ויקדש בם.** במשה ואהרן כטעם בקרובי אקדש:


AND HE WAS SANCTIFIED IN THEM. Through Moses and Aaron.⁶⁴ (in them) does not refer to the water. Our verse has the same meaning as *Through them that are nigh unto Me I will be sanctified* (Lev. 10:3).⁶⁵

footnotes:
⁶⁴ In other words, the term bam
⁶⁵ God is sanctified when he punishes the righteous. See Rashi.



### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:14:1)


**מקדש.** שם מקום עיר ואיננו קדש ברנע כי אותו הוא מדבר וכן הכתוב אומר יחיל ה' מדבר קדש:


FROM KADESH. Kadesh is the name of a place where a city is located. It is not to be identified with Kadesh-barnea,⁶⁶ for the latter was a wilderness.⁶⁷ Thus Scripture reads, *The Lord shaketh the wilderness of Kadesh* (Ps. 29:8).⁶⁸

footnotes:
⁶⁶ Deut. 1:2,19; 2:14; 9:23.
⁶⁷ See Deut. 1:19.
⁶⁸ I.E. assumes that the reference is to Kadesh-barnea, for Kadesh-barnea, unlike Kadesh, was a wilderness. However, his reasoning is circular.

**התלאה.** פירשתיו:


THE TRAVAIL. I have already explained the meaning of the word *tela’ah* (travail).⁶⁹

### Verse 15

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:15:1)


**לנו מצרים ולאבותינו.** שמתו והטעם שארך זמן רע תם:


EGYPTIANS DEALT ILL WITH US, AND OUR FATHERS. Who died. The meaning of our clause is that their evil⁷⁰ lasted a very long time.

footnotes:
⁷⁰ The evil which the Egyptians did to us, for they afflicted us and our parents.



### Verse 16

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:16:1)


**וישלח מלאך.** כמשמעו וכן הכתוב אומר ומלאך פניו הושיעם ורבים פירשוהו על משה כי מצאו ויאמר חגי מלאך ה' ואין זאת דעתי:


AND SENT AN ANGEL.⁷¹. The word *malakh* can be rendered as messenger or angel. Hence I.E.’s comment. This is to be taken literally. Scripture similarly states, *And the angel of His presence saved them* (Is. 63:9). Many said that the reference is to Moses⁷² for they found *Then spoke Haggai the Lord’s messenger* (malakh)⁷³. (Haggai 1:13). However, this is not my opinion.

footnotes:
⁷¹ Hebrew, malakh
⁷² See Rashi.
⁷³ We thus see that Scripture refers to a prophet by the term malakh



### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:17:1)


**בשדה ובכרם.** שלא ישחיתו ואפי' מי הבארות שלכם לא נשתה:


THROUGH FIELD OR THROUGH VINEYARD. So that they do not destroy it. We will not even drink the water of your wells.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:17:2)


**דרך המלך נלך.** אם הדרך שהמלך הולך בה או פירושו הדרך שיצוה המלך שהוא מלך אדום נלך:


WE WILL GO ALONG THE KING’S HIGHWAY. The major highway upon which the king travels. Or its meaning is, the road that the king, namely, the king of Edom, will command us to travel in.⁷⁴

footnotes:
⁷⁴ According to this interpretation the meaning of our verse is: we will go on any road which you the king will choose for us to travel on.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:19:1)


**במסלה נעלה.** מה יזיקכם שנעלה במסלה ואם הוצרכו למים תמכרום לנו וזה טעם מכרם כמו דמיהם:


WE WILL GO UP BY THE HIGHWAY. What harm will it cause you if we up by the highway? If we need water you will sell it to us. This is the meaning of *mikhram* (v. 19). It means the price thereof.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:22:1)


וטעם **בני ישראל כל העד'.** בעבור שיצא אדום להלחם בם הגיד הכתוב שלא נפקד איש מבני ישראל בבואם מעיר אדום אל הר ההר:


[THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL.] Its meaning is, *the entire congregation*. Because Edom went to fight against them, Scripture relates that no one was missing from the children of Israel when they came from the city of Edom to Mount Hor.⁷⁵

footnotes:
⁷⁵ In other words, no Jews were killed by the Edomites.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:1)


**יאסף אהרן.** כמו ומות כהר שיתקן עצמו למות ויעלה אל ההר:


AARON SHALL BE GATHERED UNTO HIS PEOPLE. This is similar to *and die in the mount* (Deut. 32:50).⁷⁶ It means that Aaron should prepare himself to die, and go up the mountain.⁷⁷ means Aaron shall go and be gathered to his people.

footnotes:
⁷⁶ The meaning of which is: and prepare yourself to die, for it is not in one’s hand to determine the moment of death. See I.E. on Gen. 1:26 (Vol. 1, p. 47).
⁷⁷ Aaron shall be gathered unto his people


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:2)


**מריתם.** פירשתיו:


YE HAVE REBELLED. I have explained this.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 20:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/20:24:3)


**למי מריבה.** כמו במי מריבה שם המקום או בעבור מי מריבה:


AT THE WATERS OF MERIBAH. *Le-me merivah* means at the waters of Meribah.⁷⁸ prefixed to a word means to. Thus *le-me meribah* might be taken to mean to the water of Meribah. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is the name of a place. Or *le-me merivah* means because of the waters of Meribah.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁸ A lamed
⁷⁹ See previous note.



### Verse 26

AND STRIP…AND PUT THEM UPON. *Ve-hafshet* (and strip) and *ve-hilbashtam* (and put them upon) are *hifils*.

[AND AARON SHALL BE GATHERED.] Its meaning is, as soon as you strip Aaron of his garments, he shall die.

AND AARON WAS GATHERED TO HIS PEOPLE.⁸⁰ I have explained this.⁸¹

## Chapter 21


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:1:1)


**וישמע הכנעני מלך ערד.** אמרו הקדמונים שהוא סיחון ונקרא הכנעני כי כל אמורי כנעני ורבים אמרו כי זאת הפרשה יהושע כתבה והראיה מלך ערד אחד ומצאו שבני יהודה קראו שם המקום חרמה ולא אמרו כלום כי אותו המקום יקרא בתחלה צפת וזה מלך ערד והאמת שני מקומות ורבים במקרא כמו הם ומלך ערד על פשוטו הוא מעבר לירדן מזרחה ונסמכה זאת הפרשה למות אהרן כי הכתוב ספר מה שאירע בהר ההר קודם נסעם משם והעד וישמע הכנעני מלך ערד ואחריו ויסעו מהר ההר ואם תבקש מה שמע הנה מפורש:


AND THE CANAANITE, THE KING OF ARAD. The ancients¹ said he² 3a: “Sihon, Arad, and Canaan are one and the same.” is Sihon. He is called a Canaanite, for all Amorites are Canaanites.³ Many say that Joshua wrote this section.⁴ *The king of Arad, one* (Josh. 12:14) is proof of this.⁵ They also found that Scripture states that the children of Judah called the name of the place Hormah.⁶ However, they are wrong, for that place⁷ was originally called Zephath⁸. while the place spoken of in our verse was ruled by the king of Arad.⁹ In reality they are two places.¹⁰ There are many such instances in Scripture.¹¹ According to the plain meaning of the text the king of Arad ruled over the eastern bank of the Jordan.¹² This portion¹³ was connected to the death of Aaron, because Scripture relates what happened on Mount Hor¹⁴ before they [Israel] journeyed from there. *And the Canaanite, the king of Arad… heard…and they journeyed from mount Hor* (v. 4) is proof of this.¹⁵ If you want to find what the king of Arad heard, observe, it is clearly stated.¹⁶ (v. 1).

footnotes:
¹ The sages of the Talmud.
² The king of Arad. See Rosh Ha-Shanah
³ Sihon was king of the Amorites. See verse 21.
⁴ Verses 1-3.
⁵ Josh. 12 lists the kings that Joshua smote. The king of Arad is included in this list. It thus appears that it was Joshua, not Moses, who defeated the king of Arad.
⁶ In the days of Joshua. See Josh. 12:14.
⁷ The Hormah spoken of in Josh. 12:14.
⁸ See Jud. 1:17: And Judah went with Simeon his brother, and they smote the Canaanites that inhabited Zephath, and utterly destroyed it. And the name of the city was called Hormah
⁹ It was always called Arad.
¹⁰ The Hormah mentioned in our verse is not to be identified with the Hormah in Josh. 12:14.
¹¹ Of two places having the same name.
¹² And was thus defeated by Moses.
¹³ Verses 1-3.
¹⁴ The place of Aaron’s death.
¹⁵ That the king of Arad fought Israel at Mount Hor.
¹⁶ The king heard that Israel came by the way of Atharim


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:1:2)


**כי בא ישראל דרך האתרים.** האתרים י"א כי האל"ף נוסף והטעם התרים את הארץ וכן אל"ף אזרועך:


THAT ISRAEL CAME BY THE WAY OF ATHARIM. Some say that the *alef* of *atharim* is superfluous. They say that the meaning of *ha-atharim* is, those who spy. The *alef of ezro’akha* (your arm) is similar.¹⁷ is, your arm (*zero’akha*). The reference is probably to Jer. 32:21. However, the text there reads *ezro’a*.

footnotes:
¹⁷ For the meaning of ezro’akha



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:4:1)


**לסבוב את ארץ אדום.** צלמונה ופונון כי כן כתוב:


TO COMPASS THE LAND OF EDOM. Zalmonah and Punon, for this is explicitly stated in Scripture.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ See Num. 33:41-45. Scripture here omits the places where Israel camped when they compassed the land of Edom. It does include them in Num. 33:41-45. It tells us there that Israel camped at Zalmonah and Punon when it went around Edom.



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:5:1)


**הקלקל.** כמו קל הקל והמלה כפולה ורבים כמו הם:


THIS LIGHT BREAD. The word *ha-kelokel* (this light) means the very light. The word is formed by doubling the word *kal*.¹⁹ is an intensified form. Hence the meaning very light. There are many similar instances in Scripture.²⁰ (very red), *shecharhar* (very black).

footnotes:
¹⁹ Kelokel
²⁰ Compare, adamdam



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:6:1)


**וישלח וגו' השרפים.** שם תאר הנחשים ודרך דרש אם ישך הנחש בלא לחש ואין יתרון לבעל הלשון והם שלחו לשונם לנשוך כן שולח בהם:


AND THE LORD…SERPENTS. The word *ha-serafim* (fiery) is an adjective. The following is a midrashic interpretation. Scripture states, *If the serpent bite before it is charmed, Then the charmer hath no advantage* (Eccles. 10:11). These people²¹ sent their tongues to bite.²² They were repaid with snakes being sent against them.

footnotes:
²¹ Who spoke against God and Moses.
²² A metaphor for speaking evil.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:8:1)


**עשה לך.** כדמות נחש שרף מנחשת כי כן כתוב:


MAKE THEE. A form of a fiery serpent *out of brass*, for Scripture explicitly says so.²³

footnotes:
²³ In the next verse.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:8:2)


**על נס** שיהיה גבוה ויראו אותו הכל ורבים השתבשו ואמרו כי זאת הצורה לקבל כח עליונים וחליל' חלילה כי הדבר נעשה בצווי השם ואין לנו לחפש למה צורת נחש ואם לא כן יראנו היש עץ שימתיק המים המרים אפי' הדבש לא ימתיקם ומה טעם לשום דבלת תאנים על השחין ואין בתולדת הדבלה להסיר השחין והאמת כי נשגבה ממנו דעת עליון:


AND SET IT UPON A POLE. So that it be on a high place and everyone will be able to see it. Many err. They say that this was an image that had the capability of receiving powers from on high.²⁴ Far be it, far be it [for one to believe such a thing], for this thing was made by God’s command.²⁵ We should not investigate why Moses was commanded to make the form of a snake.²⁶ Should someone disagree,²⁷ let him show us if there is a tree that makes bitter waters sweet.²⁸ Even honey will not sweeten them.²⁹ What reason was there to put a cake of figs upon a boil?³⁰ It is not in the nature of figs to remove boils. The truth is that the mind of the Almighty is beyond us.

footnotes:
²⁴ It was an image in whom heavenly power resided and thus could heal those bitten by the serpents. See I.E. on Gen. 31:19 (Vol. 1, p. 300) and Ex. 20:20 (Vol. 2, p. 437). According to I.E. such an image is banned by Scripture for it is a type of magical practice.
²⁵ God would not command such an object to be made. According to I.E. this is precisely what Ex. 20:20 prohibits. See I.E. on Ex. 20:20 (Vol. 2, p. 437).
²⁶ And argue that there is a scientific reason for the copper snake.
²⁷ Those who offer scientific explanations.
²⁸ Moses healed bitter waters by casting a tree into it. See Ex. 15:22-25.
²⁹ Bitter water.
³⁰ When King Hezekiah was ill with boils the prophet Isaiah prescribed that a cake of figs be applied to his boils. See II Kings 20:7; Is. 38:21.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:10:1)


**ויסעו בני ישראל.** מארץ אדום כי כן כתוב לסבוב ואחרית גבולה פונון ומפונון נסעו אל אובות כי כן כתוב:


AND THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL JOURNEYED. From the land of Edom, for Scripture states, *to compass the land of Edom* (v. 4). Punon³¹ is on the far border of Edom. The Israelites traveled from Punon to Oboth, for Scripture says so.³²

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:14:1)


**בספר מלחמת ה'.** ספר היה בפני עצמו ושם כתוב מלחמות ה' בעבור יריאיו ויתכן שהיה מימות אברהם כי ספרים רבים אבדו ואינם נמצאים אצלנו כדברי נתן ועדו ודברי הימים למלכי ישראל ושירות שלמה ומשליו:


IN THE BOOK OF THE WARS OF THE LORD. It was a separate book in which the wars of the Lord on behalf of His servants was recorded. It is possible that it originated in the days of Abraham,³⁵ for many books, such as the work of Nathan³⁶ and Iddo,³⁷ the chronicles of the kings of Israel,³⁸ and the songs of Solomon and his proverbs,³⁹ were lost and are not found among us.

footnotes:
³⁵ The first servant of God to wage war. See Gen. 14:13-16.
³⁶ See I Chron. 29:29.
³⁷ II Chron. 12:15.
³⁸ I Kings 14:19.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:14:2)


**את והב בסופה.** מלחמות היו בימים הקדמונים במקומות הנזכרים ושם והב איננו לשון הקדש וכן ופסי ושתי ויזתא גם ושני או פירוש ושני הוי"ו לחבור וכל זה למה שלא נמצא וי"ו שרש בראש המלה רק מלת ווי העמודים רק תמצא תחת יו"ד כמו ולד ופירוש המתרגם ארמי ידוע:


VAHEB IN SUPHAH. There were wars in these mentioned places in ancient times. The word Vaheb is not Hebrew. Neither is Vophsi (Num. 13:14), Vashti (Esth. 1:9), Vaizatha (Esth. 9:9), and also Vashni (I Chron. 6:13). It is possible that the *vav* of Vashni is a connective.⁴⁰ is, and *sheni* (*sheni* being a Hebrew name). I say all this⁴¹ because we do not find the *vav* to be a root letter at the beginning of a word⁴² to be a first root letter. except in the word *vave* (hooks) in *the hooks of the pillars* (Ex. 27:10). It is found⁴³ only in place of a *yod*. Compare, *valed* (child) (II Sam. 6:23).⁴⁴. The explanation of the Aramaic translation is known.⁴⁵ (Suphah) as *yama de-suf* (the sea of reeds).

footnotes:
⁴⁰ In this case, the meaning of Vashni
⁴¹ That these words are not Hebrew.
⁴² In other words, we do not find the vav
⁴³ As a first root letter.
⁴⁴ Which comes from the root yod, lamed, dalet
⁴⁵ Onkelos renders sufah

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:15:1

**ואשד הנחלים.** י"א שהוא בלשון ארמית וסוף דבר כל אלה שמות מקומות:


AND THE SLOPE OF THE VALLEYS. Some say that the word *eshed* (slope) is Aramaic.⁴⁶ means pouring or spilling in Aramaic. The end of the matter is: all of these⁴⁷ are names of places.⁴⁸ is the name of a place.

**אשר נטה.** הטעם על ישראל כי על ער עברו:


THAT INCLINETH. The reference is to the Israelites,⁴⁹ for they passed by Ar.⁵⁰

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:15:3)


**ונשען לגבול מואב.** זהו והוא יושב ממלי ומשם נסעו ישראל אל המקום הנקרא באר כי ה"א בארה תחת אל כה"א מצרימה וזאת הבאר גם היא היתה פלא ואיננה הבאר הנקרא באר מרים לפי דעתי רק היה המקום שצוה משה וחפרוהו שרי ישראל במשענותם ומיד נבקעו מים:


AND LEANETH UPON THE BORDER OF MOAB. This is what *and they*⁵¹ *abide over against me*⁵² (Num. 22:5) refers to. Israel journeyed from there⁵³ to a place called *be’er*, for the *heh* of *be’erah* (to Beer) is in place of the word *el* (to). It is like the *heh* of *mitzraymah* (to Egypt) (Gen. 12 :10).⁵⁴. This well⁵⁵ was also⁵⁶ the result of a miracle. I believe that it is not the well which is called Miriam’s well.⁵⁷ 9a. On the contrary, it refers to a place that Moses commanded to be dug. The princes of Israel dug it with their staffs, and water immediately gushed forth.

⁵⁷ Contrary to the opinion of the rabbis. See Ta’anit

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:17:1

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:18:1)


**כרוה נדיבי העם.** כפול כמשפט:


WHICH THE NOBLES OF THE PEOPLE DELVED. Scripture repeats itself,⁶⁰ repeats *which the princes digged*. as is its style.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Which the nobles…delved


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:18:2)


**במחוקק.** ע"י מחוקקים כמו לבי לחוקקי ישראל וזו הבאר היתה במדבר רחוק מהיישוב ומזה המדבר נסעו אל מתנה ומשם אל נחליאל ודע כי אשד הנחלים והמדבר הנקרא באר ומתנה ונחליאל כולם מקומות ויש להם שם כלל והוא עלמון דבלתימה כי כן כתוב בפרשת אלה מסעי או אלה שמות מקומות עברו עליהם במסע אחר והרי העברים לפני נבו הוא הבמות והגיא אשר בשדה מואב והעד שאמר הכתוב כי מהגיא נסעו אל ערבות מואב בעבר לירדן ירחו וכתיב כי מהר העברים נסעו אל ערבות מואב על ירדן ירחו ועוד ונשב בגיא מול בית פעור עת שהתחננתי ובמקום אחר עלה אל הר העברים הר נבו גם ויקבוד אותו בגיא ושם כתוב ויעל משה מערבות מואב אל הר נבו והנה הגיא הוא ראש הפסגה ושם מת משה ואין צורך להוליכו אחר מותו:


WITH THE SCEPTRE. The meaning of *bi-mechokek* (with the sceptre) is, by the governors.⁶¹ means by. The *bet* usually has the meaning of in. Hence I.E.’s comment. Compare, *My heart is toward the governors* (chokeke) *of Israel* (Jud. 5:9). This well was in the wilderness, far from an inhabited place. From this wilderness Israel journeyed to Mattanah. From Mattanah they journeyed to Nahaliel. Note that *eshed ha-nehalim* (the slope of the valleys) and the wilderness which is called Beer and Mattanah and Nahaliel are all names of places. They are also referred to by a general name, *viz.*, *Almon-diblathiam* (Num. 33:46), which is found in the Torah portion *Ve-Elleh Mase* (*Ibid.*).⁶² (the slope of the valleys), Beer, Mattanah, and Nahaliel are not mentioned there. *Almon-diblathiam* is. Hence I.E.’s comment. On the other hand, it is also possible that these are the names of places that the Israelites passed over in another journey.⁶³ The mountains of Abarim in front of Nebo (Num. 33:47) are to be identified with Bamoth and the valley that is in the field of Moab (v. 20). The following is proof of this. Scripture states that Israel journeyed from the valley⁶⁴ to the plains of Moab by the Jordan at Jericho (Num. 22:1).⁶⁵ It also states that Israel journeyed from the mountains of Abarim to the plains of Moab beyond the Jordon at Jericho (Num. 33:48). It further states, *So we abode in the valley*⁶⁶ *over against Beth-peor* (Deut. 3:29) at the time that I [Moses] pleaded [with God].⁶⁷ Scripture elsewhere states, *Get thee up into this mountain of Abarim, unto mount Nebo* (Deut. 32:49).⁶⁸ Scripture also states, *and he was buried in the valley* (Deut. 34:6). The Torah there states, *And Moses went up from the plains of Moab unto mount Nebo* to the top of Pisgah (Deut. 34:1). The valley was thus at the top of Pisgah. Moses died there, and there was no need to move him after his death.⁶⁹

footnotes:
⁶¹ The bet of bi-mechokek
⁶² Which records the journeys of the children of Israel in the wilderness. Eshed ha-nehalim
⁶³ Hence they are not recorded in Num. 33:46.
⁶⁴ That is in the field of Moab (v. 10).
⁶⁵ I.E. connects Num. 22:1 to Num. 21:10.
⁶⁶ Which is in the field of Moab.
⁶⁷ To allow him to enter Canaan. This was shortly before Moses’ death.
⁶⁸ We thus see that Moses went up to Mount Nebo from the valley which is the field of Moab.
⁶⁹ As would be the case if the “valley” where Moses died was not at the top of the mountain. According to I.E. the “valley” was an indentation at the top of the mountain.

WHICH LOOKETH DOWN. *Ve-nishkafah* (which looketh down) is in the feminine. It refers to the valley.⁷⁰ (valley) can be masculine or feminine.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:20:2

UPON THE DESERT. The word *yeshimon* (desert) means a wasteland (*shemamah*). *And in the waste, a howling wilderness* (yeshimon) (Deut. 32:10) is the proof text. So too is *They wandered in the wilderness* (yeshimon) *in a desert way* (Ps. 107:4).

### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:24:1)


**מארנון.** שלקח ממואב:


FROM THE ARNON. Which he [the king of Edom] took from Moab.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:24:2)


**עד בני עמון.** כי כתוב כי חצי ארץ בני עמון היתה לישראל כי לקחוהו מיד האמורי שלקחו בתחלה מיד בני עמון והכתוב אמר על מואב ועמון שלא יתן השם מארצם לישראל עד מדרך כף רגל והטעם הארץ שהיתה בידם בעת ההיא על כן הוצרך הכתוב לאמר דברי המושלים בעבור חשבון שישבו שם ישראל כי כן כתוב וישב ישראל בכל ערי האמורי בחשבון:


EVEN UNTO THE CHILDREN OF AMMON. Scripture records that half of the land of Ammon belonged to the children of Israel,⁷² for they⁷³ took it from the Amorites who originally took it from the children of Ammon. Now Scripture tells us that God would not give the Israelites even the size of a footstep from any of the land belonging to the Ammonites and Moabites. The reference is to the land which Ammon and Moab possessed at that time.⁷⁴ Scripture therefore had to record the words of the poets.⁷⁵ It did so because Israel lived in Heshbon.⁷⁶ The Torah explicitly states, *And Israel dwelt in all the cities of the Amorite, in Heshbon*⁷⁷ (v. 25).

⁷⁴ In the time of Moses.
⁷⁵ To the effect that Sihon took the city of Heshbon from the Moabites.
⁷⁶ Which was originally a Moabite city.
⁷⁷ We thus see that Israel dwelt in Heshbon.

### Verse 30

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:30:1)


**ונירם.** דברי משה והמ"ם סימן מלך אמורי וגדודיו ולו שני פירושים. האחד מגזירת ניר ותהיה מלת ונירם מושכת עצמה ואחרת עמה כמו והנבואה עודד הנביא וכן הוא ונירם אבד ניר חשבון ותהיה מלת ונירם כמו בתוך האהלי הערכך וכן הוא והניר שלהם אבד שהוא ניר חשבון. והפירוש השני שתהיה מלת ונירם מגזרת ירה ויור אשר יריתי והטעם כאשר ירינו אותם אבד חשבון:


WE HAVE SHOT THEM. These are the words of Moses.⁸⁷ The *mem* of *va-niram*⁸⁸ (we shot at them) refers to the king of the Amorites and his troops. It has two interpretations. One, that *va-niram* is related to the word *nir* (kingship). It⁸⁹. is to be read as if written twice.⁹⁰ (we have shot at them—Heshbon is perished), is to be read, *va-niram avad nir cheshbon* (and their king, the king of Heshbon, was destroyed). It is like *even the prophecy, Oded the prophet* (II Chron. 15:8).⁹¹ Our verse means, and their king the king of Heshbon was destroyed. *Va-niram* is similar to the word *ha-oholi* (my tent) in *in the midst of my tent* (Josh. 7:21), and to *ha-erkekha* (thy valuation) (Lev. 27:23).⁹² beneath the *vav* is the sign of the direct object. In other words, *va-niram* is short for *ve-ha-niram*. Thus *va-niram* is similar to *ha-oholi* and *ha-erkekha* in that both words are written with the direct object even though they have suffixes. Words with suffixes usually do not come with the direct object. Hence I.E.’s comment. *Va-niram avad cheshbon* (we have shot at them—Heshbon is perished) means, their king, that is, the king of Heshbon, perished. Second, that *va-niram* is related to *yereh va-yor* (shoot; and he shot) (II Kings 13:17) and *yariti* (cast)⁹³ (Gen. 31:51). It means,⁹⁴ (we have shot at them–Heshbon is perished). when we shot them Heshbon was destroyed.

footnotes:
⁸⁷ Until now Scripture records what the poets said. Now it records the comments of Moses.
⁸⁸ A pronominal suffix meaning them.
⁸⁹ The word nir
⁹⁰ Our verse, which reads va-niram avad cheshbon
⁹¹ Which is to be read as if written, even the prophecy, the prophecy of Oded the prophet. See I.E. on Gen. 6:13 (Vol. 1, p. 100) and the notes thereto.
⁹² The pattach
⁹³ Translated literally.
⁹⁴ Va-niram avad heshbon


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 21:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/21:30:2)


**ונשים.** מפעלי הכפל והחיר"ק תחת קמ"ץ קטן כמו ויסב אלהים את העם ויאמר רבי משה הכהן כי הדגשות לחסרון האל"ף כאל"ף מלפנו מבהמות ארץ מגזרת תאשם שומרון וי"א כי חשבון היתה עיר סיחון לעולם וטעם בואו חשבון דברי המושלים למואבים הבאים בשבי אל חשבון כי אש יצאה מחשבון משל למחנות סיחון וכאשר ירינום דברי המושלים על לשון המואבים חשבנו כי תאבד חשבון ונעשה שממה כל הארץ עד נופח או הם דברי משה הדרשן:


AND WE HAVE LAID WASTE. *Va-nashim* comes from a double root.⁹⁵. It comes from the root *shin, mem, mem*. The *chirik* is in place of a short *kamatz*.⁹⁶. In other words, the word should have been vocalized *va-nashem*. Compare, *va-yasev* (led about) in *But God led the people about* (Ex. 13:18).⁹⁷. Rabbi Moses Ha-Kohen says that the *dagesh* in the *shin* of *va-nashim* compensates for a missing *alef*.⁹⁸ comes from the root *alef, shin, mem* (guilty). Compare, *Who teacheth us more than the beasts of the earth* (Job. 35:11) wherein an *alef* is missing in the word *mallefenu* (teacheth us).⁹⁹. The word *va-nashim* is related to the word *tesham*¹⁰⁰. (shall bear her guilt) in *Samaria shall bear her guilt* (Hos. 14:1). Some say that Heshbon was always the city of Sihon.¹⁰¹ In this case, *Come ye to Heshbon* (v. 27) is the line that the poets directed at the Moabites who came as captive to Heshbon. And *for a fire is gone out of Heshbon* is a metaphor for the army of Sihon. When the poets speak on behalf of the Moabites, they say, we thought that Heshbon will be destroyed¹⁰² and the entire land will be laid waste even unto Nophah. Or these¹⁰³ (v. 30). are the words of Moses.

footnotes:
⁹⁵ Even though it is spelled with one mem
⁹⁶ A tzere
⁹⁷ Which is vocalized with a tzere
⁹⁸ According to Rabbi Moses, va-nashim
⁹⁹ Which comes from the root alef, lamed, peh
¹⁰⁰ In other words, it comes from the root alef, shin, mem
¹⁰¹ See I.E. on verses 26-28.
¹⁰² They interpret our verse as follows: We (the Moabites) shot at them. W e thought Heshbon is perished…even unto Nophah.
¹⁰³ We have shot at them…unto Nophah

## Chapter 22


### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:2:1)


**לאמורי.** בקמצות הלמ"ד והטעם לידוע שהוא סיחון ועוג ולא הי' במלכי כנען גדולים כאלה והעד אשר כגובה ארזים גבהו:


TO THE AMORITES. The *lamed* prefixed to the word *emori* (Amorite) is vocalized with a *kamatz*.¹ so vocalized indicates the definite article. Its reference is to the famous Amorites Sihon and Og. There were no kings among the Canaanites as great as they. *Whose height was like the height of the cedars* (Amos 2:9) is proof of this.² [Sihon and Og] *before them, Whose height was like the height of the cedars* (Amos 2:9).

footnotes:
¹ A lamed
² The entire verse reads: Yet I destroyed the Amorite



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:3:1)


**ויגר מואב.** כמו ויירא וכן גורו לכם:


AND MOAB WAS SORE AFRAID. *Va-yagar* means was afraid. *Guru lakhem* (be ye afraid) (Job 19:29) is similar.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:3:2)


**ויקץ.** כמו הפוך וכמוהו נעלה ביהודה ונקיצנה:


AND MOAB WAS OVERCOME WITH DREAD. *Va-yakatz* (overcome with dread) has the same meaning as a similarly spelled word whose letters are inverted.³ is spelled *vav, yod, tzadi, kof. Va-yakatz* is spelled *vav, yod, kof, tzadi*. *U-nekitzennah* (and vex it) in *Let us go up against Judah, and vex it* (Is. 7:6) is similar.

³ Va-yatzak. Va-yatzak



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:4:1)


**אל זקני מדין.** יתכן שהיו החמשה מלכים זקנים:


UNTO THE ELDERS OF MIDIAN. It is possible that the five Midianite kings are these same elders.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Num. 31:8 speaks of five Midianite kings. I.E. identifies the elders in our verse with them.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:4:2)


**ילחכו כלחוך.** שנים בנינים:


NOW WILL THIS MULTITUDE LICK UP…AS THE OX LICKETH UP. *Yelachakhu* (will…lick up) and *ki-lechokh* (as…licketh up) are two different conjugations.⁵ (will…lick up) is in the *pi’el. Ki-lechokh* (as…licketh up) is a *kal*. Both words come from the root *lamed, chet, caf*.

⁵ Yelachakhu



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:5:1)


**פתורה.** כמו מצרימה אל פתור:


TO PETHOR. *Pethorah* means to Pethor.⁶ at the end of the word is in place of the preposition to. Compare, *mitzrayemah* (to Egypt) (Gen. 37:25).

footnotes:
⁶ The heh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:5:2)


**ארץ בני עמו.** והם ארמים וכן מפתור ארם נהרים ובדרש שבלעם הוא בלע כי זה ארמי ואם בעבור בעור אביו הנה בן אחשורוש קודם אחשורוש רק הם שנים:


TO THE LAND OF THE CHILDREN OF HIS PEOPLE. Who were Arameans. Scripture similarly states, *from Pethor of Aram-naharaim* (Deut. 23:5). The midrash⁷. 105a. Also see *Targum Jonathan* on Gen. 36:32. identifies Balaam with Bela.⁸ [However, the identification is untenable] because Balaam was an Aramean. If we identify Balaam with Bela because of Beor his father,⁹. then, lo and behold, the son of Ahasuerus¹⁰ lived before Ahasuerus.¹¹ 1:1), King Ahasuerus mentioned in the Book of Esther had a son called Darius. There was also a King Darius who lived before Ahasuerus mentioned in the book of Esther. If every Darius in the Bible is one and the same person, then Darius son of Ahasuerus lived before Ahasuerus his father. See I.E. on Dan. 9:1 and Esth. 1:1. They¹² are definitely two different people.

footnotes:
⁷ See San
⁸ An Edomite king mentioned in Gen. 36:32.
⁹ Both Bela and Balaam had a father called be’or
¹⁰ Darius. See Dan. 9:1.
¹¹ Esth. 1:1. I.E.’s point is that names alone cannot serve to identify historical figures, for more than one person have the same name. According to the midrash (Esther Rabbah
¹² Bela and Balaam.

WHICH IS BY THE RIVER. The reference is to Aram-naharaim.

THE FACE OF THE EARTH. I have already explained the meaning of this expression.¹³

### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:1)


**ארה לי.** לשון צווי מפעלי הכפל בחסרון אות הכפל וכן קבה לי והנה קוב על משקל שמור וקבה לי בחסרון הה"א על דרך משקל שכב רכב צלח:


CURSE ME. The word *arah* (curse) is an imperative. It comes from a double root with one of the double letters missing. The same is true of with the word *kavah* (curse) in curse me (v. 18). The word *kov* (curse) is vocalized according to the paradigm of *shemor* (keep).¹⁴. *Kavah*¹⁵ is an elongated form of *kav*. in *kavah li* (curse me) minus the *heh*¹⁶. follows the paradigm of *shechav* (lie), *rekhav* (ride), and *tzelach* (rested on).¹⁷.

footnotes:
¹⁴ It is vocalized with a cholam
¹⁵ Kavah
¹⁶ Pronounced kav
¹⁷ They are all vocalized with a pattach


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:2)


**נכה בו.** שם הפועל ואין טענה בעבור היותו בה"א כי הנה כמוהו לכלה הפשע:


THAT WE MAY SMITE THEM. *Nakkeh* (that we may smite them)¹⁸. is an infinitive. The fact that it ends in a *heh* is no argument against the latter,¹⁹ changes to a *tav* in the infinitive in words ending in a *heh*. Thus our word should have read *le-nakkot*. Hence I.E.’s comment. for the word *le-khalle*²⁰. (to finish), in *to finish the transgression* (Dan. 9:24) is similar.²¹ rather than the more usual form *le-khallot*.

footnotes:
¹⁸ From the root nun, caf, heh
¹⁹ The heh
²⁰ From the root caf, lamed, heh
²¹ It too is an infinitive, and Scripture employs the form le-khalle


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:6:3)


**יואר.** הוי"ו הנח הנעלם תחת הדגש שהוא תחת אות הכפל כמו על כמון יושב ולא היה כמוהו מאותו בנין בעבור אות הגרון כמשפט:


IS CURSED. The silent *vav of yu’ar* (is cursed) is in place of a *dagesh*, which is in place of the missing double letter.²² is *alef, resh, resh. Yu’ar* is spelled with only one *resh*. Compare, *yusav*²³ and *yu’ar* are both *pu’als*. (turned about)²⁴ comes from the root *samekh, bet, bet*. In I.E.’s version of Scripture, unlike our versions, it does not have a *dagesh*. in *turned about upon the cummin* (Is. 28:27). There is no other word like it in this form, because of the rules concerning gutturals.²⁵. A guttural according to the rules of grammar does not receive a *dagesh*.

footnotes:
²² The root of yu’ar
²³ Yusav
²⁴ Yusav
²⁵ The alef

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:7:1)


**זקני מואב.** חכמים:


THE ELDERS OF MOAB. The wise men.²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ The elders were wise men (Weiser).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:7:2)


**וקסמים בידם.** אמר רבי שמואל הנגיד הספרדי ז"ל שטעמו ודמי הקסמים וראיתי מבידם ולא אמר כלום רק הוא כמשמעו וספר הכתוב ששלח אל קוסם קוסמים כמוהו ועוד שלא יוכל להתעכב לאמר לא אמצא יום נבחר ושעה נבחרת ללכת ולקוב כי הם אנשי אומנותו והעד שפירושו כאשר הוא שאמר הכתוב בימינו היה הקסם:


WITH THE REWARDS OF DIVINATION IN THEIR HAND. Rabbi Samuel Ha-Nagid the Sephardi, of blessed memory, said that the meaning of *u-kesamim* (with the reward of divination)²⁷ is payment for divination. He offers as proof the word *be-yadam* (in their hand).²⁸ refers to the money which they brought with them for the divination. However, he says nothing. *U-kesamim be-yadam* is to be taken literally.²⁹ Scripture tells us that Balak sent diviners like himself to the diviner [Balaam]. Furthermore, Balaam would not be able to tarry, saying, “I can not find a propitious day, and a propitious hour to go and curse,” for the messengers were members of his guild. *In his right hand is the instrument of divination*³⁰ (ha-kesom) (Ez. 21:27) is proof that *u-kesamim be-yadam* is to be taken literally.³¹

footnotes:
²⁷ Balaam was the diviner, not the messengers sent to him. Hence Rabbi Samuel’s interpretation.
²⁸ Rabbi Samuel believed that in their hand
²⁹ It refers to instruments of divination.
³⁰ Translated literally.
³¹ For the verse shows that instruments of divination were held in the hand.

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:9:1)


**ויבא אלהים.** לכבוד ישראל כי השם ידע דבר בעל פעור ואילו היה בלעם מקלל אותם היו כל העולם אומרים כי בעבור קללת בלעם באה המגפה:


AND GOD CAME. Out of regard for Israel, for God knew the coming incident of *ba’al pe’or*.³² Now if Balaam had cursed them then, all of the world would have said that the plague came because of Balaam’s curse.³³

³² A Moabite god worshipped by the Israelites. Twenty-four thousand Israelites died for worshipping this idol. See Num. 25:1-9.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:9:2)


**מי האנשים האלה.** פתחון ותחלת דבור כמו אי הבל אחיך והנה הוא כחש והשם אמר לו קול דמי אחיך:


WHAT MEN ARE THESE. This was a way of opening and starting a conversation.³⁴ It is similar to *Where is Abel thy brother* (Gen. 4:9). Now Cain denied [knowledge of Abel’s whereabouts]. However, God told him, *the voice of thy brother’s blood crieth out unto Me* (Gen. 4 :10).³⁵ was merely a way of starting a conversation.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:12:1)

FOR THEY ARE BLESSED. Its meaning is, you cannot curse them, for I have blessed them.



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:13:1)


**שרי בלק.** ולא הזכיר זקני מדין כי בלק הוא העיקר והוא השולח אליו אמר ר' משה הכהן הספרדי ז"ל כי אף על פי שסימן הפועל ביו"ד לא יתכן להיותו כי אם בנו"ן כמו הלהרגני לא יאמר הלהרגי והנה שכח לתתי להלך עמכם לא אבה יבמי ור' יונה המדקדק אמר כי היו"ד נוסף ואיננו נכון:


THE PRINCES OF BALAK. Scripture does not mention the elders of Midian,³⁶ for Balak was the protagonist and he sent the messengers to Balaam. Rabbi Moses Ha-Kohen the Spaniard, of blessed memory, says that even though the *yod* is the sign of the object³⁷ (give me). it can only come with a *nun*.³⁸ has to be added to the verb in order to indicate the object. Compare, *ha-lehorgeni* (thinkest thou to kill me) (Ex. 2:14). It does not read *ha-lehorgi*. However, he forgot *le-titti* (to give me) in *to give me leave to go with you* [our verse] and *yabbemi* (perform the duty of a husband’s brother unto me) in *he will not perform the duty of a husband’s brother unto me* (Deut. 25:7).³⁹ alone. Rabbi Jonah the grammarian says that the *yod* of *le-titti*⁴⁰ means the same as *la-tet*. It means to give, not to give me. is superfluous, but what he says is incorrect.⁴¹ does not mean that the object (me) is missing.

footnotes:
³⁶ See verse 4 and 7.
³⁷ When suffixed to a verb, as in titti
³⁸ That is, the suffix ni
³⁹ In both of these words the object is indicated by the yod
⁴⁰ In other words, le-titti
⁴¹ For the absence of the nun

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:15:1)

**כי כבד אכבדך.** בממון:


FOR I WILL PROMOTE THEE. With money.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:17:2

**וכל אשר תאמר.** שיש צורך כדי שתקללם והעד כי כבד אכבדך כן שאמר בלעם אם יתן לי בלק מלא ביתו כסף וזהב:


AND WHATSOEVER THOU SAYEST UNTO ME I WILL DO. If this is necessary for you to curse them. Balaam’s statement, *If Balak would give me his house full of silver and gold*, is proof that the meaning of *For I will promote* is, and I will promote you with money.⁴⁷

### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:18:1)


**מלא ביתו.** ביתו מלא וכן מלא כל הארץ כבודו כי רבים טעו שחשבו כי כבוד השם הוא מלא העולם כטעם לה' הארץ ומלואה:


HIS HOUSE FULL. *Melo beto* means his house full.⁴⁸ *Melo khol ha-aretz kevodo* (the whole earth is full of His glory) (Is. 6:3) is similar. Many err who thought that the meaning of the latter is that the contents of the earth is God’s glory,⁴⁹). However, this interpretation is incorrect, for God’s glory consists of much more than the contents of the earth. as in *the earth is the Lord’s, and the fulness thereof* (Ps. 24:1).

⁴⁸ And not the fullness of his house.

**קטנה או גדולה.** תאר השם ויחסר מקום השם וכן ועשיר יענה עזות ומאכלו בריאה:


SMALL OR GREAT. The words *ketannah* (small) and *gedolah* (great) are adjectives. However, the nouns⁵⁰ are missing. *But the rich man answers impudent*⁵¹ (Prov. 18:23)⁵² is short for, but the rich answers impudent words. Thus here too the noun which the adjective modifies is missing. and, *and his food fat*⁵³ (Hab. 1:16)⁵⁴ is short for, And his food a fat lamb. See I.E. on Hab. 1:16. are similar.

### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:19:1)


**בזה.** במקום הזה אמר הגאון ז"ל אם יטעון טוען ויאמר אחר שאמר השם לא תלך עמהם איך אמר קום לך אתם יש להשיב כי השם לא רצה שילך עם האנשים הראשונים עד שיבואו שרים נכבדים מהם ולפי דעתי אין צורך רק טעמו כטעם שלח לך אנשים כי השם אמר לישראל עלה רש והם לא האמינו רק אמרו נשלחה אנשים לפנינו אז שאל משה את השם ואמר לו שלח לך אנשים ואחר שאמר השם לבלעם לא תאור את העם מה צורך היה לו לומר ואדעה מה יוסף ה' דבר רק חשב בלבו מחשבה רעה והשם אמר לו לך עם האנשים רק השמר לך שלא תדבר רק מה שאומר לך והעד על פירושי ויחר אף אלהים כי הולך הוא:


HERE.⁵⁵, literally means in this. Hence I.E.’s comment. In this place. The Gaon, of blessed memory,⁵⁶ says: Should someone argue and say, “How could God tell Balaam rise up, *go with them* (v. 20) after telling him *Thou shalt not go with them* (12)?”⁵⁷ Then we can answer him that God did not want Balaam to go with these messengers but to wait until Balak sent messengers more honorable than they. However, I believe that there is no need for the Gaon’s interpretation. Its meaning is similar to God telling Israel, *Send thou men* (Num. 13:2) and *go up, take possession* (Deut. 1:21). Israel did not believe.⁵⁸ On the contrary, they said, *Let us send men before us* (Deut. 1:22). Moses then asked God.⁵⁹ God told him, *Send thou men* (Num. 13:2).⁶⁰ Now after God told Balaam, *thou shalt not curse the people* (v. 12), what need did Balaam have to say, *that I may know what the Lord will speak unto me more* (v. 19)? Balaam had an evil thought in his heart. God told him go with the men, but take care that you do not say anything save that which I will tell you. *And God’s anger was kindled because he went* (v. 22) is proof that my explanation is correct.⁶¹

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Hebrew, ba-zeh
⁵⁶ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁵⁷ According to the medieval Jewish philosophers, God does not change His mind.
⁵⁸ That they could conquer the Canaanites without first spying out the land.
⁵⁹ Whether to send spies or not.
⁶⁰ God told Balaam not to go to Balak to curse Israel. When Balaam told God that he wanted to go, God told him, go but do not curse Israel. In other words, the prohibition was on the cursing, not on the going.
⁶¹ Otherwise, God would not have been angry at him for going, after he had received permission to go.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:24:1
