### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:28:1)


**ויפתח ה'.** אחז"ל שי' דברים נבראו בערב שבת בין השמשות ולפי דעתי שהטעם שגזר השם לחדש באותות האלה שהם חוץ לתולדות ויאמר הגאון כי לא דברה האתון ורב שמואל בן חפני תפסו ורב שמואל הספרדי בעל השירים חשב להציל הנתפס ודע כי אנשי שקול הדעת הוצרכו להוציא הדברים ממשמעם כי אמדו שהשם לא יחדש אות בעולם לשנות המנהג שברא רק להצדיק נביאו ולא אמרו אמת כי הנה חנניה מישאל ועזריה נעשה להם אות ולא היו נביאים ויש מהם אומרים כי בלעם נביא היה והאמת כי נבואתו היתה בעבור כבוד ישראל כי קוסם היה וכן קראו הכתוב ויש אומרים כי היה יודע דעת עליונים לקבל כחם למטה בצורות וזה טעם ואשר תאור יואר והנכון בעיני כי היה יודע מזלות ובעת ראותו במזל של שום אדם שהגיע עת רעתו היה מקלל אותו ובבא הרעה אל המקולל יחשבו הרואים והשומעים הנמצאים כי בעבור קללתו באה הרעה והעד שדבר במרמה עם שרי בלק וזה טעם לא אוכל לעבור את פי ה' אלהי כי אין יכולת בנוצר לשנות מעשה היוצר או גזרתו והסוד כי החלק לא ישנה החלק רק גזירות הכל תשנה גזירת החלק ולא אוכל לגלות זה הסוד כי עמוק הוא והישר כי האתון דברה ואם תבין סוד מלאכי אברהם גם יעקב אז תבין האמת:


AND THE LORD OPENED THE MOUTH OF THE ASS. The rabbis, of blessed memory, said that ten things were created on Sabbath eve at twilight.⁷² 5:6. I believe that this means that God specifically decreed the creation of these miraculous phenomena,⁷³ 5:6. for they are beyond the laws of nature.⁷⁴ 5:6. The Gaon⁷⁵ says that the ass did not speak. Rabbi Samuel the son of Hofni attacked the Gaon.⁷⁶ However, Rabbi Samuel the Spanish poet tried to save the one who was attacked.⁷⁷ Note, the rationalists had a need for a non-literal interpretation of our text, for they said that God would not create signs in the world to change the laws which he created.⁷⁸ He would do so only to justify his prophet.⁷⁹ However, they did not speak the truth, for, look, a miracle was done for Hananiah, Mishael, and Azariah, who were not prophets.⁸⁰ Some of them⁸¹ say that Balaam was a prophet.⁸² However, the truth is that his prophecy⁸³ was because of Israel’s glory, for Balaam was a diviner. Indeed, this is how Scripture refers to him.⁸⁴ (Josh. 13:22). Some say that Balaam knew the mind of the heavenly beings and was able to channel them by images that he made.⁸⁵ This is the meaning of *and he whom thou cursest is cursed* (v. 6). However, it appears to me that Balaam was an astrologer. When Balaam saw in the star of a given person that an evil time had befallen him, he would curse him. When evil befell the one whom he cursed, then those who witnessed and heard the imprecation thought that the evil came because of Balaam’s curse. The fact that Balaam spoke with guile to the lords of Balak is proof of this.⁸⁶ The meaning of *I can not go beyond the word of the Lord my God* (v. 18) is, it is not in the hand of any created being to change a deed or a decree of God. The secret is, the part⁸⁷ can not change a part.⁸⁸ It is only the decree of the All⁸⁹ which can change the decree of the part.⁹⁰ I cannot reveal this secret for it is very deep. The correct interpretation is, the ass spoke. If you understand the secret of the angels of Abraham⁹¹ and of Jacob,⁹² then you will understand the truth.⁹³

footnotes:
⁷² Among them the mouth of Balaam’s ass. See Abot
⁷³ Mentioned in Abot
⁷⁴ In other words, these miraculous phenomena, one of which was the mouth of Balaam’s ass, were not actually created on Sabbath eve at twilight. God decreed that they come into being at their proper time. Maimonides makes a similar point in his commentary on Abot
⁷⁵ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁷⁶ For saying this.
⁷⁷ Rabbi Samuel the Spanish tried to defend Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁷⁸ The rationalists argue that God would not change the laws of nature.
⁷⁹ The rationalists argue that God would only change the laws of nature to prove that what a prophet said was true. This was not the case with Balaam’s ass.
⁸⁰ They were saved from a fiery furnace. See Dan. 2.
⁸¹ The rationalists.
⁸² Hence God suspended the laws of nature for him.
⁸³ God’s appearance to Balaam.
⁸⁴ Balaam…the soothsayer
⁸⁵ Balaam made images which had the power to manipulate the heavenly powers.
⁸⁶ First he told them that he couldn’t curse Israel. Then he hinted that he could.
⁸⁷ A human being. A human being is part of creation.
⁸⁸ Any of the laws of nature.
⁸⁹ God.
⁹⁰ Of nature.
⁹¹ The angels that appeared to Abraham at Elon Moreh took the form of people. See Gen. 18:1,2.
⁹² The angel that appeared to Jacob at Jabok had the form of a human. See Gen. 32:2; Gen. 32:25-31
⁹³ The angel that appeared to Balaam took the form of a man.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:30:1

### Verse 32

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:32:1)


**כי ירט.** הטעם עוות וכן על ידי רשעים ירטני שהוא מן רטה שאילו היה מן ירט היה ראוי להיות כמו יעטני ואם הם שני שרשים וכמוהו מעיל צדקה יעטני שהוא על משקל אשר יעצני כמו עוטה אור:


BECAUSE THY WAY IS CONTRARY UNTO ME. The meaning of *yarat* (contrary) is perverse.¹⁰³ The word *yirteni* (twists me) in *And twists me by the hands of the wicked*¹⁰⁴ (Job 16:11), which comes from the root *resh, tet, heh*, is similar.¹⁰⁵ comes from the root *resh, tet, heh*. If *yirteni* comes from the root *yod, resh, tet*, then it should be vocalized like *ye’atani*¹⁰⁶ (He hath covered me) (Is. 61:10).¹⁰⁷. *Yarat* and *yirteni* have similar meanings even though they come from two different roots.¹⁰⁸ is *yod, resh, tet* and *yirteni* comes from *resh, tet, heh*. We find the same to be the case with the word *ye’atani* (*Ibid*.), which is vocalized like the word *ye’atzani* (given me counsel) (Ps. 16:7).¹⁰⁹ comes from the root *yod, ayin, tet*. *Ye’atani* is similar in meaning to *oteh* (coverest)¹¹⁰. in (Who coverest Thyself with light) (Ps. 104:2).¹¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰³ I.E. renders our clause, because the way [you have chosen] is perverse in my sight.
¹⁰⁴ Translated according to I.E.
¹⁰⁵ Thus yarat
¹⁰⁶ In meaning.
¹⁰⁷ Yeratani
¹⁰⁸ The root of yarat
¹⁰⁹ Ye’atani
¹¹⁰ From the root ayin, tet, heh
¹¹¹ Both of these words come from different roots but have a similar meaning.



### Verse 33

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:1)


**ותראני האתון.** בקמץ גדול תחת קטן שהיה ראוי להיות ותראני ורבים כן אולי יתחלק לטעמים רבים ופה הוא כמו לולי ומלת גם לעד שמתה האתון אחר שדברה:


AND THE ASS SAW ME. *Va-tirani* is spelled with a *pattach*¹¹². However, this appears to be an error. See Filwarg. in place of a *tzere*. It should have been vocalized *va-tireni*.¹¹³ There are many such cases.¹¹⁴ in place of *yireni*.

footnotes:
¹¹² I.E. reads a long kamatz
¹¹³ See I.E. on Ex. 14:11 (Vol. 2, p. 277) and the notes thereto.
¹¹⁴ See Ex. 33:20, which employs yirani


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:2)


**ואותה החייתי.** דרך קצרה וכן הטעם כאשר הרגתיה הרגתיך או הרגתיך לבדך ואותה החייתי:


UNLESS. The word *ulai* (unless) has many meanings. Here it means unless. The word *even*¹¹⁵, literally, also. is evidence that the ass died after she spoke.¹¹⁶ you implies that he killed the ass.

footnotes:
¹¹⁵ Hebrew, gam
¹¹⁶ I would have slain also


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 22:33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/22:33:3)


וטעם **ואותה החייתי.** כי בן אדם בראותו המלאך ימות והעד ותנצל נפשי ודבר מנוח לעד אף כי הבהמה שאין לה שום שותפות עם רוח המלאך כבן אדם:


AND SAVED HER ALIVE. Scripture is being brief. Its¹¹⁷. meaning is, as I killed her I would have killed you, or I would have killed you and left her alive.¹¹⁸ is short for, I would have killed you and left her alive. The above is explained by the following.¹¹⁹ implies that the ass died. The question arises, “Why?” When a person sees an angel, he dies. *And my life is preserved* (Gen. 32:1),¹²⁰ and the words of Manoah¹²¹ are proof. How much more so the case of a beast that has nothing in common with the spirit of an angel as a human being does.

footnotes:
¹¹⁷ The meaning of surely now I had even slain thee, and saved her alive
¹¹⁸ And saved her alive
¹¹⁹ And saved her alive
¹²⁰ Stated by Jacob with reference to seeing an angel.
¹²¹ Manoah saw an angel and was afraid that he would die. See Jud. 13:22.

GO WITH THE MEN. With these men.

BUT ONLY. The word *efes* means only. The word *efes* (howbeit) in *Howbeit there shall be no needy among you* (Deut. 15: 4) and in *Howbeit* (efes) *the people…are fierce* (Num. 13:28) is similar.

## Chapter 23


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:1:1)


**שבעה מזבחות.** יש סודות עמוקים לא יבינום כי אם מתי מספר ושביעי בימים ובחדשים ובשנים ושבעה כבשי העולה ושבעה הזאות גם אמר השם לאיוב קחו לכם שבעה פרים ושבעה אילים ובתת שלם לשלם אז תתחדש רוח בינה והמשכיל יבין:


SEVEN ALTARS. There are deep secrets which only a few can fathom. The number seven occurs with regard to days,¹ months,² years;³ the seven lambs offered for a burnt offering;⁴ the seven sprinklings.⁵ God also told Job, *take unto you seven bullocks and seven rams* (Job 42:8). When the complete⁶ is added to the complete,⁷ then a spirit of understanding is newly created.⁸ The intelligent will understand what I speak of.

footnotes:
¹ The seventh day is holy.
² Rosh Ha-Shanah falls on the first day of the seventh month.
³ The seventh year is a sabbatical year. The year of jubilee similarly follows seven sabbatical years.
⁴ Offered on the new moon and the festivals, with the exception of Sukkot. See Num. 28 and 29. On Sukkot, 14 lambs (2 times 7) were offered daily as a burnt offering.
⁵ On Yom Kippur and on the leper. See Lev. 16:14; 14:7. All of these are among these secrets.
⁶ Seven is a complete number.
⁷ To seven. When seven bullocks are added to seven lambs and placed on seven altars. Or when seven animals are sacrificed to God the complete, the celebrant is prophetically inspired.
⁸ In the celebrant; i.e., he is prophetically inspired.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:2:1)


פר **ואיל במזבח.** בכל מזבח והיא דרך קצרה כאילו אמר במזבח האחד מכל המזבחות:


ON EVERY ALTAR A BULLOCK AND A RAM.⁹ On each and every altar.¹⁰ Scripture is being brief. It is short for, on one of the many altars.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ Literally, a bullock and a ram on the altar.
¹⁰ Balaam built seven altars. It is unlikely that he built seven altars and used only one. Hence I.E.’s comment.
¹¹ Balaam used all seven altars. However, Scripture tells us what he offered on one of the altars.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:1)


**יקרה.** מגזרת לקראתי:


WILL COME. *Yikkareh* (will come) is derived from *li-kerati* (to meet me).¹² means will meet me. AND WHATSOEVER¹³. HE SHOWETH ME. The word *devar* is connected to *mah*.¹⁴ is connected by hyphen to *yareni* (He showeth me), for the word *devar* is in the construct. If the word were in the absolute it would be vocalized *davar*. Thus our text should be read, *u-devar-mah yareni* (And whatsoever He showeth me), rather than *u-devar mah-yareni*. (And a thing of, what will He show me).

footnotes:
¹² Thus yikkareh
¹³ Hebrew, devar mah
¹⁴ Even though the word mah


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:2)


**ודבר.** סמוך אל מה:


The term *devar* means “thing of.” *Mah* literally means what.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:3:3)


**וילך שפי.** דברי המתרגם ארמית ידועים שהוא לבדו והנה שפי לבדו גם הוא בלי אח ואחרים אמרו כי הוא כמו נכאה לכב מגזרת ושפו עצמותיו והנכון בעיני שהוא מגזרת קול על שפיים וימצא ביו"ד גם באל"ף כמלת פתאים והטעם שהלך אל שפי ותחסר מלת אל כמו ויבא ירושלים וכן נסעו העם חצרות ורבים כן והנה הוא מנחש בלכתו כי כן כתוב ולא הלך כפעם בפעם לקראת נחשים והנה גם פה רמזתי לך סוד חתום:


AND HE WENT TO A BARE HEIGHT. The manner in which the Aramaic translator renders *shefi* (bare) is known. He translates *shefi* as alone.¹⁵ Now *shefi* is alone; it is also without a brother.¹⁶ Other say that the word *shefi* means *broken in heart* (Ps. 109:16).¹⁷ They say that the word *shefi* is related to *shuppu* (corrode) in *and his bones corrode* (Job 33:21). However, I believe that the word *shefi* is related to *shefayim* (high hills) in *upon the high hills is heard* (Jer. 3:20). The word is found spelled both with a *yod*¹⁸ and with an *alef*.¹⁹. We find the same thing with the word *peta’im* (simple) (Prov. 1:4).²⁰, and other times with a *yod*. The meaning of *va-elekh shefi* (and he went to a bare height) is, he went to a high hill. The word *el* (to) has been omitted from the phrase.²¹. Our texts reads *va-elekh shefi*, which literally means, and he went to high place. Hence I.E.’s comment. We find the same in *va-yavo yerushalayim* (and he came to Jerusalem)²² (and he came to Jerusalem), for the literal meaning of *va-yavo yerushalayim* is, and he came Jerusalem. (I Kings 3:15) and in *nase’u ha-am hazerot* (the people journeyed unto Hazeroth)²³ (the people journeyed unto Hazeroth), for *nase’u ha-am hazerot* means the people journeyed Hazeroth. (Num. 11:35). There are many such instances.²⁴ has to be added to the text. Observe, Balaam was divining²⁵ when he went,²⁶ for Scripture clearly states, *he went not, as at other times*²⁷ *to meet with enchantments*²⁸ (Num. 24:1). See, I have given you a hint regarding a sealed secret.²⁹

footnotes:
¹⁵ Onkelos renders our clause, and he went alone.
¹⁶ That is, it is not found again in Scripture.
¹⁷ These commentaries render our clause: And he went with a broken heart.
¹⁸ In Job 33:21.
¹⁹ Is. 49:9. See I.E. on Is. 49:9. It should be pointed out that our texts of Is. 49:9 do not spell the word with an alef
²⁰ It is sometimes spelled with an alef
²¹ Our text should be read as if written va-elekh el shefi
²² This should be read as if written va-yavo el yerushalayim
²³ This should be read as if written nase’u ha-am el hazerot
²⁴ Where the preposition el
²⁵ I.E. does not describe the divination which Balaam employed. He may have cast lots, read the entrails of sacrificed animals, interpreted clouds, or the like.
²⁶ To the mountain.
²⁷ Which implies that until then he divined.
²⁸ Divining.
²⁹ That Balaam practiced enchantment using the number seven.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:7:3

**לכה ארה לי יעקב.** תחסר מלת ואמר ובשיר השירים כמוהו רבים והיא דרך קצרה:


COME, CURSE ME JACOB. “And he said” has been omitted from our clause.³³ We find many such instances in the Song of Songs. This is an abridged style.

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:7:4)


**זועמה ישראל.** הטעם כפול כמשפט שהוא דרך לדבר טעם אחד במלות משונו' וישנוהו לחזוק והיתה ראויה מלת זועמה להיותה על משקל זכרה לי אלהי רק כאשר נפתח העי"ן בעבור היותו מן הגרון היה היה בינו ובין הזי"ן חול"ם תחת קמץ חטף:


EXECRATE ISRAEL. Scripture repeats itself ³⁴ repeats *Come curse me Jacob*. as is its custom. Its manner is to employ different words to express the same thought. It repeats itself for emphasis. The word *zo’amah* (execrate)³⁵ is vocalized with a *cholam*. should have been vocalized like the word *zokhrah* (remember)³⁶ is vocalized with a short *kamatz*. in *Remember unto me, O my God* (Neh. 5:19). However, since the *ayin*³⁷. was vocalized with a *pattach*³⁸. because it is a guttural, a *cholam* rather than a short *kamatz* was placed between it and the *zayin*.

footnotes:
³⁴ And come execrate Israel
³⁵ Zo’amah
³⁶ Zokrah
³⁷ Of zo’amah
³⁸ Rather than with a sheva



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:8:1)


**מה אקוב לא קבה אל.** כטעם קללה והה"א תחת וי"ו כה"א אהלה וכן ה"א כי פרעה אהרן והמלה מפעלי הכפל והשלמה קבבו כמו סבבו ואם היא מלה זרה:


HOW SHALL I CURSE, WHOM GOD HATH NOT CURSED. The word *kabboh* means to curse. Its *heh* is in place of a *vav*.³⁹ rather than *kabbo*. The latter is the usual form. Hence I.E.’s comment. It is like the *heh* in *oholoh*⁴⁰ rather than the more usual *oholo*. (his tent) (Gen. 12:8) and the *heh* in *fera’oh*⁴¹. (let them loose) in *for Aaron had let them loose* (Ex. 32:25). *Kabboh* comes from a double root.⁴². Its full form⁴³ is *kavevo* (they curse him). Compare, *savevo*⁴⁴. Both double root letters are present in *savevu*. (they surrounded him) although it is irregular.⁴⁵ is not found in Scripture (Krinsky). The form used is *sevavuhu* (I Kings 5:17). For other interpretations see Filwarg and Meijler.

footnotes:
³⁹ The word is spelled kabboh
⁴⁰ The word is written oholoh
⁴¹ Rather than fe’aro
⁴² Its root is kaf, bet, bet
⁴³ In the perfect.
⁴⁴ From the root samekh, bet, bet
⁴⁵ The word savevo


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:8:2)


**ומה אזעם לא זעם ה'.** מגזרת זעם והטעם כפול:


AND HOW SHALL I EXECRATE, WHOM GOD HATH NOT EXECRATED. The words *ezam* (shall I execrate) and *za’am* (execrate) come from the root, *zayin, ayin, mem* (anger). Scripture repeats itself.⁴⁶

### Verse 9

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:9:1)


**כי מראש צורים אראנו.** כי על השפי היה והוא גבוה והעד על השפי שהוא כפירושי ולא הלך כפעם בפעם לקראת נחשים וישת אל המדבר פניו ולא אל שפי גם יתכן להיות מראש צורים משל לגזירות היורדות מהעליונים כי ראה בחכמתו שתעמוד זאת האומה לבד' ולא תתערב עם אחרות מתגברת עליה לעזוב תורתה כאשר עשו כל האומות:


FOR FROM THE TOP OF THE ROCKS I SEE HIM. Balaam said this because he was high up on the hill. He went not, as at other times, to meet with enchantments. *But he set his face toward the wilderness* (Num. 24:1)⁴⁷ and not toward the high place⁴⁸ is proof that my interpretation of *shefi*⁴⁹ is correct.⁵⁰ (wilderness), which is a flat area, with *sefi*. Thus *sefi* must refer to an elevated area. It is also likely that *from the top of the rocks* is a parable symbolizing the decrees that come down from heaven. For Balaam in his wisdom⁵¹ saw that this nation would stand alone and not intermingle with other nations that would overpower her and try to force her to give up God’s Torah, as the nations did.

⁴⁷ I.E. assumes that the wilderness is a flat area.
⁴⁸ Which he previously faced.
⁴⁹ See I.E. on verse 3.
⁵⁰ For Scripture contrasts midbar
⁵¹ Which came to him from on high.

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:9:2)


**ובגוים לא יתחשב.** אפרשנו בפסוק יצב גבולות עמים:


AND SHALL NOT BE RECKONED AMONG THE NATIONS. I will explain this in my comments on *When the Most High gave to the nations their inheritance* (Deut. 32:8).



### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:1)


**מי מנה.** והתימה שלא יתחשב עם הגוים והם עם רב כחול שיתכן שלא יתחשב המעט:


WHO HATH COUNTED. It is a wonder that Israel is not counted with the nations, when they are a nation as numerous as the sand. It is likely for a small nation not to be counted.⁵²

footnotes:
⁵² Because of its insignificance, but not a large one whose number is like the sand.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:2)


**עפר יעקב.** הטעם שהוא רב כעפר הארץ:


THE DUST OF JACOB. Its meaning is: Jacob is as numerous as the dust of the earth.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:3)


**ומספר את רבע ישראל.** דגל ישראל. דגל אחד כטעם אפס קצהו תראה וכלו לא תראה:


OR NUMBERED THE STOCK OF ISRAEL? One standard.⁵³ (stock) as a quarter. The camp of Israel was divided into four standards (Num 2:1-31). Thus a quarter of Israel was one standard. It means the same as *thou shalt see but the utmost part of them, and shalt not see them all* (v. 13).⁵⁴

footnotes:
⁵³ I.E. interprets rova
⁵⁴ The idea of our verse is, who can count even part of Israel.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:10:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:10:4)


ויפה פירש המתרגם ארמית מלת ישרים וטעם לפי דעתי שהתאוה למות ותהיה אחריתו כאחרית ישראל שהם חלק השם ולא חלק כוכבים כי הוא היה קוסם ויש אומרים שמלת ותהי אחריתי כפולה בטעם והוא היה מתאוה שימות מות ישרים כמו הישרים של ישראל בעבור דעתו כי בחרב ימות והגאון אמר כי מי מנה הוא השם והטעם אתה שתוכל למנות עפר יעקב המת נפשי מות ישרים אם כן מה טעם ומספר את רובע ישראל ולפי דעת רבים שפירושו כמו ארחי ורבעי זרית שהוא כענין רבצי ויפרשו עפר יעקב על עת נסעו:


[LET ME DIE THE DEATH OF THE RIGHTEOUS, AND LET MINE END BE LIKE HIS!] The Aramaic translator beautifully explained the meaning of *yesharim* (the righteous).⁵⁵ as the honest.I believe that Balaam wanted to die. He wanted his end to be like the end of Israel, who are God’s portion and are not ruled by the stars, for he was a magician.⁵⁶ Others say that *mine end* repeats *let me die*. Balaam wanted to die like the righteous of Israel, for he knew that he would die by the sword.⁵⁷ The Gaon⁵⁸ says that *Who hath counted* refers to God. Our verse means: You who are able to count the dust of Jacob, give me the death of the righteous. However, if this is so, then what meaning is there to *Or numbered the stock of Israel*?⁵⁹ after saying, *You who are able to count the dust of Israel*. Many explain that *rova* (the stock of) is similar in meaning to *rivi*, which means my lying down. Compare, *Thou measurest my going about and my lying down* (rivi) (Ps. 139:3). They explain that the *dust of Jacob* refers to the time that Jacob journeys.⁶⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁵ Onkelos renders yesharim
⁵⁶ According to this interpretation Balaam made two requests: to die the death of the righteous and to be granted a share in the world to come.
⁵⁷ According to this interpretation Balaam had one request, to die the death of Israel. The Bible merely repeats itself.
⁵⁸ Rabbi Saadiah Gaon.
⁵⁹ For Balaam would not say to God, You who are able to count a standard of Israel
⁶⁰ They interpret our verse as follows: Who can count Israel when Israel camps; Who can count Israel when Israel journeys.



### Verse 11

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:11:1)


משפט הלשון להקדים שם הפועל בלשון עבר ועתיד ובצווי הדבר הפוך ויש מלות זרות כמו ברכת ברך ובא בא (ושטף ועבר):


[THOU HAST BLESSED THEM ALTOGETHER.] It is normal usage to place the infinitive first in the perfect and then the imperfect.⁶¹ The reverse is true in the imperative.⁶² However, there are irregular phrases such as *u-verakhta barekh*⁶³) comes last. (thou hast blessed them altogether) (Num. 24:10); *and yava vo*⁶⁴) comes last. (and he shall come on) in *and he shall come on, and overflow, as he passeth through* (Dan. 11:1).

⁶² In the imperative the infinitive comes first and the imperative second. See Is. 6:9.
⁶³ Here the infinitive (barekh
⁶⁴ Here too the infinitive (vo



### Verse 13

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:13:1)


**אפס קצהו.** רק קצהו:


BUT THE UTMOST. The meaning of *efes katzehu* is, but the utmost.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:13:2)


**וקבנו.** מלה זרה ולפי הדקדוק כי הצווי כעתיד ואין ביניהם רק הסימן ירדפהו ירדפנו ירדפוהו אויב ירדפו ובאה זו המלה מורכבת עם נו"ן ובחול"ם ולפי דעת רבי יהודה המדקדק הראשון שהוא מן שורש קבן א"כ לא יתכן להיותה צווי כי ימצא כן מהבנין הקל אולי תהיה שם הפועל על משקל ומשמרו את השבועה:


AND CURSE ME THEM. *Ve-kovno* (and curse them) is irregular.⁶⁵, for that is the rule when a *nun vav* is used to indicate the pronoun. According to the rules of grammar the imperative is the same as the imperfect.⁶⁶ The only difference between them is the prefix.⁶⁷ Compare, *yirdefehu* (he shall pursue him); *yirdefenu* (he shall pursue him); *yirdefuhu* (they shall pursue him); and *yirdefo* (shall pursue him⁶⁸ is represented by a *cholam* only when a *heh vav* or a *nun vav* is not used to indicate the pronoun.) in *The enemy shall pursue him* (Hos. 8:3).⁶⁹ However, *kovno* comes with a *nun* vocalized with a *cholam* joined to the root. According to Rabbi Judah, the first grammarian, the word *kovno* comes from the root *kof, bet, nun*.⁷⁰ is not a sign of the pronoun. The word *kovno* is similar to *yirdefo*; i.e., the pronoun is represented by a *cholam*. If this is the case, then *kovno* cannot be an imperative, for we do not find such a vocalization in the *kal*.⁷¹ signified by a *vav* (Filwarg). Perhaps *kovno* is an infinitive vocalized like the word *shomro* (keep) in *and because He would keep the oath* (Deut. 7:8).

footnotes:
⁶⁵ The word should have read ve-kavnu
⁶⁶ It is vocalized similarly.
⁶⁷ A prefix is placed before the verb in the imperfect.
⁶⁸ The pronoun him
⁶⁹ These are examples of verbs in the imperfect combined with the pronoun.
⁷⁰ Thus the nun
⁷¹ We do not find the imperative combined with the pronoun him



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:1)


**וישא משלו.** הוא כתועפת ראם כלביא יקום:


AND HE TOOK UP HIS PARABLE. The reference is to *like the lofty horns of the wild-ox* (v. 22) and *a people that riseth up as a lioness* (v. 24).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:2)


**האזינה עדי.** מגזרת עד וכן לא שבתם עדי והטעם כפול. וי"ו בנו בעור נוסף כמו למעינו מים:


GIVE EAR UNTO ME. The word *adai* (unto me) is related to the word *ad* (unto). The word *adai* (unto Me) in *Yet have ye not returned unto Me* (Amos 4:6) is similar. Scripture repeats itself.⁷² The *vav* in *beno tzippor* (son of Zippor) is superfluous.⁷³ means son of, not his son. It is similar to the *vav* of *le-mayeno* (a fountain) in *into a fountain of waters* (Ps. 114:8).⁷⁴ in this verse means a fountain of, not a fountain.

footnotes:
⁷² The second part of the last clause repeats its first part.
⁷³ Here beno
⁷⁴ Le-mayeno


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:18:3)


וטעם **לא איש אל.** בעבור שאמר וקבנו לי משם וכבר אמר לא קבה אל:


[GOD IS NOT A MAN.] Balaam said this in response to Balak’s request, *and curse me them from thence* (v. 13). However, Balaam had already told him, *God hath not cursed* (v. 8).⁷⁵

footnotes:
⁷⁵ He therefore told him now that God is not a man and consequently does not alter His words.



### Verse 19

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:19:1)


**אמר ולא יעשה.** אמרו:


WHEN HE HATH SAID, WILL HE NOT DO IT? His word.⁷⁶

footnotes:
⁷⁶ It refers to His word.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:19:2)


**ודבר.** דברה:


OR WHEN HE HATH SPOKEN. A word.



### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:20:1)


**הנה ברך לקחתי.** שם הפועל כמו שם:


BEHOLD, I AM BIDDEN TO BLESS. *Barekh* (to bless)⁷⁷ is an infinitive. It has the same meaning as a noun.⁷⁸) with the meaning of a noun (*borakhah*). The meaning of our clause is, I have taken a blessing; i.e., God has given me a blessing.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ Literally, blessing.
⁷⁸ Scripture employs an infinitive (barekh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:20:2)


**וברך.** פועל עבר והטעם והשם ברך ברכה ולא אשיבנה:


AND WHEN HE HATH BLESSED. *U-verekh* (and when he hath blessed) is a verb in the perfect. Its meaning is, when God pronounced a blessing, I cannot call it back.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:1)


**לא הביט און.** לפי דעתי כי מזאת המלה למד בלק לשלוח נשי מואב במחנה ישראל והטעם כי אין השם כבן אדם שיתנחם כי לא ראה און בישראל והנה אם יהיה בהם און לא יקום דבר השם כי כל דבריו הם על תנאי רק במקום שבועה וכן הנביא אומר רגע אדבר וזה טעם בדבר בלעם:


NONE HATH BEHELD INIQUITY IN JACOB.⁷⁹ I believe that Balak learned from this utterance to send Moabite women into the camp of Israel.⁸⁰ Scripture’s meaning is that God is not like a man that he should repent (v. 19), for He did not see iniquity in Israel. However, if there be iniquity in Israel, then God’s word will not come to pass, for all of God’s words are conditional unless accompanied by an oath. Compare, *At one instant I may speak concerning a nation…to build and to plant…but if it do evil in My sight…then I repent of the good, wherewith I said I would benefit it*. (Jer. 18:7-10). This is the meaning of Balaam’s statement.

footnotes:
⁷⁹ I.E. renders this as: He (God) did not see iniquity in Israel.
⁸⁰ Therefore Balaam reasoned that it would pay for Balak to send Moabite women to the camp of Israel in order to make them sin. See Num. 25:1-3.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:2)


**ולא ראה עמל.** הטעם כפול והוא אחי האון והטעם מה יביא האדם לעמל בסוף און. והנה כל עת שלא הביט און ביעקב השם עמו:


NEITHER HATH ONE SEEN PERVERSENESS IN ISRAEL.⁸¹ Scripture repeats itself.⁸² The word *amal* (perverseness) is brother to *aven* (iniquity). *Amal* (perverseness) is so called because iniquity (*aven*) ultimately leads to it. Look, as long as God does not see iniquity in Jacob, He is with Israel.⁸³

footnotes:
⁸¹ I.E. renders this as: Neither hath He (God) seen perverseness in Israel.
⁸² Neither hath He (God) seen perverseness in Israel repeats, He (God) did not see iniquity in Israel
⁸³ I.E.’s interpretation of The Lord God is with him…


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:21:3)


**ותרועת מלך בו.** במחנה ישראל וזה ותקעתם תרועה:


AND THE SHOUTING FOR⁸⁴. THE KING IS AMONG THEM. In the camp of Israel. The reference is to *and when ye blow an alarm* (tru’ah) (Num. 10:5).⁸⁵). The word used for shouting in our verse is *tru’at*. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
⁸⁴ Hebrew, tru’at
⁸⁵ Shouting refers to the sounding of the shofar in honor of God (tru’at.



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:22:1)


**אל מוציאם ממצרים.** וזה השם שהיה עמו הוא תקיף והראה אותות במצרים:


GOD WHO BROUGHT THEM FORTH OUT OF EGYPT. This God who brought them out of Egypt is mighty and showed his miracles in Egypt.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:22:2)


**כתועפת.** כמו קרני ראם ופירוש תועפות תוקף וכן ותועפות הרים לו גם יתכן היות פירושו וקרני הרים וכסף תועפות לך פירושו וכסף תועפות הרים ואמר לו כמו ותרועת מלך בו והטעם שזה האל שם תוקף לישראל וכל זה בעבור שהוא דבוק בשם ולא יבקש כי אם מאתו כי אין להם צורך למנחש וקוסם:


LIKE THE LOFTY HORNS OF THE WILD-OX. *Ke-to’afot re’em* (like the lofty horns of the wild-ox) is similar to *karne re’em* (the horns of the wild-ox) (Deut. 33:17).⁸⁶ refers to an animal. The meaning of *to’afot is*, the might of.⁸⁷ *To’afot* (the heights of) in *The heights of the mountain are His also* (Ps. 95:4) is similar.⁸⁸ It is also possible that the meaning⁸⁹ of *ve-to’afot harim lo* (the heights of the mountains are His) is, the horns of the mountains are also His.⁹⁰. The meaning of *ve-kehsef to’afot lakh* (and precious silver unto thee) (Job 22:25) is, and silver, as mighty as the mountains.⁹¹ is short for *to’afot harim* (might of the mountains). Scripture reads *lo* (to him). [It means to them.] Compare, *and the shouting for the King is among them* (v. 21). It means that this God made Israel strong. God did all this for them because Israel cleaves to God and does not seek anything from anyone but the Lord, for they have no need for an enchanter and a diviner.

footnotes:
⁸⁶ In other words, re’em
⁸⁷ The horns represent the might of the animal.
⁸⁸ According to I.E., Ps. 95:4 should be rendered: the might of the mountain are also His.
⁸⁹ The literal meaning of.
⁹⁰ Horns representing might. There is no difference in meaning between the two interpretations. They only differ with regard to the literal meaning of to’afot
⁹¹ See I.E. on Job 22:25. To’afot



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:1)


**כעת יאמר ליעקב.** כעת הזאת והטעם בכל עת יאמר להם בלי דעת נחוש:


NOW IT IS SAID OF JACOB. *Ka-et* (now) is to be rendered, like this time.⁹² is, like a time. I.E. explains that this means like this time. Its meaning is: at all times it will be told to them without the assistance of an enchanter.⁹³

footnotes:
⁹² The literal meaning of ka-et
⁹³ What God will do.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:2)


**מה פעל אל.** מה יפעל אל ומלת עבר בעבור היות כל דבר נגזר ואם הוא לעתיד כבר נגזר בתחלה וזה יאמר לישראל בדרך נבואה שהוא האמת:


WHAT HATH GOD WROUGHT. *Mah pa’al el* (what hath God wrought) is to be rendered, what God shall do. All things decreed, even those decreed for the future, are decreed in the past. Hence Scripture employs a perfect (*pa’al)*.⁹⁴ This will be told to Israel prophetically. What is told in prophecy is the truth.

footnotes:
⁹⁴ For what God will do.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:23:3)


**ליעקב ולישראל.** דבר כפול בנבואה וכן מראש שניר וחרמון:


OF JACOB AND OF ISRAEL. Scripture repeats itself in a prophetic utterance. Compare, *From the top of Senir and Hermon* (Song of Songs 4:8).⁹⁵

footnotes:
⁹⁵ Senir and Hermon refer to the same mountain. See Deut. 3:9.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:24:1)


**הן עם כלביא יקום.** רמז כי ינצח מלכי כנען והקרוב אל הדעת הדבר הקרוב כמו מלחמת מדין שלא נשמע מלחמה כמוה שלא נפקד מזרע ישראל אחד ומתו חמשה מלכים ונפש אדם אשר לא ידעו משכב זכר היו שלשים ושתים אלף:


BEHOLD A PEOPLE THAT RISETH UP AS A LIONESS. This hints that Israel will conquer the kings of Canaan. It seems most logical to interpret it this way regarding a soon-to-occur event,⁹⁶ such as the war against Midian.⁹⁷ A war like it was unprecedented. No Israelites were lost in this war⁹⁸ and five kings died in it,⁹⁹ along with thirty-two thousand females who never slept with a man.¹⁰⁰

footnotes:
⁹⁶ That it relates to a war that occurred prior to the conquest of Canaan.
⁹⁷ See Num. 31:1-12.
⁹⁸ See Num. 31:49.
⁹⁹ Num. 31:8.
¹⁰⁰ Num. 31:35.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:24:2)


**ודם חללים.** המלקוח ואת כל חילם ועל דרך הדרש לא ישכב משה עד יאכל טרף כן כתוב אחר תאסף אל עמך:


AND DRINK THE BLOOD OF THE SLAIN. The spoil and all their wealth.¹⁰¹ According to the midrashic interpretation it means that Moses would not die until he ate of the prey.¹⁰² Scripture similarly reads: *Afterward shalt thou be gathered unto thy people* (Num. 31:2).

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ The wealth of the slain. See Num. 31:11.
¹⁰² Moses wouldn’t die until he first defeated and looted the Midianites.



### Verse 27

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:27:1)


**וקבתו לי.** מפעלי הכפל כמו וסבותי אני:


THAT THOU MAYEST CURSE ME THEM. *Ve-kabbota* (that thou mayest curse) comes from a double root.¹⁰³. Compare, *ve-sabboti ani* (therefore I turned) (Eccles. 2:20).¹⁰⁴ comes from the root *samekh, bet, bet*.

footnotes:
¹⁰³ Its root is kof, bet, bet
¹⁰⁴ Sabboti



### Verse 28

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 23:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/23:28:1)


**ראש הפעור.** ולא הלך לקראת נחשים על כן היתה עליו רוח אלהים וזה עשה בעבור שראה כי לא נחש ביעקב:


UNTO THE TOP OF PEOR. Balaam did not seek to use sorcery, so God’s spirit came upon him. Balaam acted like this because he saw *that there is no enchantment with Jacob* (Num. 23:23).



## Chapter 24


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:1:1)


**וישת אל המדבר פניו.** ששם ישראל בערבות מואב:


BUT HE SET HIS FACE TOWARD THE WILDERNESS. Where Israel was, in the plains of Moab.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:2:1)


**שכן לשבטיו.** ראה כל הדגולים י"א כי בלעם היה רע עין הנקרא מרע וברעת עינו היה עושה רעות וכבר הודעתיך דעתי:


DWELLING TRIBE BY TRIBE. He saw all the standards.¹ Some say that Balaam had a diabolical eye. Such people are called evildoers. Balaam did evil by employing his diabolic eye. I have already made my opinion known to you.²

footnotes:
¹ He saw Israel camped around their standards.
² See I.E. on Num. 22:28.



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:1)


**וישא משלו.** הטעם שנשא קול במשלו והמשל כנחלים נטיו:


AND HE TOOK UP HIS PARABLE.³, literally, and he lifted up his parable and said. Hence I.E.’s comment. This means he lifted up his voice and uttered his parable. The parable was, *As valleys stretched out*⁴ (v. 6).

footnotes:
³ Hebrew, va-yissa meshalo va-yomar
⁴ The parable ends with verse 9. What comes before is a description, not a parable.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:2)


**נאם.** דבר כמו וינאמו נאם י"א כי טעם שומע גם יחזה דבר הנחוש והקרוב אל הדעת שהוא דרך הנבואה בחלום כדברי אליפז תמונה לנגד עיני דממה וקול אשמע:


THE SAYING OF. The word *ne’um* means the saying of. Compare, *va-yimamu ne’um* (and say: He saith) (Jer. 23:31). Some say that *who heareth* and also *Who seeth* means who hears and who sees by divination. However, it most likely refers to that which is heard and seen in a prophetic dream. Compare Eliphaz’s statement, *A form was before mine eyes; I heard a still voice* (Job 4:16).⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Eliphaz was describing a vision he had when asleep.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:3:3)


**שתם העין.** אין לו אח רק פירושו כפי מקומו הפך סתום וי"א כי עין אחד היתה לבלעם וזה דרך דרש:


WHOSE EYE IS OPENED. The word *shetum* (opened) has no brother.⁶ It can be interpreted only in context. *Shetum* is the opposite of *setum* (shut).⁷ means whose eye is opened. Some say that Balaam had only one eye.⁸ However, this is a midrashic interpretation.

footnotes:
⁶ It does not occur elsewhere in Scripture.
⁷ Thus shetum ha-ayin
⁸ See Rashi.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:4:1)


**נפל.** כמו תרדמה נפלה על אברם:


FALLEN DOWN. Compare, *a deep sleep fell upon Abram* (Gen. 15:12).⁹ means fallen down in sleep.

footnotes:
⁹ In other words, fallen down


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:4:2)


**וגלוי עינים.** אף על פי שהוא ישן הוא רואה בעיניו כטעם ולבי ער:


YET WITH OPENED EYES. Even though he sleeps, he sees with his eyes. Compare, [*I sleep*] *but my heart waketh* (Cant. 5:2).



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:5:1)


**מה טבו.** פעל עבר כמו כי אורו עיני:


HOW GOODLY. *Tovu* (goodly) is a verb in the perfect. It is similar to *oru* (are brightened) in *how mine eyes have brightened* (I Sam. 14:29).



### Verse 6

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:1)


**נטיו.** דגשות הטי"ת להתבלע נו"ן נטה כי יש נחלים שינטו כה וכה ועליהם אילנים:


STRETCHED OUT. The *tet* of *nittayu* (stretched out) rceives a *dagesh* to compensate for the *nun* of *natah* (stretched),¹⁰ comes from the root *nun, tet, heh*. which is swallowed.¹¹ is a *nifal*. Its *nun* is the *nun* of the *nifal* form, not the *nun* of the root. If the *nun* of the root were present, the word would read *nintayu*. Scripture reads *stretched out* because there are rivers that turn here and there and have trees over them.

footnotes:
¹⁰ In other words, nittayu
¹¹ Nittayu


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:2)


**כאהלים.** כמו מר ואהלות והנה דמה האהלים כנחלים נטיו:


AS ALOES. *Ahalim* (aloes) is similar to the word *ahalot* (aloes) in *myrrh, and aloes* (Ps. 45:9).¹² (aloes) is in the masculine and *ahalot* (aloes) in the feminine. Note, Balaam compares the tents¹³ to stretched-out rivers.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹² Even though ahalim
¹³ Of Israel.
¹⁴ Next to which cedar and aloe trees are planted.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:6:3)


**כארזים.** כפול בטעם והזכיר הארזים כארז בלבנון ישגה:


AS CEDARS. Scripture repeats itself.¹⁵ Balaam mentions cedars in the sense of *He shall grow like a cedar in Lebanon* (Ps. 92:13).

footnotes:
¹⁵ Cedars are parallel to aloes.



### Verse 7

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:1)


**יזל מים.** הנה מים לשון יחיד כמו מי נדה לא זורק עליו:


WATER SHALL FLOW. Look, *mayim* is in the singular.¹⁶ is a plural form. The word *yizzal* (shall flow), which modifies *mayim* (water), is in the singular. Hence I.E. comment. Compare, *the water of* (mai) *sprinkling was not dashed* (zorak)¹⁷, which modifies *mayim*, is in the singular. See I.E. on Gen. 1:1 (Vol. 1, p. 31). *against him* (Num. 19:13).

¹⁶ Even though mayim
¹⁷ Zorak


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:2)


**מדליו.** כמו מדליותיו:


FROM HIS BRANCHES. *Dalyo* (his branches) is similar to *daliyyotav* (his branches) (Jer. 11:16).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:3)


**וזרעו במים רבים.** כזרע שרוה במים והטעם שכל יום יצמח ויגדל:


AND HIS SEED SHALL BE IN MANY WATERS. Like seed saturated with water. This means he shall flourish and grow every day.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:7:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:7:4)


**וירום מאגג מלכו.** נבואה על שאול שהוא המלך הראשון כי קודם שאול היו שופטים ולא מלכים:


AND HIS KING SHALL BE HIGHER THAN AGAG. This is a prophecy regarding Saul, Israel’s first king, for prior to Saul there were judges but no kings.



### Verse 8

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:1)


**אל מוציאו.** והטעם כי האל המוציאו ממצרים שם כח לו כתועפות ראם ואל יטעון טוען בעבור מ"ם אל מוציאם ויחשוב כי מלת לו סימן לשם כי כן משפט הלשון כמו תכו לרגליך ישא מדברותיך עד אנה ינאצוני העם הזה ורבים כן:


GOD WHO BROUGHT HIM FORTH. The meaning of our verse is: the God who brought him [Israel] out of Egypt made him as powerful as the lofty horns of the wild-ox. Let not the one who would dispute this claim that the word him¹⁸ (him) in the clause *Is for him like the lofty horns of the wild-ox*. refers to God, because Scripture earlier used the plural form *motzi’am* (brought them forth) when it stated, *God who brought them forth* (Num. 23:22).¹⁹) like the lofty horns of the wild-ox. Our verse reads: God who brought him out of Egypt, for him (*lo*) like the lofty horns of the wild-ox. Now, just as the word *lo* (him) in the first verse applies to God, so must the word *lo* (him) in the second verse apply to God. For such is the style of Scripture.²⁰ in Num. 23:22 applies to Israel and similarly in our verse. Compare, *And they*²¹ *sit at Thy feet; he*²² *receives* (yissa) *Thy words* (Deut. 33:3); and *How long will this* (ha-zeh)²³ (this) is in the singular. *people despise Me* (yena’atzuni)²⁴ (will despise me). (Num. 14:11). There are many such instances.

footnotes:
¹⁸ The reference is to the word lo
¹⁹ Num. 23:22 literally reads: God who brought them forth out of Egypt, for Him (lo
²⁰ To refer to the nation of Israel both in the singular and in the plural in one verse. Thus the word lo
²¹ Israel.
²² Israel; translated literally.
²³ Zeh
²⁴ Yena’atzuni


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:2)


**יאכל גוים צריו.** מלכי כנען:


HE SHALL EAT UP THE NATIONS THAT ARE HIS ADVERSARIES. The reference is to the kings of Canaan.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:3)


**ועצמותיהם יגרם.** ישבר הגרם שהוא העצם וכן מלת עצמו וככה מסעף פארה לא תפאר:


AND SHALL BREAK THEIR BONES IN PIECES. *Garem* means a bone. *Yigarem* means he shall break their bones.²⁵ means a bone. The verb formed from this root means to break a bone. The word *itzemo*²⁶ (bone). (hath broken his bones) (Jer. 50:17) is similar. So too is the word *mesa’ef*²⁷ (bough). (shall lop) in *Shall lop the boughs with terror* (Is. 10:33) and *tefa’er*²⁸ (bough). (go over the boughs) in *thou shalt not go over the boughs* (Deut. 24 :20).²⁹

footnotes:
²⁵ Garem
²⁶ A verb related to the noun etzem
²⁷ A verb related to the word se’if
²⁸ A verb related to the noun pe’er
²⁹ All of these verbs mean to destroy whatever their noun roots refer to.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:8:4)


**וחציו ימחץ.** כל חץ מחציו ימחץ וכן וצדיקים ככפיר יבטח בנות צעדה וכן מברכיך ברוך כל אחד ממברכיך יהיה ברוך וכן אורריך:


AND PIERCE THEM THROUGH WITH HIS ARROWS. Each one of his arrows shall pierce.³⁰ (arrows) is in the plural, and *yimchatz* (pierce), the verb which governs it, is in the singular. Hence I.E.’s comment. So too, *But the righteous are secure as a young lion* (Prov. 28:1);³¹ (the righteous) is in the plural while *yivtach* (is secure) is in the singular. *Its branches run over the wall* (Gen. 49:22); *Blessed be every one that blesseth thee* (v. 9).³² (blessed be) is in the singular; *me-varekhekha* (they that bless thee) is in the plural. Similarly, *korerekha* is in the plural, and *arur* is in the singular. The latter means, everyone that blesses you wil be blessed. So too, *and cursed be everyone that curseth thee* (*Ibid*.).

footnotes:
³⁰ According to I.E. when a verb in the singular governs a noun in the plural, the verb refers to each one within the plural (see Vol. 1, p. XVII). In our verse the word chitzav
³¹ It means that each one of the righteous is secure as a young lion, for tzadikim
³² Barukh

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:9:1

**כרע שכב.** הטעם שיירש ישראל ארץ כנען ואחר כך תשקוט הארץ:

### Verse 10

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:10:1

### Verse 14

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:14:1)


**לכה איעצך.** י"א הטעם על בנות מואב וזה דבר רחוק בעבור שאמר באחרית הימים והנכון בעיני שהוא כמשמעו אתן לך עצה שתדע מה תעשה כי זה העם כן יעשה לעמך:


AND I WILL ANNOUNCE³⁴, literally, I will advise thee. TO THEE. Some say that its meaning is: I will advise you with regard to the daughters of Moab. However, this is far-fetched, for Scripture says, *in the end of days*.³⁵ I believe that we should take it according to its plain meaning; namely, I will advise you, so that you know what you should do, for this people will do this to your people.³⁶

### Verse 17

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:1)


**אראנו ולא עתה.** הקרוב אלי כי זאת הנבוא' על דוד אמר ולא עתה כי אחרי ארבע מאות שנה היתה:


I SEE HIM, BUT NOT NOW. It appears to me that this prophecy refers to King David. Balaam said but not now, for it referred to an event four hundred years later.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:2)


**אשורנו ולא קרוב.** הטעם כפול והמשל הוא כוכב ושבט:


I BEHOLD HIM, BUT NOT NIGH. Scripture repeats itself.³⁷ repeats, *I see him, but not now*. The star and the sceptre are the parable.

³⁷ I behold him, but not nigh


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:3)


**דרך כוכב.** יש דמות כוכבים דורכים ברקיע שלא היו בתולדת ולא נודעו:


THERE SHALL STEP FORTH A STAR. There are images of stars that step forth in the sky that never existed and were never known.³⁸

footnotes:
³⁸ New stars appear that were never known. I.E. is probably referring to comets. His point is that the king referred to in our verse shall suddenly appear.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:4)


**וקם שבט.** מלכות והנכון בעיני כי מלת דרך שיראה בדרכו כטעם הכוכבים ממסילותם ורבים פירשוהו על המשיח והקדמונים אמרו סנחריב בלבל הגוים והנה מואב ועמלק ואשור ועוד למה השלים וענו עבר רק היה ראוי להיות דרך כוכב בסוף משלו וחסירי דעת יחשבו כי המפרש דרך כוכב על דוד הוא מכחש ביאת המשיח חלילה חלילה כי המשיח מבואר היטב בנבואת דניאל כאשר פירשתי שהזכיר עמידת מלכי יון וקום החשמונים ועמידת בית שני ושני המצור והגליות והישועה זה אחר זה ואין צורך לנביא בעולם עם דברי משה שהוא העיקר אם יהיה נדחך בקצה השמים ושב ה' אלהיך את שבותך:


AND A SCEPTRE³⁹ (a rod). Hence I.E.’s comment. SHALL ARISE. The reference is to a royal sceptre.I believe that *shall step* means that the star shall be seen making its way, as in *The stars in their courses* (Jud. 5:20). Many explained that the reference is to the messiah. The ancients said that Sanherib⁴⁰ mixed up the nations.⁴¹ 54a. However, note that Scripture speaks of Moab, Amalek, and Asshur.⁴² Furthermore, why does Balaam conclude with *and shall afflict Eber* (v. 24)?⁴³ On the contrary, Balaam should have ended his parable with *There shall step forth a star out of Jacob*.⁴⁴ Those who lack intelligence think that anyone who explains that *There shall step forth a star* refers to King David denies the coming of the messiah.⁴⁵ Far be it, far be it, for the messiah is clearly stated in the prophecy of Daniel. I have already explained that Daniel first mentions the coming into existence of the Greek kings⁴⁶ and the arising of the Hasmoneans. He mentions the existence of the second temple, the years of the siege, the exile, and the salvation. He notes these things in order. There is no conceivable need for another prophet,⁴⁷ since we have the words of Moses, who is the most important prophet: *If any of thine that are dispersed be in the uttermost parts of heaven… the Lord thy God will bring thee* (Deut. 30:4,5).⁴⁸

footnotes:
³⁹ Hebrew, shevet
⁴⁰ An Assyrian king who lived in the eighth century.
⁴¹ See Yoma
⁴² Thus our verse deals with the pre-Sanherib period. Therefore it cannot pertain to the messiah.
⁴³ Eber refers to the Israelites. If Balaam’s prophecy refers to the messiah, why does he conclude with the affliction of Israel? Israel will not be afflicted in the messianic era.
⁴⁴ For that is what will take place in the messianic era after Israel is afflicted.
⁴⁵ For “those who lack intelligence” believe that this verse is necessary for belief in the coming of the messiah. Therefore one who interprets this verse as referring to King David denies the coming of the messiah.
⁴⁶ The kingdoms of the Greeks.
⁴⁷ We would know about the coming of the messiah even if we did not have the Book of Daniel.
⁴⁸ Back to the Land of Israel. In other words, although our verse does not allude to the messiah, Deuteronomy does. I.E. notes this because he wants to show that Moses predicted the coming of the messiah.

**ומחץ פאתי מואב.** כן עשה דוד:


AND SHALL SMITE THROUGH THE CORNERS OF MOAB. This is what David did.⁴⁹

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:6)


**וקרקר.** כמו מקרקר קיר והטעם הורס הקיר כמו מסעף:


AND BREAK DOWN. The word *karkar* (break down) is similar to *mekarkar kir* (Is. 22:5), the meaning of which is, breaking the wall. It is similar to the word *mesa’ef* (shall lop) (Is. 10:33).⁵⁰

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:17:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:17:7)


**בני שת.** כמו בני אדם כי הוא העיקר כי נח מבני בניו היה ויאמר היצחקי כי פירשו על בני עמון גם מואב והטעם כפול גם אמר שהוא מגזרת וחשפי שת רמז לבנות לוט והוא רחוק בעיני כי השת הוא האחור שהוא כמו היסוד כמו כי השתות יהרסון ורבים פירשו כל בני שת מגזרת כי השתות והטעם כי יהרום המדינות ואיננו רחוק:


THE SONS OF SETH. People. Seth was the most important son of Adam, for Noah was descended from him. Yitzchaki⁵¹ says that the reference is to the Amonites and the Moabites, and that Scripture repeats itself.⁵² He also says that the word Seth is derived from the word *shet* (buttocks) in *and with buttocks uncovered* (Is. 20:4) and that it alludes to the daughters⁵³ of Lot.⁵⁴ However, this appears to me to be farfetched, for *shet* refers to the backside,⁵⁵ which is the foundation of the body.⁵⁶ is foundation, not buttock. Compare, *ki ha-shatot yeharesun* (When the foundations are destroyed) (Ps. 11:3). Many others have explained that the word *shet* (Seth) in *all the sons of Seth*, is related to the word *ha-shatot* (foundations) in *When the foundations are destroyed*. It means that the cities will be destroyed.⁵⁷ (and break down all the sons of Seth) means: and will break down all foundations. This interpretation is not far-fetched.

⁵² Sons of Seth is another term for the Moabites.
⁵³ The ancestresses of the Moabites and the Ammonites. See Gen. 19:37,38.
⁵⁴ Their father, whose nakedness his daughters uncovered. See Gen. 19:30-38.
⁵⁵ While Lot’s daughters uncovered his front. The point is, Scripture would not allude to intercourse by employing the phrase “uncovering the buttocks.”
⁵⁶ In other words, the basic meaning of shet
⁵⁷ According to this interpretation, ve-karkar kol bene shet



### Verse 18

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:18:1)


**והיה אדום ירשה.** מגזרת ואם לא תורישו והוא שם במשקל קח לך לבנה והוא תחת תאר השם כמו והיתה הארץ שממה ויודע כי גברה יד דוד על אדום והר שעיר:


AND EDOM SHALL BE A POSSESSION. The word *yereshah* (possession) is related to the word *torishu* (drive out)⁵⁸ destroy. See I.E. on Ex. 15:9. in *But if ye will not drive out* (Num. 33:55).⁵⁹ (And Edom shall be a possession) as, and Edom shall be destroyed. *Yereshah* is a noun vocalized according to the pattern of the word *levenah* (tile) in *take thee a tile* (Ezek. 4:1). It is in place of an adjective. Compare the word *shemamah* (desolation) in *and it shall be a desolation* (Is. 17:9).⁶⁰) in place of an adjective (*shomemah*). We know that David’s hand overpowered Edom⁶¹ and Mount Seir.⁶²

### Verse 20

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:20:1)


**וירא את עמלק.** בדרך נבואה והמשל ראשית גוים ופירושו כי הוא הגוי הנלחם עם ישראל בראשונה ואחריתו עד שיהיה אובד וכן היה בימי שאול שהמית מאיש ועד אשה מעולל ועד יונק וזקן ורבים פירשו ראשית גוים כי הם יחשבו בראש הגוים ולא היה כן:


AND HE LOOKED ON AMALEK. Via prophecy.⁶⁵ *The first of the nations* is the parable. It means Amalek was the first nation to wage war against Israel, but his end shall be utter destruction. This⁶⁶ took place in the days of Saul, who slew *man and woman, infant and suckling* (I Sam. 15:3) and old men. Many explain that the meaning of *the first of the nations* is that Amalek was considered to be the head of the nations. However, this was not the case.⁶⁷

footnotes:
⁶⁵ He did not actually look at Amalek.
⁶⁶ The destruction of Amalek.
⁶⁷ Amalek was never the chief of the nations.



### Verse 21

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:21:1)


**וירא את הקיני.** משפחת יתרו וכתוב המה הקינים וחבר הקיני והמשל ושים בסלע קנך. והטעם כפול כי אילו היה מושבך גבוה כקן העוף לא יצילך:


AND HE LOOKED ON THE KENITE.⁶⁸ The family of Jethro. Scripture writes, *These are the Kenites* (I Chron. 2:55) and *Now Heber the Kenite* (Jud. 4:11).⁶⁹. This shows that the Kenites are descended from Moses’ father-in-law. *And though thy nest be set in the rock* is the parable.⁷⁰ The latter repeats the preceding clause.⁷¹ repeats *Though firm be thy dwelling place*. Its meaning is, though your dwelling place be as high as the nest of a bird, it will not save you.

footnotes:
⁶⁸ The father-in-law of Moses.
⁶⁹ The verse reads, Now Heber the Kenite had severed himself from the Kenites, even from the children of Hobab the father-in-law of Moses
⁷⁰ Mentioned earlier in the verse.
⁷¹ And though thy nest be set in the rock



### Verse 22

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:22:1)


**לבער קין.** כמו ושבה והיתה לבער וכן כאשר יבער הגלל ובערת הרע וקין הוא הקיני:


KAIN SHALL BE WASTED. *Le-va’er* (wasted) is similar to the word *le-va’er* (eaten up) in *it shall again be eaten up* (Is. 6:13); and *yeva’er* (sweepeth) in *as a man sweepeth away dung* (I Kings 14:10); and *u-vi’arta* (so shalt thou put away) in *So shalt thou put away the evil from thee* (Deut. 13:6).⁷² means to destroy. Kain and the Kenite are one and the same.

footnotes:
⁷² In other words, le-va’er


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:22:2)


וטעם **עד מה אשור תשבך.** כאומר עד מתי תשבך אשור לא יעזבך כי אם היתה לבער ואשור לשון נקבה והטעם מחנה וכן ותפול שבא ותקחם ותערוך ישראל:


[HOW LONG? ASSHUR SHALL CARRY THEE AWAY CAPTIVE.] Balaam, as it were, said: How long shall Asshur carry thee⁷³ away captive? He will not let go of you until you are wasted. Asshur is feminine. Asshur is short for the camp of Asshur. Compare, *va-tipple sheva va-tikkachem* (and the Sabeans made a raid) (Job 1:15); *Va-ta’arokh yisra’el* (and Israel…put the battle in array) (I Sam. 17:21).

footnotes:
⁷³ The Kenites.



### Verse 23

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:23:1)


**וישא משלו ויאמר.** הוא וצים מיד כאשר הזכיר שאשור ישבה הקיני אמר גם יבא עת שיעונה אשור ועבר עמו והטעם העברים שישבה אשור כי הקינים היו דרים עם ישראל ופירוש המתרגם ארמית ידוע רק אין מנהג הכתוב להזכיר עבר בלא נהר. יש מפרשים וצים לשון רבים מגזרת וצי אדיר וי"א שפירושו כמו ופגשו ציים את איים כי דמם לציים שישום אדם מראותם:


AND HE TOOK UP HIS PARABLE, AND SAID. The reference is to *But ships shall come from the coast of Kittim* (v. 24). When Balaam mentioned that the Kenite would be carried away captive, he also said that a time would come when Asshur would be afflicted and Eber along with him. The latter refers to the Hebrews that Asshur carried away captive, for the Kenites lived with the Israelites. The interpretation of the Aramaic translation is well known.⁷⁴ (Eber) by *ever perat* (the other side of the Euphrates). However, it is not the practice of Scripture to mention the word *ever* without the word *nahar* (river) following it.⁷⁵ Some explain that *tzim* (ships) is a plural. It is related to the word *tzi* (ship) in gallant ship (Is. 33:21). Others say that the word *tzim* is related to the word *tziyim* (wild-cats) *in And the wild-cats shall meet with the jackals* (Is. 34:14). Balaam compared the Kittim to wild-cats because people will be gripped by fear when they appear.⁷⁶

footnotes:
⁷⁴ Onkelos renders ever
⁷⁵ When referring to the other side of the Euphrates.
⁷⁶ These commentators explain our verse as follows: wild-cats shall come from the land of Kittim.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:23:2)


וטעם **אוי מי יחיה משמו אל.** הטעם על מלך אשור ששם נפשו כמו אל וכן כתיב בספר ישעיה או טעמו מי יחיה מהגזרות ששם אל בעולם על ידי אשור כי מלך אשור נצח את כל הגוים כי כן כתוב:


[ALAS, WHO SHALL LIVE AFTER GOD HATH APPOINTED HIM.] The reference is to the king of Asshur who made himself into a god. The latter is noted in the Book of Isaiah.⁷⁷ Or its meaning is: Who shall survive the decrees which God placed in the world via Asshur, for the king of Assyria conquered all the nations. The latter is clearly stated in Scripture.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ See Is. 10:7. Isaiah did not actually say this. He intimated that the king of Assyria, in destroying nations, acted like a god.



### Verse 24

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:1)


**וכתים.** מבני יון כי כן כתוב ויתכן להיות רמז למלכות יון וכבר ביארתי בספר דניאל כי מלכות יון וכתים אחת היא והיא החיה השלישית בראיות:


OF KITTIM. They are descended from Javin, for the latter is clearly stated in Scripture (Gen. 10:4). It is also possible that Scripture alludes to the kingdom of the Greeks. I have already explained in the Book of Daniel, that the kingdom of the Greeks and that of the Kittim are one and the same.⁷⁸ instead of *kittim*. *Aram* stands for the Romans. See I.E. on Dan. 2:39. There is clear proof that it is the third beast.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁸ See I.E. on Dan. 2:39. Some texts read aram
⁷⁹ See Dan. 7:6.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:2)


**מיד כתים.** ממקום כמו על יד הירדן ויד תהיה לך:


FROM THE COAST OF KITTIM. *Mi-yad* (from the coast of) means from the place. Compare the word *yad* in *the side* (yad) *of the Jordan* (Num. 13:29) and *Thou shalt have a place* (Deut. 23:13).


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:24:3)


**וגם הוא.** כתים שהוא שם יחיד ואם הוא על משפט לשון רבים והעד ובני יון אלישה ותרשיש כתים:


AND ALSO HE. The Kittim. Kittim is singular even though it comes in the plural form. *And the sons of Javan: Elishah, and Tarshish, Kittim* (Gen. 10:4) is proof of this.



### Verse 25

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 24:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/24:25:1)


**ויקם בלעם.** כי שוכב היה ותרדמה נפלה עליו וכן אמר נופל וגלוי עינים:


AND BALAAM ROSE UP. For he was lying down. A deep sleep had fallen over him. Scripture clearly states, *Fallen down, yet with opened eyes* (v. 16).



## Chapter 25


### Verse 1

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:1:1)


**בשטים.** כי כן כתוב ויחנו על הירדן מבית הישימות עד השטים והכל בערבות מואב כי לא זזו משם גם יהושע משם שלח מרגלים:


IN SHITTIM. For Scripture clearly states, *And they pitched by the Jordan, from Beth-jeshimoth even unto Abel-shittim* (Num. 33:49).¹ Everything took place in the plains of Moab, for Israel did not move from there.² Also, Joshua sent spies from there (Josh. 2:1).

footnotes:
¹ In other words, Israel was in Shittim during the incident of Balaam. They did not journey there after the incident.
² Until Israel crossed the Jordan and entered Canaan.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:1:2)


ולפי דעתי כי **ויחל** מהבנין הכבד הנוסף:


[AND THE PEOPLE BEGAN.] I believe that *va-yachel* (began) is a *hifil*.



### Verse 2

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:2:1)


**וישתחוו.** ישראל ובנות מואב:


AND BOWED DOWN. Israel and the daughters of Moab.³ (va-yokhal), *and bowed down* (va-yishtachavu). *Va-yokhal* is in the singular. *Va-yishtachavu* is in the plural. Hence I.E.’s comment.

footnotes:
³ Our verse reads: And the people did eat



### Verse 3

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:3:1)


**ויצמד ישראל.** מגזרת צמד בקר והטעם הנשים והנה נצמדו עמם לדת פעור:


AND ISRAEL JOINED HIMSELF. The word *va-yitzamed* is related to the word *tzemed* (yoke) in *a yoke of oxen* (I Sam. 11:7). The reference is to the Moabite women; that is, Israel was joined with the women in the religion of *pe’or*.



### Verse 4

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:4:1)


**והוקע אותם.** הטעם על הנצמדי' והעד שהוא כתוב ויאמר משה אל שופטי ישראל:


AND HANG THEM. The reference is to those who were joined.⁴ The proof text is, *And Moses said unto the judges of Israel* (v. 5).⁵

footnotes:
⁴ Not to the chiefs of the people. The text is vague. Hence I.E.’s comment.
⁵ I.E. identifies the chiefs with the judges.


###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:4:2)


**והוקע אותם.** כמו ויוקיעם בהר כדברי המתרגם ארמית והעד נגד השמש והטעם בפרהסיא כמו לעיני השמש הזאת:


AND HANG THEM. *Ve-hoka otam* (and hang them) is similar to *va-yaki’um* (and they hanged them) in *and they hanged them in the mountain* (II Sam. 21:9). It is to be understood in accordance with the words of the Aramaic translation.⁶: execute those who are guilty of a capital crime. However, I.E.’s reference might be to Targum Jonathan, who renders *ve-hiki’um, u-tzelovinun* as, and hang them (Krinsky). However, it should also be noted that *u-tzelovinun* can also be rendered, and crucify them. The proof text is, *In the face of the sun*.⁷ The meaning of *in the face of the sun* is, in public. Compare, *in the sight of this sun* (II Sam. 12:11).⁸. The next verse continues, *but I will do this thing before all Israel, and before the sun*.

footnotes:
⁶ Onkelos renders u-ketol de-chayyav ketol
⁷ That the reference is to public execution. For they were hung in the mountain so that all in the vicinity could see them executed.
⁸ The entire clause reads, and he shall lie with thy wives in the sight of this sun



### Verse 5

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:5:1)


**הרגו איש את אנשיו.** הטעם מאיזה שבט יהיו וידוע כי הרגו ואם לא הזכיר הכתוב:


SLAY YE EVERY ONE HIS MEN. From whatever tribe they may be. It is known that they slew,⁹. even though Scripture does not mention this.¹⁰.

⁹ Those who worshipped pe’or
¹⁰ That the judges slew those who worshipped pe’or

###### Ibn Ezra on Numbers 25:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Ibn%20Ezra%20on%20Numbers/r/25:6:1)


**ויקרב אל אחיו.** נתנה למשפחתו:


AND BROUGHT UNTO HIS BRETHREN. He gave her to his family.
